# D2: the step map with the Wilson loop insertion

Companion of WILSON_IT1_LINE_DEFECT_2026_10_21.md (the *IT-1 note*). Its §4 defines the step-1 class and the target D1, and its gaps block lists the D2 items. D2's first two items are in the IT-1 note: §3i (the thin form of E^∂) and §3j (the carrier dependence of the t > 0 species). This file holds the rest, from D2.0 on.

*Base file.* The base is WILSON_FIRST_RG_STEP_V4.tex (hash d463c554b76f). D2.3 cites V4 line numbers. Everything before D2.3 cites V3 (WILSON_FIRST_RG_STEP_V3.tex, hash a520a59f363d), and its "L<n>" cites are V3 lines. They map to V4 as follows: V3 L88–L179 → +31; V3 L475–L3241 → +64; V3 L3242 onwards → +290. V4 inserts Theorem 2 (thm:two-loop-theorem, rem:two-loop-regime, V4 L211–242) and Section 10 (sec:two-loops). Every V3 passage and label that D2 cites has the same text in V4, apart from typography (\leqslant, \coloneqq, \bigcdot, braces), checked with a normalised comparison (scratchpad map_v3_v4.py, diff_ctx.py).

## WHAT THE CONSTRUCTION IS

- **After one RG step the Wilson loop is no longer a Wilson loop.** As a function of the block field it is a dressed loop, a line defect: κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}[f_k + Σ_𝔅Q_k(𝔅)·words]. Here f_k is the holonomy of Bałaban's background U_k(V_k), not of V_k; the words carry gauge-covariant insertions Y_W along C; near large fields it depends on the pending (outer) variables. Already V4's leading term is W_χ(C_n;U₁) with U₁ the rebuilt fine field.
- **Consequences.**
  - V4's Theorem 1 cannot be iterated: its input is a Wilson loop and its output is not one. The induction runs on the class D(k), and everything rests on the step map D(k) → D(k+1) with constants uniform in k (D2.2–D2.4).
  - Bałaban's construction of the measure is reused unchanged, but none of his estimates control the dressed loop: every fluctuation integral with the loop needs its own cluster expansion (Bałaban/Dimock bound partition functions only).
  - The dressing has couplings: the perimeter part (grows like L^{K−k}) and the corner part must be absorbed exactly into δm_k and z_k at every step; the deviation insertions must contract (L^{−1}(1+β₀)⁸ per step) and are summed only at k = K.
  - The words are only gauge-covariant, so the representative of the gauge orbit matters (the representative gap); inside Bałaban's construction it never did.
  - The dependence on outer variables near large fields means the large-field terms cannot be counted; they are carried as local activities (D2.6).
  - The answer is E[W(C)] = κ^{(K)}·⟨𝒲_K⟩_final + errors; the continuum limit exists along subsequences of lattice spacings, one subsequence for every admissible loop, as a weak limit point of the stopping-scale effective measures tested against the fixed coarse loop plus a bounded remainder (WILSON_IT1_CONTINUUM_LIMIT_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md item (8), derived given D(K′) on every labelled term; the smallness of the remainder's large-field part is that note's item (7), reduced to a bound (Q) on one residual quotient, not derived); its convergence along the full sequence needs the Cauchy property across K of the renormalised expectation, a comparison of two runs, not in D2 (that note's item (9)).
- The target stays the bare Wilson loop; the dressing is how its renormalisation is computed, not a change of observable.
- How far the dressed object is from the coarse Wilson loop after one step: next section. A V4-ready version (proposition, proof, remark, in V4's notation and labels) is WILSON_V4_COARSE_LOOP_INSERT_DRAFT_2026_10_23.tex. It is in V4 as Proposition prop:coarse-loop-form and Remark rem:coarse-loop-form, after Corollary cor:main-leading-size (V4 L239 on; hash 5154b2eadc35; builds, no undefined references; not reviewed). From here on in this file, V4 line numbers after L238 are shifted by +76 (5496 → 5572 lines). It uses V4's Lip_χ, giving |W_χ(C_n;V) − W_χ(C_n;U₁)| ≤ Lip_χ(e^{6c_hLn_Cε₁} − 1). At one step there is no fluctuation integral left over which the difference could average: both loops are functions of V, and any average is over the coarse ensemble E_h, i.e. the later steps.
- **That bound is the pointwise bound integrated; it uses no cancellation and is not an expectation-level comparison.** The expectation-level comparison, with the step-2 fluctuation average taken before any absolute value, is the section ONE MORE STEP below. There the first-order insertions are even in the coupling at step 2, so their first-order mean vanishes in the interior (away from S₂, the layer Ω₂∖Ω₂^{∼−1} and σ-dependent terms); a size bound is derived (gaps block, the class (i)/(ii) entry, 26 Oct): for activities without an old-factor link through the complex-coupling sup (the scaling identity), c_λρ₂·(decay), and for activities with a link through the 𝐄₁ link's absolute radius ε₁^{RG2}/g₁, the same bound. The perimeter and corner parts are multiplicative and are defined exactly via D2.3, given its inputs (derived; the gaps block). An additive background term Δ_bg remains, of order B₃ε₂L^{−2} per scale-1 bond, and the exponent difference of the two dressings is bounded on the flat term ℓ₀ by (6) and is D2.6's on the other terms.

## ONE STEP: THE COARSE WILSON LOOP AGAINST V4'S LEADING TERM (derived; not reviewed)

*Result.* On A_cov, |W_χ(C_n;V) − W_χ(C_n;U₁)| ≤ e^{4nc_πξ} − 1 with ξ ≤ 2.12c_hA_Lε₁, A_L ≤ (d−1)(d+4)L²/8; for d = 4, ≤ e^{6.4c_πc_hLn_Cε₁} − 1. Per fine contour bond the exponent is O(Lε₁) = O(Lg polylog), against O(ρε₁) = O(g³ polylog) for V4's E_def. So the coarse loop differs from V4's leading term at first order in the curvature, and the difference is of order gℓ_C/ε for a loop of fixed physical side ℓ_C, where E_def is of order g³ℓ_C/ε.

*Setting.* V4 Theorem 1, fine units, d = 4 where numbers are given. C_n has 4n coarse bonds c_0, …, c_{4n−1}; each is the straight fine path of L bonds from c_− to c_+ = c_− + Le_μ. U₁(c) is the product of U₁ along the fine bonds of c, so V4's leading word is W_χ(C_n;U₁) = d_π^{−1}tr π(U₁(c_0)⋯U₁(c_{4n−1})), and the coarse Wilson loop is W_χ(C_n;V) = d_π^{−1}tr π(V(c_0)⋯V(c_{4n−1})). ‖·‖ is the operator norm, and c_π := sup{‖π_*(Y)‖ : Y ∈ 𝔤, ‖Y‖ ≤ 1}, a constant of G ⊂ U(N) and π.

*Inputs.*
- (I1) CRE (1.12), p.248: U₁ is the critical point of A + 𝐆 on {U = V₀ on Ω₁ᶜ, Ū = V on Ω₁^{(1)}}. V4 L4672 records U₁ as this critical minimum, and V4 Theorem 1(i) fixes RG1 (0.4) as the average. So V(c) = Ū₁(c) for every coarse bond c of Ω₁^{(1)}.
- (I2) RG1 (0.4), p.253: Ū(c) = e^{iX_c}U(c) with X_c := Σ_{x∈B(c_−)}L^{−d}Σ_{Γ∈G(c_−,x)}|G(c_−,x)|^{−1}Σ_{Γ′∈G(c_+,x′)}|G(c_+,x′)|^{−1}(1/i)log U(ℓ), ℓ = ℓ(x,Γ,Γ′) := Γ∪[x,x′]∪(−Γ′)∪(−c), a closed fine loop at c_−, and [x,x′] the translate of c to x. The weights are nonnegative and sum to 1, so X_c is a convex combination of the (1/i)log U(ℓ).
- (I3) RG1 (0.3), p.252: B(y) is the set of fine points of the cube of side L (the coarse spacing) centred at y. With the weight L^{−d} of (0.4) this means x = y + Σ_νn_νe_ν with |n_ν| ≤ (L−1)/2, L odd; the print "|n_μ| ≤ L − 1/2" is read as (L−1)/2, the only bound that gives L^d points. G(y,x) consists of the monotone staircase paths of displacement n, one for each order of the nonzero directions.
- (I4) V4 Lemma c2-plaquette-deviation (L3886–3889): on every branch, ‖U₁(∂p) − 1‖ ≤ 2c_hε₁, with ε₁ = g₁p₀(g₁), for every fine plaquette p with a bond within distance 3R_c of I. On A_cov, Λ_b ⊆ I = Λ₁^* for every contour bond b (V4 L2223, L261), so each coarse bond c of C_n has a site in Λ₁. Every bond and plaquette used below lies in the two endpoint blocks of c and the paths between them, within ℓ^∞ distance 2L of that site. Lemma collar-coverage(i) puts all sites within ℓ^∞ distance LMR₁ − 1 > 2L of Λ₁ in Ω₁^{∼−1}, so c is a bond of Ω₁^{(1)} and (I1) applies; 3R_c ≥ 3(2R_loc + 1) = 12L + 15 > 8L + 1, so (I4) applies to all those plaquettes.

*(1) Area of ℓ.* Let Γ₊ be the translate of Γ by Le_μ; it lies in G(c_+,x′), since x′ − c_+ = x − c_−.
- (a) Γ′ and Γ₊ have the same displacement n. Going from the order of Γ′ to that of Γ₊ by adjacent transpositions inverts each pair of directions at most once, and swapping two consecutive straight segments of lengths |n_α|, |n_β| moves the path across |n_α||n_β| plaquettes.
- (b) Moving [x,x′] back along Γ one bond at a time crosses L plaquettes for a bond of direction ±ν, ν ≠ μ, and none for a bond of direction ±μ: there the two paths agree after cancelling backtracks.
- So ℓ is contracted to the trivial loop in at most A_L := Σ_{α<β}|n_α||n_β| + L(|n|₁ − |n_μ|) plaquette moves. With |n|₁ − |n_μ| ≤ (d−1)(L−1)/2, and Σ_{α<β}|n_α||n_β| ≤ (d−1)d(L−1)²/8 by Cauchy–Schwarz (Σ_{α<β}a_αa_β ≤ (d−1)(Σa)²/(2d), |n|₁ ≤ d(L−1)/2), A_L ≤ (d−1)(d+4)L²/8, i.e. A_L ≤ 3L² for d = 4.

*(2) Per coarse bond.* A move across a plaquette p multiplies the holonomy at the base point by gU₁(∂p)^{±1}g^{−1}, with g ∈ G the transport from the base point along the current loop. So U₁(ℓ) is a product of at most A_L such conjugates. For unitary W and g, ‖gWg^{−1} − 1‖ = ‖W − 1‖ = ‖W^{−1} − 1‖, and ‖∏(1 + a_m) − 1‖ ≤ ∏(1 + ‖a_m‖) − 1, so
  ‖U₁(ℓ) − 1‖ ≤ (1 + 2c_hε₁)^{A_L} − 1 ≤ e^{s} − 1 =: t, s := 2c_hA_Lε₁.
For unitary W with ‖W − 1‖ ≤ t < 2, the principal logarithm satisfies ‖(1/i)log W‖ ≤ 2arcsin(t/2), since |e^{iφ} − 1| = 2|sin(φ/2)| for φ ∈ (−π, π]. By (I2), ‖X_c‖ ≤ ξ := 2arcsin(t/2), and ‖V(c)U₁(c)^{−1} − 1‖ = ‖e^{iX_c} − 1‖ ≤ 2sin(ξ/2) = t. For s ≤ 1/10, ξ ≤ 1.06s. For d = 4: ‖V(c)U₁(c)^{−1} − 1‖ ≤ e^{6c_hL²ε₁} − 1 and ‖X_c‖ ≤ 6.4c_hL²ε₁.

*(3) The loop.* Put R_j := e^{iX_{c_j}}, H_j := U₁(c_0)⋯U₁(c_{j−1}) (H_0 := 1) and R̃_j := H_jR_jH_j^{−1}. Then V(c_0)⋯V(c_{4n−1}) = R̃_0⋯R̃_{4n−1}·U₁(c_0)⋯U₁(c_{4n−1}). Since ‖π(e^{iY}) − 1‖ ≤ ‖π_*(Y)‖ ≤ c_π‖Y‖ for Y ∈ 𝔤, and |d_π^{−1}tr((π(R) − 1)π(H))| ≤ ‖π(R) − 1‖ for unitary π(H),
  |W_χ(C_n;V) − W_χ(C_n;U₁)| ≤ (1 + c_πξ)^{4n} − 1 ≤ e^{4nc_πξ} − 1 ≤ e^{6.4c_πc_hLn_Cε₁} − 1 on A_cov (d = 4, n_C = 4nL).
Expanded, the difference is a sum of words with insertions π(R̃_j) − 1 of norm ≤ c_πξ on nonempty sets of coarse bonds, the shape of V4's deviation words, with insertions of first order in the curvature instead of order ρε₁.

*(4) In V4's identity.* On A_cov, write W_χ(C_n;U₁) = W_χ(C_n;V) + [W_χ(C_n;U₁) − W_χ(C_n;V)]. Then
  E_ε[W_χ(C_n)] = κ_{C_n}E_h[1_{A_cov}e^{E°_ξ}W_χ(C_n;V)] + E_coarse + E_def + E_bdry,
  |E_coarse| ≤ |κ_{C_n}|(e^{6.4c_πc_hLn_Cε₁} − 1)E_h[1_{A_cov}e^{|E°_ξ|}] ≤ |κ_{C_n}|(e^{6.4c_πc_hLn_Cε₁} − 1)exp[Cτ²(2 + 1/c) + n_Cδ^I_q],
the last step by eq:thm-main-moment under the additional hypotheses of Theorem 1(iii).

*(5) What this says.*
- Per fine contour bond the exponent is 6.4c_πc_hLε₁ = O(Lg polylog), against C_χρ(ε₁ + e^{−cb/(LM)}) = O(g³ polylog) in V4's E_def: the coarse loop is a factor of order L/ρ further from the leading term than V4's error.
- For a loop of fixed physical side ℓ_C, n_C ≈ 4ℓ_C/ε, so the bound is e^{O(gℓ_C/ε)} − 1. It is small only for loops with n_C ≪ 1/(Lε₁) fine bonds.
- *Not derived here.* Whether the first-order part is smaller for the minimiser U₁ than for a general regular field: it is, by L^{−2} (ONE MORE STEP (1), from V4's own lemma proof). Whether it averages out under E_h: ONE MORE STEP (5) shows that the step-2 average removes it at first order, by parity in the coupling, in the interior scope of (5) (away from S₂, the layer Ω₂∖Ω₂^{∼−1} and σ-dependent activities), given D2.4's (S-w) (derived, SW closure, 24 Oct; (T) is not used). Off A_cov (contour bonds near large-field regions) there is no bound.
- *Not used.* RG1's second average (0.12), p.254, which RG1 calls equally good: its loops contain averaged contour variables U(c_−, x), and the same bound would need the distance of that mean from the U(Γ). V4 fixes (0.4).

## ONE MORE STEP: THE DRESSED LOOP AGAINST THE COARSE LOOP IN EXPECTATION (derived; repairs not re-reviewed)

*What the objects are, and what that forces.*
- D(V₁) := f₁(V₁) − g₁(V₁), with f₁(V₁) := W_χ(C;U₁(V₁)) (V4's leading word) and g₁(V₁) := χ(Hol^{(1)}_C V₁) (the coarse Wilson loop, V4's W_χ(C_n;V)), is a function of the coarse field. At step 1 nothing averages it. The first average that can cancel anything is the step-2 fluctuation integral. Below, D is written exactly in the step-2 variables, and the average is taken before any absolute value.
- **Result in one line.** After the step-2 integral, in the small-field interior, D splits into:
  - (a) a perimeter and corner part. It is not small: O(g₁²) per scale-1 bond at Gaussian level, so O(g²n_C/L) in total, unbounded as ε → 0 at fixed ℓ_C. For both loops it is defined exactly as cluster sums (D2.3(5)) and absorbed, given D2.3's input (S-w) (derived, SW closure; from which (T′), (Z) and the Kotecký–Preiss condition follow); for g₁ this is D2.2(5)–(6), D2.3 and D2.4 run on the coarse word, whose insertions have the same structural form as D2.1's (not a special case: M(𝐔)-transports, leading word outside D(2), slots on two blocks; (6), first bullet). The values are not computed; their Gaussian (linear-insertion) parts are convergent self-energy sums ((6)).
  - (b) an additive background part Δ_bg = f₂ − g̃₂, a function of V₂ and the pending variables. Its pointwise bound is the step-1 pointwise bound times (1+β₀)B₃L^{−2}. By (7)(c), each step's background term is at every later step j the same comparison with U_j in place of U₂ plus insertion words, pointwise; the B′ = 0 end at the final scale is O(B₃ℓ_Cε_KL^{−(K−k)}) on the terms whose blocks lie in Ω_K, summing to O(B₃ℓ_Cε_K/L) uniformly in K ((7)(d)). The shed words carry the rest pointwise and are the differences of the two chains' insertion words, averaged at the next step as part of both chains' step maps; the coarse chain's absorption is its own D2.3 at every step, and the normalisation it induces is κ^{g_k,(j)}, with the ratio r_j to the fine chain's bounded ((7)(e), 26 Oct, reviewed in round 47: the reorganisation correct, items (i)–(ii) reduced to the interfaces (iii-a), (iii-c), (iv-a)–(iv-c), the remainder R_bad over the labels without the coarse D(K) form added and reduced to (iv-a)–(iv-c), the step map positive at real fields; repairs not re-reviewed).
  - (c) averaged insertion activities. They are even in the coupling at step 2 in the interior scope of (5), so their first-order mean vanishes there. Class (i), without old-factor link: O((δ₁/(θε′))²)·sup per activity by parity and Schwarz in the coupling; the complex-coupling sup is derived (class-activities closure of 26 Oct, a1–a6; reviewed in round 46, it1-class-20261026-r1: structure correct, the premise on the 𝐄₁ link's radius added and the minors applied, not re-reviewed): the deformed integrand is the real-coupling integrand at B̃ = (λ/g₁)B inside RG2 Lemma 2's disc, so the sup is ≤ (C₁^{RG2}θε′/ε_H)·(D2.4(10)'s factors) and the activity is ≤ c_λρ₂·(decay), second order with the amplitude 𝔞₂ = max(τ₁², ρ₂·pl) that D2.3 and (S-w) assume. Class (ii), with a link: the 𝐄₁ difference is an RG2 Lemma 1 term, analytic on |B| < ε₁^{RG2}/g₁ with ε₁^{RG2} absolute (the closure's Premise: the step-1 interior 𝐄^{(1)} is RG1's restricted to Λ₁, CRE p.254 after (1.30), with RG1's absolute widths α₀, α₁, α₂, so RG2 p.7's 4B₀C₁e^{16κ₁}ε₁ ≤ ¼α₂ fixes ε₁^{RG2} before the coupling; the 𝐑₁ difference does not occur for k ≤ N by R-h), so the real point lies at the fraction δ₁/ε₁^{RG2} = O(g₁·polylog) of the link's radius, class (ii) has class (i)'s radius and the same c_λρ₂ bound (closure b1, b4), and directly the Lipschitz bound L_F = 2E_Y/ε₁^{RG2} = O(1)E_Y holds on the real segment (b3) ((5)).
  - The perimeter and corner factors enter multiplicatively, but an additive Δ_bg remains, and it dominates at scale 1: E[f₁] ≈ e^{−(δm₂^f−δm₂^g)P₁(C)+4(ζ₂^f−ζ₂^g)}E[g₁] + (b) + (c) + large fields (corner factor (z₂^f/z₂^g)⁴ with z = e^ζ, D2.3(5), (8)).
- **Consequence for the RG construction.** Pointwise at step k+1 the background term is P_k(C)·O(ε_{k+1}L^{−2}) = O(ℓ_Cε_{k+1}L^{K−k−2}), small only on the last ≈ 2 + log(1/(ℓ_Cε_{k+1}))/log L steps. By (7)(c), at each later step the term splits again, pointwise, into a background part and insertion words, and the background part at the final scale is W_χ(C;U_K) − W_χ(C^{(k)};M^kU_K) = O(B₃ℓ_Cε_KL^{−(K−k)}) by (7)(a)–(d), the iterated-average lemma, on the terms whose blocks lie in Ω_K; summed over the steps this is O(B₃ℓ_Cε_K/L), uniformly in the number of steps. What survives in expectation is reduced to the interfaces (iii-a), (iii-c), (iv-a)–(iv-c) ((7)(e), 26 Oct, reviewed in round 47, repaired, not re-reviewed): E[W(C)] − N_k⟨g_k^{(k)}⟩_k = κ_f^{(K)}⟨e^{𝓔^f_K}β_k^{(K)}⟩_K^{good} + (the exponent difference) + (the insertion difference) + R_bad, the last the whole-label contribution of the labels on which a scale-k block of C leaves Ω_K (the coarse chain has no D(K) form there without (iv-a)'s carrier item), N_k := κ_f^{(k)}e^{E^f_{const,k}}∏_{j>k}r_j with r_j the ratio of the two chains' normalisations, |log r_j| ≤ 2C_S′𝔞^∨_jP_{j−1}(C) + 8C_ζ𝔞^∨_j; the shed words are the differences of the two chains' insertion words, averaged at step j+1 as part of both step maps, their first-order parts even by (5) run at step j+1, their flat second-order parts the chains' own δm_{j+1}, ζ_{j+1}; the class D(j) is extended by the species "composed coarse word of level k" ((7)(e)(iii)); at k = K the chain is trivial and only the fine chain is needed.

*(0) Units.* Bałaban's step-1 units: block spacing 1, fine spacing η = L^{−1}. V4's C_n has P₁(C) = 4n scale-1 bonds c_j and n_C = 4nL fine bonds. Norms: ‖·‖ the operator norm, |·| CRE's matrix norm, ‖Y‖ ≤ c_h|Y| (V4 Conventions, L396 of the current V4).

*(1) The step-1 plaquette bound loses L² (derived from the proof of V4 Lemma lem:c2-plaquette-deviation, V4 L3970).*
- The proof has sup_{p′⊂□′^∼}|U_{1,□′}(∂p′) − 1| < ε₁L^{−2} for the local minimum, and ‖U₁(e) − U_{1,□′}(e)‖ ≤ c_hO(1)B₃e^{−δ_cLM₂R₁}ε₁ on the bonds of □′^{∼2}. The V4 proof bounds the second by c_hε₁L^{−2}/4.
- Keep the exponential: ‖U₁(∂p) − 1‖ ≤ c_hε₁L^{−2} + 4c_hO(1)B₃e^{−δ_cLM₂R₁}ε₁ ≤ 2c_hε₁L^{−2} once 4O(1)B₃L²e^{−δ_cLM₂R₁} ≤ 1, a condition on g₀ (R₁ → ∞ as g₀ → 0, V4's own threshold argument).
- In prop:coarse-loop-form this gives s = 6c_hε₁ in place of 6c_hL²ε₁, and (iii) becomes |f₁ − g₁| ≤ Lip_χ(e^{6c_hn_Cε₁/L} − 1): per scale-1 bond O(ε₁), the curvature of one coarse plaquette. In V4 (Lemma lem:c2-plaquette-deviation proves only the sharpened bound; Proposition prop:coarse-loop-form carries s = 6c_hε₁; reviewed: correct and sufficient).

*(2) The step-2 variables (quoted; D2.1(1)).*
- CRE (3.10)–(3.11), p.266: V^{(1)} = M(U₂) on Ω₂^{(1)}, and V₁ = e^{iB′}V^{(1)} on B(Γ₂). RG1 pp.267–268: B′ = g₁CA − hD̃(g₁CA), with A the scaled fluctuation (B in RG1 (2.12)) and C the operator that eliminates the variables B′(b₀(c)) through the constraint on the corridor B(c).
- RG1 (2.12)–(2.13), p.268: the step-2 integral is ∫dμ_{C^{(1)}}(A)χ₁exp[P^{(1)}(g₁,U₂,A) + {𝐄₁(U₁(e^{iB′}V^{(1)})) − 𝐄₁(U₁(V^{(1)}))}], with μ_{C^{(1)}} the centred Gaussian of covariance (C*Δ^{(1)}C)^{−1}. RG1 p.267: the linear term vanishes, "−(1/g_k)⟨H₁B′,J⟩ = 0", since U₂ is the constrained critical point. RG1 p.268: "the expression under the exponential above vanishes at g_k = 0".
- CRE (3.16), p.268: the small-field cutoff is χ^{(1)}(R₂ᶜ) = ∏_{□′}χ({sup_{b∈(□′^{∼2})^{(1)*}}|A₁(b)| < δ₁}). The print has Σ on the R^c factor; it is read as ∏, the transcriber's flagged intent. That (3.16) tests the unscaled field g₁A, so that |A| < δ₁/g₁ =: r₁ in (5), is a reading of which variable A₁ denotes there (both labelled).
- χ′₁. CRE p.268: "the characteristic functions χ_k′ depend now on the field V^{(k)} … We want to have characteristic functions depending on the fluctuation field only". So χ′₁ is a function of A₁ and V^{(1)}. On cubes of R₂ᶜ it is 1 on the support of χ^{(1)}(R₂ᶜ); on R₂ and on Ω₂∖Ω₂^{∼−1} it remains.
- RG1 (2.12) is quoted for the single-domain case. CRE's multi-domain (3.15), pp.267–268, has more in the curly bracket: the 𝐑₁, 𝐁₁ and A(1/g₁²(·) − 1/g₁², ·) differences, and the factor χ′₁(Ω₂). They are functions of B′ with coefficients that are not substituted in (5), so (5)'s parity is unchanged in the interior.
- Multi-domain operators through CRE p.267's transfer, accepted as Bałaban's statement, as in D2.1(1).

*(3) The exact decomposition (derived).*
- Dressed loop: D2.1(2), (4) give f₁(e^{iB′}V^{(1)}) = f₂ + Σ_{𝔊≠∅}d_π^{−1}tr(Y_{G₁}U_{(1)}⋯Y_{G_n}U_{(n)}), with f₂ := χ(Hol_CU₂) and every Y_G vanishing at B′ = 0.
- Coarse loop: the transport identity of D2.1(3) on the scale-1 lattice gives Hol^{(1)}_C(e^{iB′}V^{(1)}) = ∏_jexp(iAd(Γ_j)B′(c_j))·Hol^{(1)}_C(V^{(1)}), Γ_j the V^{(1)}-transports. Writing each factor as 1 + (e^{iX_j} − 1) gives g₁(e^{iB′}V^{(1)}) = g̃₂ + Σ_{S≠∅}(words with factors e^{iX_j} − 1 on the bonds j ∈ S), with g̃₂ := χ(Hol^{(1)}_CM(U₂)). Each factor vanishes at B′ = 0, and e^{iB′(c_j)} − 1 depends on A near c_j (on a designated bond b₀(c′) ⊂ C, B′(b₀(c′)) depends on the corridor B(c′), still local; the transports Γ_j do not depend on A). g̃₂ is the value at B′ = 0 only where C ⊂ B(Γ₂); elsewhere V₁'s pending values appear in place of M(U₂).
- So D = Δ_bg + [Σ f-words − Σ g-words], with Δ_bg := f₂ − g̃₂ = W_χ(C;U₂) − W_χ(C^{(1)};M(U₂)), a function of V₂ and the pending variables only.

*(4) The background term (derived).*
- Δ_bg is prop:coarse-loop-form's comparison with (U₁, V) replaced by (U₂, M(U₂)). Here V^{(1)}(c) = M(U₂,c) holds by definition, so the location step is not needed.
- Plaquette input: CRE p.268, |∂U₂ − 1| < 2B₃ε₂(L^{−1}η)² on Ω₂, with η = L^{−1}. So ‖U₂(∂p) − 1‖ ≤ 2c_hB₃ε₂L^{−4} for the fine plaquettes of Ω₂. "Plaquettes of Ω₂" is read as plaquettes with all vertices in Ω₂ (labelled reading; D2.0(2) records that CRE's convention was not located).
- If every scale-1 bond c_j of C has its two blocks in Ω₂: for each c_j the Area, One loop, One coarse bond and Loop steps of the proposition give the insertion bound t₂ = e^{s₂} − 1, with s₂ := 3L²·2c_hB₃ε₂L^{−4} = 6c_hB₃ε₂L^{−2}, under s₂ ≤ 1/10 and t₂ ≤ r_G. Hence
  |Δ_bg| ≤ Lip_χ(e^{4ns₂} − 1) = Lip_χ(e^{6c_hB₃n_Cε₂L^{−3}} − 1).
- Against (1): the exponent changes by the factor B₃(ε₂/ε₁)L^{−2} ≤ (1+β₀)B₃L^{−2}, by CRE (2.8), p.256, first line at n − m = 1: ε_n ≤ (1+β₀)(n−m)^{1/2}ε_m. Whether this is a gain, (1+β₀)B₃ < L², is not shown: B₃ depends on L (VAR Theorem 1, p.279; so D2.0(5)(c)(β) does not use 2B₃(1+β₀) ≤ L²). It does not need to be: the per-step factor is never iterated, (7)(c)–(d). (The route through CRE (3.2), (3.17) doesn't work: it costs a factor L^{k+1}e^{−R_k} at step k+1.) Per scale-1 bond it is one scale-1 plaquette of U₂, O(ε₂L^{−2}), as in D2.0(2)'s derived coarse-plaquette bound.
- Where a block of C meets Ω₂ᶜ, there is no bound here (step-2 large fields, D2.6).
- Not averaged at step 2. Its value at every later step and at the final scale: (7).

*(5) Parity in the coupling: the first-order parts average out (derived from RG1 (2.12) and CRE (3.16), given the OPEN inputs).*
- Fix the background (V₂, the pending data, U₂) and the cutoffs. For λ ∈ ℂ, replace g₁ by λ in every occurrence of g₁CA (and g₁A in 𝐆₃) and in every prefactor g₁^{−2} in RG1 (2.12), and in B′ = λCA − hD̃(λCA) inside the insertions. Call the exponent W(λ,A).
- Term by term, (2.12) consists of functions of λCA alone (Tr log(I − h(δD̃/δB)(λCA)), log σ, the 𝐄₁ difference) and of λ^{−2}F(λCA) with F analytic and starting at third order (𝐆₃, ⟨H₁hD̃₃,J⟩, the two D̃ cross terms, V). Hence W(−λ,−A) = W(λ,A), W(0,A) = 0, and W is analytic at λ = 0. The insertions Y_G and e^{iX_j} − 1 have the same symmetry and vanish at λ = 0.
- μ_{C^{(1)}} is centred Gaussian. RG2's s-interpolated "measures" are complex at complex s (RG2 p.15); they are symmetric under X → −X: the integrand couples B and X bilinearly through exp(−⟨B, Γ_kX⟩) (RG2 (2.6), (2.14)) with dμ₀ and dμ_C symmetric, so it is invariant under (B, X) → (−B, −X) (RG2 p.13's representation through [13] (3.185) is used as Bałaban's). CRE (3.16)'s cutoff is invariant under A → −A, since it tests |A(b)| bond by bond.
- *Scope.* The parity holds for the activities K(λ) of D2.2(5)–(6)'s joint cluster expansion that contain an insertion factor or a Mayer factor of a λ-dependent exponent (so that K(0) = 0) and are supported in Ω₂^{∼−1}∖R₂, away from S₂, with ω′ = 0 (σ-independent). There χ′₁ = 1 on (3.16)'s support (p.268), and the substitution A → −A gives K(−λ) = K(λ), K(0) = 0. It fails, and these activities are D2.6's, for:
  - activities meeting the layer Ω₂∖Ω₂^{∼−1}, where the χ′₁ tests of V₁(V^{(1)}_{□′})^{−1} remain and are not invariant;
  - activities meeting S₂, through ψ₂'s change of variables, its Jacobian and V^{(1)}(S₂, …);
  - σ-dependent activities, through the cross term −⟨A, C*Δ^{(1)}Cσ⟩, which is linear in A (D2.2(5)(e), (6)(b6)).
- *Unchecked, separately:* the χᶜ pieces inside the small-field activities. RG2 (2.14), p.15, has (−1)^{|P|}χ_{k,Y₀}(B)χ^c_{k,P}(B) inside the activities, and (2.26), p.17, shows these carry exp(−½γ₂ε₁²|P|/g_k²). Their parity needs the χ^c test to be even in B; its definition (RG2 (2.3), p.12) is not checked here.
- *Radius.* On the cutoff's support |A| < δ₁/g₁ =: r₁, so |λA| < |λ|r₁. Two classes of interior activities:
  - (i) *No old-factor link:* the activity contains only the leading-word and coarse insertions, the links of the λ^{−2}F terms of (2.12), the links of Tr log(I − h(δD̃/δB)(λCA)) and of log σ, and the Gaussian parts. These are analytic for |λA| ≤ ε′ := min(ε_{D̃}, ε₁^{RG2}, ε_H/C₁^{RG2}), with ε_{D̃} the analyticity radius of RG1 p.267's solution D̃ (p.267 gives analyticity without a number), ε₁^{RG2} and ε_H/C₁^{RG2} from D2.1–D2.2's complex domain; so for |λ| < λ_* := ε′g₁/δ₁, and g₁/λ_* = δ₁/ε′ → 0 provided ε_{D̃} does not shrink with g₁; derived, not reviewed: the equation for D̃ on RG1 p.267 has no coupling in it (re-check row 22).
  - (ii) *At least one old-factor link:* the 𝐄₁ difference of (2.12), and in CRE (3.15) also the 𝐑₁, 𝐁₁ and A(·) differences, are evaluated at U₁(e^{iB′(λ)}V^{(1)}) = e^{iη𝐇}U₂. The old factors are analytic only while the deformation stays inside the α_{1,1}-margin (D2.2(4)(f): 1/T_Y ≥ 16B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′|_Y/(min(β,1)α_{1,k})). With |B′| ≤ C₁^{RG2}|λA| this gives the radius λ_α := ρ_αg₁/δ₁, ρ_α := min(β,1)α_{1,1}/(16B₀e^{16κ_s}C₁^{RG2}), and g₁/λ_α = δ₁/ρ_α = O((log g₁^{−2})^{p₀−q₁}). This tends to 0 only if q₁ > p₀; it stays bounded, not small, if q₁ = p₀, which R-m's entailment allows. The radius ε′g₁/δ₁ of (i) is not an analyticity radius for these.
- Even and vanishing at 0 means K(λ) = ψ(λ²) with ψ(0) = 0. For any λ′ below the class's radius (the sup is taken on a circle inside the domain), the Schwarz lemma gives
  |K(g₁)| ≤ (g₁/λ′)²·sup_{|λ|=λ′}|K(λ)|,
  so for a fixed λ′ = θλ_*, θ < 1, the gain is (δ₁/(θε′))²·sup_{|λ|=λ′}|K(λ)| for class (i) (the sup ≤ (C₁^{RG2}θε′/ε_H)·(D2.4(10)'s factors) by the scaling identity W(λ,B) = W(g₁,(λ/g₁)B), class-activities closure a1–a4, 26 Oct; the product is c_λρ₂·(decay), a6; reviewed in round 46, minors applied, not re-reviewed). For a link with analyticity space CRE (3.43) (𝐁₁, A(·), V^{(1)}(S₂); D2.6's by b5) the same step with λ′ = θλ_α (it needs λ_α > g₁, i.e. ρ_α > δ₁, a constant comparison when q₁ = p₀) gives only (δ₁/(θρ_α))² = O(polylog), and the sup on |λ| = λ′ contains the insertion at |λA| ≈ ρ_α ∝ g₁, so the λ-route gives O(g₁·polylog)·S_F for such a link; the 𝐄₁ links of class (ii) are RG2 Lemma 1 terms with radius ε₁^{RG2}/g₁, ε₁^{RG2} absolute by the closure's Premise (CRE p.254 after (1.30) with RG1's absolute α₂; the 𝐑₁ link is absent for k ≤ N by R-h), so class (ii) takes λ′ = θλ_* as class (i) does and gets the same c_λρ₂ (closure b1, b4).
  Pointwise, a single first-order insertion has size up to C₁^{RG2}δ₁/ε_H (D2.1(4) Claim, |B′|/ε_H), with δ₁ = g₁r₁ = A₁g₁(log g₁^{−2})^{p₀} on the support |A| < r₁. For class (i) the step-2 integral gains one more factor of order δ₁/ε′. At the Gaussian level the first-order mean is 0 and the second-order mean is O(g₁²).
- *Class (ii) is second order: the 𝐄₁ link's domain is RG2 Lemma 1's disc, radius ε₁^{RG2}/g₁, not the α-disc (class-activities closure b1–b4 and its Premise, 26 Oct; reviewed in round 46: the radius claim was unsupported under CRE (2.28)'s g-scaled width alone, and is now derived from CRE p.254 after (1.30), the step-1 interior 𝐄^{(1)} being RG1's restricted to Λ₁ with RG1's absolute α₂, so that RG2 p.7's condition fixes ε₁^{RG2} absolute; not re-reviewed); the α-disc computation below applies to links with analyticity space CRE (3.43), which are D2.6's.* An old-factor link is a Mayer or exponential factor of the 𝐄₁ difference (or, in CRE (3.15), of the 𝐑₁, 𝐁₁, A(·) differences) localised to the polymer; call it F(B′). It vanishes at B′ = 0 (RG1 (3.4), 𝐇₁(0) = 0 by RG2 (1.21)); the 𝐄₁ difference's terms are analytic on |B|_Y < ε₁^{RG2}/g₁ with |F| ≤ E_Y (Lemma 1, (1.33)–(1.36); CRE p.276; ε₁^{RG2} absolute by the Premise, whereas CRE (2.28)'s inductive width alone would give only O(α_{1,1}/g₁) and the first-order bound below; the 𝐑₁ difference is absent for k ≤ N by R-h), whereas the α_{1,1}-domain of D2.2(4)(f), |B′|_Y < r_α := min(β,1)α_{1,1}/(16B₀e^{16κ_s}) (in the units in which |B′| ≤ C₁^{RG2}g₁|A|, r_α ∝ g₁(log g₁^{−2})^{q₁}), is the complex domain of the touched old insertions. On the α-disc alone the argument runs as follows. Let S_F be F's supremum there. Schwarz's lemma in the variable B′ on that disc gives |F(B′)| ≤ S_F|B′|_Y/r_α at the real point, and on the support |B′|_Y ≤ C₁^{RG2}δ₁, so |F| ≤ S_FC₁^{RG2}δ₁/r_α = S_F·O((log g₁^{−2})^{p₀−q₁}): the real point lies at a fixed fraction of the link's radius, so F is O(S_F), bounded, not small, when q₁ = p₀, and only a power of log better when q₁ > p₀. The activity also contains an insertion factor, of size ≤ C₁^{RG2}δ₁/ε_H (D2.1(4) Claim) or ≤ 1.06c_πc_hC₁^{RG2}δ₁ for a coarse slot ((6)). So the activity is at most S_F·O(g₁(log g₁^{−2})^{2p₀−q₁})·(Gaussian factor) pointwise on the support: first order in g₁, even in λ by *Scope*. Its second-order size is not derived. With the bounds available, such a contribution to the flat cluster weights exceeds the amplitude 𝔞₂ = max(τ₁², ρ₂·pl) that D2.3(5)–(6) and (S-w) assume (ρ₂·pl ≤ Cτ₁² up to the polylog), unless S_F carries a factor of order g₁; since τ₁² ≍ g₁²(log g₁^{−2})^{6p₀}, the precise condition is S_F ≲ g₁(log g₁^{−2})^{4p₀+q₁}. *The requirement:* S_F as defined gives the first-order bound above; second order needs either (α) S_F = O(g₁·polylog) on the α-disc, or (β) a Lipschitz bound |F(B′)| ≤ L_F|B′|_Y with L_F = O(polylog) on the real segment |B′|_Y ≤ C₁^{RG2}δ₁. On the α-disc, L_F = O(polylog) and S_F = O(g₁·polylog) are equivalent, so (β) on the real segment is the only weaker form. Both are derived from Lemma 1's disc (closure b3): Schwarz in the field gives |F(B)| ≤ E_Yg₁|B|_Y/ε₁^{RG2}, hence (α) with S_F ≤ 2E_Yr_α/ε₁^{RG2} = O(g₁(log g₁^{−2})^{q₁})E_Y and (β) with L_F = 2E_Y/ε₁^{RG2} = O(1)E_Y, under 8C₂O(1)²δ₁ ≤ 1 and |B′|_Y ≥ g₁|B|_Y/2 (RG2 (1.20); exactly |B′|_Y ≥ g₁|B|_Y since hX vanishes off the designated bonds, RG1 p.267, which drops the factor 2 and the condition). The printed bounds consulted before are field-uniform and show no such gain: CRE Theorem 2, p.263, (2.43), bounds Σ_z[E^{(j)}(U_k,z) − E^{(j)}(1,z)] − β_jA(φ,U_k) by E₁Σ(L^{j−n})^β|Γ_n ∩ Ω|, uniformly in the field with no factor of the deviation (per point: |F| = O(1) plus a β·A term with small Lipschitz constant); CRE p.261's complex domain enters only through a Cauchy estimate on a disc of radius ∝ α_{1,n}, which is exactly the 1/g₁ loss. RG2 (2.26)'s link factor 2E₀ε₁C₁α₄^{−1} has the right shape only if α₄ is independent of g; p.17's α₅ = … + O(1)α₄ + … shows only that α₄ is small. The deviation part of such an activity is D2.4(4)'s business. Derived (gaps block, the class (i)/(ii) entry): (α) and (β) with the constants above, and the Schwarz step in λ applies to class (ii) with class (i)'s λ′ = θε′g₁/δ₁, since on |λ| ≤ λ′ the link is 𝐕₁′(Y,B̃) with g₁|B̃| ≤ θε′ < ε₁^{RG2} inside Lemma 1's disc, giving c_λρ₂·(decay) directly (closure b4).
- Uses: the form of RG1 (2.12) (multi-domain through CRE p.267), the symmetry of μ and of (3.16). **Derived (class-activities closure a3–a4, 26 Oct; reviewed in round 46, minors applied, not re-reviewed):** the Kotecký–Preiss condition of the deformed vacuum gas and the marked-activity bounds at complex coupling on |λ| ≤ λ′ for both classes (the convergence of the whole joint expansion at complex λ is not claimed and not needed: a5–a6 use these two statements). D2.4's (S-w) (with its consequence (T′)) is stated at real coupling; its complex-coupling version is RG2 (2.19)–(2.26) and Lemma 3 verbatim at B̃ = (λ/g₁)B inside Lemma 2's disc, by the scaling identity, the Gaussian domination of the λ^{−2}F terms at complex λ being (2.19)–(2.25) at B̃. Large-field terms (R₂ ≠ ∅, χ^{(1)c}) and the excluded activities of *Scope* are D2.6's.

*(6) The perimeter part and the comparison after step 2 (given (5), D2.2–D2.3 applied to g₁, and their OPEN inputs).*
- *g₁'s step-2 expansion (derived).* By (3), g₁(e^{iB′}V^{(1)}) = d_π^{−1}tr∏_j(1 + X_j)π(V^{(1)}(c_j)) in loop order, where C ⊂ B(Γ₂), X_j := π(e^{iB′(c_j)}) − 1 for a positively traversed c_j and X_j := π(V^{(1)}(c_j)e^{−iB′(−c_j)}V^{(1)}(c_j)^{−1}) − 1 for a negatively traversed one: a closed word X₁U₁X₂U₂⋯ of D2.2(5)(a)'s form, with the slots at the scale-1 bonds c_j and U_j = π(V^{(1)}(c_j)), V^{(1)} = M(U₂), a function of U₂ only. D2.2(5) uses of the insertions only that each depends on A on a bounded domain D_j through B′ ((5)(a), (c): D_J^#, the (2.9)–(2.10) linking), and boundedness on the support ((5)(e)); D2.2(6) uses covariance and analyticity in the slot variables; D2.3 uses Schur at the flat reference and contour locality; D2.4 uses the per-slot size. For X_j: it depends on A through B′(c_j) only, B′ being the local function of A of RG1 (3.2) (RG2 p.3; D2.1(4)(b)'s "locality of B′ in B"), so D_j is the π₁-hull of the bonds on which B′(c_j) depends (c_j, or the corridor of c_j if c_j is a designated bond b₀(c′), (3)); it carries no s-parameter and no propagator; ‖X_j‖ ≤ e^{c_πc_h|B′(c_j)|} − 1 ≤ 1.06c_πc_h|B′(c_j)| ≤ 1.06c_πc_hC₁^{RG2}g₁|A|_{D_j} on the support at real points (c_h from the norm convention of (0), V4 L396; the 1.06 needs c_πc_hC₁^{RG2}ε′ ≤ 1/10); at complex points ‖X_j‖ ≤ 2.12c_πc_h|B′(c_j)| under 2c_πc_hC₁^{RG2}ε′ ≤ 1/10, and X_j is entire in B′(c_j); it is covariant, X_j^u = π(u(c_{j−}))X_jπ(u(c_{j−}))^{−1}, by the formula RG1 (0.4) for M (log is Ad-covariant; (0.6) is the covariance of the set average) and the covariance of B′ (D2.2(6)(b)); at the flat reference U₂ = 1 the words are invariant under constant gauge transformations, so Schur applies as in D2.3(2); X_j depends on C only through c_j. So D2.2(5)–(6), D2.3 and D2.4 run with the slot set {c_j} and the per-slot factor 2.12c_πc_hC₁^{RG2}|λA|_{D_j} at complex λ (1.06c_πc_hC₁^{RG2}g₁|A|_{D_j} at the real point) in place of D2.1(4)'s Claim, with three changes: the transports between slots are M(U₂)-transports, so D2.3(2)'s split uses T_{M(𝐔)}(I) in place of T_𝐔(I) ((6)(b)'s covariance covers it since M is covariant); the leading word is g̃₂, outside D(2) ((7)(e)(i)); and the coarse slots depend on U₂ through M(U₂) on the two blocks of c_j, which enlarges X(𝒢) and ℓ_X by those blocks in D2.4's (LPG′)/Cauchy step. The output is the display below with δm₂^g, ζ₂^g, E°_2^g and g̃₂'s deviation words, given the same input (S-w) for the coarse gas (derived, SW closure with the three changes of the first bullet; from which (T′), (Z) and the Kotecký–Preiss condition follow), which has fewer and simpler slot factors than f₁'s.
- The cluster series of D2.3(5) at the flat background is Π₂-periodic along a side, not translation invariant (D2.3(4), R-tr). On sides whose length is commensurate with Π₂ its flat part equals P₁(C)μ^f + 4ζ^f + E°_2^f(C,T) for f₁ and P₁(C)μ^g + 4ζ^g + E°_2^g(C,T) for g₁ (D2.3(8): log S°(ℓ₀) = P₁(C)μ + 4ζ + E°₂(C,T)), with μ = (4Π₂)^{−1}Σ_{v∈𝒱_□}κ_v the average of the period sums κ_v over the orientations of C's sides (all four sides have the same length; μ = −δm₂, D2.3(5)), 4ζ the corner part, and E°₂ the corner and torus remainder, bounded by D2.3(6)(a) and not zero; what vanishes at the flat reference are the deviation words of the bracket, not part of the series. D2.3 absorbs the flat parts exactly into δm₂, ζ₂, separately for the two loops, given its input (S-w), derived in the SW closure. By (5), μ^f − μ^g is a sum of interior activities, each ≤ c_λρ₂·(decay) (class (i), the sup derived; class (ii), second order by the 𝐄₁ link's absolute radius; gaps block, the class (i)/(ii) entry).
- Gaussian (linear-insertion) level, flat background (the colour covariance is δ^{ab} there): the second-order mean of d_π^{−1}tr π(∏_ie^{iX_i}) with the insertions replaced by their linear parts is −(C₂(π)/(2d_G))Σ_{i,i′}E⟨X_i,X_{i′}⟩, with C₂(π) the Casimir of π in the normalisation π_*(t^a)π_*(t^a) = C₂(π)·1 for an orthonormal basis t^a of 𝔤 in ⟨·,·⟩, and d_G = dim 𝔤. The sum runs over the fine bonds with X_i = η(H_{1,1}B′)(b_i) for f₁, and over the scale-1 bonds with X_j = B′(c_j) for g₁. RG1 (2.6) writes 𝐇 as H_{1,k}B′ + 𝐀_{1,k}; whether 𝐀_{1,1} has a part linear in B′ is not settled on the transcribed pages: RG1 (2.7)–(2.8) send ½⟨𝐀₁, Δ₁𝐀₁⟩ into "terms of at least third order" and state ⟨𝐀₁, J⟩ = 0, which is consistent both with 𝐀₁ of higher order and with Δ₁𝐀₁ = 0 (a gauge-type mode); PBG (3.127)–(3.128), where H₁ and 𝐀₁ are defined, are not checked here. If 𝐀_{1,1} has a linear part, it belongs in X_i, and the decay below survives for any linear part whose kernel decays (not checked). Whether H_{1,1} is RG2's H₀ is not on the transcribed pages either. So the Gaussian (linear-insertion) part of μ^f − μ^g, per scale-1 bond, is −(C₂(π)/(2d_G))[L·Σ_mE⟨X^f_0,X^f_m⟩ − Σ_mE⟨X^g_0,X^g_m⟩], the self-energy sums along a straight line; the covariances E⟨X,X⟩ below already contain g₁², so there is no further factor g₁². At order g₁² the full mean also receives the second-order parts of the insertions (of 𝐇 and of B′), the cubic vertices of (2.12) paired with a first-order insertion, and the linear vertices paired with a first-order insertion: the Tr log term (linear in λCA at lowest order, D̃ starting quadratic) and the linear parts of the 𝐄₁ difference and of CRE (3.15)'s 𝐑, 𝐁 and A(·) differences. These are the perturbative coefficients α_L; their values are not computed.
  - *Convergence of the self-energy sums (derived).* X_b is linear in A: by RG1 p.268 B′ = g₁CA − hD̃(g₁CA), and by RG1 p.267 D̃ "has an expansion beginning with quadratic terms", so the linear part of B′ is g₁CA, local (RG2 p.4: "the function B′ defined by (I.3.2) is a local function of B"), X_b = ηg₁(𝒦A)(b) with 𝒦 := H_{1,1}C for f₁, and X_j = g₁(CA)(c_j) for g₁ (RG2's D(H(s),A′) of (1.12)–(1.14) is the analogue of RG1's H_kD_k in (2.6), p.266, a different object from hD̃, the linearisation of the δ-function constraint). So E⟨X_b, X_{b′}⟩ = η²g₁²(𝒦C^{(1)}𝒦*)(b,b′) (and without η², 𝒦 = C, for g₁). Kernels: C^{(1)} = (C*Δ^{(1)}C)^{−1} (RG1, the text just before (2.12)); PBG Theorem 3.15, p.432, (3.187): |C^{(k)}(Λ;y,y′)| ≤ B₀e^{−δ₀|y−y′|} with B₀, δ₀ depending on d and L only; RG2 p.13 identifies C(xI + C*Δ_kC)^{−1}C* with the integral (3.185) [13] (= PBG (3.185)), with an extra term vanishing at x = 0, so (3.187) is the bound for CC^{(1)}C* (reading R-cov: RG1's C^{(1)} at the flat background is PBG's C^{(k)}(Λ) sandwiched by C, and RG2's Δ_k is RG1's Δ^{(k)}; evidence RG2 p.13). C is a local operator (its locality comes from RG1 p.267's corridor constraint; its left inverse is the restriction to the non-designated bonds), so C^{(1)} itself has the decay with a range-L loss. H_{1,1} has a random-walk expansion of RG2 (1.6)–(1.7)'s type (*a Bałaban reading*: (1.6)–(1.7) are printed for RG2's H, G̃, H₀, and RG1 p.266 refers H₁ to PBG (3.127)–(3.128), not checked here), each term bounded by O(1)O(M₁^{−1/2})^{|ω|}exp(−δ₀d(ω,y,y′)), so its kernel decays exponentially. A finite composition of kernels on T_η with |K_i(y,y′)| ≤ B_ie^{−δ_i|y−y′|} is bounded by Be^{−δ′|y−y′|} with δ′ < min δ_i (the sums over intermediate points converge, with a polynomial prefactor absorbed into δ′). Hence |E⟨X_b, X_{b′}⟩| ≤ η²g₁²B′e^{−δ′|b−b′|}, and along a straight line Σ_m|E⟨X_0, X_m⟩| ≤ η²g₁²B′(1 + 2/(e^{δ′η} − 1)) = O(g₁²η/δ′) per fine bond for δ′η ≤ 1, O(g₁²/δ′) per scale-1 bond; for g₁, Σ_m ≤ g₁²B″(1 + 2/(e^{δ′} − 1)) = O(g₁²) per scale-1 bond. Both self-energy sums converge absolutely, so the Gaussian (linear-insertion) part of μ^f − μ^g is a well-defined O(g₁²) number. Its value is not computed. (D̃'s analyticity radius ε_{D̃} enters ε′ in (5); RG1 p.267 gives it without a number; RG2's ε₂, p.5, is the radius for D(H(s), A′), a different object, and is not used.)
- **The comparison after step 2.** With D2.2's output for each loop, writing 𝓔₂^f, 𝓔₂^g for the step-2 exponents and g̃₂ for g₁'s averaged word,
  e^{δm₂^fP₁(C)−4ζ₂^f}E_h[1_{A_cov}e^{E°_ξ}f₁] − e^{δm₂^gP₁(C)−4ζ₂^g}E_h[1_{A_cov}e^{E°_ξ}g₁] = E^{(2)}[Δ_bg·(common dressing)] + E^{(2)}[(e^{𝓔₂^f} − e^{𝓔₂^g})g̃₂] + (difference of the averaged deviation words) + (large-field terms),
  where 1_{A_cov} is taken constant on each labelled term of ρ₂. V4 lem:covered (L1171–1173) calls a branch ξ covered when every contour bond is a bond of Ω₁ and every free-input stencil F_r of a contour bond lies in the region I of the branch; so 1_{A_cov} is a function of the branch data Ω₁ and I. That I is determined by the step-1 history labels that ρ₂'s labelled terms keep is checked: V4 L2404 and L5136 give I = Λ₁^*, CRE p.254 labels the terms of ρ_k by {Ω_j}, {Λ_j}, and D2.2(2)(a) keeps V3's Λ = Λ₁^fin, so the constancy is derived (re-check row 25).
  - The first term is bounded by Lip_χ(e^{6c_hB₃n_Cε₂L^{−3}} − 1) times the step-2 moment of the dressing (B₃ as in (4), not shown < L²/(1+β₀)).
  - The second. By D2.3(8), 𝓔₂ − 𝓔₁ = E^∂_2(ℓ) + E°_2(C,T) for each loop, with E^∂_2(ℓ) := log S°(ℓ) − log S°(ℓ₀) and E°_2 the corner and torus remainder of the flat reference gas. On the flat term ℓ₀, E^∂_2 = 0, so the exponents are E°_2^f and E°_2^g, numbers, each at most C_Eτ₁²[(s₁+1)e^{−c_Es₁} + s₁e^{−cι_{T,2}}] by D2.3(6)(a), given (S-w) for the reference gas of the loop, from which (T′) and (Z) follow (derived, SW closure; for g₁ by the first bullet; amplitude 𝔞₂ = max(τ₁², ρ₂·pl)). The second-order amplitude holds for the coarse gas since the class (ii) activities of (5) are second order (gaps block, the class (i)/(ii) entry). Hence |e^{𝓔₂^f} − e^{𝓔₂^g}| ≤ e^{Re 𝓔₂^g}·(e^{|E°_2^f| + |E°_2^g|} − 1) ≤ 4C_Eτ₁²[(s₁+1)e^{−c_Es₁} + s₁e^{−cι_{T,2}}]·e^{Re 𝓔₂^g} on ℓ₀, under 2C_Eτ₁²[(s₁+1)e^{−c_Es₁} + s₁e^{−cι_{T,2}}] ≤ 1, step 2 commensurate (D2.3(4)) and the torus condition (so that the bound is uniform in the size of C): the exponential of the exponent difference is 1 + O(τ₁²) and the corner term decays in s₁. By D2.3(3) the non-flat parts are the deviation words Y^d_𝒢 of the bracket, the third term below, and the exponent on ℓ₀ is the flat cluster series, all of which but E°_2 is absorbed into δm₂ and ζ₂. On labelled terms ℓ ≠ ℓ₀ the difference E^∂_2^f(ℓ) − E^∂_2^g(ℓ) remains, D2.6's with the large-field terms.
  - The third: D2.4(4) gives e^θ‖Y^d_𝒢‖ ≤ b_𝒢Dev_X with b_𝒢 = (C_g/(c₀ᾱ₂))e^θS_𝒢, and Dev_X = O(ε₂ℓ_X) in step-2 normalisation (D2.0(3)(b)). So the remainder is S_𝒢·(C_g/(c₀ᾱ₂))·Dev_X = O(ε₂ℓ_X/ᾱ₂)·S_𝒢, and ε₂/ᾱ₂ = O((log g₂^{−2})^{p₀−min(q₀,q₁)}) is a power of log, bounded under R-m and not small; smallness comes only from the later contraction (D2.4(6)). S_𝒢 bounded as in (5). It needs (LPG′) (derived, SW closure §5; not reviewed). No further gain ε₂L^{−2} and no product of the parity gain with the deviation gain is claimed: that would need a two-variable argument, analytic and even in λ and vanishing at Dev = 0, which is not given.
  - The fourth is D2.6's, together with (5)'s excluded activities.
- Sizes, per scale-1 bond of C:

  | part | pointwise (before averaging) | after the step-2 average |
  |---|---|---|
  | first-order insertions (fluctuation), interior | O(δ₁/ε′) | 0 at first order; per activity O((δ₁/(θε′))²)·sup (class (i), the sup derived: c_λρ₂·(decay), gaps block) or the same c_λρ₂·(decay) (class (ii), second order by the 𝐄₁ link's absolute radius, gaps block) |
  | flat second-order (perimeter, corners) | — | absorbed exactly into δm₂, ζ₂ via D2.3, given its input (S-w) for the coarse gas (derived, SW closure; its second-order amplitude 𝔞₂ holds, class (ii) being second order), for both loops; Gaussian (linear-insertion) part −(C₂(π)/(2d_G))·Δ(self-energy), O(g₁²), the covariances carrying g₁², absolutely convergent (R-cov, H_{1,1}'s walk expansion), value not computed |
  | exponent difference (e^{𝓔₂^f} − e^{𝓔₂^g})g̃₂ | — | on ℓ₀: |e^{𝓔₂^f} − e^{𝓔₂^g}| ≤ 4C_Eτ₁²[(s₁+1)e^{−c_Es₁} + s₁e^{−cι_{T,2}}]e^{Re 𝓔₂^g}, under 2C_Eτ₁²[…] ≤ 1, step 2 commensurate, torus condition, given (S-w) for the coarse gas (D2.3(6)(a), amplitude 𝔞₂; the second-order amplitude holds since class (ii) is second order, the class (i)/(ii) entry); on ℓ ≠ ℓ₀: D2.6 |
  | background Δ_bg (additive) | O(ε₁) (step 1, by (1); O(ε₁/L) per fine bond) | O(B₃ε₂L^{−2}) (function of V₂ and the pending variables); the B′ = 0 end at the final scale O(B₃ε_KL^{−2(K−1)}) per scale-1 bond, (7)(d), on terms with C's blocks in Ω_K; the shed words are the two chains' insertion-word differences, averaged by both step maps ((7)(e), 26 Oct, reviewed in round 47, reduced to the interfaces (iii-a), (iii-c), (iv-a)–(iv-c)) |

- *Not derived here.* The values of μ^f, μ^g; the exponent difference on ℓ ≠ ℓ₀ (on ℓ₀ it is bounded above); the second-order size of the class (ii) activities; the large-field terms and (5)'s excluded activities. The step-3 average of Δ_bg and the iteration to the final scale: (7).

*(7) The background terms, iterated to the final scale (derived: the pointwise parts (a), (b), (d) and the pointwise identity (c); the expectation-level part (e) reduced to the interfaces (iii-a), (iii-c) (derived with the enlarged counts #(X^g) ≤ 81#(X), ℓ_{X^g} ≤ 25921ℓ_X in WILSON_IT1_COARSE_CHAIN_INTERFACES_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md; round 48, it1-coarse-20261026-r1: no fatal, three serious repaired, not re-reviewed) and (iv-a)–(iv-c), WILSON_IT1_BACKGROUND_ITERATION_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md, 26 Oct, reviewed in round 47, it1-bg-20261026-r1: no fatal; two serious, the labels overstated and the remainder R_bad over the labels without the coarse D(K) form unassigned, both repaired, not re-reviewed: the shed words are the differences of the two chains' insertion words and are averaged at the next step as part of both chains' step maps; at k = K nothing beyond the fine chain is needed; the composed coarse species carries the clauses (D1)–(D5) and the slot amplitude ε_H/(b_d^c)^k, on which (S-w) and the class (i) sup run as for the fine chain).*
- *Setting.* K the final step; the sides of C are multiples of L^K fine bonds, so C is a union of scale-k bonds for every k ≤ K, with P_k(C) = 4ℓ_CL^{K−k} scale-k bonds, ℓ_C the side in step-K bonds (as in D2.0(5) and D2.3(4)), n_C = 4ℓ_CL^K. A corner of C lies on the scale-K lattice (D2.3(4), *Placement*) and L is odd (ONE STEP (I3)). M^k := M∘⋯∘M is the k-fold iterate of RG1 (0.4) (*reading R-Mk of this note*, load-bearing for (a)'s induction M^{j+1} = M∘M^j: RG1 p.265 (2.3), "V^{(k)} = Ū^k_{k+1} = M^k(U_{k+1})", and CRE (3.4), (3.10) use the symbol without defining it, and D2.0(5)(c)(α) only quotes it). C^{(k)} is C read as a scale-k loop. T_c(U) is the straight fine transport along a scale-k bond c (the contour variable U(c) of RG1 (0.4), as in ONE STEP (I2); "R-str" names that reading). a_d := 0.3(d−1)(d+4), so a_d = 7.2 for d = 4.
- *(a) Lemma (iterated average against the straight transport).* Let U be a fine field with ‖U(∂p) − 1‖ ≤ t on every fine plaquette of a region Ω, let L ≥ 3(d+3), and let k ≥ 1 with a_dL^{2k}t ≤ min(1/10, log(1 + r_G)) (the chart condition; r_G as in V4 L693, so that ‖W − 1‖ ≤ r_G puts W ∈ G in the chart domain, where (0.4)'s log is the principal logarithm with values in 𝔤). Then for every j ≤ k and every scale-j bond c with B^j(c_−) ∪ B^j(c_+) ⊂ Ω,
  (M^jU)(c) = e^{iE_c}T_c(U), ‖E_c‖ ≤ κ_j := a_dL^{2j}t.
  Proof by induction on j; j = 0 is E = 0. Step j → j+1: by RG1 (0.4) applied to the scale-j field M^jU (the blocks of the scale-(j+1) lattice in the scale-j lattice), (M^{j+1}U)(c) = e^{iX_c}·(M^jU)(c), with X_c a convex combination of the (1/i)log(M^jU)(ℓ) over the loops ℓ = Γ∪[x,x′]∪(−Γ′)∪(−c) of (I2), now made of scale-j bonds, and (M^jU)(c) = ∏_{b⊂c}(M^jU)(b) the product over the L scale-j bonds of c. Three facts:
  - (M^jU)(c) = ∏_{b⊂c}e^{iE_b}T_b(U) = (∏_{b⊂c}g_be^{iE_b}g_b^{−1})·T_c(U), with g_b the partial transports (unitary), so (M^jU)(c) = e^{iF_c}T_c(U) with ‖F_c‖ ≤ 1.06Lκ_j (the product bound of ONE STEP (2), and ONE STEP (2)'s log bound, since Lκ_j ≤ a_dL^{2k}t/L ≤ 1/10).
  - (M^jU)(ℓ) = (∏_{b∈ℓ}g_be^{iE_b}g_b^{−1})·U(ℓ), with U(ℓ) the fine holonomy around ℓ read as a fine loop. ℓ has at most (d+2)L scale-j bonds (Γ, Γ′ at most d(L−1)/2 each, [x,x′] and c L each), and ONE STEP (1)'s contraction of ℓ uses at most A_L ≤ (d−1)(d+4)L²/8 scale-j plaquette moves, each a conjugate of a scale-j plaquette holonomy of U, i.e. of a product of L^{2j} conjugated fine plaquette factors. All paths of the contraction lie in B^{j+1}(c_−) ∪ B^{j+1}(c_+) ⊂ Ω (a box; staircases and translates of c between its points stay in it), and the fine blocks of the scale-j bonds of ℓ lie in the same box (blocks nest). So ‖(M^jU)(ℓ) − 1‖ ≤ e^{s_ℓ} − 1 with s_ℓ := A_LL^{2j}t + (d+2)Lκ_j ≤ [(d−1)(d+4)/8 + (d+2)a_d/L]L^{2j+2}t ≤ a_dL^{2j+2}t ≤ min(1/10, log(1+r_G)); so (M^jU)(ℓ) ∈ G (by the induction hypothesis E_b ∈ 𝔤, so that M^jU is G-valued) lies in the chart domain, (0.4)'s log is the principal logarithm with value in 𝔤 (V4's chart step, prop:coarse-loop-form (i)), ‖(1/i)log(M^jU)(ℓ)‖ ≤ 1.06s_ℓ, and X_c ∈ 𝔤 with ‖X_c‖ ≤ 1.06s_ℓ by convexity.
  - e^{iX_c}e^{iF_c} = e^{iE_c} with ‖E_c‖ ≤ 1.06(‖X_c‖ + ‖F_c‖) ≤ 1.124[A_LL^{2j}t + (d+3)Lκ_j] ≤ 1.124·a_d[1/2.4 + (d+3)/L]L^{2j+2}t ≤ a_dL^{2j+2}t for L ≥ 3(d+3) (the arcsin step needs 1.06(‖X_c‖ + ‖F_c‖) ≤ 1/10, which the same chain gives); and ‖e^{iX_c}e^{iF_c} − 1‖ ≤ e^{1.06(‖X_c‖+‖F_c‖)} − 1 ≤ r_G, so E_c ∈ 𝔤 by the chart step again, and M^{j+1}U is G-valued. ∎
  The per-level loss is a factor (d+3)/L on the previous level's deviation, not a constant: that is why the constant a_d does not grow with j. The contraction of ℓ at level j and (I2)'s convexity are scale-independent, so ONE STEP (1)–(2) are used verbatim at each level.
- *(b) Comparison lemma.* Under (a), with k such that every scale-k bond c of C has B^k(c_−) ∪ B^k(c_+) ⊂ Ω: Hol_{C^{(k)}}(M^kU) = ∏_ie^{iE_{c_i}}T_{c_i}(U) (E conjugated for negatively traversed bonds, norm unchanged) and Hol_C(U) = ∏_iT_{c_i}(U), so by V4 prop:coarse-loop-form (iii)'s argument (the Lipschitz chain over the P_k(C) factors, ‖e^{iE} − 1‖ ≤ ‖E‖ ≤ κ_k)
  |W_χ(C;U) − W_χ(C^{(k)};M^kU)| ≤ Lip_χ(e^{P_k(C)a_dL^{2k}t} − 1)
  (ONE STEP (3)'s form e^{c_πP_kκ_k} − 1 without Lip_χ also holds, since (a) gives E_c ∈ 𝔤).
- *(c) The background term of step k+1 at step j and at the final scale (derived).* For j ≥ k+1 put β_k^{(j)}(V_j) := W_χ(C;U_j(V_j)) − W_χ(C^{(k)};M^kU_j(V_j)); then Δ_bg of (3)–(4) is β_1^{(2)}(V₂), and the step-(k+1) background term of the step-k comparison (the same decomposition one scale up) is β_k^{(k+1)}(V_{k+1}). Under the step j → j+1 substitution V_j = e^{iB′_j}V^{(j)}, by D2.0(5)(c)(α) at step j (U_j(V^{(j)}) = U_j(M^jU_{j+1}) = U_{j+1}: CRE (3.10), p.266, V^{(j)} = M^j(U_{j+1}), and RG1 p.265; U_k′(1) = 1 is not printed on p.265 but is D2.0(5)(c)(α)'s derivation from CRE p.256, and equality up to gauge suffices since β is gauge invariant), on the terms whose scale-k blocks of C lie in Ω_K (the premise of (d), stated here), where C ⊂ B(Γ_{j+1}) at every j by the nesting Ω_K ⊆ Ω_j (*reading*, consistent with CRE (2.34)'s layering by Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}; CRE p.256 states the nesting) (elsewhere the pending values replace M^jU_{j+1}; those terms are D2.6's),
  β_k^{(j)}(e^{iB′_j}V^{(j)}) = β_k^{(j+1)}(V_{j+1}) + w_j, w_j := β_k^{(j)}(e^{iB′_j}V^{(j)}) − β_k^{(j+1)}(V_{j+1}), a word vanishing at B′_j = 0.
  This is the pointwise split f(B′) = f(0) + [f(B′) − f(0)]; it is gauge-safe (β is a difference of traces of closed loops, M covariant), so the representative gap of D2.2(4)(d) does not touch it. Iterated, β_k^{(k+1)}(V_{k+1}) = β_k^{(K)}(V_K) + Σ_{j=k+1}^{K−1}w_j with β_k^{(K)}(V_K) = W_χ(C;U_K) − W_χ(C^{(k)};M^kU_K), a function of V_K and the pending variables of the term (CRE p.266: U_{k+1} is determined by the sequence {V_{k+1}|Γ_{k+1}, V_k|Γ_k, …}). The per-step factor (1+β₀)B₃L^{−2} of (4) is not iterated in this decomposition; but the words carry, pointwise, exactly what the B′ = 0 end sheds: Σ_jw_j = β_k^{(k+1)} − β_k^{(K)}. (d) bounds the B′ = 0 end only.
- *(d) Bound at the final scale (for K ≤ N; for K > N the inputs at step K−1 and D2.0(5)(c)(α) are D2.7's, as D2.0(3)(b) and D2.0(6) assign them: (5)(c)(α) and (3)(b) on the post-R term are derived in WILSON_IT1_D27_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md items 10 and 12 (round 48, they stand); the inputs at step K−1 for K > N are D2.7's observable part, derived in that note's item 15 given the reading R-far″, its round-52 findings (it1-d27-20261027-r3) repaired and not re-reviewed, l.342).* CRE p.268 at step K−1: |∂U_K − 1| < 2B₃ε_K(L^{−1}η)² on Ω_K, i.e. t_K := 2c_hB₃ε_KL^{−2K} in the operator norm on the fine plaquettes of Ω_K (the reading of "on Ω_K" as in (4); the conversion uses η = L^{−(K−1)} at step K−1, CRE p.276's convention). If every scale-k bond of C, k ≤ K−1, has its two scale-k blocks in Ω_K, then (b) with U = U_K, t = t_K and j = k gives
  |β_k^{(K)}(V_K)| ≤ Lip_χ(e^{8a_dc_hB₃ℓ_Cε_KL^{−(K−k)}} − 1), since P_k(C)L^{2k}t_K = 4ℓ_CL^{K−k}·2c_hB₃ε_KL^{2k−2K},
  under a_dL^{2k}t_K = 2a_dc_hB₃ε_KL^{−2(K−k)} ≤ min(1/10, log(1+r_G)), which holds for all k ≤ K−1 once 2a_dc_hB₃ε_K ≤ L²min(1/10, log(1+r_G)) (a condition on g_K). Summing over k = 1, …, K−1, with 8a_dc_hB₃ℓ_Cε_K ≤ L/10 so that e^x − 1 ≤ 1.06x on each term,
  Σ_{k=1}^{K−1}|β_k^{(K)}(V_K)| ≤ Lip_χ·8.48a_dc_hB₃ℓ_Cε_K·Σ_{m=1}^{K−1}L^{−m} ≤ Lip_χ·8.48a_dc_hB₃ℓ_Cε_K/(L−1).
  This is O(ℓ_Cε_K/L), uniformly in the number of steps K, for the background values β_k^{(K)} on the terms whose scale-k blocks of C lie in Ω_K (D2.0(5)(f) does not show these terms dominate): the term of step k+1 contributes L^{−(K−k)}, so the first steps contribute least at the final scale, although the exponent of their pointwise bound at step k+1, O(ℓ_Cε_{k+1}L^{K−k−2}) by (4) and (b) with U = U_{k+1}, is the largest. B₃ enters once, as a constant independent of the step: VAR Thm 1, p.279, "a₀, a₁, B₃, depend on d and L only" (quoted in D2.0(5)(c)(β)), with CRE's reuse of the symbol at every step (V4's (B3)(iii) records only its independence of the coupling and of the branch, and uses it at step 1); for the background values (4)'s question whether (1+β₀)B₃ < L² does not arise. For the words w_j it is not settled: (e).
- *(e) What the words of (c) are, and the expectation-level closure (WILSON_IT1_BACKGROUND_ITERATION_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md, 26 Oct; reviewed in round 47: the identification of the words, the parity at the general step, the identity's algebra and the k = K form derived; the expectation-level closure reduced to the interfaces (iii-a), (iii-c), (iv-a)–(iv-c), with the remainder R_bad over the labels on which the coarse chain has no D(K) form; repaired, not re-reviewed).* At each step j the words vanishing at B′_j = 0 are, for the fine loop, D2.1(2)'s slot-insertion words of C at step j, and, for the coarse loop, the expansion of the composed function V_j ↦ M^kU_j(V_j) along C^{(k)}: by (a)'s structure, (M^kU_j)(c) is a composition of (0.4)-averages (analytic near the identity because the chart logarithm is; RG1 p.252 introduces G^c for this; (0.5)–(0.10) are the axioms of the set average, not of (0.4)) of transports of U_j(V_j) along contours in B^k(c_−) ∪ B^k(c_+), and each transport expands by D2.1(2) into the transport of U_{j+1} with slot insertions (U_j(e^{iB′}V^{(j)}) depends on B′ through the minimiser, so the slots are the local objects, not the dependence). So the coarse words are analytic functions of D(j+1)-type slot insertions whose slots lie in the tubes of C's scale-k bonds, vanishing when the insertions vanish, with the symmetry of (5) (λ → −λ, A → −A) preserved by the composition since everything depends on (λ, A) through B′. For k = 1 the composed words begin at the step 2 → 3 substitution; the step-2 g-words of (3) come from g₁ itself and coincide with words of MU₁(V₁) on Ω₁^{(1)} by (I1). The first-order averages of those step-2 words of (3) vanish at step 2 in the interior by (5). No w_j is averaged at step 2: w_j (j ≥ k+1 ≥ 2) arises at the step j → j+1 substitution and is first averaged at step j+1 ≥ 3. The closure (26 Oct): w_j = Ξ^f_j − Ξ^{g_k}_j is the difference of the two chains' insertion words at the step j → j+1 (Ξ^f_j := f_j(e^{iB′_j}V^{(j)}) − f_{j+1}(V_{j+1}), Ξ^{g_k}_j likewise, from CRE (3.10)'s V^{(j)} = M^j(U_{j+1}) and U_j(M^jU_{j+1}) = U_{j+1}, D2.0(5)(c)(α)), and the step map is linear and observable-independent (D2.2(2)'s seven operations; CRE (3.2), (3.3), (3.8)–(3.11), (3.16)–(3.19)), so each w_j is averaged at step j+1 as part of both chains' step maps and is never a separate object: (i) its first-order parts are the two chains' class (i) activities at step j+1, even in the coupling by (5) run at step j+1 (RG1 (2.12) at the general step with "the expression under the exponential above vanishes at g_k = 0", p.268; CRE (3.15) at the general step), bounded by the class (i)/(ii) entry's c_λρ_{j+1}·(decay) with the composed slot amplitude; its flat second-order parts are the two chains' own δm_{j+1}, ζ_{j+1}, E°_{j+1}, absorbed by each chain's D2.3, with the ratio r_{j+1} := κ^f_{j+1}/κ^{g_k}_{j+1} of the two normalisations, |log r_{j+1}| ≤ 2C_S′𝔞^∨_{j+1}P_j(C) + 8C_ζ𝔞^∨_{j+1}; its deviation parts are the two chains' Y^d insertions; (ii) the bonds whose scale-k blocks leave Ω_K are excluded at every step by the nesting Ω_K ⊆ Ω_j (CRE (2.1)), and the tubes meeting (5)'s excluded regions are D2.6's attached species with the carrier item ((iv-a)–(iv-c) of the closure: the whole-label remainder R_bad ≤ Cκ_f^{(K)}N_tube·w_K, N_tube = 4ℓ_C·3·2^{d−1}, w_K ≤ 4e^{−2p₀(g_K)} reduced to LFR II (1.80)–(1.89) with the D2.6 closure §7.2–7.3 and (iv-a)'s carrier item, the anchored E^∂ form); (iii) the expectation-level statement is the identity E[W(C)] − N_k⟨g_k^{(k)}⟩_k = κ_f^{(K)}⟨e^{𝓔^f_K}β_k^{(K)}⟩_K^{good} + (the exponent difference) + (the insertion difference) + R_bad, N_k := κ_f^{(k)}e^{E^f_{const,k}}∏_{j>k}r_j (closure §3.1, the three terms over the labels where both chains have their D(K) forms), the first term bounded by (d), the second by the ratios r_j, the third by both chains' D(K)(ii) summed at K; At k = K the chain is trivial and E[W(C)] − κ_f^{(K)}⟨e^{𝓔^f_K}g_K^{(K)}⟩_K = κ_f^{(K)}⟨e^{𝓔^f_K}(β_K^{(K)} + I^f_K)⟩_K with |β_K^{(K)}| ≤ 8.48a_dc_hB₃ℓ_Cε_K·Lip_χ on the covered terms (closure §3.4): the form the construction uses at the stopping scale, needing neither the sum over k nor any shed word. - **Derived (D2.4; reviewed in round 45, no fatal or serious finding, six minors applied and not re-reviewed): the hull constant A_h depends on neither κ nor M.** V4 lem:c4-8-2(b) states |H_γ| ≤ A_hLMn_γ with A_h "fixed before the coupling", and its proof derives it "from the square geometry and the wall connectivity of a component's domains"; the pair section's re-proof says the contacts of γ lie within 2L of X_γ. So: X_γ is a union of at most n_γ distinct LM-cubes, wall-connected (incompatible domains share a cube or a wall, V4 app:domain-geometry; corner contact is the footprint convention of sec:c4-12-4 and is not needed here), hence of maximum-norm diameter < n_γLM; the contact set 𝖳_γ lies within 2L ≤ LM/4 of it (M ≥ 8), so its diameter is D ≤ 2n_γLM; and the square geometry of sec:extent-weights (an arc of length ≤ 4D + 1 contains contacts of diameter D < s/2; otherwise the whole contour, of length 4s ≤ 8D) gives |H_γ| ≤ 8D + 1 ≤ 17n_γLM, and likewise the contour distance d_C(a, a′) from a ∈ H_γ to a contact a′, which lem:c4-8-2(b) also bounds by A_hLMn_γ: d_C ≤ |H_γ| for a short hull and ≤ n_C/2 = 2s ≤ 4D for the whole contour. So A_h := 17 serves, an absolute constant; A_x is bounded the same way (the distance from a contact to a cube of X_γ, halos included, is ≤ 2n_γLM + 2R_h with R_h the halo radius; M ≤ R_h < 4M, since the hosts are rectangles of at most 4 M-cubes per direction (V4 eq:part1-potential-host, eq:c-probe-host), so 2R_h < 8M < LM for L > 11 and A_x ≤ 3). The halo index bound R_e of lem:c4-6-env (R_e = ⌈2max(R_h, L)/(LM)⌉ = 1) enters the envelope constants A_E, A_F, A′_E (hence V3's C_F := A_E), the footprint pin's constant C, and the threshold CρLM ≤ η/8 on g₀, not A_h or η. Hence ν = η can be fixed as an absolute number: with A_h := 17, A_s = 18, and ϖ := 1/16 (ϖ = min(1/16, λ_*/(64C_g)), and λ_* ≥ 2C_g log 128 by V4 eq:c2-kappa-choice gives λ_*/(64C_g) > 1/16 for every admissible κ), the only condition on the reserve η is 1000ηA_s < ϖ/8, met by η := 1/2 400 000 (V4 app:thresholds lets η depend on L, M, and κ; an absolute choice is within that clause); so κ_D′'s term ¼log(9/(2ν)) is independent of κ, M, and ε₁, and the order of choice (κ before M and ε₁) holds. Checked against V4 (lem:c4-8-2 and its proof, the pair section's item (b), sec:extent-weights, lem:c4-6-env); V3's A_h (L4780, L4790, L4798) is the same constant.

(2.7) at p = r and p = p₀ (D2.3(4), stopping scale) is not a reading: p.256 l.3 prints "where p is a positive integer", p₀ is a positive integer (p.244 l.23), and p = r follows from p = ⌈r⌉. Of this entry only the ℓ_C restriction remains for the user's review (R-tr derived on ℓ₀ by RTR_PROOF_PASS_2026_10_23.md, with the PBG p.411 tie averaged); the status of the other items is the entry above.
- **Partly derived (D2.3(4), bullet "The stopping scale per loop"; reviewed, last minor repairs not re-reviewed): the stopping scale.** For a square of physical side pL^{−q}u the steps stop at uL^{−j(C)}, with j(C) the least j ≥ q + q̄(j), q̄(j) := ⌈log_L(L³M(1+γ²β′(j+1))^{β₀}(log g_min^{−2})^r)⌉ from CRE (2.5), (2.7) ((2.7) at p = r derived from the printed p = ⌈r⌉, re-check row 7a; R-cpl₀ printed, row 10), g_min := inf_K g_K > 0, γ := sup_K g_K. Derived: j(C) depends on q, L, M, β₀, β′, r, g_min, γ only, not on ε or K, under the coupling prescription inf_K g_K > 0 (the coupling item: RG I Thm 2's first assertion, p.259, Bałaban's for general G by RG I p.260, accepted, proof not printed; for simple G proved in the Bałaban project, ONE_LOOP_PROOFS.md Corollary 8.2; re-check rows 11, 26); for two loops j ≥ max(q₁, q₂, q₁₂) + q̄(j). Checked at K′ = K − j(C), for K′ ≤ N: the places identified so far where the final step enters (D2.0(1), D2.3(4) q*, D2.3(7) with g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K, ONE MORE STEP (7)(d) with ε_{K′} ≤ (1+β₀)(1+g_K²β′j(C))^{β₀}ε_K (CRE (2.8)) and ℓ_Cε_{K′} → 0 as γ → 0 with γ/g_min bounded, D2.3(4)'s torus condition (TC) (implied at K′ by the unit-scale condition), D2.3(8), and D2.0(5)(c), whose only K-dependent constants are the coupling sup g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K and the torus count of (ε)'s wrap term, which improves by L^{j(C)}; all checked; reviewed in round 45, no fatal or serious finding, minors applied and not re-reviewed); the identity E_ε[W(C)] = κ^{(K′)}⟨𝒲_{K′}⟩_{K′} + errors on terms where D(K′) holds. At K′ > N_{K′}, i.e. for all large K (N = N_k ≤ O(1)(log g_k^{−2})^ν, LFR I p.198): D2.7's, derived in WILSON_IT1_D27_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md item 15 given the reading R-far″, its round-52 findings (it1-d27-20261027-r3) repaired and not re-reviewed (l.342; the round-48 review killed the note's earlier per-block accounting, items 8, 9 and 11, which item 15 replaces; (5)(c)(α) and (3)(b) derived), including D2.0(5)(f)'s large-field induction at k > N (item 11 read with item 15's species). - **Derived for every k (D2.2(4)(d); reviewed in rounds 40–44, minors applied and not re-reviewed): the representative of the ω-dependent factors.** D(k)'s identity holds at its representative ρ_k(V_k), while the step map evaluates at the representative in Ax_k(𝔅_k, U_{k+1}). The two differ by RG1 p.265's first transformation v₁(x) = U_{k+1}(Γ^{(k)}_{y,x})^{−1}ρ̄_k(V_k)(Γ^{(k)}_{y,x}), the mismatch of the hierarchical transports of D(k)'s representative and of U_{k+1} down the chains (REG (1.19) with (1.20) telescoped; derived in the route (ii) bullet), which depends on B′ through ρ_k. For k ≥ 2 (derived in (d′) of the route (ii) bullet; Steps 1–7 reviewed in round 42, no fatal or serious finding): D(k)'s frame is explicit under (c)'s output convention ρ_{k+1}(W) = ρ_k(V^{(k)}(W)). Every hierarchical average M^n(ρ_k(V_k)), n < k, has the mean variables of RG1 (0.11) equal to 1 in every (n+1)-block (the hierarchical mean axial gauge), because V^{(k)} satisfies RG1 p.265's 𝐆(V) = 0, which by (0.19) is V^{(k)}(y,x) = 1 for y ∈ T^{(k+1)}, x ∈ B(y), printed outright in CRE (3.10) (p.266: "V_k(y,x) = 1 for y ∈ Γ_{k+1}, x ∈ B(y)"), and V4's U₁ is in CRE p.248's axial mean gauge at the root; the reference U_{k+1} = ρ_k(V^{(k)}) is in the same frame, which fixes the gauge of the reference (VAR p.280's U₀ is not used). So at every level n the transports ρ̄^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}) and U^n_{k+1}(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}) are means of level-n holonomies based at x_{n+1} (the k = 1 identity, RG1 (0.5)–(0.6)); v₁(x) is the ordered product over the levels of the mismatches of these means, conjugated by the real partial transports of U_{k+1}; with ρ_k(V_k) = (e^{iη𝐇_k}U_{k+1})^{g}, g = v₁^{−1}u_k, the level-n mismatch is bounded through the holonomy difference of M^n(e^{iη𝐇_k}U_{k+1}) and M^n(U_{k+1}) by 2d(L−1)L^nη sup_{B^k(y)}|𝐇_k|(1 + o(1)) for n ≥ 1 and by d(L−1)η sup_{B^k(y)}|𝐇_k|(1 + o(1)) at n = 0 (CMP 98 (97), (131), transferred to RG1's average by p.254), plus terms geometric in L^{n−k} from the conjugations. Result: |log v₁(B′)| ≤ 2d(1 + o(1))sup_{B^k(y)}|𝐇_k| ≤ 3d sup_{B^k(y)}|𝐇_k| uniformly in k; v₁ analytic in 𝐇_k on B^k(y) (g is an explicit composition of RG1's averages and means applied to e^{iη𝐇_k}U_{k+1}, so ρ_k's hierarchical transports are analytic in V_k); v₁(0) = 1; on a layer with top level j < k the same sum over the levels n < j gives 2d(1 + o(1))L^{j−k}sup_{B^j(y)}|𝐇_k|, the older-layer form with 3dL^{j−k} in place of C_w; at the complex configurations of D(k+1)(iii)'s domain, and at the complex backgrounds the step map produces (the reference at complexified data, in the frame, reached through its Landau representation over the real reference), the same bound holds with a factor 1 + o(1) from the G^c-valued conjugating transports (e^{8c_Gdι(1 + o(1))} at a field-small base, ι ≤ α_{1,n} the per-bond radius of the domain in units of the layer's scale, times e^{O(c_G(C_w + 3d)dι)} at the reference; (d′), Step 8; reviewed in rounds 43–44); at a general complex configuration 𝐔 = e^{iηa′}U_{k+1} of that domain v₁ = c₀(𝐔)v̂₁, with v̂₁ the same bounded mismatch relative to the minimiser Ū at 𝐔's averages, the step at 𝐔 being the c₀-conjugate of the step at Ū (w_tot = c₀ŵ with ŵ = û_k^{−1}v̂₁, û_k the u_k at Ū), and c₀(𝐔) a B′-independent change of representative, absorbed by (6)(b7) in its covariant form (G^c-valued u; the ω-clause at 𝐔 stated for the slot values σ with R(c₀(𝐔))^{−1}σ in the polydisc, D(k)(iii)), as the real c₀ at the real points of the polydisc already is (Step 8; reviewed in round 44, minors applied and not re-reviewed). So (4)(c)–(e) hold at D(k)'s representative with w_tot = u_k^{−1}v₁ and C_w + 3d in place of C_w (C_w + 2d at k = 1). At k = 1 (derived below; reviewed in round 41, no fatal or serious finding, minors applied and not re-reviewed): ρ₁ is V4's U₁ in CRE p.248's axial mean gauge, v₁(x) = U₂(Γ_{y,x})^{−1}M({Ad_{u₁(y)}h_Γ(e^{iη𝐇₁}U₂)}_{Γ∈𝐆(y,x)}) with h_Γ the holonomy of Γ_{y,x} ∪ (−Γ) based at y (RG1 (0.5)–(0.6), (0.10)–(0.11)), analytic in 𝐇₁ on B(y) and |log v₁(B′)| ≤ d(1 + o(1))sup_{B(y)}|𝐇₁| ≤ 2d sup_{B(y)}|𝐇₁|, so at the root w_tot = u₁^{−1}v₁ enters (4)(c)–(e) with C_w + 2d in place of C_w. Withdrawn (round 40 F1): the derivation with U₁ = e^{iη𝐇_k(B′)} in CMP 98 (77), which computes the Landau-to-axial map ũ, not v₁. The two routes, for the record. (i) The step-(k−1) map delivers D(k) at VAR Sect. G's axial configuration relative to U_{k+1}, so v₁ = 1. The sources do not give this: RG1 transforms "at first", V4's U₁ is in CRE p.248's axial mean gauge, and D(k)'s frame is the hierarchical mean axial gauge ((d′), Step 1), not that configuration. (ii) Prove block locality, analyticity, a B′-independent value at B′ = 0, and a k-uniform size bound for v₁ (all derived; reviewed in rounds 40–42), and put w = u_k^{−1}v₁ in D2.2(4)(c)–(e). Affects only the ω-dependent factors (uncovered terms).
  - *At the next step.* The same problem recurs at the next step: (6)(b7)'s pointwise rotation covers only changes that do not depend on the integration variable, while v₁ of step k+2 depends on B″, so by Cov(k+1) the insertions become functions of R(v₁(B″))^{−1}σ. (4)(e)'s joint expansion and (6)(e)'s radius propagation interpolate only R(w(s))σ. Route (ii) with the composite w_tot = u_k^{−1}v₁ (R-g's w) and (4)(e)'s s-interpolation repairs both; it needs the three properties of v₁.
  - *Route (ii) from REG (1.19) (D2.2(2)(vii)).* The gauge of (1.19) is fixed blockwise by the recursion r(x_n) = Ū_0^n(Γ)^{−1}r(x_{n+1})Ū^n(Γ) down each chain from r(x_k) = 1. So: (a) v₁ is block-local, since the conditions on B^k(y) involve the configuration on B^k(y) only; (b) its value at B′ = 0 is 1, independent of B′, since D(k)'s representative at V^{(k)} is U_{k+1} under (c)'s output convention (RG1's U_k′(1) = 1; (d′), Step 7); (c) it is analytic in B′ where the averages Ū^n are, D(k)'s representative being an explicit analytic gauge transform of e^{iη𝐇_k}U_{k+1} ((d′), Step 7; VAR p.305 gives analyticity in a fixed gauge only). *The size bound: what CMP 98 (64)–(77) give (round 40; reviewed, repairs not re-reviewed).* CMP 98 poses the axial conditions (64), (66), (67) for U′ relative to a fixed U₀ (R₀, R̄₀^j the transports of U₀ and of its averages), with the relative averages Ũ′^j = (overline{U′U₀})^j(Ū₀^j)^{−1} ((65), (68), (69)), and solves them for U′ = U₁^u: (76) (R̄^j_{0,x_{j+1}}u)(x_j) = u(x_{j+1})(R̄^j_{0,x_{j+1}}Ũ₁^j)(Γ_{x_{j+1},x_j}) for x_j ∈ B(x_{j+1}), so down each chain x = x₀ ∈ B(x₁) ⊂ … ⊂ B^k(x_k) the value u(x) is u(x_k) times the ordered product, over the levels j = k−1, …, 0 from the left, of the level-j relative average Ũ₁^j transported along the block path Γ_{x_{j+1},x_j} (at most d(L−1) level-j block bonds) and conjugated by the real transports R̄₀ of U₀'s averages, with an overall R(U₀(Γ^{(k)}_{y,x}))^{−1} on the product ((77), (101)). Telescoped with (1.20), REG (1.19) says Ū^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}) = Ū₀^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}) at every level of every chain: the hierarchical transports agree (round 40, (I1)). Hence, with U₀ = U_{k+1}, U′U₀ = ρ_k(V_k) D(k)'s representative and u = v₁ with v₁ = 1 at the top centres (RG1 p.265), v₁(x) = U_{k+1}(Γ^{(k)}_{y,x})^{−1}ρ̄_k(V_k)(Γ^{(k)}_{y,x}), the mismatch of the hierarchical transports of D(k)'s representative and of U_{k+1}. Withdrawn (round 40 F1, S1): putting U₁ = e^{iη𝐇_k(B′)} (VAR Sect. G's Landau frame) into (77) computes the Landau-to-axial map ũ = R(U₀(Γ^{(k)}))^{−1}(u_k(y)^{−1})u_k ((77) against (101) with (81), (84), (99)), not v₁; U_k′(V′) = ρ_k(V_k)U_{k+1}^{−1} equals e^{iη𝐇_k} only if D(k)'s representative is the Landau configuration, and then R-g's w would be 1. For ũ the correct count is |log ũ| ≤ 6d(1 + o(1))sup_{top block}|𝐇_k| ((127)–(131) bound the double-bar averages Ū̄₁^j, and Ũ₁^j = (Ū̄₁^j)^{v_j} by (97), each factor of v_j with phase up to d(L−1)L^jη|𝐇_k|; the geometric sum Σ_{j<k}2d(L−1)L^jη = 2d(L^kη − η) is right), and (106) runs in the same order as (77), normalised by (81) instead of u(y) = 1. (a) Block locality of v₁: both transports use the bonds of B^k(y) (the averages at a level-j bond c use B^j(c₋) ∪ B^j(c₊), CMP 98 (113), p.34). (b) At B′ = 0: v₁ = 1, since D(k)'s representative at V^{(k)} is U_{k+1} under (c)'s output convention ((d′), Step 7). (c) Analyticity in B′: derived in (d′), Step 7 (ρ_k's hierarchical transports are analytic in V_k). (d) Size at k = 1 (derived; reviewed in round 41, minors applied and not re-reviewed): ρ₁ = V4's U₁(e^{iB′}V^{(1)}) in CRE p.248's axial mean gauge (V4 sec:branch-data, (B1); CRE p.247: "U(y,x) are the variables introduced by (0.11) [I]"): M({ρ₁(Γ)}_{Γ∈𝐆(y,x)}) = 1 for the mean contour variables of RG1 (0.10)–(0.11), while the step's Ax₁(𝔅₁, U₂) is REG's single-contour (1.19) (RG1 p.265 writes Ax_k(T^{(k)}, U_{k+1}) in REG p.79's notation; VAR p.280 cites "(1.19) in [6]"), so v₁(x) = U₂(Γ_{y,x})^{−1}ρ₁(Γ_{y,x}) with Γ_{y,x} the canonical path inside B(y), and v₁(0) = 1 (ρ₁ = U₂ at B′ = 0). These are the sources' readings (round 41); the alternatives would not hurt: were p.248's condition the single-contour one, v₁ ≡ 1 under (c)'s convention; were RG1's Ax₁ a relative mean-contour gauge with x-based relative variables, v₁(x) would be the mean of the mismatches U₂(Γ)^{−1}ρ₁(Γ), bounded as below. Exact form: with h_Γ := ρ₁(Γ_{y,x})ρ₁(Γ)^{−1} the holonomy of the closed contour Γ_{y,x} ∪ (−Γ) ⊂ B(y) based at y (at most d(L−1) fine bonds: RG1 p.252 defines 𝐆(y,x) as the shortest monotone contours, |n_μ| ≤ (L−1)/2), the mean condition M({h_Γ^{−1}ρ₁(Γ_{y,x})}_Γ) = 1 and RG1 (0.5)–(0.6) give ρ₁(Γ_{y,x}) = M({h_Γ}_{Γ∈𝐆(y,x)}) exactly (in logarithms: log ρ₁(Γ_{y,x}) is the mean of the log h_Γ up to BCH corrections of relative size o(1), every ρ₁(Γ) being O(L²ε₁) from 1 in the mean gauge). The holonomy is gauge-covariant: in the Landau frame of 𝐇₁, h_Γ(ρ₁) = Ad_{g(y)}h_Γ(e^{iη𝐇₁}U₂) with g(y) = u₁(y)^{±1} (v₁(y) = 1), ‖Ad_{g(y)}‖ ≤ e^{2c_G|log u₁(y)|} = 1 + o(1); and h_Γ(e^{iη𝐇₁}U₂)h_Γ(U₂)^{−1} = ∏_b exp(i Ad_{T_b}η𝐇₁(b)) with T_b real partial transports of U₂, so |log(h_Γ(e^{iη𝐇₁}U₂)h_Γ(U₂)^{−1})| ≤ d(L−1)η sup_{B(y)}|𝐇₁|(1 + o(1)) < d sup_{B(y)}|𝐇₁|(1 + o(1)) (Lη = 1 at k = 1); the term (Ad_{g(y)} − 1)log h_Γ(U₂) is o(1)sup|𝐇₁|. So v₁(x) = U₂(Γ_{y,x})^{−1}M({Ad_{g(y)}h_Γ(e^{iη𝐇₁}U₂)}_{Γ∈𝐆(y,x)}), analytic in 𝐇₁ (M is analytic, RG1 p.253; u₁ is analytic for |𝐇₁| < a₁, RG1 p.266), depending on 𝐇₁ on B(y) only, hence evaluable at 𝐇(s,tB′) on (4)(e)–(f)'s polydisc as u₁ is, with |log v₁(B′)| ≤ d(1 + o(1))sup_{B(y)}|𝐇₁(B′)| ≤ 2d sup_{B(y)}|𝐇₁(B′)|, with 𝐇₁'s localisation and decay, the inputs (4)(c) and (6)(e) use for u₁ (sup_{B(y)}|𝐇₁| ≤ h₁, l.942–943); at k = 1 (4)(c)–(e) hold with w_tot = u₁^{−1}v₁ and C_w + 2d in place of C_w. (d′) Size at k ≥ 2 (derived; Steps 1–7 reviewed in round 42, no fatal or serious finding, minors applied and not re-reviewed; Step 8 reviewed in rounds 43–44: the bound at field-small backgrounds correct in round 43, the factorisation at the general configurations and the covariant clause correct in mechanism in round 44, whose eight minors are applied and not re-reviewed). Notation: a chain x = x₀ ∈ B(x₁) ⊂ … ⊂ B^k(x_k), x_k = y, x_n ∈ T^{(n)}, with Γ_n := Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n} ⊂ T^{(n)} REG's path, one of RG1 p.252's shortest monotone contours 𝐆(x_{n+1},x_n); for a configuration U on T, U^n := M^n(U) on T^{(n)}, and the hierarchical transport U(Γ^{(k)}_{y,x}) := U^{k−1}(Γ_{k−1})U^{k−2}(Γ_{k−2})⋯U^0(Γ_0); α₀ the curvature of U_{k+1} on T, |U_{k+1}(∂p) − 1| < α₀η², η = L^{−k} ((6)(e)'s α_{0,k+1}); α₁ := sup_{B^k(y)}|𝐇_k(B′)|. *Step 1, D(k)'s frame.* Under (c)'s output convention, ρ_{k+1}(W) = ρ_k(V^{(k)}(W)) and M^k(ρ_k(V)) = V (the δ-function, CRE (1.11) at k = 1; M(V^{(k)}(W)) = W). Claim: for every k and every V in ρ_k's domain, M^n(ρ_k(V))(x_{n+1}, x_n) = 1 for n = 0, …, k−1, x_{n+1} ∈ T^{(n+1)} (on Γ_{n+1} = Ω_{n+1}^{(n+1)} in the multi-domain construction, CRE (3.10) and p.248's "y ∈ Ω₁^{(1)}"; T^{(n+1)} is the single-domain reading of RG1), x_n ∈ B(x_{n+1})∖{x_{n+1}}, in the mean variables of RG1 (0.11): the hierarchical mean axial gauge. By induction on k. At k = 1 it is CRE p.248's "axial gauge conditions U(y,x) = 1 for x ∈ B(y), x ≠ y" for V4's U₁. At k+1 the levels n ≤ k−1 are the claim for ρ_k at V = V^{(k)}(W), and the level n = k is M^k(ρ_k(V^{(k)})) = V^{(k)} with V^{(k)}(y,x) = 1 for y ∈ T^{(k+1)}, x ∈ B(y): RG1 p.265 takes V^{(k)} as "the element of the orbit satisfying the axial gauge conditions 𝐆(V) = 0", where 𝐆(V) = Σ_{y∈T^{(k+1)}}Σ_{x∈B(y),x≠y}[1 − Re tr V(y,x)] ((0.19), (2.1)), each term ≥ 0 for G-valued V with equality iff V(y,x) = 1; CRE (3.10) (p.266) prints the condition outright, V^{(k)} being the minimum over "V_k: V̄_k = V_{k+1} on Γ_{k+1} = Ω_{k+1}^{(k+1)}, V_k(y,x) = 1 for y ∈ Γ_{k+1}, x ∈ B(y)", "given by V^{(k)} = M^k(U_{k+1})". The frame is consistent and unique in each orbit of REG (1.14)'s subgroup (u = 1 on T^{(k)}): by RG1 (0.6), M^n(U^u)(x_{n+1}, x_n) = u(x_{n+1})M^n(U)(x_{n+1}, x_n)u(x_n)^{−1}, so the conditions are solved by u(x_n) = u(x_{n+1})M^n(U)(x_{n+1}, x_n) down each chain from u(x_k) = 1, and only so. In particular the reference U_{k+1} = ρ_k(V^{(k)}) is in this frame (the gauge of the reference), and the frame is not Ax_k(𝔅_k, U_{k+1}) (REG's single-contour condition relative to U_{k+1}), so route (i)'s v₁ = 1 fails and route (ii) is the route. *Step 2, transports as means of holonomies.* For a T^{(n)}-configuration ρ^n with ρ^n(x_{n+1}, x_n) = 1 and h_Γ := ρ^n(Γ_n)ρ^n(Γ)^{−1}, Γ ∈ 𝐆(x_{n+1}, x_n) (the holonomy of the closed contour Γ_n ∪ (−Γ) ⊂ B(x_{n+1}) based at x_{n+1}, of at most d(L−1) level-n bonds), 1 = M({ρ^n(Γ)}_Γ) = M({h_Γ^{−1}ρ^n(Γ_n)}_Γ) = M({h_Γ^{−1}}_Γ)ρ^n(Γ_n) by (0.6), so ρ^n(Γ_n) = M({h_Γ}_Γ) by (0.5): the k = 1 identity at every level, for ρ^n = M^n(ρ_k(V_k)) and for U^n_{k+1} = M^n(U_{k+1}) alike (Step 1). *Step 3, the level mismatches.* With δ_n := U^n_{k+1}(Γ_n)^{−1}ρ^n(Γ_n) and P_n := U^{n−1}_{k+1}(Γ_{n−1})⋯U^0_{k+1}(Γ_0) (P₀ = 1), v₁(x) = U_{k+1}(Γ^{(k)}_{y,x})^{−1}ρ̄_k(V_k)(Γ^{(k)}_{y,x}) = ∏_{n=k−1}^{0}Ad_{P_n^{−1}}δ_n (n = k−1 leftmost): a product of exponentials conjugated by the real partial transports of U_{k+1}, so in (4)(c)'s convention |log v₁(x)| = Σ_{n<k}|log δ_n|. *Step 4, the Landau representation.* As in (d), ρ_k(V_k)^{v₁} = (e^{iη𝐇_k}U_{k+1})^{u_k}, so ρ_k(V_k) = Ṿ^{g} with Ṿ := e^{iη𝐇_k(B′)}U_{k+1} and g := v₁^{−1}u_k pointwise (the order is immaterial below). The averages are gauge-covariant (the loop in (0.12)'s exponent is based at c₋, so M(U^u) = M(U)^{u} with u restricted to T^{(1)}; CMP 98 (93) for the relative averages), hence h_Γ(ρ^n) = Ad_{g(x_{n+1})}h_Γ(Ṿ^n) for loops based at x_{n+1}, and δ_n = M({h_Γ(U^n_{k+1})}_Γ)^{−1}M({Ad_{g(x_{n+1})}h_Γ(Ṿ^n)}_Γ). *Step 5, the bound on δ_n.* The holonomies are near 1: a closed contour of ℓ ≤ d(L−1) level-n bonds bounds at most ℓ² level-n plaquettes, and |U^n_{k+1}(∂p) − 1| < 2α₀(L^nη)² (CMP 98 Proposition 2, (54), in the scaled form of p.37 l.8–9; RG1 p.254 l.8 transfers CMP 98's results to the average (0.12)), so |log h_Γ(U^n_{k+1})| ≤ 2d²L²α₀L^{2(n−k)}(1 + o(1)) =: κ_n. M is analytic near 1 with M({e^{iZ_Γ}}_Γ) = exp(i·avg_ΓZ_Γ + O(sup_Γ|Z_Γ|²)) ((0.10) solved to second order), so |log δ_n| ≤ (1 + o(1))sup_Γ|log(h_Γ(U^n_{k+1})^{−1}Ad_{g(x_{n+1})}h_Γ(Ṿ^n))| ≤ (1 + o(1))[A_n + (e^{2c_G|log g(x_{n+1})|} − 1)(κ_n + A_n)], A_n := sup_Γ|log(h_Γ(U^n_{k+1})^{−1}h_Γ(Ṿ^n))| ((4)(c)'s pattern |R(w)ω − ω| ≤ (e^{2c_G|log w|} − 1)|ω|). (α) A_n.
