Ṿ^n = Ũ₁^nU^n_{k+1} with Ũ₁^n := Ṿ^n(U^n_{k+1})^{−1} the relative average of CMP 98 (68)–(69) at U₁ = e^{iη𝐇_k}, U₀ = U_{k+1}, and (97) gives Ũ₁^n = (Ū̄₁^n)^{v_n} in the relative action, i.e. Ṿ^n = (Ū̄₁^nU^n_{k+1})^{v_n} in the ordinary one, v_n(x) the product of the n means of transported Ū̄₁^j, j < n, each ≤ d(L−1)L^jηα₁(1 + o(1)) (a block path of at most d(L−1)/2 level-j bonds, each < 2α₁L^jη by (131); the count of the ũ bullet above), so |log v_n(x)| ≤ d(L^n − 1)ηα₁(1 + o(1)) ≤ dL^{n−k}α₁(1 + o(1)). Then h_Γ(Ṿ^n)h_Γ(U^n_{k+1})^{−1} = Ad_{v_n(x_{n+1})}[h_Γ(Ū̄₁^nU^n_{k+1})h_Γ(U^n_{k+1})^{−1}]·[Ad_{v_n(x_{n+1})}h_Γ(U^n_{k+1})·h_Γ(U^n_{k+1})^{−1}]. The first bracket is ∏_b exp(i Ad_{T_b}log(Ū̄₁^n)_b) over the bonds b of the loop, T_b the real partial transports of U^n_{k+1} from x_{n+1}, with |log(Ū̄₁^n)_b| = |Q_n(U₀, η𝐇_k, b)| < 2α₁L^nη for 1 ≤ n < k by CMP 98 (131) (p.38; Proposition 4's dependence set B^n(b₋) ∪ B^n(b₊) ⊂ B^k(y)) and = η|𝐇_k(b)| ≤ ηα₁ at n = 0; so its logarithm is ≤ 2d(L−1)L^nηα₁(1 + o(1)) for n ≥ 1 and ≤ d(L−1)ηα₁(1 + o(1)) for n = 0. The second bracket is ≤ (e^{2c_G|log v_n(x_{n+1})|} − 1)κ_n = o(1)L^{n−k}α₁. Hence A_n ≤ 2d(L−1)L^nηα₁(1 + o(1)) + o(1)L^{n−k}α₁. (β) The conjugation by g(x_{n+1}) = v₁(x_{n+1})^{−1}u_k(x_{n+1}): |log g(x_{n+1})| ≤ |log u_k(x_{n+1})| + |log v₁(x_{n+1})| ≤ (C_w + 2d)(1 + o(1))α₁, by (4)(c) for u_k and, for v₁ at the level-(n+1) point x_{n+1}, by this derivation applied to the levels m ≥ n+1 of the chain (downward from v₁(x_k) = 1, where g(x_k) = u_k(y) as at k = 1); so the second term of the bracket is ≤ 2c_G(C_w + 2d)α₁(1 + o(1))(κ_n + A_n) = o(1)L^{n−k}α₁, as L²α₀, Lα₁, and (C_w + 2d)α₁ are small. *Step 6, the sum.* |log v₁(x)| = Σ_{n<k}|log δ_n| ≤ (1 + o(1))[d(L−1)η + Σ_{n=1}^{k−1}2d(L−1)L^nη]α₁ + o(1)α₁Σ_{n<k}L^{n−k} = (1 + o(1))[d(L−1)L^{−k} + 2d(1 − L^{1−k})]α₁ + o(1)α₁ ≤ 2d(1 + o(1))sup_{B^k(y)}|𝐇_k(B′)| ≤ 3d sup_{B^k(y)}|𝐇_k(B′)|, uniformly in k (at k = 1 the sum is the single term d(L−1)L^{−1}α₁, (d)'s bound); the o(1) are O(L²α₀ + c_G(C_w + 2d)(dLα₁ + d²L²α₀)) per level, none summed over k (in (4)(c)'s convention |log v₁| is the sum Σ_n|log δ_n| itself, Step 3; the principal logarithm, not used, differs from it by O((Σ_n|log δ_n|)²)). *Step 7, analyticity, the value at 0, the layers.* g is the hierarchical mean gauge fixing of Ṿ with the top values g(x_k) = u_k(x_k): g(x_n) = g(x_{n+1})Ṿ^n(x_{n+1}, x_n) down each chain (Step 1's recursion), an explicit composition of RG1's averages (0.12), means (0.10)–(0.11), and products applied to e^{iη𝐇_k}U_{k+1}, hence analytic in 𝐇_k where the averages are (CMP 98 Proposition 4, for |𝐇_k| below its c₄(d, L); M analytic on its domain near 1, RG1 p.253; u_k analytic for |𝐇_k| < a₁, p.266) and depending on 𝐇_k on B^k(y) only; the thresholds, these three and the smallness of L²α₀, dLα₁, and c_G(C_w + 2d)α₁ behind Steps 5–6's o(1), are met on (4)(e)–(f)'s polydisc for g_K small, where α₁ ≤ h_k ((6)(e), l.942–943) and α₀ = α_{0,k+1} are small (l.1496: α_{1,k} → 0 with g_K); so ρ_k(V_k) = Ṿ^g, its hierarchical transports, and v₁ are analytic in 𝐇_k, hence in B′ on (4)(e)–(f)'s polydisc as u_k is: (c) holds. v₁(0) = 1 since ρ_k(V^{(k)}) = U_{k+1} ((b)). On a layer of 𝔅_k with top level j < k (CRE p.267's transfer, as for u_k in (d)): the chains have the levels n < j, each of whose mean conditions was imposed at step n on Γ_{n+1} = Ω_{n+1}^{(n+1)}, which contains the layer's (n+1)-block centres (CRE (3.10)), so Steps 1–6 run with j for k after the rescaling e^{iη𝐇_k} = e^{iη_j(L^{j−k}𝐇_k)}, η_j := L^{−j} (Propositions 2 and 4 at the scale L^{−j}, with L^{j−k}sup_{B^j(y)}|𝐇_k| for α₁), U_{k+1}'s regularity at the scale L^{−j} there (reading R-bg) in place of α₀η², and give |log v₁| ≤ 2d(1 + o(1))L^{j−k}sup_{B^j(y)}|𝐇_k| ≤ 3dL^{j−k}sup_{B^j(y)}|𝐇_k|: the older-layer form of (6)(e) holds for v₁ with 3dL^{j−k} ≤ 3d in place of C_w, from the same bound on 𝐇_k over the layer-j blocks that (6)(e) uses for u_k. Steps 1–7 are stated at G-valued U_{k+1}. *Step 8, complex backgrounds (derived; reviewed in rounds 43–44, no fatal or serious finding; the round-44 minors F1–F8 applied and not re-reviewed).* Notation: for P = e^{iZ}, Z = X + iY with X, Y ∈ 𝔤, Z is the phase and Y = Im Z its imaginary part. The complex backgrounds are 𝐔 = e^{iηa′}U_{k+1} with a′ 𝔤^c-valued (D2.1(4)'s parametrisation, l.448), in D(k+1)(iii)'s domain (l.313–314 with k+1 for k): |ηA′| < α_{1,n}L^{−n} per fine bond on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, real and imaginary parts alike, so |Im Z(𝐔(b))| ≤ ιη per fine bond with ι ≤ α_{1,n} on the layer n in units of the layer's scale L^{−n} (l.1496 states the same radius as Lα_{1,k} in η_{k+1}-units on Ω_k and as α_{1,n}L^{k−n} in η_k-units on the older layers); max_nα_{1,n} is small for g_K small, by g_n ≤ (1 + β₀)g_K (l.499, l.982) and the monotonicity of g ↦ gC₁(log g^{−2})^{q₁} for small g; l.503's 4c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 is a further condition on a′, not the radius. *The two families.* (I) The frame family: the configurations ρ_k(V) at complexified level-k data V, the continuation of D(k)'s representative in the data that define it. It contains the step-(k−1) map's outputs at complexified data, U_{k+1}(W_c) = ρ_k(V^{(k)}(W_c)), which are the backgrounds the step map itself produces (at step k+1 with complex B″ the step-k reference is ρ_k(V^{(k)}) at V^{(k)} = M^k(𝐔_{k+2}), complexified data; l.972's reference minimiser at complex data is this object), and it contains the minimisers Ū below. On this family Step 1's mean conditions hold by the identity theorem (both sides analytic in V, equal at real V), M^k(ρ_k(V)) = V, and Steps 2–7 run as below with v₁(0) = 1. (II) The general configurations of the domain, e^{iηa′}U_{k+1} with arbitrary a′: not in the frame and not minimisers in general (round 43's example: U_{k+1} = 1, a′ = iιH on the e₁-bonds with H in a Cartan subalgebra, flat; the hierarchical mean gauge fixing of 𝐔 is g₀(x) = e^{−iιη(x−y)₁H} ≠ 1). There v₁(𝐔, B′) = c₀(𝐔)v̂₁(𝐔, B′): with Ū := ρ_k(M^k(𝐔)) the minimiser at 𝐔's own averages (in family (I), the data M^k(𝐔) being complex), v̂₁ := Ū(Γ^{(k)}_{y,x})^{−1}ρ̄_k(V_k)(Γ^{(k)}_{y,x}) is family (I)'s v₁ at Ū (M^k(Ū) = M^k(𝐔) by Step 1's M^k(ρ_k(V)) = V, so ρ_k(V_k) at V_k = e^{iB′}M^k(𝐔) is the same object at 𝐔 and at Ū), and c₀(𝐔) := 𝐔(Γ^{(k)}_{y,x})^{−1}Ū(Γ^{(k)}_{y,x}) is the mismatch of the hierarchical transports of 𝐔 and of Ū: a gauge transformation with c₀ = 1 on T^{(k)} (it fixes the block variables, RG1 (0.6)), independent of B′, analytic in 𝐔 (𝐔 ↦ M^k(𝐔) by RG1's averages on G^c, or by CMP 98 Proposition 4 with (127)–(131) over the real base; the minimiser in its data by VAR p.307 on the domain (172), with ε₁ enlarged as at l.972; the passage to the mean frame by Step 7; the transports are analytic), equal to 1 on family (I) (there Ū = ρ_k(M^k(ρ_k(V))) = ρ_k(V) = 𝐔), G-valued at real 𝐔 (M^k(𝐔) is then G-valued, RG1 (0.10)–(0.12) at G-valued input, and Ū a real minimiser), and equal to h when 𝐔 = Ū^h with h = 1 on T^{(k)} (𝐔(Γ) = Ū(Γ)h(x)^{−1}), that is g₀(𝐔)^{−1} for the hierarchical mean gauge fixing g₀ of such a 𝐔. Its size: |log c₀| = O(dι), since c₀ = [𝐔(Γ^{(k)})^{−1}U_{k+1}(Γ^{(k)})]·v₁(U_{k+1}; B′₀) with B′₀ := (1/i)log(M^k(𝐔)M^k(U_{k+1})^{−1}) the complex datum of Ū over the real reference, the first factor ≤ 2dι(1 + o(1)) by (ii)'s telescoping below and the second ≤ 3d sup_{B^k(y)}|𝐇_k(B′₀)| = O(dι) by Steps 1–7 at the real reference with the complex datum B′₀ (|B′₀| = O(dι), *The reference Ū* below). The step at a general 𝐔 is the c₀-conjugate of the step at Ū: its words are evaluated at Ax_k(𝔅_k, 𝐔) = ρ_k(V_k)^{v₁} = (ρ_k(V_k)^{v̂₁})^{c₀} (gauge action U^u(b) = u(b₋)U(b)u(b₊)^{−1}, so (U^b)^a = U^{ab}), which depends on 𝐔 through its block data and its hierarchical transports only, both shared with Ū^{c₀} (c₀ = 1 on T^{(k)}; Ū^{c₀}(Γ^{(k)}) = Ū(Γ^{(k)})c₀(x)^{−1} = 𝐔(Γ^{(k)})); so the Landau gauge at 𝐔 is posed relative to the minimiser Ū^{c₀} (VAR's base is a minimiser; the Landau condition is covariant under the simultaneous transformation of the configuration and of the base, REG (1.18), PBG (3.33)–(3.34) as used at l.972, continued to G^c-valued transformations by analyticity), whence 𝐇_k(𝐔, B′) = Ad_{c₀}𝐇̂_k(B′) and u_k(𝐔, B′) = c₀û_k(B′)c₀^{−1}, with 𝐇̂_k and û_k those of the step at Ū ((e^{iη𝐇̂_k}Ū)^{c₀} = e^{iηAd_{c₀}𝐇̂_k}Ū^{c₀} and (e^{iη𝐇̂_k}Ū)^{c₀û_k} = ((e^{iη𝐇̂_k}Ū)^{c₀})^{c₀û_kc₀^{−1}}), 𝐇̂_k(0) = 0, and the B′-independent factor stands outermost in the composite: w_tot = u_k^{−1}v₁ = c₀û_k^{−1}c₀^{−1}·c₀v̂₁ = c₀ŵ with ŵ := û_k^{−1}v̂₁, family (I)'s w_tot at Ū. So at a general 𝐔 the change of representative from D(k)'s ρ_k(V_k) to the step's Ax_k(𝔅_k, 𝐔) is the B′-dependent v̂₁, bounded below, followed by the B′-independent c₀(𝐔). *c₀ is absorbed by (6)(b7) in its covariant form.* (6)(b7) already absorbs c₀ at the real points of the polydisc (there c₀ is G-valued and B′-independent, and the step's words at the two representatives differ by the rotation R(c₀)^{−1} of the slot values, which preserves the slot polydisc). At a complex 𝐔 the same covariance holds with the G^c-valued c₀ ((6)(b7), *G^c-valued u*: Cov(k) for G^c-valued u by analytic continuation, closed words being analytic in u and G a totally real uniqueness set in G^c), and the slot values are rotated by the G^c-valued R(c₀(𝐔))^{−1}, which does not preserve the polydisc around the real support (the continuation needs c₀ inside the connected domain of analyticity in u, which |log c₀| = O(dι) gives; the domain is rotated, not shrunk). So D(k+1)(iii)'s ω-clause at a complex configuration 𝐔 is stated in the frame of 𝐔's minimiser: Ax_k at 𝐔 is Ax_k at Ū transformed by c₀, so by Cov ((6)(b7)) word(Ū^{c₀}, R(c₀)𝐉, σ) = word(Ū, 𝐉, R(c₀)^{−1}σ), the plain clause holds at Ū (family (I)), and the clause at 𝐔 holds for the slot values σ with R(c₀(𝐔))^{−1}σ in the polydisc around the real support (D(k)(iii), *The ω-clause at complex configurations*). At real 𝐔 this is the present clause (R(c₀) real orthogonal); on family (I) it is the present clause (c₀ = 1); it is the analytic continuation of the real clause, with σ̂ := R(c₀(𝐔))^{−1}σ as the analytic coordinate in l.448's identity theorem, jointly with a′ (the words at 𝐔 are the words at Ū in σ̂ composed with ŵ, analytic in (a′, σ̂); (a′, σ̂) ↦ (a′, R(c₀(a′))σ̂) is biholomorphic and maps real points to real points). The propagation (l.961) and the frame bookkeeping (l.982) concern the B′-dependent ŵ = û_k^{−1}v̂₁ only, as the backgrounds at which D(k) is invoked at step k (ρ_k(V_k) at real or complexified data) lie in family (I), where c₀ = 1 and ŵ = w_tot; so they are unchanged, with Step 8's bound on v̂₁ below in place of v₁'s. The one further use of D(k)(iii) at general complex configurations, (11)(c)(β)'s polydisc points e^{iη(𝐇(s,tB′) + a″)}U_{k+1} (l.1498), is read through the frame point ρ_k(V_k): ŵ goes through (4)(c)'s budget as w does, and c₀ into the covariant clause, never into (4)(c)'s budget (which would cost dα_{1,j} against the margin r_{ω,j}2^{−(k+1−j)}). *The bound at a field-small base, and at the reference Ū.* The bound is first derived at a G^c-valued base 𝐔 = e^{iηa′}U_{k+1} that is field-small per bond over the real reference (|Z(ηa′(b))| ≤ ιη per fine bond, as the configurations of the domain are). The minimisers Ū and the members of family (I) are not of this kind in the mean frame (round 43's flat example: Ū = 𝐔^{g₀} is 1 inside the top blocks and carries the phase ι on the bonds between them, ιL^k in units of η; l.972: the complexified minimiser is field-small in the Landau frame and only phase-small in the axial-type frames); they are reached through their Landau representation over the real reference, *The reference Ū* below. RG1 defines the means and the averages on G^c-valued configurations (p.252 l.39–40 introduces G^c; p.253 l.17 and l.36 define M on G^c, for families of small diameter; (0.12) on p.254), so Step 1's frame, g, ρ_k(V_k) = Ṿ^g, its hierarchical transports, and v₁ are the same compositions at 𝐔, jointly analytic in (𝐔, 𝐇_k) where the means (small diameter), Proposition 4 (phase below c₄(d, L)), and u_k (|𝐇_k| < a₁) are defined; the identity theorem of (4)(c)'s bullet *Complex A_k and complex backgrounds* applies jointly, as for u_k. Size, at a field-small base 𝐔: Steps 2–6 run with 𝐔 for U_{k+1} with three changes. (i) CMP 98's Propositions 2 and 4 ((54), (131)) at the G^c-valued 𝐔: RG1 p.254 l.8–10 ("all results of the paper [12] are valid for it", the modifications being "connected … with the fact that we consider G^c-valued configurations"), Bałaban's, with α₀ the curvature constant of (2.34) for 𝐔 in the full domain (l.313–314; k ≥ 2, (d′)'s range) and α₁ as before; the quantitative modification (R₀ is no longer an isometry in (126)) is not printed and enters o(1) terms only. The printed alternative (round 43): Ṿ = e^{iηA}U_{k+1} with A := (1/iη)log(e^{iη𝐇_k}e^{iηa′}) ∈ 𝔤^c and Propositions 3–4 over the real base ((109), (127)–(131)) carry (97) and (131) without the reading, and at a field-small base (iii)'s holonomy bound follows from (97), (131), and the real reference's κ_n, so the alternative is complete modulo Proposition 2 over the real base; at the base Y of *The reference Ū* below the reading is used. (ii) The conjugating transports are G^c-valued. Over the real base, (97) and (131) with A = a′ give 𝐔^m(b) = v_m(b₋)Ū̄₁^m(b)U^m_{k+1}(b)v_m(b₊)^{−1} with |Z(Ū̄₁^m(b))| < 2ιL^{m−k} and |Z(v_m)| ≤ dL^{m−k}ι(1 + o(1)) (the count of Step 5 (α)); telescoping the v_m along a path of at most d(L−1) level-m bonds and summing over the levels, |Im Z(P_n)| ≤ Σ_{m<n}2d(L−1)ιL^{m−k}(1 + o(1)) ≤ 2dιL^{n−k}(1 + o(1)) and |Im Z(T_b)| ≤ 2dιL^{n+1−k}(1 + o(1)), both ≤ 2dι(1 + o(1)). (The sup bound on 𝐔's phases alone does not give this: (0.12)'s paths have L + 2Σ_ν|n_ν| bonds, and an abelian example with the transverse bonds oriented outwards gives Im Z(𝐔^1(c)) ≈ 2.1Lιη at d = 4; the route through (97) and (131) is the one used.) Ad_P = e^{i ad_X − ad_Y} with i ad_X skew-Hermitian and ad_Y Hermitian on 𝔤^c (the Hermitian extension of the Ad-invariant inner product), so by the log-norm bound ‖e^{S+H}‖ ≤ e^{λ_max(H)}, ‖Ad_P‖ ≤ e^{‖ad_Y‖} ≤ e^{2c_G|Im Z(P)|}, c_G as in (4)(c); hence ‖Ad_{P_n^{−1}}‖, ‖Ad_{T_b}‖ ≤ e^{4c_Gdι(1 + o(1))} = 1 + o(1), as c_Gd max_nα_{1,n} is small. These enter Step 3's convention, |log v₁(x)| := Σ_{n<k}|Ad_{P_n^{−1}}log δ_n| ≤ e^{4c_Gdι(1 + o(1))}Σ_{n<k}|log δ_n| (the convention of (4)(c)'s bullet *R(w)ω stays in the ω-domain*: w = ∏_je^{Z_j} with Σ_j|Z_j|), Step 5 (α)'s first bracket ∏_b exp(i Ad_{T_b}log(Ū̄₁^n)_b), whose logarithm is bounded by e^{4c_Gdι(1 + o(1))} times the real bound, and, in o(1) terms only, the transports R₀ and R̄₀^j inside (97)'s v_n and inside (126): a factor 1 + o(1) per level, not summed over k. (iii) M's Lipschitz bound in Step 5 holds on a complex neighbourhood of 1 (M analytic, p.253; Cauchy estimates) once the holonomies lie in it, |log h_Γ(𝐔^n)| ≤ κ_n + 2dιL^{n+1−k}(1 + o(1)), small. (β)'s bound on u_k at complex 𝐔: (6)(e) (l.941) bounds u_k's chain (106) with real transports; at complex 𝐔 the chain's conjugations are G^c-valued, each within (ii)'s bound, so |log u_k| ≤ C_we^{4c_Gdι(1 + o(1))}α₁ = C_w(1 + o(1))α₁ (l.941 carries the note). *The reference Ū* (and any member of family (I), ρ_k(V) at complex V, with a real datum V_r near V in place of V^{(k)}). With B′₀ := (1/i)log(M^k(𝐔)M^k(U_{k+1})^{−1}), |B′₀| = O(dι): by (97) and (131) over the real base, M^{k−1}(𝐔) = (Ū̄₁^{k−1}U^{k−1}_{k+1})^{v_{k−1}} with |Z(Ū̄₁^{k−1})| < 2ιL^{−1} and |Z(v_{k−1})| ≤ dL^{−1}ι(1 + o(1)), and one more mean along paths of at most d(L−1) level-(k−1) bonds (the count of Step 5 (α)) gives the datum. Then Ū = ρ_k(e^{iB′₀}V^{(k)}) = Y^{g} with Y := e^{iη𝐇_k(B′₀)}U_{k+1} the Landau configuration at the real reference and the complex datum B′₀ (Step 4 with B′₀ for B′) and g := g(B′₀) its hierarchical mean gauge fixing (Step 7), |log g| ≤ (C_w + 3d)sup_{B^k(y)}|𝐇_k(B′₀)| = O((C_w + 3d)dι) (|𝐇_k(B′₀)| = O(B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′₀|) by (6)(e)'s bound on 𝐇 by its datum, l.942). Y is field-small over the real reference with ι₀ := sup|𝐇_k(B′₀)| = O(dι) for ι, and its curvature is that of the Landau configurations e^{iη𝐇(s,tB′)}U_{k+1} with B′₀ for B′ ((6)(e) *The curvature conditions*, (4)(f)'s chain condition); so (i)–(iii) hold at the base Y, with α₀ the curvature constant of Y, which is Ū's (the plaquettes of Ū are the g-conjugates of Y's), not (2.34) for 𝐔. Steps 2–6 at the reference Ū: by the covariance of the averages (Step 4), Ū^n = (Y^n)^{g}, the hierarchical transports are Ū^n(Γ_n) = g(x_{n+1})Y^n(Γ_n)g(x_n)^{−1}, so P_n(Ū) = g(y)P_n(Y)g(x_n)^{−1} and ‖Ad_{P_n(Ū)^{−1}}‖ ≤ e^{2c_G(|log g(y)| + |log g(x_n)|)}‖Ad_{P_n(Y)^{−1}}‖; the holonomies are h_Γ(Ū^n) = Ad_{g(x_{n+1})}h_Γ(Y^n), so (iii) holds at Ū with the factor e^{2c_G|log g|}; and ρ_k(V_k) = (e^{iη𝐇̂_k(B′)}Ū)^{ĝ} = ((e^{iηX̂}Y)^{g})^{ĝ} with X̂ := Ad_{g^{−1}}𝐇̂_k(B′), |X̂| ≤ e^{2c_G|log g|}sup|𝐇̂_k(B′)|, ĝ := v̂₁^{−1}û_k, so the level-n mismatch is δ_n = M({Ad_{g(x_{n+1})}h_Γ(Y^n)}_Γ)^{−1}M({Ad_{g(x_{n+1})ĝ(x_{n+1})}h_Γ((e^{iηX̂}Y)^n)}_Γ), Step 5 at the field-small base Y with the fluctuation X̂ and the conjugation g(x_{n+1})ĝ(x_{n+1}) in place of g(x_{n+1}). Every g-conjugation costs a factor e^{2c_G|log g|} = e^{O(c_G(C_w + 3d)dι)} = 1 + o(1), none summed over k. Result: at a field-small base, |log v₁(B′)| ≤ 2d(1 + o(1))e^{8c_Gdι(1 + o(1))}sup_{B^k(y)}|𝐇_k(B′)| (the factor e^{4c_Gdι(1 + o(1))} of Step 3's convention times that of Step 5 (α)'s first bracket); at the reference Ū, hence for v̂₁ at every 𝐔 of the domain, |log v̂₁(B′)| ≤ 2d(1 + o(1))e^{8c_Gdι₀(1 + o(1))}e^{O(c_G(C_w + 3d)dι)}sup_{B^k(y)}|𝐇̂_k(B′)|; both are ≤ 3d sup_{B^k(y)}|𝐇_k(B′)| (𝐇̂_k at Ū), uniformly in k, once c_G(C_w + 3d)d max_nα_{1,n} and Steps 5–6's o(1) are small (g_K small); on a layer of top level j the same with Step 7's rescaling and ι = α_{1,j}, giving 3dL^{j−k}sup_{B^j(y)}|𝐇_k|; v₁(0) = 1 on family (I), v̂₁(0) = 1 everywhere (𝐇̂_k(0) = 0). So (c)–(e) hold at D(k)'s representative at real backgrounds and on family (I) with w_tot = u_k^{−1}v₁ and C_w + 3d in place of C_w, and at the general complex configurations with ŵ = û_k^{−1}v̂₁ in place of w_tot and the B′-independent c₀(𝐔) absorbed by (6)(b7)'s covariant form. Inputs: RG1 (0.5)–(0.6), (0.10)–(0.12), (0.19), p.252 (l.39–40 for G^c), p.253 l.17 and l.36, p.254 l.8–10, p.265 with (2.1); CRE p.248 l.37, (3.10), p.267; REG (1.14), (1.19); CMP 98 Proposition 2 ((54)) with p.37 l.8–9, (68)–(69), (93), (97), (131), Proposition 4; (4)(c)'s bound on u_k; R-bg for the older layers only. Note that v₁ is not block-constant on the top blocks (it is 1 at their centres only), which is what the frame bullet of (6)(e) accounts for.
- **D2.2(4) (reviewed, repairs not re-reviewed):** the older-layer margin rests on (6)(e)'s decay, under R-dec (Bałaban's, accepted).
- **D2.2(5) (reviewed: correct and sufficient as a draft step):** D_J^# is over the partition π_k (CRE p.278); R-vac(ii)'s bound is c|M|; the identity needs R-vac(i) only (evidence CRE pp.276, 279).
- **D2.2(6)(c)–(f) (reviewed, repairs not re-reviewed):** the change of variables is analytic in σ_j at frozen cutoff values, and V^{(j)}'s analyticity in σ_j is derived with O6 (WILSON_IT1_O6_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md §6, 26 Oct 2026; reviewed in round 46, it1-o6-20261026-r1: the domain repaired to R-m's, given R-m and (R-m′), the Gaussian-form part of V^{(j)} bounded, not re-reviewed; l.956, l.958); U_{k+1}'s membership is derived (CRE p.276's η = L^{−k}), with the entailment q₀ ≥ p₀.
- **D2.4 decisions (D2.4(9)).** Accepted by the user: the bound κ ≥ κ_D′ := max{κ_vac′, ¼log(9/(2ν)), 3240A/(L−2)}, κ_vac′ = 162(1+17 log2), on Bałaban's κ (D2.4(1)–(3), (12)(g), (13)(d)); the size count in (12)(a)'s form σ_{k+1} := #_{k+1} + n_{k+1}/M, which supersedes D2.4(3)'s n_{k+1} + #_{k+1}; β₀ ≤ 1/2 and L ≥ L₀ = 5000, under which the condition (1+β₀)⁸ < L of D2.4(6) holds (1.5⁸ < 26); q₀ = q₁ > p₀ with (R-m′), provisionally, which gives D2.4(6)(ζ)'s q₀ ≤ q₁. For the user's review: the amendment ρ̃_k = ρ_k/ᾱ_k of D(k)(ii)'s dev constant, with the per-insertion form (D2.4(5)); the tail clause (ii-t) of D(k)(ii) (D2.4(5)); R-vac(i′) (D2.4(2), Bałaban's, accepted). R-H′ is not a reading: it is the H′ bound, a part of (S), derived (l.170).

- **Coupling upper bound for general G (D2.0(5)(b)): not needed on the route of D2.3(7), once D2.4 proves (S-w) with constants independent of k ((12)(f), given its inputs (13)(d); (T′), (Z) and the Kotecký–Preiss condition follow from it); used by the D2.6 carry lemma (*Its users* below), for simple G through Cor. 8.2.** D2.3 absorbs the corner parts exactly at every step. Its remainder at a commensurate step is at most C_Eτ_k²(s_k+1)e^{−c_Es_k}, and the remainders of all steps sum to C_Στ̄² uniformly in K with g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K only (R-cpl₀). On D2.3's route, (5)(b) is the only user of the upper bound below. Without exact absorption, the corner remainders are summable uniformly in K if g_k² ≤ C(K−k+1)^{−θ} with θ(1 + δ/2) > 1, e.g. g_k² ≤ C/(K−k+1). RG I (0.31), Thm 2, p.259 prints this for G = SU(2) (proof announced for a separate paper; accepted as Bałaban's result; for simple G proved in the Bałaban project, ONE_LOOP_PROOFS.md Corollary 8.2). For general compact simple G it is not printed as a theorem; CRE (2.6) gives only the lower bound on g_k and g_m ≤ (1+β₀)g_n. What closes it: the lower bound β_{k+1} ≥ β_min > 0 for general G, computed from Bałaban's definition of the β-functions (RG I (1.20)–(1.22), p.264; Sect. 5), or exact absorption of the corner parts with zero remainder (D2.3).
  - *Group scope (RG I and CRE transcriptions).* RG I p.259, the paragraph after Theorem 1: Theorem 1 "will be proved in this paper for an arbitrary semisimple compact Lie group G ⊂ U(N)". RG I p.264, the paragraph after (1.22): β_{j+1} is smooth on [0, γ], "uniformly bounded on this interval together with all derivatives"; other properties are left to a separate paper. RG I p.260, first line, finishing the paragraph after Theorem 2: "restriction to the group SU(2) is superficial and is done only to simplify calculations".
  - *CRE (2.6), first line.* g_n ≤ (1 + g_n²β′(n−m))^{1/2}g_m follows from (0.20) and |β_k| ≤ β′ alone: 1/g_m² = 1/g_n² + Σ_{m<k≤n}β_k ≤ 1/g_n² + β′(n−m). So it holds for general G by RG I pp.259, 264.
  - *CRE (2.6), last inequality (its third printed line).* g_m ≤ (1+β₀)g_n uniformly in n − m needs Σ_{m<k≤n}β_k ≥ −(1 − (1+β₀)^{−2})g_n^{−2}, a lower bound that RG I p.264 does not give. Theorem 2 (0.31) gives it (β > 0).
  - *Its users.* Besides (5)(b): D2.0(3)(c)'s uniform bound of α_{0,k}; (5)(c)'s feedback factor (1+β₀)²g_K² and quadratic term; (5)(d)'s factor (1+β₀)⁸L²g_K² ((1+β₀)^{10}L²g_K² against ρ̃_{k+1}, D2.4(6)); (5)(f)'s τ̄²; D2.2(4)'s smallness conditions on g_K. Only (5)(b) needs the upper bound g_k² ≤ C/(K−k+1) itself. The D2.6 carry lemma (D2.6 closure §5.0) is a second user: its S_R = Σ_me^{−(25/8)(ν_e/L)R_{m+1}} is summed with the pairwise running u_m ≥ u_{m′} + b(m′ − m) − C of Cor. 8.2 (ONE_LOOP_PROOFS.md, simple G, proved in the Bałaban project), i.e. with the coupling upper bound.
  - *R-cpl₀ (weaker than R-cpl; printed in RG1's setting; for simple G derived from Cor. 8.2, entry above).* CRE prints (2.6) as following "from the renormalization group equations (0.20) [I], and from the properties of the β-functions" (p.255), in the setting of [I, II] (p.243), i.e. for RG I's group. So (2.6)–(2.7), uniformly in n − m, are Bałaban's presented-as-true statement at general G, accepted under the accepted-claims rule (the first lines of (2.6) and (2.7), used in D2.2(4)(f), come from the same printed derivation on p.255 as its last inequality). The users above rest on R-cpl₀; only (5)(b) needs R-cpl.
  - *R-cpl (Bałaban's statement, accepted under the accepted-claims rule; for simple G proved, Cor. 8.2).* Theorem 2 with (0.31) holds for general compact simple G, by RG I p.260, first line. The proof is announced for a separate paper, as it is for SU(2); for simple G it is proved in the Bałaban project (ONE_LOOP_PROOFS.md Corollary 8.2, RG1_Thm2_REP). With ε = L^{−K}, (0.31) gives g_k² ≤ (g_K^{−2} + β(K−k)log L)^{−1} ≤ C/(K−k+1), and with it CRE (2.6) at general G. Under R-cpl this item is closed with θ = 1; without it, it stays open as stated above.
- **Large-field lifetime cost (D2.0(5)(f)).** The count closes against LFR II (1.81); Bałaban's induction pp.384–389 is rerun with the extra per-cube summand. Belongs to D2.6. *Rerun in WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §7.2–7.3: the attached and exposed costs over the lifetime are ≤ ε_A(d_j′(Z)+1), of the form of (1.80)'s summand, covered by the induction with its O(1) doubled; p.387's future sums read and unchanged. Accepted from Bałaban, not reviewed.*
- **D2.1, the leading word at step k+1:** derived; reviewed, repairs not re-reviewed. The bound holds for groups in the scale-k region, case (A) of D2.1(4); case (B) goes to D2.6. Open inside D2.1: the size of case (B) (D2.6) and the complex-domain radii g_k|A_k| ≤ ε_H/C₁^{RG2} and 4c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 (D2.2, D2.4).
- **D2.0:** reviewed; the repairs of D2.0(3) and (5)(c) (the species of D2.0(3), the species-by-species D(k)(ii′), the 𝐉-term j*^{(k)}_n(𝐉) in D(k)(iii), the collar species att(j, coll)) are not re-reviewed. D(k) of D2.0(3) is a schema until D2.2 and D2.6 define the groups, the labels at every scale and ρ_k. (3.19)'s derivation is checked against p.268 and CMP98 (106)–(108), (159)–(163).
- **D2.2:** (1)–(6) reviewed; the last repairs are not re-reviewed, except (6)(b) (entry below). (5)(d) is in per-polymer form, carried into (6)(c). The older-layer margin rests on (6)(e)'s decay, under R-dec (Bałaban's, accepted); it does not use L ≥ 3 or β₀ ≤ 0.22. (6)(e) amends D(k)(iii) for k ≥ 2 to CRE's domain with the gradient condition, and needs L ≥ 3 for the ω-propagation. (6)(b) amends it for k ≥ 2 to functions of (𝐔, 𝐉, ω) with ω in slot variables.
- **R-m entails a constant comparison (D2.2(4)(f); derived given (R-m′), accepted provisionally).** The expansion of D2.2(4)(e) needs 8B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)ε_k < min(β,1)α_{1,k}/2. On Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} this is what CRE p.277's "much greater than bounds on this function" asserts (accepted as Bałaban's statement); on Ω_{k+1} the same inequality closes the margin directly. With CRE (2.4) and (2.28) it is q₁ > p₀ for g_K small, or q₁ = p₀ with C₁/A₀ > 16B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)/min(β,1); (6)(e)'s complex data need C₁/A₀ > 32C₁^{RG2}B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)/min(β,1) in the equality case. Under (R-m′), q₁ > p₀, the factor 2 and the complex-data form hold for g_K small; only the equality case would need the constant forms. No printed comparison of q₁ with p₀ was found (q₁ occurs only on CRE p.259 and LFR II p.368). A printed q₁ < p₀ would be a counterexample to R-m.
  - *Curvature conditions (D2.2(6)(e)).* The curvature conditions (2.34)–(2.35) of D(k)(iii)'s full domain need the same inequality with α_{0,k}: q₀ > p₀, or q₀ = p₀ with C₀/A₀ large. R-m's p.277 statement covers the 𝐉-bounds (2.36)–(2.37), which carry α_{0,n}. The constant in the q₁ = p₀ alternative is C₁/A₀ > 32C₁^{RG2}B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)/min(β,1).
- **ψ_{k+1} is a bijection between the supports (D2.2(2)): closed by D2.2(6)(a), reviewed (correct).** It rests on inputs whose status is the entry above (printed, derived or Bałaban's; none a reading): the (k)* convention (CRE p.247's X* at step k+1, with the at-least-one-endpoint convention, presupposed by p.269 and by RG1 p.268's elimination of B′(b₀(c))); R-g_k (g at (g_k, δ_k)); the k-independence of the O(1) in p.269's bound on 𝐇^{(k)}; R-cpl₀ (g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K); and p.269's bound "on the set S_{k+1}" applied to bonds leaving S_{k+1}.
- **Covariance of RG2's s-localised operators and Cov(k+1) (D2.2(4)(e)): closed by D2.2(6)(b); reviewed: correct and sufficient as a draft step within its scope; last minor repairs not re-reviewed; the representative part is derived (entry above; reviewed in rounds 40–44, the round-44 minors applied and not re-reviewed).** Cov(k) carries 𝐉 for k ≥ 2, and 𝐉′ of RG1 (3.13) is covariant (derived in (b4), with reading R-F₁ and P₁'s covariance as Bałaban's); (b6) lists σ_k's three channels through the old exponent at (σ_k, A) under R-x, with RG2 (1.41) and Lemma 2 for 𝐏^{(k)}; the data domain is invariant by RG1 p.263. Built from PBG (3.30)–(3.34), (3.119), pp.412–413, RG1 pp.267, 272–273, 276, RG2 pp.2, 11, 13, 21 and VAR pp.285–286. The insertions are functions of (𝐔, 𝐉, ω′); ω′ is in slot variables, so ψ_{k+1}'s covariance is neither used nor claimed, and representatives are changed by rotating slot values; the uniqueness sets of D̃ and of RG2 (1.13)–(1.16) are invariant balls. The Bałaban results used without a proof read here are listed in (6)(b8).
- **The bound on |ω| for k ≥ 2 and its propagation (D2.2(4)(c), (g)): closed by D2.2(6)(e) except the slot-value item below; reviewed, repairs not re-reviewed.** It uses CRE (3.14) on the kept indicators and CRE (2.6) with n = k, m = 0 for the j = 0 component. w is bounded on the whole polydisc, by h_k = O(α_{1,k}); on older layers the chain's gain L^{j+1−k} is cancelled by VAR (190)'s prefactor, and the complex-data decay rests on R-dec (Bałaban's, accepted), with θ = e^{−δ₀RM₁/32}; without R-dec the propagation is closed for j = k−1 only. The ω-domain is a polydisc around the real slot values. V^{(j)} depends on the integrated field and is handled in (6)(e), *The radii*.
- **R-adm (D2.2(6)(e); printed).** RG2 (1.18)'s "same bound for all admissible norms" includes the scale-weighted gradient norm of CRE (2.39). The basis is RG2 p.5 ("in all norms of Theorems 3.1.-3.10 [13]"), PBG (3.42) and RG2 (1.15). (6)(e) uses it to carry (4)(f)'s margin condition over to the gradient part of D(k)(iii)'s amended domain. *Decay.* RG2 (1.18)'s bounds carry no decay factor, so R-adm has no "decay form". The decay used by (6)(e) and by D2.2(4)(f)'s older regions is R-dec (next entry).
- **R-dec (D2.2(6)(e); Bałaban's statement, accepted; the rate computed from the print).** On CRE's multi-domain lattice, 𝐇(s, tB′) on the complex polydisc obeys VAR (190)'s kernel bound with the rate halved, in the sup and the scale-weighted gradient norm (and, for the 𝐉-conditions, the Laplacian and P₁ norms). Hence sup_{B^{j+1}(y)}|𝐇| ≤ O(1)L^{k−j−1}θ^{k−j−1}h_k, θ = e^{−δ₀RM₁/32}. Basis: RG2 p.5's (1.11) argument, which keeps half the walk decay at complex s for one scale once δ₁M ≥ 2κ₁; CRE p.267 ("Their properties are essentially the same as before, because of the localization of the field A_k"); VAR (190) with R-v; CRE p.277's "the exponential decay of the minimizing function 𝐇_k imply that this difference is still much greater than bounds on this function". The older-layer margins and the ω-propagation for j ≤ k−2 rest on it; without it the supplied pages give the propagation for j = k−1 only.
  - *Decay form (D2.2(6)(e)).* PBG (3.42)'s kernel bounds carry the factor e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)}, d PRT2's weighted distance; VAR (190)'s decay bound is printed for VAR's 𝓗 at s = 1. Its complex-data form is R-dec (entry above).
  - *Extended (D2.2(4)(f), carrying 𝐉).* The admissible norms also include |P₁𝐀| and |Δ_𝐔𝐀|, needed for RG1 (3.14) at 𝐀 = 𝐇(s, ·). Basis: PBG (3.42) lists |(Δ_UG′(U)λ)(x)|; RG1 p.272 promises "bounds for the covariant Laplace operator"; RG1 p.273 asserts (3.14) for 𝐇_j(B(t)) at s = 1; and RG2 p.2 substitutes 𝐇_k(s) into the (I.3.7)-type terms, which needs it.
- **Partly derived (D2.2(6)(e), bullet "The slot values at complex backgrounds"; reviewed, this form not re-reviewed): slot values at complex backgrounds.** Derived given the inputs listed below: the slot value is 𝐔 ↦ 𝐇^{(j)}(𝐔|_{D_j}) on p.269's determining set D_j (layers n = j+1 and n = j); every evaluation point restricts to R-𝐇's domain 𝒰_j on Y_j ((2.34), (2.38), (2.39), per bond and cube on each layer, with no choice of X; (2.35) and (2.37) are not used, since the argument bound runs on 𝐔 and its averages, REG (1.65)); so |𝐇^{(j)}| ≤ S_H := C_H(α_{0,j+1} + α_{1,j+1})e^{−R_j} at every evaluation point (R-𝐇), the total move is |Δσ_j| ≤ 4S_H/g_j, a″ included, and it lies within r_{ω,j}/4 of the real value, inside every polydisc of D(m)(iii)'s ω-clause, once 16C_H(α_{0,j+1} + α_{1,j+1})g_j^{−3/2}e^{−R_j} ≤ 1 (r_{ω,j} = g_j^{1/2}; small g_K); *Propagation* uses 16 and θ ≤ 1/4. Merged insertions evaluate 𝐇^{(j)} at composite points, covered by (4)(f) at each step i ≥ j+1. Inputs used: R-𝐇 (on 𝒰_j; analyticity derived from VAR Sect. G, CMP 98 Prop. 3 and CRE (1.3), re-check row 35; the bound S_H derived from REG Lemma 1, CMP 98 Prop. 3 and VAR (190) at complex data, with Schwarz's lemma for the passage from the minimiser function to 𝐇^{(j)}, the factor L^{n_b} of CMP 98 (128)–(129) cancelled by (190)'s field normalisation, and the decay under (δ₀/32)M ≥ L+1), R-𝐇₀ (j = 0, at the points reached, the same derivation; the axial-gauge decay is p.251's at real external U, Bałaban's, and at complex external U derived as the j = 0 case of R-𝐇's passage, p.309's 𝓗 with the block-local u, not reviewed), R-dec (Bałaban's), R-m (derived given (R-m′)), R-cpl₀ (derived from Cor. 8.2), R-adm (printed). The gauge frame of σ_j (pre- or post-w; how 𝐇^{(j)} transforms under w): derived given the representative item D2.2(4)(d), itself derived for every k (reviewed in rounds 40–44; l.1418). The base points on Ω₁∖Ω₂: derived at the root k = 1 (rounds 40–41; the entry above), reaching every k through (4)(f); for k ≥ 2 by the response chain of (6)(e), *For k ≥ 2* (derived, not reviewed).
- **Bałaban's, printed or identified (none a reading):** R-vac (D2.2(5)(b): (i) CRE p.279's "treated in exactly the same way" covers the source term at full strength for every t ∈ [0,1], Bałaban's, accepted, in the bound form (i′) of D2.4(2); (ii) Bałaban's "sufficiently small" in Kotecký–Preiss form, derived in D2.4(2) from RG2 Lemma 3 for κ ≥ 162(1 + 16 log2), with c = (9/64)e^{−4κ}, using R-L3 of D2.4(1), reviewed), R-I7 (D2.2(5)(b): CRE's "Sect. 7 [I]" is RG2 Sect. 2, identified by content), R-REG81 (D2.2(4)(d), printed: on B^j(y) the gauge transformation of REG p.81 is CMP 98's (106) at order j).
- **Derived:** R-F₁ (D2.2(6)(b4)): 𝐅₁ of RG1 (3.13), which the print does not define, is 𝐅 of (3.11) plus the lower-order operator D*D + DRD* − Δ_𝐔^ξ + P₁ applied to 𝐀; this is forced, since it is the only form under which (3.13) follows from (3.10)–(3.12) as printed (RG1 p.272's identity with RD*𝐇_j = 0). Also used as Bałaban's there: the covariance of P₁ (not defined in RG1), for the |P₁𝐀| clause of (3.14). R-F₁ is not load-bearing for Cov(k+1) (the unreplaced form is covariant too); and P₁'s covariance reduces to that of the unspecified "(lower order, local operator)" L of RG1 p.272, since the printed identity gives R = 1 − P.
- **Bałaban's, accepted (CRE p.269):** R-x (V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}) and 𝐏^{(k)} are the exact changes, D2.2(2)), and the p.269 statements on the characteristic functions under ψ_{k+1}.
- **Scalars of the step-(k+1) output (D2.2(5)(f)(i)): decided and derived in D2.2(6)(c), not reviewed.** Reference extraction: Q_{k+1}(𝔐) is exp(−ΣΨ°) times a sum of products of (e^{−δΨ} − 1), with Ψ° the value at the flat background, and the δΨ-factors join the insertions of scalar-linked groups. The extraction of δm and z from the flat parts is D2.3's. **D2.3** (exact absorption of δm_{k+1} and z_{k+1}): reviewed, repairs not re-reviewed. ℓ_C is restricted to multiples of L^{q*} ≤ L²MR_{K−1} (D2.3(4)), and D2.3(6)(b) gives the bound without the restriction. D2.3 hands (T′) (from (S-w)), (Z), the Kotecký–Preiss condition of the reference gas, the absolute convergence of (3), the bounds on Q_{k+1}(D) and Φ_{k+1}(S), and the sizes of the Y^d_𝒢 to D2.4 (D2.3(9)), and amends D(k) (notes in D2.0(3)). **D2.4** (the contraction count): reviewed, repairs not re-reviewed; its open items and decisions are at the top of this block. **D2.5–D2.7:** not started in this map (D2.6: WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md). D2.0(4) lists them, with what each needs and its sources. D2.1(5) adds two items to D2.2: the old insertions, and merging the parameters of D2.1(4) with the covariance decoupling. The carrier item of D2.6 is an input of D2.2 and is done with it.
- **D2.0, D2.1 repairs:** reviewed, no fatal or serious finding.
  - In D(k)(ii), earlier steps' attached parts are local activities of their large-field component (D2.6). VAR's scale-k set is Ω_k, and VAR's Λ_j are not CRE's (VAR p.277).
- **D2.2 (0)–(4), D2.0 (3), (5)(c):** reviewed; status in the entries above.
- The physical side of the loop is ℓ_C, kept apart from V3's ℓ = LMR₁; the contour count per cube is explicit in (5)(f).

## D2.0 The inductive form at step k, and the count check

### (1) Units

- *Bałaban's units at step k.* The block lattice of step k has unit spacing, and the fine lattice is T_η with η = L^{−k}. The scale-j lattice is T_{L^{−(k−j)}} in these units. Checked against three prints:
  - CRE (2.17) tests the fine plaquettes of T_η at threshold ε_kη²;
  - CRE p.268 states the regularity of U_{k+1} at the threshold 2B₃ε_{k+1}(L^{−1}η)², with (L^{−1}η)² the squared fine spacing of step k+1;
  - LFR I p.178 reads the LM₂R_j-cubes of T_{L^{−j}} as the L^{−(k−j)}LM₂R_j-cubes of T_η.
- *V3's units at step 1* are fine units (the fine lattice has unit spacing, the block lattice spacing L). V3's ℓ = LMR₁ and the side L²M₂R₁ of the CRE (1.18) cubes are in fine units (IT-1 note §3f(2) R1).
- *The contour.* C is a closed contour of fine bonds that is a union of block bonds at every scale j ≤ K (V3's squares C_n; IT-1 note §4). V3's C_n is a coarse translate of a square of side nL in fine units. It is a union of scale-j bonds for every j ≤ K when L^{K−1} divides n and the translate is by a scale-K lattice vector, with torus periods that are multiples of L^K. This restricts n and the periods to arithmetic progressions, which stay arbitrarily large. P_j(C) is the number of scale-j bonds of C, so P_j(C) = L^{−j}P_0(C). For a square loop of physical side ℓ_C at spacing L^{−K}, P_j(C) = 4ℓ_CL^{K−j}. (ℓ_C is not V3's collar width ℓ = LMR₁.)
- *Scales.* ε_j = g_jp₀(g_j), R_j and the couplings obey CRE (2.4)–(2.9). In particular ε_n ≤ (1+β₀)(n−m)^{1/2}ε_m, ε_m ≤ (1+β₀)²(n−m)^{β₀}ε_n and R_n ≤ LR_m for n > m (CRE (2.8)–(2.9)).

### (2) Regularity of the background (quoted, with one derived step)

- *Quoted.*
  - CRE (2.17), p.257: on χ_k(Ω_k), sup_{p⊂□^∼}|U_{k,□}(V_k,∂p) − 1| < ε_kη² for every LM₂R_k-cube □ ⊂ Ω_k, the sup over the fine plaquettes p ⊂ □^∼.
  - CRE p.268: "The field U_{k+1} satisfies the regularity condition |∂U_{k+1} − 1| < 2B₃ε_{k+1}(L⁻¹η)² on Ω_{k+1}".
  - CRE (2.34)–(2.39), p.261: the analyticity spaces Ũ_j^c(X, α̃₀, α̃₁), X ∈ 𝐃_j, of configurations (𝐔, 𝐉) with 𝐔 = U′U. On X ∩ (Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}) for n = 1, …, j−1, and on X ∩ Ω_j for n = j: |∂U − 1|, |∂𝐔 − 1| < (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(j−n)}))α_{0,n}ξ²(L^nξ)^{−2} (2.34), the analogous bounds (2.35)–(2.37) for the averaged configurations and for 𝐉, and U′ = exp iξA′ with L^nξ|A′|, (L^nξ)²|∇_U^ξA′| < (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(j−n)}))α_{1,n} (2.39). (2.38) asks for local gauges with U^u = exp iξA, L^nξ|A|, (L^nξ)²|∇^ξA| < BCMα_{0,n}, on cubes of size CML^nξ (CM for n = j). β is a small constant, e.g. 1/4. These conditions define the domain of analyticity; they are not bounds proved for U_k.
- *Derived: the deviation of coarser plaquettes.* Let P be a plaquette of the scale-j lattice, j ≤ k + 1, all of whose fine plaquettes lie in Ω_{k+1}, at a configuration in the support where p.268's bound holds. (CRE p.255 refers its set convention for plaquettes to "Sect. 0 [I]", RG I by CRE p.285; it was not located in the RG I transcription, so the condition on P is stated explicitly here.) In step-(k+1) units its side is L^{−(k+1−j)}, so it is a union of m² fine plaquettes with m = L^{−(k+1−j)}/(L^{−1}η) = L^{j}.
  - The lattice lasso identity writes the holonomy of ∂P from a corner as an ordered product ∏_p γ_p(∂p)γ_p⁻¹ over its fine plaquettes, with γ_p paths in P.
  - In a unitary representation, |∏_iA_i − 1| ≤ Σ_i|A_i − 1| (telescoping, since products of unitaries have norm 1), and |γ(∂p)γ⁻¹ − 1| = |∂p − 1|.
  - So |∂P(U_{k+1}) − 1| ≤ m²·2B₃ε_{k+1}(L^{−1}η)² = 2B₃ε_{k+1}L^{−2(k+1−j)}. No small-field expansion is used.
  - For j = k: a plaquette of the step-k lattice has deviation at most 2B₃ε_{k+1}L^{−2} under the step-(k+1) background.
- *This closes the unit question of IT-1 note §6(c).* (L^{−1}η)² is the area of a fine plaquette in step-(k+1) units. So the step-k plaquettes of the step-(k+1) background deviate by at most 2B₃ε_{k+1}L^{−2}, the L^{−2} of the heuristic. This is a statement about the background only. Whether the insertions contract is D2.4.

### (3) The class at step k (definition, draft; a schema until D2.2 and D2.6 fix the groups and the labels at every scale)

This generalises the IT-1 note §4 from k = 1. A *dressed loop of step k* on a labelled term of ρ_k (CRE (2.18), with the labels of D1.0(P) at every scale) is a function of V_k and of the outer variables that the term holds constant, of the form

𝒲_k = κ^{(k)} e^{𝓔_k} [ f_k + Σ_{𝔅≠∅ compatible} Q_k(𝔅)·dim(π)⁻¹ tr(Y_{W₁}U_{(1)} ⋯ Y_{W_m}U_{(m)}) ].

- *Outer variables:* the pending variables of T_k: the V_j on Ω_{j+1}ᶜ and the fluctuation fields A_j on Z_{j+1} ∩ Ω_{j+1} (CRE (2.20)–(2.21)), the exterior and de-younged variables of the labels (IT-1 note §4, D1.1).
- *κ^{(k)} := ∏_{j=1}^{k} κ_j* with κ_j := e^{−δm_jP_{j−1}(C)}z_j⁴, the exact step-j perimeter and corner coefficients. With δm_1 := δm^σP(C_n)/P_0(C_n) and z_1 := z_c^σ, κ_1 is V3's e^{−δm^σP(C_n)}(z_c^σ)⁴ (D1(i)); this holds for any normalisation of P(C_n) proportional to n.
- *Leading word f_k := dim(π)⁻¹tr π(Hol_C(U_k)),* a scalar. At k = 1, D1's leading word is V3's f_ξ(m̂) = W_χ(C;U_ξ(m̂)), which contains the shift (IT-1 note §3j(4)); D(1) ⊇ D1 writes f_ξ(m̂) − f_1 as att(1, ·) insertions by the telescoping of *Species* below: by IT-1 note §3j(2) it depends on Λ only within R_N of C, and it is driven by the large-field and collar data through ℓ_Λ (its size and its place in the label sum are the D2.6 item of IT-1 note §3j(0)). The holonomy is read as a product, over the step-k bonds of C, of the transports of U_k along their fine bonds. Where C crosses a large-field region, outer variables enter as in D1.1 (IT-1 note §3d(1)).
- *Groups, families, insertions, words, interactions:* as in IT-1 note §4, with V3's groups replaced by the groups that the step-k map produces (D2.2). Their sizes are measured in step-k units.
  - *Interaction clause in cluster form (D2.3(3); not reviewed).* At every step, Q_k(𝔅) = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits 𝔅}φ_k(Γ)), the sum over the clusters Γ of the step-k reference group gas (a pure hard-core gas) that are incompatible with some member of 𝔅, with φ_k(Γ) numbers given the labels that depend on C only through J_Γ, the traversals in the arcs of Γ's groups, and Σ_{Γ hits W}|φ_k(Γ)| ≤ θ(W). At k = 1 this is IT-1 note §4's form. (The subset form Q_k(𝔅) = exp(−Σ_SΦ_k(S)) doesn't work: from step 2 on it breaks contour locality.)
- *Scalar exponent* 𝓔_k = Σ_{j≤k}Σ_X e^{(j)}_X + E_const,k, a sum of local terms (the carrier parts and the thin E^∂ of IT-1 note §3i at every scale) plus a constant of the contour and the torus.
- *The deviation quantity.* Put η_k := L^{−k}. For a traversal n of a step-k bond of C,
  δ*_n^{(k)}(U) := η_k^{−2}Σ_{t≥1}w_tΔ_n^{(k)}(R_t^{(k)}), w_t := (t+1)³e^{−η_wt}, η_w := (5/16)η_V (an amendment, for the user's review: D2.4(12)(b) with ν_e := ν/(2C_box) and the complex factor e^{η𝔰/8} of D2.6 closure §6.5's budget; the step-1 proof of (ii-t) also carries the reserve and extent weights and §3j(6)'s share),
  Δ_n^{(k)}(R) := max{δ_p(U) : p a fine plaquette of Ω_k (of Λ₁ at k = 1) within step-k distance R of n}, with max ∅ := 0. The step-k distance is V3's d_T times η_k.
  δ_p is V3's plaquette deviation, η_V is V3's exponent η (renamed, since η is the fine spacing here), and the radii R_t^{(k)} are fixed by D2.2's group scale, with R_t^{(1)} := C_boxM(t+1), V3's C_boxLM(t+1) in step-1 units. (Note from D2.4(6)(γ): R_t^{(k)} := C_boxM(t+1) in step-k units at every k.)
  - (a) At k = 1, δ*_n^{(1)}(U) ≥ L²δ*_{n_f}(U) for every fine traversal n_f ⊂ n, with δ*_{n_f} of IT-1 note §3a(6): η_1^{−2} = L², and the neighbourhoods of n contain those of n_f. A block-plaquette deviation would not do: it does not dominate D1(ii)'s fine-plaquette deviations, and there is no pointwise bound of step-k block deviations of U_{k+1} by step-(k+1) block deviations, since sub-plaquette fluxes can cancel.
  - (b) For k ≥ 2, on the support of the step-k characteristic functions on which p.268 states the regularity (k ≤ N; for k > N, D2.7), δ*_n^{(k)}(U_k) ≤ 2c_σB₃ε_kΣ_tw_t, by p.268 at the step k−1 → k (|∂U_k − 1| < 2B₃ε_kη_k² on Ω_k), with c_σ converting CRE's matrix norm into V3's |·|_σ. At k = 1, δ_p(U₁) ≤ Cε₁ for p ⊂ Λ₁ (V3 L5033, Lemma c2-plaquette-deviation), so δ*_n^{(1)}(U₁) ≤ CL²ε₁Σ_tw_t.
  - (c) For the same plaquettes and radii, η_{k+1}^{−2}max = L²·η_k^{−2}max for every U. This is the exact form of the L^{−2} of (5). The plaquette set changes from Ω_k to Ω_{k+1} (Λ₁ to Ω₂ at the first step), so the maximum in δ*^{(k)}_n(U_{k+1}) is split: the plaquettes of Ω_{k+1} give ≤ L^{−2}δ*^{(k+1)} after D2.4's matching; those of Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} contribute at most η_k^{−2}·sup_{p⊂Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}}δ_p(U_{k+1})·Σ_{t: R_t^{(k)} ≥ dist(n, Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1})}w_t, which by the membership condition (2.34) at j = k+1, n = k (|∂U − 1| < (1 − β/2)α_{0,k}η_k²) is ≤ c_σ(1 − β/2)α_{0,k}Σ_{t: …}w_t once U_{k+1} is shown to lie in the space. Here α_{0,k} = g_kC₀(log g_k⁻²)^{q₀} (CRE (2.28), p.259) is bounded uniformly in k, since x(log x⁻²)^{q₀} increases for log x⁻² > 2q₀ and g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K by the last inequality of CRE (2.6), under the condition (1+β₀)g_K < e^{−q₀} (its users and its status at general G: coupling item of the gaps block). A weaker input suffices: g_k ∈ [0, γ] with γ < e^{−q₀} gives α_{0,k} ≤ C₀γ(log γ^{−2})^{q₀} with no uniform form of (2.6). RG1 Theorem 3 (p.264) is stated "for every coupling sequence in ]0,γ]" (Lipschitz note §5); that CRE's sequence stays in ]0, γ] at every step is part of the coupling item. If it does, this use of R-cpl₀ can be dropped. That part decays with the distance to Ω_{k+1}ᶜ and joins the scale-(k+1) attached part. Both the matching of the neighbourhoods and radii of an old traversal n with those of the new traversal n′ ⊇ n and this split are D2.4 items. The block-plaquette bound of (2) is a corollary (lasso step), not the class quantity.
- *Species.* Every insertion Y_W carries a species, fixed by the labels and the group geometry, at production and at each step's reclassification: *dev*; *att(j, Z)*, attached at step j to a large-field component Z by a non-good group; *att(j, coll)*, a collar part made at step j, with no component (one collar insertion is bounded by the marks of all of W's domains and inputs, which can be assigned to different components, so a species att(j, coll Z) is not defined for it; the bound is split by component instead of the insertion, (ii′-b)); *lw(Z)*, the telescoped leading-word parts assigned to Z; *wrap(j)*. The words are expanded multilinearly in the species. A split of one insertion by the sizes of its bounds would be field-dependent and not analytic, and would break D(k)(iii); so the split is made in the formulas.
  - *Assignment rules.* Fix an order of the components of B_ξ at each step. A non-good group goes to the first component that its Q_W meets. Each bond mark, host mark and free input b of the collar data goes to the nearest component of the step-j large-field set Z_j, ties broken by the order. A telescoped bond i goes to the component nearest to i.
  - At step 1 the split exists: Υ¹_W = (Υ¹_W − Υ⁰_W) + Υ⁰_W (IT-1 note §3a(1)). Υ⁰_W is dev for good groups (§3a(3)); otherwise it is att(1, Z) in case (i) of §3a(4), with Z the component of B_ξ that Q_W meets first, and wrap(1) in case (ii), where Q_W does not lift, bounded by Cρe^{−cN_min/(LM)} and decaying from no component. Υ¹_W − Υ⁰_W is att(1, coll) (§3a(2), (7)).
  - *The telescoped leading word (slot form).* f_ξ(m̂) − f_1 = Σ_idim(π)^{−1}tr(T_1⋯T_{i−1}Y_iT_iT̂_{i+1}⋯T̂_P), Y_i := π(T̂_iT_i^{−1}) − 1, with T, T̂ the transports of U₁ and U_ξ(m̂), over the fine bonds i of C in order. The identity is exact; each term is a one-insertion word with unitary transports, slot Y_i of species lw(Z) for the Z that i is assigned to, θ := 0, and "i ∈ n" read for "W ∋ n" in ν_n. Σ_{i∈n}‖Y_i‖ is added to the displayed D(1)(ii). Its size is the D2.6 item of IT-1 note §3j(0): per bond ‖Y_i‖ is of the order of g₀ times the shift m̂ near its source, not ρ̂₁. Keeping the shift in the leading word is the alternative; it is not taken, since D2.1 is written for f_k = tr Hol(U_k). *Amendment by the D2.6 closure §6.1, §6.5 (for review): in place of the one-insertion form, the subset expansion Π_i(1 + Y_i)π(T_i) = Σ_S(the ordered product with Y_i at the positions of S), each S-term's Λ-dependence local within R_dep of its bonds; lw slots have Q = 1 against each other and against every other insertion of the word. On every labelled term (closure §6.1–6.5, not only on V3's covered event A^fin_cov) T_i := U₁(r_i) and T̂_i := U_ξ(m̂)(r_i) = e^{iΦ_i}U₁(r_i) (V3 (c-reconstructed-field), L455), so Y_i = π(e^{iΦ_i}) − 1 with no background factor, Y_i = 0 off Ω₁. On rows within 2R_c + 2L of I (class (I)) Φ_i = q_U(Ψ_ξ(m̂ + a_old + A_e))(r_i) and ‖Y_i‖ ≤ C′g₀r; on the far rows (class (III), within 2L of Λ₁ᶜ) Φ_i = q_U(Ψ_ξ(A_e))(r_i) is Λ-independent and ‖Y_i‖ ≤ C_III, a constant; complex data by R-st (derived, closure §6.1). m̂ is the analytic rescaling λm̃ (closure §6.4 *Shift*, an amendment of IT-1 §3j(1)). Near slots (within R_lw of Λ₁ᶜ) are of species lw(Z); retreat slots ride with the label activities (closure §6.5).*
    - *In the family sum.* An lw slot forms a one-element family with Q_k = 1. With the D2.6 closure §6.5's subset expansion (for review) several lw slots occur in one word, so lw slots are compatible with each other and with every other insertion of the word (Q = 1 for every such pair), and the family sum reproduces the subset expansion exactly. On a term with no large-field component the lw slots, if any, take lw(∅) and are bounded as a whole by (ii′-c) with the sum over all i; "the component nearest to i" is used only when a component exists.
  - At step k+1 the old dev insertions are reclassified: dev if their comparison box (the plaquettes their deviation bound uses) lies in Ω_{k+1}, att(k+1, Z) if it meets Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}. Z is the first component, in the fixed order, of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ that the comparison box meets; one exists, since Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} ⊆ Ω_{k+1}ᶜ. The review's form, a component of the step-(k+1) large-field set, needs Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} inside that set. That can fail where Ω_{k+1}, a union of the coarser cubes of CRE p.256, loses MR_k-cubes next to older regions. The charge of such a part to the data its component of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ contains, of any step ≤ k+1, is D2.6's. This is the structural form of the split of the maximum in (3)(c).
  - *Insertions made at step k+1.* The fresh insertions of D2.2(5)(e) and (6) take the step-(k+1) split of D2.2(6)(c): the deviation parts are dev, and the parts meeting Λ_{k+1}ᶜ or depending on ω′ are attached, with the kind and component fixed by the step-1 rule at step k+1:
    - a part whose group has Q_W meeting a step-(k+1) large-field component is att(k+1, Z), Z the first such component;
    - a part that depends on outer data only through collar marks is att(k+1, coll);
    - a part whose Q_W does not lift at step k+1 is wrap(k+1).
  - A fluctuation part Y(U_k) − Y(U_{k+1}) keeps its parent's species. An insertion made from several parents is dev only if all are dev. Otherwise it takes the first attached species of its parents in the total order: by step j ascending, then by kind att(j, Z) < att(j, coll) < lw < wrap, then by the fixed order of the components.
  - *Units of carried groups.* For att(j, ·) groups, θ(W) and σ(W) are those of step j, when the group was made; the bound θ(W) ≤ νσ(W)/16 is read at that step.
- *D(k)(ii), per-traversal form.* For every traversal n of a step-k bond of C, with ν_n^{(k)} := Σ_{W∋n}e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ over the insertions of species dev, att(k, ·) and wrap(·),
  ν_n^{(k)} ≤ C_χρ_kδ*_n^{(k)}(U_k) + Cρ̂_ke^{−cd_k(n)/M} + Cρ_{t,k}(Σ_Δ𝔪̂^{(k)}_Δe^{−η_Vdist(Δ,n)/(C_FM)} + C′_χg_kΣ_bh(m^{(k)}_b)e^{−η_Vdist(b,n)/(C_FM)}) + Σ_{j=1}^{k}CL^{k−j}ρ̂_je^{−cN^{(j)}_min/M}.
  - *Amended by D2.4(5) (not reviewed).* For k ≥ 2 the first term is C_χρ̃_kδ*_n^{(k)}, ρ̃_k := ρ_k/ᾱ_k, ᾱ_k := min(α_{0,k}, α_{1,k}), and ρ̃_1 := ρ_1. The factor comes from D2.4(4)'s Cauchy step. On the support ρ̃_kδ*_n = O(ρ_k) under R-m's entailments, and at complex data the size gains 1 + (log g_k^{−2})^{(q₁−q₀)₊}; on 𝒟_k^{sub} the gain is 2 on every layer under q₀ = q₁ (SW closure §2–§3). Each dev insertion also carries its own bound e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ ≤ b_WDev_{Q^#_W}, with Σ_{W∋n}b_WDev_{Q^#_W} ≤ C_χρ̃_kδ*_n (the per-insertion form, used by the reclassification).
    - *Tail clause (D2.4(5)).* Dev_{Q^#_W}, ℓ_W and b_W stay in the normalisation of the step at which W was made, and with β_W := b_Wℓ_Wη_{j(W)}^{−2}, (ii) also contains (ii-t) Σ_{W∋n, t^{(k)}(W)≥t}β_W ≤ C_χρ̃_kη_k^{−2}W_t for every t, W_t := Σ_{t′≥t}w_{t′}. The sum form above follows from it by Abel summation; at k = 1 it follows from IT-1 note §3a(6)'s proof.
    - *Reserve and extent weights (D2.4(12)(b); accepted by the user).* (ii) and (iii) carry the weight e^{ν_rσ_k(W) + ν_e·ext_k(W)} inside their per-position sums, ν_r := ν, ν_e := ν/(2C_box) (closure §6.5's budget, for review), ext_k(W) the extent of X(W) ∪ Q^#_W from every traversal of W in π_k-sides (production units for carried insertions; C_box ≥ 4A_E ≥ 4 so that ν_e ≤ ν/8): the form (ii-w) of D2.4(12)(b), per traversal only (a per-cube clause does not carry; cube positions by the box reduction of D2.4(12)(e)), for the dev species. At k = 1 it is (c4-6-5), V3 L4619–4622, with L2791 (e^{4νσ} pins) and L2795–2798; it carries k → k+1 as (ii-t) does, and D2.4(12)–(13) derive it for the new groups ((S-w)). It is what pays for merged old members in the step-(k+1) count.
  - *Per component.* The second term is stated per component: for each step-k large-field component Z, Σ_{W∋n, W∈att(k,Z)}e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ ≤ Cρ̂_ke^{−cd_k^Z(n)/M}, with d_k^Z(n) the step-k distance from n to Z's data; Cρ̂_ke^{−cd_k(n)/M} in the display is read as this family of bounds. At k = 1 it follows from the argument of IT-1 note §3a(4)(i) with Q_W ∩ Z ≠ ∅, which gives d^Z(n) ≤ diam(Q_W) + 1. The collar term is the att(k, coll) part and keeps its form; its split by the assigned component of the marks is a split of the bound ((ii′-b)). At k = 1 the display also has the lw term Σ_{i∈n}‖Y_i‖.
  - Only the attached parts made at step k stay in (ii). Those made at earlier steps are bounded by (ii′) below, per large-field component. Reason: an object made at step j has decay length λ_{k,j} := ML^{−(k−j)} in step-k units (V3's LM, fine units at step 1, M in step-1 units; the scale-j lattice is T_{L^{−(k−j)}} in step-k units, (1)). A term Cρ̂_je^{−cd_j(n)/λ_{k,j}} kept in (ii) costs a factor L per step, as the collar parts do, so D(k)(iv) would fail. (The form e^{−c(d_j(n)−1)/λ_{k,j}} doesn't work: it carries the decay length but not the shift −1.) The wrap terms stay in (ii) for every j: each grows by at most L per step, so the scale-j one is ≤ CL^{k−j}ρ̂_je^{−cN^{(j)}_min/M}, small under the torus condition of (5)(g); ρ̂_j here is max(C_S′ρ̂_j, C_S″b_j) (D2.6 closure 2.1; (5)(g) amended). Distances are step-k distances.
  - d_j(n) is the distance from n to the scale-j large-field data: E(B_ξ) of the step-j labels, together with the de-younged bonds of the step-j carrier on uncovered terms (d(n), d^Λ(n) of D1(ii) at j = 1). N^{(j)}_min is the smallest torus period in step-j units (V3's N_min/L at j = 1). 𝔪̂^{(j)}_Δ and m^{(j)}_b are the marks and shifts of the step-j labels (IT-1 note §3a(7), §3j). ρ̂_j = max(ρ_j, ρ_{ph,j}) and ρ_{t,j} are the step-j analogues of ρ̂ and ρ_t. *Fixed here:* r_k := A₁(log g_k⁻²)^{p₀}, ρ_k := C_ρg_k²(1+r_k)⁴ (the step-k analogue of V3's ρ = O(g₀²(1+r)⁴)), ρ_{t,k} := C′_χg_k(1+2r_k)/u_* and ρ_{ph,k} := C_phg_k²(1+2r_k)⁴, as at k = 1 below. D2.2 has to prove its bounds with these ρ's.
  - At k = 1 this is D1(ii), summed over the L fine traversals of each coarse bond, with C_χ ↦ L^{−1}C_χ by (a) (V3's traversals are fine, L122), and:
    - the attached constants C ↦ e^{2c/M}LC: L fine traversals, each with e^{−c(d(n_f)−1)/(LM)} ≤ e^{c/(LM)}e^{c/M}e^{−cd_1(n)/M};
    - D1(ii)'s collar term has g₀ and D(1)(ii) has g₁: C′_χ ↦ (1+β₀)C′_χ by g₀ ≤ (1+β₀)g₁ (the last inequality of CRE (2.6), p.255, n = 1, m = 0); likewise V3's ρ (at g₀) ≤ C(1+β₀)⁶ρ₁ with ρ₁ := C_ρg₁²(1+r₁)⁴ as in (5)(c).
    - The remaining conversions. ρ_t ≤ (1+β₀)²ρ_{t,1} with ρ_{t,1} := C′_χg₁(1+2r₁)/u_*: V3's ρ_t = u_t/u_* = C′_χg₀(1+2r)/u_* (V3 L3973, item 4), g₀ ≤ (1+β₀)g₁ as above, and r ≤ (1+β₀)r₁ by the second inequality of CRE (2.7) (n = 1, m = 0); u_* is fixed before the coupling (V3 L3968). In the same way ρ_ph ≤ C_phg₀²(1+2r)⁴ (V3 L2865, under its (iii)) gives ρ_ph ≤ (1+β₀)⁶ρ_{ph,1} with ρ_{ph,1} := C_phg₁²(1+2r₁)⁴, so ρ̂ ≤ C(1+β₀)⁶ρ̂₁, with C absorbing V3's O(·) in ρ = O(g₀²(1+r)⁴). The collar term of D(1)(ii) is the sum of D1(ii)'s collar terms over the L fine traversals of n, so its constant is L times D1(ii)'s; the decay converts exactly, e^{−ηdist(Δ,n_f)/(C_FLM)} ≤ e^{−η_Vdist(Δ,n)/(C_FM)} (IT-1 note §3a(7): fine distances are L times step-1 distances, and dist(Δ,n_f) ≥ dist(Δ,n)).
  - The first term bounds the species dev; the others bound att(k, ·), att(k, coll), lw(·) (at k = 1) and wrap(·), which carry no deviation. The attached species made at step j leave (ii) at the end of step j + 1's map and are bounded from then on by (ii′).
- *D(k)(ii′), attached parts of earlier steps, species by species.* For every j < k and every step-j large-field component Z, with the outer sums over the step-k traversals n of C, the sums in N_j over the step-j traversals n_j of C, and d_j^Z(n_j) the step-j distance from n_j to Z's step-j data:
  - (ii′-a) att(j, Z): Σ_nΣ_{W∋n, W∈att(j,Z)}e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ ≤ Cρ̂_jN_j(Z), N_j(Z) := Σ_{n_j}e^{−cd_j^Z(n_j)/M}. *Amendment (D2.6 closure §5.0, §5.2 (T0); the lineage product form (T0) accepted by the user, the carried weights of §5.0 for review; at every later step n it takes the place of the display above):* each insertion is counted once, at the first of its traversals, with the carried weight w(W) := e^{ν_rσ_j(W)}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}_n(W)}e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ (ext^{coll}_n in π_n-sides from Coll_j); the statement is the product form: the att(j, Z) products present at step n, summed over the terms of the multilinear expansion, are ≤ Π_{D∈𝔅_j}(1 + e^{c_W}w(D)Π_{m=j}^{n−1}(1+δ_m)) ≤ exp(2A_j(Z)), 𝔅_j the production set, A_j(Z) the production total of (5)(f) with its constant enlarged by e^{c_W}, so that Σe^{c_W}w ≤ A_j(Z) (closure §5.0, §7.1), δ_m as in the stability bullet below;
  - (ii′-b) att(j, coll), a total split by component: Σ_nΣ_{W∋n, W∈att(j,coll)}e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ ≤ Σ_ZB_j(Z), B_j(Z) := Cρ_{t,j}[Σ_{Δ→Z}𝔪̂^{(j)}_ΔN_j(Δ) + C′_χg_jΣ_{b→Z}h(m^{(j)}_b)N_j(b)], N_j(x) := Σ_{n_j}e^{−η_Vdist(x,n_j)/(C_FM)}, the inner sums over the marks Δ and inputs b assigned to Z. The right side is the sum over n_j of D(j)(ii)'s collar term, regrouped by the assignment of the marks. (5)(f) charges B_j(Z) to Z through ∏_W(1+‖Y_W‖) ≤ exp(Σ_W‖Y_W‖) ≤ ∏_Ze^{B_j(Z)}, so its A_j(Z) is unchanged. The split is of the bound, not of the insertion, so it does not depend on the field and analyticity is kept; the carry below applies to the total;
  - (ii′-c) lw(Z) (j = 1): Σ_nΣ_{i∈n, i→Z}‖Y_i‖ ≤ Σ_{i→Z}‖π(T̂_iT_i^{−1}) − 1‖. Its size is the D2.6 item named under *Species*: bounded in WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §6.3 on every labelled term (classes (I)–(III), not only A^fin_cov; the slots Y_i of closure §6.1 in slot form), with the weighted production total lw_1(Z) ≤ (C_{lw}g₀(r+1) + C_{lw}^{III})M_VR₁(d₁′(Z)+1) of closure 6.3 (class-(I) slots bounded by C_I″g₀(r+1) on the polydisc; 7.1 doubles the class-(I) part for the label cost); the label sum of IT-1 §3j(0) is answered in WILSON_IT1_O2PRIME_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md §5 (derived 26 Oct 2026, for review; formerly OPEN as O2′): Z₁ = Λᶜ (CRE (2.3) with IT-1 l.740; the closure's "Λ₁ᶜ" at 6.2, 7.2 corrected), every free bond of J(Λ) lies in E(Z₁), so every slot with Y_i ≠ 0 is an lw(X) insertion of the nearest component X of Z₁ within R_t + 2L + 1 ≤ ℓ, no merge of label polymers is made within D2.6, and the slots' Λ-dependence within 2ℓ of a second component is a link of D2.7's joint linking expansion; the lineage weights and reserve counts of the carried slots (O5) are in closure §6.4 *Extent and reserve count* (for review): the class-(I) slots are carried as the pieces Y_{i,X}, the lineage extent is measured from the 2ℓ-neighbourhood 𝒩₁′ of Λᶜ (closure 5.6 with the O2′ note §3: Ret(Λ) ∩ Ω₂ = ∅ by CRE (3.5), the bridge at every step), and the carried total is ≤ 2·lw_1(X) (status in closure §8).
  - The constant of (ii′-a) does not fit the other two: ρ_{t,j} = C′_χg_j(1+2r_j)/u_* is of order g_j(log g_j^{−2})^{p₀}, ρ̂_j of order g_j²(log g_j^{−2})^{4p₀}, and a collar mark can be a fixed constant (IT-1 note §3a(8)), so the collar parts exceed Cρ̂_j by a factor of order 1/(g_j(log g_j^{−2})^{3p₀}). Each species therefore keeps the bound it has when it is made.
  - The carry from k to k+1 does not increase the left sides: a group that meets a new traversal n′ meets one of the L old traversals in n′, and distinct new traversals contain disjoint old ones. So no factor L per step appears, for each species line by line.
  - Only the per-component totals are controlled. At step n > j a step-n segment parallel to step-j data contains L^{n−j} step-j traversals, each carrying up to Cρ̂_j, so these species do not satisfy the per-segment cost e^{Cτ_n²} of (5)(f); (5)(f) pays them through (ii′), (ii′-b) at its own scale ρ_{t,j} times the marks, and (ii′-c) once D2.6 bounds it.
  - Stability of (ii′) under the evaluation at U_{k+1} and under the fluctuation parts of (5)(d) is a D2.6 item: derived in WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §5 (carry lemma): the att bounds are sup bounds on 𝒟^{sub} over D(j)(iii)'s ω-polydiscs, so the evaluation at U_{n+1} does not grow them (§5.1); a touched part must bridge from Coll_j to Ω_{n+1}, ≥ 5R_{n+1} π_n-sides, paid by the lineage weight e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}} and the bridge's reserve, so the descendants made at step n+1 have weighted mass ≤ δ_n times the mass present, δ_n := C_δc̄e^{−(25/8)(ν_e/L)R_{n+1}} (L ≥ 16, closure §5.0), and Σ_nδ_n ≤ C_δc̄S_R ≤ log 2 by the running of the coupling (Cor. 8.2's pairwise form). The statement is a product form: the att(j, Z) products at step n are bounded by Π_{D∈𝔅_j}(1 + e^{c_W}w(D)Π_m(1+δ_m)) ≤ exp(2A_j(Z)), A_j(Z) the production total normalised as Σe^{c_W}w ≤ A_j(Z) (c_W := c_sh, the share constant of (12)(d), taken once per insertion; closure §5.0, §5.5) (closure §5.0, §5.2 (T0) with the first-member injection, §5.3); multi-parent and merged insertions (§5.5); the older layers' transports (§5.4). Amendments for review: the carried weights e^{ν_rσ}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}} inside (ii′-a), at the current scale, each insertion counted once at its first traversal; the re-run (T0) of (12)(e)–(f) is accepted by the user.
- *Norms, at every scale j ≤ k, in step-k units except where stated:*
  - |Q_k(𝔅)| ≤ ∏_{W∈𝔅}e^{θ(W)} with θ(W) ≤ νσ(W)/16; the insertions of species dev, att(k, ·), wrap(·) by (ii), those of species att(j, ·), j < k, by (ii′), as in IT-1 note §4. *At steps k ≥ 2 (D2.4(3), not reviewed):* σ is σ_k = #_k + n_k/M, the π_k-cubes of the constituents' domains plus the step-k traversals of the arc in units of the π_k-side (D2.4(12)(a)), since the vacuum weight is proportional to volume; θ_k = θ_vac + θ_ref is the fresh weight of the step-k gas and does not carry θ_{k−1};
  - the step-j local terms e^{(j)}_X of 𝓔_k are indexed by ∗-connected unions X of boxes of the lattice integrated at step j, anchored at the step-j segments i, with Σ_{X∈𝒳^{(j)}_i}|e^{(j)}_{i,X}|e^{κ#boxes(X)} ≤ Cτ_j²e^{−c_thd^{(j)}_i}, and Σ_{X∩Q≠∅}|e^{(j)}_X|e^{κ_{a₂}#cubes(X)} ≤ (a₂/ℓ₀+2)⁴C_rτ_j² for every cube Q of side a₂ (IT-1 note §3i(7) at j = 1, where a₂ is written a), with e^{(j)}_X := Σ_ie^{(j)}_{i,X}. Here ℓ₀, a₂, κ_{a₂} and d^{(j)}_i (rate c_th) are in the units of the lattice integrated at step j, not in step-k units: a cube of side a₂ in step-k units meets up to (a₂L^{k−j+1}/ℓ₀ + 2)⁴ boxes of the lattice integrated at step j (the scale-(j−1) lattice), so a per-step-k-cube bound of the scale-j terms is not uniform in k − j. (5)(f) therefore pays the old terms in the anchored, per-component form (D2.6);
  - |E_const,k| ≤ Σ_{j≤k}Cτ_j²(n_je^{−cι_{T,j}} + (n_j+1)e^{−cn_j}), with ι_{T,j} indexed by the step ((5)(g)). *Amended by D2.3(7) (not re-reviewed):* the clause reads |E_const,k| ≤ Σ_{j≤k}Cτ_{j−1}²(n_je^{−cι_{T,j}} + (n_j+1)e^{−c_En_j}), the remainder of step j carrying τ_{j−1}², with n_j := s_{j−1} = ℓ_CL^{K−j+1}, the side of C in step-(j−1) bonds ((5)(g)'s n_k ≍ ℓ_CL^{K−k} is the same count with the index of the step's input lattice), and holds for the admissible sides of D2.3(4), where every step is commensurate. Without that restriction each of the at most a* other steps adds Cτ_{j−1}²(Π_j + 1 + 4s_{j−1}), Π_j the period of D2.3(4), and a* does not grow with K.
- *D(k)(iii):* for real U_k in the support of the term's indicators, which are kept as factors, the background-dependent factors extend analytically to 𝐔 = e^{iη_kA′}U_k, for complex parts satisfying the per-bond bounds of the complex part of (2.39) on each Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} (the loop's factors are not localised in Bałaban's domains X ∈ 𝐃_k): |η_kA′| < (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(k−n)}))α_{1,n}L^{−n} per fine bond on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} (n < k) and on Ω_k (n = k), G-covariantly. For k = 1 this sup-only domain is the domain; it is larger than CRE's Ũ_k^c, which also asks (L^nξ)²|∇_U^ξA′| < (1−β(1−2^{−(k−n)}))α_{1,n}, and it is D1(iii)′'s form. For k ≥ 2 the domain is the full complex part of CRE's Ũ_k^c: (2.34) for 𝐔 and (2.36) for 𝐉 per plaquette and bond, (2.38) per cube and (2.39) per bond with both the sup and the gradient condition, posed on every Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}; and (2.35), (2.37) for the averaged configurations U_{p,X}(M^·(𝐔)), J_{p,X}(M^·(𝐔)), which are built on a domain X, posed for each factor that uses them on that factor's own domain (X ∈ 𝐃_k for Bałaban's terms, CRE p.261; the domain Z of RG2 p.15's covariance operators for the step's Ĥ(M), D2.2(6)(c), l.917), as stated in the amendment below. (ii) holds there with δ*^{(k),30}_n(U_k) + a*^{(k)}_n(A′), and with ρ_{ph,k} replaced by ρ^c_{ph,k} := C_phg_k²(1+2r_k)⁴(1 + (log g_k⁻²)^{(q₁−p₀)₊}) and the tilt marks 𝔪^δ_Y of D1.2b included in 𝔪̂^{(k)}, as the IT-1 gaps block records at k = 1; the tolerance this needs is D2.4's, δ*^{(k),30}_n the quantity δ*^{(k)}_n with weights (t+1)³e^{−(30/8)η_Vt}, the rate D1(iii)′ gives at complex data (IT-1 gaps block, D1.2b), and a*^{(k)}_n(A′) := η_k^{−1}Σ_{t≥1}(t+1)e^{−(30/8)η_Vt}max{|η_kA′(b)| : b a fine bond within step-k distance R_t^{(k)} of n}, the step-k form of IT-1 note §3e(0)'s a*_n(a′) := Σ_{k≥1}(k+1)e^{−(30/8)ηk}A_n(C_boxLM(k+1)). [Amended by D26 closure 6.5 *(iii)'s complex rate*: with V3's θ°-booking the rate in δ*^{(k),30} and a*^{(k)} is (61/16)η_V, not (30/8)η_V.] The normalisation η_k^{−1} makes it a block-scale field: along a step-k bond the first-order change of the transport is Σ_bη_kA′(b) over its L^k fine bonds, of size |A′|. At k = 1, a*^{(1)}_n ≥ L·a*_{n_f} for every fine traversal n_f ⊂ n, as in (a). At k = 1 the radius is ϱ_{an,1} := α_{1,1}/L, the radius written ρ₁ in D1(iii)′ (renamed here, since ρ₁ is the insertion scale of (5)(c)).
  - *Amended for k ≥ 2 by D2.2(6)(e) (full space).* For k ≥ 2 the domain is CRE's: the full complex part of Ũ_k^c at 𝐔 = e^{iη_kA′}U_k, that is (2.34) for 𝐔 and (2.36) for 𝐉 per plaquette and bond, (2.38) per cube, and (2.39) per bond with both the sup and the gradient condition, (L^nξ)²|∇_U^ξA′| < (1−β(1−2^{−(k−n)}))α_{1,n}, posed on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} for n = 1, …, k−1 and on Ω_k; (2.35) and (2.37), which constrain the averaged configurations built on a domain X, are posed for each factor that uses them on that factor's own domain (Bałaban's X ∈ 𝐃_k; RG2 p.15's Z for the step's covariance operators). The step-(k−1) → k integration produces factors with RG2's propagators, which are analytic only on a gradient-controlled domain (RG2 p.15). The sup-only form above stays at k = 1. The ω-clause below has radii r^{(k)}_{ω,j} per layer j (D2.2(6)(e)). *The ω-clause at complex configurations (derived in D2.2(4)(d), (d′) Step 8, and D2.2(6)(b7), *G^c-valued u*; reviewed in round 44, minors applied and not re-reviewed).* At a configuration 𝐔 of the polydisc that is not the step's output at its own data, the step's representative Ax_k at 𝐔 is Ax_k at the minimiser Ū at 𝐔's averages transformed by the B′-independent c₀(𝐔), so by Cov (D2.2(6)(b7)) a slot value σ at 𝐔 is the slot value R(c₀(𝐔))^{−1}σ in the frame of Ū, where the plain clause holds; here c₀(𝐔) := 𝐔(Γ^{(k)})^{−1}Ū(Γ^{(k)}) the mismatch of the hierarchical transports of 𝐔 and of the minimiser Ū at 𝐔's averages, G-valued at real 𝐔 and G^c-valued at complex 𝐔, analytic in 𝐔, and equal to 1 at the output and at its continuation to complexified data (the frame family, where the step map invokes the clause). So the ω-clause at 𝐔 holds for the slot values σ with R(c₀(𝐔))^{−1}σ in the polydisc of radius r^{(k)}_{ω,j} around the real support. At real 𝐔 the rotation is real orthogonal and preserves the polydisc, so the clause is the one above; at complex 𝐔 this is its analytic continuation, with σ̂ := R(c₀(𝐔))^{−1}σ as the analytic coordinate in D2.1(2)'s identity theorem jointly with the configuration. The radii and their propagation (D2.2(6)(e), l.961) are unchanged, as the propagation runs on the frame family.
    - *Conditions on the base point.* CRE's space also has two conditions on the real factor U, and its analyticity statements are made on that space: the real-U part of (2.34), |∂U − 1| < (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(j−n)}))α_{0,n}ξ²(L^nξ)^{−2}, and condition (ii), (2.38) (p.261): for each cube □ ⊂ Ω_n∖Ω_{n+2} with □ ∩ Ω_{n+1}ᶜ ≠ ∅ of size CML^nξ, or □ ⊂ Ω_j of size CM, a gauge transformation u on □ ∩ X with U^u = exp iξA, L^nξ|A|, (L^nξ)²|∇^ξA| < BCMα_{0,n}. Both are required of the base point U_k here.
  - *Amended for k ≥ 2 by D2.2(6)(b).* For k ≥ 2 the factors are functions of (𝐔, 𝐉, ω), and the domain is posed on (𝐔, 𝐉): with the conditions above, CRE (2.36)–(2.37), p.261, |𝐉| < (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(k−n)}))α_{0,n}(L^nξ)^{−3} on each Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} and on Ω_k (CRE's space Ũ_k^c is "the set of configurations (𝐔,𝐉)", p.261). Covariance is under (𝐔, 𝐉, ω) ↦ (𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)ω) (CRE p.261: "(G-valued) gauge transformations of 𝐔, 𝐉, A (the variables 𝐉, A are transformed by the adjoint representation of the gauge transformations)"). The factors are evaluated at 𝐉 = J_k. At k = 1 the factors are V3's, functions of 𝐔 and, on uncovered terms, of the exterior fields; there is no 𝐉.
    - *The 𝐉-term of (ii).* The dev parts are functions of 𝐉 that vanish at (1, 0, 0), so the bound of (ii) on this domain needs a deviation term in 𝐉 − J_k; without it, at flat U_k with A′ = 0 and 𝐉 ≠ J_k, the bound would force the dev parts to vanish. With r_J(b) := (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(k−n)}))α_{0,n}(L^nξ)^{−3}, the (2.36) radius at b, and ϱ(b) := (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(k−n)}))α_{1,n}L^{−n}, the A′-radius, put
      j*^{(k)}_n(𝐉) := η_k^{−1}Σ_{t≥1}(t+1)e^{−(30/8)η_Vt}max{ϱ(b)|𝐉(b) − J_k(b)|/r_J(b) : b within step-k distance R_t^{(k)} of n}. [Rate (61/16)η_V in place of (30/8)η_V, as for δ*^{(k),30} and a*^{(k)} above (D26 closure 6.5 *(iii)'s complex rate*).]
      For k ≥ 2, (ii) holds on the (𝐔, 𝐉) domain with δ*^{(k),30}_n(U_k) + a*^{(k)}_n(A′) + j*^{(k)}_n(𝐉). At the real point 𝐉 = J_k the new term vanishes, so (ii) at real data is unchanged, and D(1) has no 𝐉. *Sub-domain form (SW closure §3, for review):* this dev-term bound at complex data is asserted on 𝒟_k^{sub} only (the top-layer radii α_{1,k}L^{−k}, α_{0,k}(L^kξ)^{−3} with the layer's factor on every layer); analyticity stays on the full domain; the per-insertion form likewise, with Dev_W over the per-traversal region of SW closure §5(e). The 𝐉-part of the dev bound is to be proved with the rest (D2.2(6), D2.4(4)): a Cauchy estimate in 𝐉 on the (2.36) polydisc, as for A′.
  - The outer fields ω_j, j ≥ 1, are slot variables: the old A_j, which receive the values ψ_{j+1}(A_j^{new}; 𝐇^{(j)}) on S_{j+1}^{(j)*} and A_j elsewhere (D2.2(6)(b)). The ω-clause is analyticity in the slot variables. *Amendment (D2.6 closure §6.1 *The ω-polydisc*; for the user's review):* the same holds at j = 0. On the bonds of (R₁^{∼2} ∩ S₁)^* V3's variable is A′, with A = Ψ_ξA′ through the C^∞ cutoff ω_b (V3 L538), and the slots are not analytic in A′ there. So at j = 0 the outer variable ω₀ is CRE (1.15)'s A = Ψ_ξA′, a slot variable; A′ and the cutoff values ω_b(A′(b)), frozen as real factors at the real configuration (as in D2.2(6)(e), *The radii*), only give its values, and the later rotations R(w) act on these values (l.216). The lw slots' bounds on the r_{ω,0} = 1 polydisc are in the closure (§6.1 (a)–(b)).
  - *Outer fluctuation fields.* The ω-dependent factors (the insertions that consult outer fluctuation fields, on uncovered terms) extend analytically also in ω, jointly with 𝐔, to the ω within distance r_ω^{(k)} of a real point of the support, per bond (per layer j, r^{(k)}_{ω,j}: the polydiscs of D2.2(6)(e)), with the same bounds. (The tube |Im ω| < r_ω^{(k)} doesn't work: the real part of a rotated ω could leave the support.) The radii r^{(k)}_{ω,j} = r_{ω,j}(1/2 + 2^{−(k−j)}) are in the units of the slot variable σ_j (the old A_j, in step-j units), and r_{ω,j} is fixed at step j and does not change with k (D(k)(iv) concerns constants, and r_{ω,j} is a scale-j radius); the step-1 exterior fields have index j = 0, with r_{ω,0} = 1 in B-units. The original field V₀ on Ω₁ᶜ is part of 𝐔, not of ω. *At k = 1 (derived).* In V3's free variables B (phases A = g₀C_UB), the exterior fields A_e and a_old enter the ω-dependent factors in three ways. They enter the cross term s⟨a, H_{ξ,IJ}A_e⟩ of (c4-11-1), which is linear, hence entire. They enter the near primitives 𝔭(a + sA_e) of (c4-11-1). They enter the row solutions and path products of the rows they feed. Lemma charts (L475) makes the row solution and the functions it lists analytic in B|_{𝓔_c∩E_*} on 𝒰_c = {|B(b)| < 2 + 4r}, for every row within 2R_c + 2L of I, on every branch. On the support of the term's indicators the real inputs of these rows satisfy |B(b)| ≤ 2r on D(Λ) and |B(b)| < r on I∖D(Λ) and on J (IT-1 note §3d(2)(d), after S-A). So every ω-dependent factor is analytic, jointly with the background, for |Im B(b)| < 2 per bond, which gives r_ω^{(1)} := 1 in B-units with room to spare. The joint background radius is that of §c4-10-1 (L4880). Its propagation to D(k+1)(iii) is part of D2.2(6). *Status of the bound clause (D2.6 closure §4.2(iii)):* at k = 1 the analyticity is derived above, and the bounds at complex ω₀ are the closure 5.0 amendment, for the t > 0 species on the sub-polydisc 𝒟^{sub}_{ω,0} of radius ρ_ω ≪ r_{ω,0} around the term's step-1 exterior data, which contains every point the consumers evaluate at (WILSON_IT1_O4_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md §4(f); for review; for the lw slots derived, closure §6.1 *The ω-polydisc*). For the ω-dependent members made at steps k ≥ 2 the bounds over the polydiscs rest on O6 (closure §2.2 (1′)), and the evaluation of every factor on Ω₁∖Ω₂ rests on the base points of (6)(e) *The inclusion* (O3, closed: derived at every k, reviewed in rounds 40–41 and 45), at every k.

At k = 1 this is V4 L1130 (the step-1 lemmas "apply to U = id directly", on every branch); at step k+1 D2.3(2) derives it from D2.2(6)(e)'s argument at U_{k+1} = 1. The bounds at the reference point are D2.4's.

For k ≤ N the basic step of the R-operation (LFR I Sect. 1) acts on no component (reading R-h); that no other part of R acts for k ≤ N is a D2.7 check, and with it N_k^C is T applied k times to W(C;U)ρ₀ with CRE's expansions. From step N + 1 on, R acts on the numerator; D2.7.
  - *R-h (derived for the basic step).* LFR I p.177 applies the R-operation to components of Λ_k^c that satisfy (ii): "in the preceding N renormalization steps no new large field regions were created inside this component". Every component of Λ_k^c contains a region created at some step j ∈ [1, k], so for k ≤ N, (ii) fails if "the preceding N steps" includes all of the k steps that exist. p.178 (1.2) writes T_k(Z) through the last N one-step operations, from h = k − N, which needs h ≥ 0, i.e. k ≥ N; and (ii) excludes k = N. The print does not state the case k ≤ N. For the basic step this is derived from (ii) as printed (re-check row 23), since ρ₀ has no large-field region and every region inside a component was created at a step j ≤ k ≤ N, i.e. within the preceding N steps; it is not a reading. That only the basic step's class is touched by R (D2.7) is not stated in the pages read.

### (5) Count check of D(k) → D(k+1) (count × size × weight per scale)

Regime: a loop of physical side ℓ_C, K steps, P_k(C) = 4ℓ_CL^{K−k}. τ_k and ρ_k are the step-k analogues of V3's τ = C[g(1+r)³ + e^{−cr²}] and ρ = O(g²(1+r)⁴), r = A₁(log g⁻²)^{p₀}, so τ_k² and ρ_k are g_k² times a power of log g_k⁻². The question for each class is whether D(k+1) can hold with the constants of D(k).
- *The couplings.* CRE (2.6) with n = K, m = k gives the lower bound 1/g_k² ≤ 1/g_K² + β′(K−k), and g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K. The upper bound g_k² ≤ C/(K−k+1) is RG I (0.31) (Thm 2, p.259): with ε = L^{−K}, 1/g_k² ≥ 1/g_K² + β(K−k)log L. It is printed for G = SU(2) only, with its proof announced for a separate paper; accepted as Bałaban's result for SU(2). For general G it is not printed (gaps block). For simple G it is proved in the Bałaban project, ONE_LOOP_PROOFS.md Corollary 8.2 (RG1_Thm2_REP): g_k^{−2} ≥ g_K^{−2} + (2c log L − δ)(K−k) − C, so g_k² ≤ C′/(K−k+1). On D2.3(7)'s route (b) does not use the upper bound (note in (b)). The D2.6 closure's §5.0 uses it. The carry lemma's sum S_R = Σ_me^{−(25/8)(ν_e/L)R_{m+1}} is bounded through Cor. 8.2's pairwise running (a window of length b in u_m = g_m^{−2} holds at most 2 + C/b indices), i.e. through the lower bound on u_m, which is the upper bound on the coupling; for simple G this is Cor. 8.2 (RG1_Thm2_REP), so the use is covered. The uniform form g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K (the last inequality of CRE (2.6) at n − m = K − k) is used by (3)(c)'s bound of α_{0,k}, (c)'s feedback factor, (d)'s factor L²g_k²ρ_k/ρ_{k+1}, (f)'s τ̄ and D2.2(4)'s smallness conditions; its status at general G is the coupling item of the gaps block.

- *Convention for the deviation.* δ*_n^{(k)} is the quantity of (3): fine-plaquette deviations near n divided by the fine-plaquette area in step-k units, η_k² = L^{−2k}. The same fine plaquette has area L^{−2(k+1)} in step-(k+1) units, so the same maximum gives δ*^{(k+1)} = L²δ*^{(k)} for every U ((3)(c)). By (2), the fine plaquettes of Ω_{k+1} have |∂p − 1| < 2B₃ε_{k+1}η_{k+1}² under U_{k+1}, so δ*^{(k+1)}(U_{k+1}) ≤ 2c_σB₃ε_{k+1}Σ_tw_t there, with c_σ of (3)(b); by the lasso step a step-k plaquette of Ω_{k+1} deviates under U_{k+1} by at most 2B₃ε_{k+1}L^{−2}, a corollary only.
  - (3)(a) matches δ*^{(1)} with IT-1 note §3a(6) at k = 1. Matching the neighbourhoods of n with those of n′ is in D2.4.
