- **(a) Perimeter scalar parts.** Size of order τ_k² per step-k bond (V3 L158, at hash a520a59f363d: |κ^σ_v| ≤ Cτ² (the sentence following eq:marginal-full-perimeter, by eq:marginal-cluster-tail, with eq:marginal-square-mass) gives |δm^σ|P(C_n) = n|Σ_vκ^σ_v| ≤ 4Cnτ². L158 is one long TeX line, the "(1) The scales" remark after the main theorem, and it also holds the perimeter-rate sentence that the definitions quote). Count P_k. P_kτ_k² ≍ L^{K−k}(K−k+1)^{−1}·polylog → ∞ (with the SU(2) upper bound; without it, CRE (2.6) gives g_k² ≥ g_K²/((1+β₀)²(K−k)) and CRE (2.7) (p = 6p₀) gives (log g_k⁻²)^{6p₀} ≥ (1+β₀)^{−1}(log g_K⁻²)^{6p₀}, so P_kτ_k² ≥ cℓ_CL^{K−k}(K−k)^{−1}g_K²(log g_K⁻²)^{6p₀}/(1+β₀)³, also → ∞). The flat-reference, period-invariant (Π_{k+1}-periodic) part is **absorbed exactly** into δm_{k+1}, with zero remainder (D2.3). Background-dependent scalar parts of deviation size stay as scalar insertions, since 𝓔_{k+1} holds numbers only (D(k+1)(v)), and history-dependent parts near large-field regions go to (f).
- **(b) Corner parts.** Four per step. After exact absorption into z_{k+1}, a remainder r_k = O(τ_k²g_k^δ), δ > 0, is summable uniformly in K under the upper bound of the couplings: Σ_k(K−k+1)^{−1−δ/2}·polylog(K−k+1) < ∞. With g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K only, the available bound Σ_{k≤K}r_k ≤ CKg_K^{2+δ}polylog is not uniform in K. A remainder of order τ_k² is not summable in either case (iteration audit §4(2)). **Absorb exactly, remainder O(τ_k²g_k^δ)** (D2.3), and for general G either the upper bound or a zero corner remainder is needed (gaps block). *Closing route D2.3(6)–(7) (reviewed):* the exact absorption leaves, at a commensurate step, the remainder C_Eτ_k²(s_k+1)e^{−c_Es_k} with s_k = ℓ_CL^{K−k}, which decays in the contour size at that step rather than carrying a factor g_k^δ. For the admissible sides of D2.3(4) every step is commensurate; without that restriction the at most a* other steps leave O(τ_k²(Π_{k+1}+1+s_k)). For the admissible sides the sum over k is at most C_Στ̄² uniformly in K and ℓ_C, using g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K only (R-cpl₀). This holds once D2.4 proves (S-w) with constants independent of k ((12)(f), given its inputs (13)(d); (T′), (Z) and the Kotecký–Preiss condition follow from it). So (b) needs neither the upper bound of the couplings nor a zero remainder.
- **(c) Deviation parts of the old insertions.** They are carried, never summed in absolute value.
  - Each new traversal n′ (a step-(k+1) bond of C) contains L old traversals.
  - Split an old insertion as Y(U_k) = Y(U_{k+1}) + [Y(U_k) − Y(U_{k+1})].
  - The first part is bounded by C_χρ_kδ*^{(k)}_n(U_{k+1}) ≤ C_χρ_k[L^{−2}δ*^{(k+1)}_{n′}(U_{k+1}) + B_n]: the first term after (γ)'s matching, and B_n the Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} part of (3)(c), which joins the scale-(k+1) attached part (species att(k+1, ·), (3)). The equality of the convention above holds only for the same plaquettes and radii; here the plaquette set changes from Ω_k to Ω_{k+1}, and the traversal and radii from n to n′.
  - So, for the first term, the L old traversals of n′ contribute at most L^{−1}(ρ_k/ρ_{k+1})·C_χρ_{k+1}δ*_{n′}^{(k+1)}(U_{k+1}). Here ρ_k/ρ_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)⁶, with ρ_k := C_ρg_k²(1+r_k)⁴: g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_{k+1} (CRE (2.6)) and (log g_k⁻²)^p ≤ (1+β₀)(log g_{k+1}⁻²)^p (the second inequality of CRE (2.7), with m = k, n = k+1).
  - With the feedback of (d) the constants obey C_χ^{(k+1)} ≤ C_new + (L^{−1} + C_dL²(1+β₀)²g_K²)(1+β₀)⁶C_χ^{(k)} + C_d′ρ_k(C_χ^{(k)})², which is bounded uniformly in k for L ≥ 2, β₀ small and g_K ≤ g(L). The factor (1+β₀)²g_K² and the quadratic term use g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K, the last inequality of CRE (2.6) at n − m = K − k, uniformly in K − k (reading R-cpl₀ of the gaps block).
  - *Corrected by D2.4(6) (not reviewed).* With D2.4(5)'s ρ̃_k the ratio is ρ̃_k/ρ̃_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)⁸, since α_{i,k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)²α_{i,k} by CRE (2.6)–(2.7) at n − m = 1. So the factor (1+β₀)⁶ above becomes (1+β₀)⁸, and "β₀ small" means (1+β₀)⁸ < L. CRE p.255 allows β₀ that small for small g. C_new = C_gC_S·pl/c₀ comes from D2.4(4), which rests on (LPG′) and (S-w) (both derived, SW closure §5–§6; not reviewed).
  - **Needs D2.4**: that the deviation part of an old insertion is bounded by the deviation of its argument, as D1(ii)'s first term states at step 1. Then the contraction L^{−1} per step follows from this count. The count also uses:
    - (α) the evaluation point: by RG I (2.3), p.265, V^{(k)} = M^k(U_{k+1}). By the unique minimal orbit (CRE p.256 l.44–51) and the gauge condition, U_k(M^k(U_{k+1})) = U_{k+1}, i.e. U_k′(1) = 1 in "U_k(V′V^{(k)}) = U_k′(V′)U_{k+1}" (RG I p.265);
    - (β) V^{(k)} in the support of the step-k indicators. CRE (2.17) tests U_{k,□}(V_k) on the cubes □ ⊂ Ω_k, and p.268 bounds U_{k+1} only on Ω_{k+1}. So the plaquette test at V_k = V^{(k)} needs the comparison of U_{k,□}(V^{(k)}) with U_{k+1} on □^∼. *Derived from Bałaban's own steps.* Let □ ⊂ Ω_{k+1}^∼ with □ ⊂ □′ ⊂ R_{k+1}^c, □′ a cube of (3.16) (this holds on Λ_{k+1}^∼, CRE (3.20), p.269). By (3.19), p.268, |V^{(k)}(b)(V^{(k)}_{□′}(b))^{−1} − 1| < δ_k for b ∈ (□′^{∼2})^{(k)*}, so V_k = V^{(k)} passes (3.3)'s test (threshold 2δ_k). The argument of 𝐇_{k,□} in (3.6) is then bounded by 2δ_k ≤ 4δ_k off the boundary layer, since |(1/i)log x| ≤ 2|x − 1| for |x − 1| ≤ 1/2, as p.266 requires, and U_{k+1,□′} passes (3.2)'s test since □′ ⊂ P_{k+1}^c. So (3.7) and (3.8) hold at V_k = V^{(k)} as printed, and χ_k(□) = 1 there (p.266: "Thus χ_k(□) = 1 for □ ⊂ Ω_{k+1}^∼"). (3.8) uses the test of (3.2) at threshold ε_{k+1}(L^{−1}η)², not p.268's regularity at 2B₃ε_{k+1}(L^{−1}η)², and needs only 2(1+β₀)L^{−2} + 4/10 < 1. (The route through 2B₃(1+β₀) ≤ L² doesn't work: B₃ depends on L, VAR Thm 1, p.279: "a₀, a₁, B₃, depend on d and L only".) *The cubes with □′ ⊂ R_{k+1} (checked against p.268 and CMP98 pp.33, 42).* p.268 derives (3.19) from (3.17), the regularity of U_{k+1} on Ω_{k+1} (which bounds 𝐇_{k+1,□′} on □′^{∼2}), (3.18), and CMP98 (106)–(108), (159)–(163). (106)–(108) are identities for the gauge transformation u of CMP98 (p.33 does not name the gauge), and (159)–(163) bound Ū^k(Ū_0^k)^{−1} for U = U′U_0 from |(1/i)log \bar{\bar U}′^j| < 2α₁L^jη (the doubly barred Ū′ as printed in (161)) (CMP98 (161), from its Proposition 4); here U′ is the factor exp iL^{−1}η𝐇_{k+1,□′} of (3.17). (3.19) bounds V^{(k)}, a function not of the fluctuation A_k (p.268: "the characteristic functions χ_k′ depend now on the field V^{(k)}, hence on the new field variables V_{k+1}"), and no step of this derivation uses (3.16)'s test, which bears on the fluctuation A_k. So (3.19) holds for every cube □′ of (3.16), and the cubes □ ⊂ □′ ⊂ R_{k+1} are covered by the argument above. D2.6's carrier item: the cubes □ ⊂ Ω_{k+1}^∼ in the band Ω_{k+1}^∼∖Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1}, which lie in no cube □′ of (3.16), since (3.16) partitions only Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1} (p.268), and the cubes □ ⊂ Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}^∼ whose localisation domain □^{∼4} (CRE (2.16)) meets Ω_{k+1}, which see V^{(k)} there too. Pages: (3.2), (3.3), P_{k+1} and δ_k on p.265 (printed "δ_k = g_kA_1/A_0p_0(g_k)"; (3.19) uses ε_k = (A₀/A₁)δ_k); (3.16)–(3.19) on p.268; (3.20) on p.269; B₃ in VAR Thm 1, p.279. *Status: the cubes of Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}^∼ are closed in WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §9.2 for M ≥ 4LM₂ (□^{∼4} has width ≤ 4LM₂R_k ≤ 4L²M₂R_{k+1} ≤ LMR_{k+1}, CRE (2.9) with (2.5), p.245's "M much larger than M₂"; reading R-avg). The band cubes Ω_{k+1}^∼∖Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1} need a localisation identity for the mixed-constraint minimiser, since (3.17) with 𝔹_{k+1}(□′^{∼4}) is not an identity for U_{k+1} where □′^{∼4} meets Γ_k (scale-k constraints there); (3.12)'s coefficient doesn't work there. The identity is printed neither in CMP98 Sect. C (single domain) nor in CRE pp.266–269. CRE p.257 prints mixed-set localisation in a single-layer case (with a 2M₁ distance condition that fails where ∂□′^{∼4} crosses ∂Ω_{k+1}) and a several-layer sentence without conditions, and p.269 uses it on Λ_{k+1}ᶜ ∩ Λ_k, which is not a crossing instance. The identity for crossing domains is derived (closure §9.3 (1)–(2): 𝔹_mix is the intersection of two admissible nested sequences, VAR p.277 (1); gluing with R-avg and VAR Theorem 1's uniqueness give (3.17) for it). The band cubes with □′^{∼4} ⊂ Ω_{k+1} are derived (closure §9.3, given R-avg, [15], [12], [14] (1.65)'s reasoning); the others (□′^{∼4} ⊄ Ω_{k+1}) are derived by route (a), with (3.7)'s requirement |𝐇_{k,□}| < (1/10)ε_k met by the bound B₃C_9δ_k + O(1)e^{−R_k}ε_k + (1/40)ε_k, C_9 = 4 + 4c₁₂B₃, for A₁/A₀ < 1/(20B₃C_9) and γ small; all sets on the one fine lattice of U_{k+1}; O1 closed.*
    - (γ) the step-k neighbourhoods of the L old traversals lie in the step-(k+1) neighbourhood of n′ (D2.4, with the radii of (3));
    - (δ) ρ_k := C_ρg_k²(1+r_k)⁴ with r_k := A₁(log g_k⁻²)^{p₀}, as fixed in (3);
    - (ε) the attached parts of the old insertions at U_{k+1}. Kept in D(k+1)(ii), the L old traversals of n′ would add them, a factor L per step (n′ parallel to the data), which is not controlled. So they leave (ii) at the end of step k+1's map and keep their species att(k, ·), bounded by (ii′) with j = k and paid per component in (f) (as att(k+1, Z) they would sit in D(k+1)(ii), the factor-L situation excluded here; they cannot be scalar local activities); D2.6. (A "rescaling gain" for the large-field term doesn't work: it needs a shift −1 that D(k)(ii) does not carry.) The wrap term stays: it grows by at most L per step and is bounded by CL^{K−j}ρ̂_je^{−cN^{(j)}_min/M} at step K. With N^{(j)}_min ≍ T_phL^{K−j} ((g)) and x := L^{K−j} ≥ 1 this is Cρ̂_jxe^{−c′T_phx/M}, and xe^{−ax} ≤ e^{−a} for x ≥ 1 once a ≥ 1; so each term is at most Cρ̂_je^{−c′T_ph/M}. That alone gives K terms of this size, which is not uniform in K. With a := c′T_ph/M ≥ 1 and L ≥ 2, L^ie^{−aL^i} = e^{−a}L^ie^{−a(L^i−1)} ≤ e^{−a}L^ie^{−(L^i−1)}, and Σ_{j≤K}L^{K−j}e^{−aL^{K−j}} ≤ e^{−a}Σ_{i≥0}L^ie^{−(L^i−1)} ≤ 2e^{−a}: the terms are 1, ≤ 0.74, ≤ 0.20, ≤ 0.01, …, their values at L = 2, since xe^{−(x−1)} decreases for x ≥ 1. So the sum over j is at most 2C(sup_jρ̂_j)e^{−c′T_ph/M}, small under the condition T_ph ≥ C·M added to (g). At j near K the condition T_ph ≫ ℓ_C alone would not do;
    - (ζ) the plaquettes of Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}, where the maximum of δ*^{(k)} is not matched by δ*^{(k+1)}: the split of (3)(c), a D2.4 item.
- **(d) Fluctuation parts of the old insertions. Needs D2.1–D2.5**. Y(U_k) − Y(U_{k+1}) is of order ρ_kg_k, with zero mean at first order in the step-(k+1) fluctuation.
  - Its second-order mean is O(ρ_kg_k²), and it is perimeter-proportional (P_kρ_kg_k² → ∞). At flat U_{k+1} the mean is scalar, by invariance under constant gauge transformations and Schur's lemma; that part goes to (a). The rest is O(ρ_kg_k²·curvature) and stays in the class, as for the pair contractions below.
  - Its pair contractions are of order ρ_k²g_k² per pair with another old insertion, and ρ_kg_k² per pair with the fluctuation of the leading word, which is O(g_k) per step-k bond. They decay over one new block, since the step-(k+1) covariance decays at the scale-(k+1) block scale (the analogue of μ_H = γ_L/(8Lm_L) in fine units; IT-1 note (D0)). So there are O(L²) pairs per new traversal, with a constant that depends on L through the decay rate (μ_H, not supplied here).
  - Gauge covariance makes the leading part of each pair contraction scalar. Convention: t_a is an orthonormal basis of 𝔤 for the invariant metric, T^a := π_*(t_a), C₂(π) := Σ_aT^aT^a (positive for 𝔤 realised by Hermitian matrices, U = exp iA as in CRE), Ad(X)t_b = Σ_aAd(X)^{ab}t_a, and X is the transport between the two insertion points in the orientation of the word. If the covariance of the fluctuations at n and n″ is c(n,n″)·Ad(X_{n→n″}), then XT^bX^{−1} = Σ_cAd(X)^{cb}T^c gives Σ_{a,b}T^aXT^bAd(X)^{ab} = Σ_{a,c}T^aT^cX(Ad(X)Ad(X)ᵀ)^{ac} = (Σ_aT^aT^a)X = C₂(π)X, since Ad(X) is orthogonal and Σ_aT^aT^a is scalar by Schur. (With the transposed convention the identity fails, e.g. for SU(2).) For covariant families M^a, N^a (π(g)M^aπ(g)^{−1} = Σ_cAd(g)^{ca}M^c and likewise for N^a, for all g ∈ G), the same computation gives Σ_{a,b}M^aXN^bAd(X)^{ab} = (Σ_aM^aN^a)X, and Σ_aM^aN^a commutes with π(G), hence is scalar; this covers the pairs of old insertions. The scalar part goes to (a). The non-scalar part is the deviation of the covariance from the transported form, which is of the order of the background curvature between n and n″.
  - Within one new block the curvature between n and n″ is summed over at most L² step-k plaquettes, so it is O(L²δ*^{(k)}) = O(δ*^{(k+1)}). So the non-scalar remainder per new traversal is O(L²ρ_kg_k²δ*^{(k+1)}). Against the class bound ρ_{k+1}δ*^{(k+1)} this is the factor L²g_k²ρ_k/ρ_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)⁸L²g_K², small for g_K ≤ g(L) (g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K, CRE (2.6)). With D2.4(5)'s ρ̃ the old dev bound is ρ̃_kδ*, and against ρ̃_{k+1}δ*^{(k+1)} the factor is L²g_k²ρ̃_k/ρ̃_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)^{10}L²g_K², which is D2.4(6)'s C_dL²(1+β₀)²g_K²·(1+β₀)⁸.
- **(e) Fresh insertions.** These come from integrating the step-(k+1) fluctuations of the leading word f_k. They are of order C_newρ_{k+1}δ*^{(k+1)} per new traversal: V3's §3a deviation form at step k+1, with D2.1's form of f_k. **Needs D2.1–D2.2.**
- **(f) Large-field attached parts and exposed segments.**
  - Count: P_k·p_k, with p_k the probability that a step-k bond lies near a large-field region. p_k ≤ e^{−c(log g_k⁻²)^{2p₀}} is proved; a lower bound of the same order is expected (IT-1 note §3d) and not proved. The available bound P_kp_k = 4ℓ_CL^{K−k}e^{−c(log g_k⁻²)^{2p₀}} diverges as K − k → ∞, since (log(K−k))^{2p₀} = o(K−k), so the available estimates cannot bound these terms: **carry them as local activities attached to the large-field regions**, as V3's E_bdry cannot be bounded (iteration audit §4(5)).
  - *Payment, per component and over its lifetime.* LFR II p.381 gives the fundamental factor exp(−½γ₀A₁²p₀²(g_j)(d_j′(Z_j^{(i)})+1) − 2p₀(g_j)) for each large-field region Z_j^{(i)} *created at step j* (IT-1 note R-d″(ii) gives the step-1 form, with ½c₄ϑ²A₁²p₀²(g₁)). By p.384 the factor of a component Z of Z_j is exp(−κ_j(Z) − 2p₀(g_{j(Z)})), and it "controls K renormalization steps" through (1.80). The attached and exposed costs near Z recur at every one of these steps, so the payment must cover all of them.
  - *Per-step cost.* The contour has at most c_C·MR_n scale-n bonds per MR_n-cube, c_C depending only on the shape of C (c_C = 4 for a rectangle: each straight side meets a cube of side MR_n in at most MR_n bonds). So at step n the costs e^{Cτ_n²} per segment cost at most e^{Cc_CMR_nτ_n²} per MR_n-cube of the current region, S^{n−j}(Z) if no large fields are created near Z.
  - *Lifetime sum.* D_j(Z) := Σ_{n=j}^{j+K_Z}Cc_CMR_nτ_n²·#cubes_n(S^{n−j}(Z)), with K_Z the lifetime of Z (p.384's "K renormalization steps"). Bałaban's chain on p.384–385 bounds exactly this volume count: #cubes_n(S^{n−j}(Z)) = (MR_n)^{−d}|S^{n−j}(Z)| ≤ 63^d(MR_n)^{−d}|Z^{(n−j)}| ≤ 3(126)^d2^{−(n−j)}d_j′(Z) for n ≤ n₀, and S^{n−j}(Z) lies in a cube of size 64MR_n, so #cubes_n ≤ 64^d, for the at most R_j steps after n₀ (K_Z ≤ n₀ − j + R_j, p.385). With R_n ≤ LR_j (CRE (2.9)), τ_n² ≤ τ̄² := sup_{n≤K}τ_n² ≤ Cg_K²(log g_K⁻²)^{6p₀} (τ = C[g(1+r)³ + e^{−cr²}] is increasing in g for small g, and g_n ≤ (1+β₀)g_K by CRE (2.6)), and the step n = j itself (#cubes_j(Z) ≤ 3·2^{d−1}d_j′(Z) for d_j′(Z) > 0 by p.384's inequality at n = j, and ≤ 2^d otherwise), this is (1.81) with O(1)M^dR_n^{d+1} replaced by Cc_CMR_nτ_n², and d_n′(S^{n−j}(Z)) by #cubes_n, bounded by the same middle member:
    D_j(Z) ≤ C_dc_CMLR_j²τ̄²(d_j′(Z)+1), C_d := C(3(126)^d + 64^d + 3·2^{d−1}).
  - *The attached species.* D_j(Z) covers only the per-segment costs. The species att(j, Z), att(j, coll) (per component through B_j(Z)) and lw(Z) are carried without growth (D(k)(ii′): the carry does not increase the left sides; by the regrouping, while their stability under the evaluation at U_{k+1}, under the fluctuation parts and under the multi-parent insertions is the D2.6 item of D(k)(ii′)), so they are paid once, not at every step of Z's lifetime. Their totals, at the scales of D(k)(ii′), are
    A_j(Z) := Cρ̂_jN_j(Z) + Cρ_{t,j}[Σ_{Δ→Z}𝔪̂^{(j)}_ΔN_j(Δ) + [j = 1]C′_χg_jΣ_{b→Z}h(m^{(j)}_b)N_j(b)] + [j = 1]Σ_{i→Z}‖π(T̂_iT_i^{−1}) − 1‖ (the inputs term at j = 1 only: for j ≥ 2 the ω′-dependence is bounded pointwise without translation, D2.6 closure §2.2 with R-src, §7.1; every N_j(·) at the one rate c := min(c^{(1)}, c_*′) of closure 7.1, c_*′ = min(η_V/C_F, ν_e/8, κ/4) of closure §2.2 (ν_e/8, so that the att(j, Z) production at ν_e/8 is dominated); the lw term, unweighted here, is closure 7.1's weighted total [j = 1](2C_{lw}g₀(r+1) + C_{lw}^{III})M_VR₁(d₁′(Z)+1), with §6.5's label factor and class (III), and h(m^{(j)}_b) is h(m̂_b) at the complex sup of closure 6.3),
    with ρ̂_j of order g_j²(log g_j^{−2})^{4p₀} and ρ_{t,j} of order g_j(log g_j^{−2})^{p₀}. The cost to be paid by Z's fundamental factor is D_j(Z) + A_j(Z). The bound of A_j(Z) by a multiple of d_j′(Z) + 1 (counting the step-j traversals within distance O(M) of Z, the marks and the inputs assigned to Z) and the size of the lw term are part of the D2.6 rerun.
  - *The condition (stated for j = 1).* At creation at step 1 the cost is paid if C_dc_CMLR_1²τ̄² ≤ ¼c₄(434)^{−d}ϑ²A₁²(log g₁⁻²)^{2p₀} − O(1)2(64)^dM^dL^{d+1}R_1^{d+2}: p.385's j = 1 condition, with ¼γ₀(14)^{−d} replaced as in IT-1 note §3f(2) Minor 4, and its right side at most doubled once C_dc_Cτ̄² ≤ O(1)2(64)^dM^{d−1}L^dR_1^d. The left side is g_K² times a polylog, so this holds for small g, by the same "p₀ large, g small" as p.385. The small domains of p.386 are paid in closure §7.3, which pays the small-domain costs D + A from ¼p₀(g_j). The comparison at general j, with (1.85)'s "γ₀A₁²p₀²(g_{j+1})(d′+1)", is part of the D2.6 rerun.
  - *Amendment (D2.6 closure §7.2–7.4).* In *Per-step cost*, *Lifetime sum* and *The condition* above, τ_n is replaced by τ̂_n (τ̂_n² := max(τ_n², Cρ̂_n), closure 7.4(a)), τ̄ by τ̂̄ := sup_{j≤n≤K}τ̂_n, and the j = 1 constant 2(64)^d by 4(1 + C_X)(64)^d (closure §7.2's ledger, run per label polymer X with K₁ ≥ 4(1 + C_X)B₁ + 12·2^dlog 2, C_X := √d·3·2^{d−1}, and ε_A(1) ≤ K₁/(8C_X)); the per-cube cost carries the collar factor 13^d inside C (closure 7.4(a)). The comparisons are closure §7.2–7.3's ledgers.
  - *What is left (D2.6; gaps block).* Bałaban's induction pp.384–389 with the summand of (1.80) enlarged by Cc_CMR_nτ_n²#cubes_n(S^{n−j}(Z)). In the merging step (1.86) the new summand needs no margin, since #cubes is subadditive under Z ⊂ X ∪ Y (Bałaban's d′ needs the +2d of p.386). The future sums of Z against those of X and Y are on p.387 (read). *WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §7.1 (A_j(Z) ≤ C_A(d_j′(Z)+1)), §7.2 (the lifetime total ε_A(d_j′(Z)+1), the induction with the O(1) doubled, future sums unchanged), §7.3 (the condition at general j with (1.85)), §7.4 (exposed segments, per-cube comparisons, label weight); the status is closure §8.*
  - *Localisation.* §3i(7) gives ∗-connected sets X meeting K_j and Dᶜ, which may meet several components. So the payment is per polymer, a union of components linked by such sets, with the weight e^{κ#boxes(X)}; the per-cube comparison of §3i(7) (*Against (0)*) is a D2.6 item.
- **(g) Torus and corner–corner parts of E°.** At step k these are Cτ_k²(n_k e^{−cι_{T,k}} + (n_k+1)e^{−cn_k}) (the torus part with the amplitude 𝔞_{k+1} = max(τ_k², ρ_{k+1}·pl) of D2.3(4)(Z-f), which changes nothing below) with n_k ≍ ℓ_CL^{K−k} and the step-k quantity ι_{T,k} := max(0, N_min^{(k)}/4 − R_0). With a torus of physical side T_ph ≫ ℓ_C, N_min^{(k)} ≍ T_phL^{K−k}, and the terms are dominated by the last O(1) steps and **summable** (iteration audit §4(3)). With a fixed ι_T the torus sum would not be summable. The wrap terms of (c)(ε) need in addition T_ph ≥ C·M, with M the decay length of D(k)(ii). Both are lower bounds on the periods, which may be taken as large as needed. *Amendment (D2.6 closure 2.1 *Precedence and units*):* a wrap(k+1) group failing (i) or (iii) has amplitude C_S″b_k, so D(k)(ii)'s wrap display carries max(C_S′ρ̂_j, C_S″b_j) at each j; the sum over j is ≤ 2max(C_S′, C_S″)sup_jmax(ρ̂_j, b_j) times the same torus factor, and the condition holds with b_j in place of ρ̂_j (b_j → 0 with g_j).
- **(h) The norm of the dressed loop.** |𝒲_k| ≤ |κ^{(k)}|e^{|𝓔_k|}∏_n(1+ν_n) is at most |κ^{(k)}|exp(CP_k(ρ_kε_k + τ_k²)); this bound is exponentially large in L^{K−k}. D(k) never uses it, and the step map may use only local bounds: Cauchy estimates on polydiscs in bounded sets of variables (IT-1 note §6(f4)) and cluster expansions with local activities. At k = K, P_K = 4ℓ_C, so |𝒲_K| ≤ |κ^{(K)}|e^{Cℓ_C}.

**Result of the count check.** Counting does not exclude D(k) with constants uniform in k and K, under four conditions:
- every perimeter-proportional scalar part is absorbed exactly ((a));
- corner remainders are O(τ_k²g_k^δ), and summable ((b): needs the coupling upper bound, printed for SU(2); after D2.3(7) (b) does not, and the D2.6 closure §5.0 does, through Cor. 8.2, proved for simple G);
- the deviation part contracts at L^{−1} per step ((c));
- every large-field-attached term is carried as a local activity and paid by its own component over its lifetime ((f)).
Their inputs: C_new uniform in k ((e)); the (d) remainder at most (1+β₀)⁸L²g_K² times the class bound against ρ_{k+1}, or (1+β₀)^{10}L²g_K² against ρ̃_{k+1} with D2.4(5); only local bounds in the step map ((h)); the torus condition of (g).

Each is a D2 item. Items (4)–(7) of the iteration audit, fatal as bounds at step 1, are carried by D(k) instead of bounded: (4) and (5) as local terms and activities attached to large-field regions ((f); a translation-invariant part of (4), if any, in (a)); (6) as the deviation part ((c)) plus attached parts ((f)); (7) by the per-traversal class without a global bound ((c), (e), (h)). The fluctuation parts (d) have no step-1 counterpart and feed (a) and (c).

### (6) Order of work and reviews

- D2.1 first, since D2.2–D2.5 take its form of the leading word as input; then D2.2 with D2.5 and D2.6's carrier item, then D2.3 and D2.4, then the rest of D2.6, then D2.7. D2.2–D2.6 are first done for k ≤ N, before D2.7.
- A review follows D2.0 and each of D2.1, …, D2.7.

## D2.1 The leading word at step k+1 (reviewed: no fatal or serious finding; last repairs not re-reviewed)

### (0) What is needed

D2.2 integrates the step-(k+1) fluctuation field with the dressed loop as the observable. Its input from the leading word is f_k(U_k), written through the step-(k+1) background U_{k+1} and the fluctuation variable of CRE (3.15), in the class form: f_{k+1} plus insertions that are local along C, analytic, and small with the fluctuation. That this is the weakest form D2.2 can use is a target, to be confirmed when D2.2 states what it consumes. The fluctuation integral is done by a cluster expansion, so every non-leading part must be a local activity, and D2.0(5)(h) excludes any bound on the whole word. The old insertions, words and scalar exponent of 𝒲_k are D2.2's, after D2.5.

### (1) Quoted

*Notation.* RG2's constant κ₁ is written κ_s in this section, to keep it apart from D2.0(3)'s step-1 perimeter coefficient κ₁; the threshold of (4) is ε_H, to keep it apart from CRE's ε₀ = g₀p₀(g₀). RG2's ε₂, ε₃ keep their names; RG2's ε₁ is written ε₁^{RG2} and VAR's ε₁ is written ε₁^{VAR}, to keep both apart from CRE's ε_k = g_kp₀(g_k) at k = 1. CRE's "[I]" is RG I, CMP 109, 249–301 (CRE reference list, p.285).

- *The step-(k+1) variables.*
  - CRE (3.10)–(3.11), p.266: V^{(k)} = M^k(U_{k+1}) on Ω_{k+1}^{(k)}, and V_k′ = V_k(V^{(k)})^{−1} on B(Γ_{k+1}).
  - CRE (3.14), p.267: |V_k′ − 1| < O(1)ε_k on Ω_{k+1}, and A_k′ = (1/i)log V_k′.
  - CRE p.267, after (3.14): "The next steps are exactly the same as in Sect. C [16], and in fact the same as in Sect. 2 [I]. … only the symbols represent different operators, determined by the sequence {Ω_j}."
  - CRE uses the symbol A_k′ twice: first A_k′ = (1/i)log V_k′ ((3.14)), then the scaling A_k′ = g_kA_k (p.267), which defines the integration variable A_k. Below, A_k is always this scaled variable. After the scaling, the integrand of (3.15) contains U_k(exp i[g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k)]V^{(k)}), and U_{k+1} where A_k = 0. So the step-k fine field is U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)}), with B′ := g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k) on B(Γ_{k+1}) (RG2 (1.19), where A_k is called B). Off B(Γ_{k+1}), V_k is a pending variable and B′ := 0.
  - RG2 (1.20): |B′| ≤ O(1)g_k|A_k| + 4C₂(O(1)g_k|A_k|)² ≤ C₁g_k|A_k| < C₁ε₁^{RG2} for g_k|A_k| < ε₁^{RG2}. RG2 p.6, after (1.20): 𝐇_k is analytic in A_k for g_k|A_k| < ε₁^{RG2}, so this is a complex domain; C₁ε₁^{RG2} ≤ ε₃^{RG2} (RG2 p.7; C₁ = C₁^{RG2}) puts B′ in the domain of (1.18). RG1 p.267: D̃ is analytic and "has an expansion beginning with quadratic terms".
  - *Derived.* Fix A_k with g_k|A_k| < ε₁^{RG2} and put f(t) := −hD̃(tg_kCA_k) = B′(tA_k) − tg_kCA_k for |t| < T := ε₁^{RG2}/(g_k|A_k|). The map t ↦ B′(tA_k) is analytic with B′(0) = 0 and derivative g_kCA_k at t = 0 (D̃ starts with quadratic terms), and |B′(tA_k)| < C₁ε₁^{RG2} by (1.20); Cauchy's estimate at t = 0 gives |g_kCA_k| ≤ C₁ε₁^{RG2}T^{−1} = C₁g_k|A_k|. So f is analytic with f(0) = f′(0) = 0 and |f(t)| ≤ 2C₁ε₁^{RG2}. The Schwarz lemma for a double zero gives |f(1)| ≤ 2C₁ε₁^{RG2}T^{−2}, so |B′ − g_kCA_k| ≤ 2C₁(ε₁^{RG2})^{−1}(g_k|A_k|)². No bound on C beyond (1.20) is used.
- *The composition.*
  - RG1 p.265 (RG1's [15] is VAR, RG1 reference list p.299): (2.3) "V^{(k)} = Ū^k_{k+1} = M^k(U_{k+1})"; "As in Sect. G [15] we write U_k(V′V^{(k)}) = U_k′(V′)U_{k+1}"; after a transformation to the axial gauge and the gauge transformation u_k to the Landau gauge, "U_k′^{u_k^{−1}} = exp iη𝐇_k(B′)".
  - RG1 (2.6), p.266: "Using the gauge invariance of the action … A(U_k(V′V^{(k)})) = A(exp iη𝐇_k(B′)U_{k+1})".
  - VAR p.305, Sect. G: with U₀ = U_k(V₀), "fix the Landau gauge for the configuration U_k(V′V₀)U₀^{−1} = U₁, and we have U₁ = exp iη𝓗(B)".
  - VAR p.307: 𝓗(B) "is an analytic function of 𝔤^c-valued configurations B defined on 𝔅_k and satisfying (172)". The transformation back to the axial gauge "is an analytic function of 𝓗, hence of B". (181) extends to G^c-valued gauge transformations.
- *The form of 𝐇_k.*
  - RG2 (1.2)–(1.4), p.2: 𝐇_k(B′) = H₀B′ + 𝐀₀ − HD(H₀B′ + 𝐀₀), with D(A′) = C(A′ − HD(A′)) and 𝐀₀ + G̃(δV/δA′)(H₀B′ + 𝐀₀) = 0.
  - RG2 (1.14), (1.16): |D(H(s),A′)| ≤ 4C₂|A′|² and |𝐀₀| ≤ 4C₄(B₀e^{16κ_s})³|B′|². So the first-order part of 𝐇_k is H₀B′.
- *The multi-domain form.*
  - CRE (3.6), p.266: U_{k,□} = (exp iη𝐇_{k,□}((1/i)log[M·(Q_k^{s*}V_k)(M·(U_{k+1,□′}))^{−1}])U_{k+1,□′})^{u_{k,□}^{−1}}; CRE (3.17), p.268, the same at step k+1. The gauge transformation acts on the product e^{iη𝐇}U_{k+1}.
  - CRE (3.7), p.266: |𝐇_{k,□}|, |∇^η_{U_{k+1,□′}}𝐇_{k,□}| < (1/10)ε_k on □^∼, for A₁/A₀ and γ small.
  - CRE p.267, continuing the quote above: "only the symbols represent different operators, determined by the sequence {Ω_j}. Their properties are essentially the same as before, because of the localization of the field A_k." On the pages read this is the only transfer to the multi-domain operators. It names RG1 Sect. 2 and Sect. C [16]. RG2 Sect. 1's construction reaches CRE's operators through this sentence together with RG2 p.3, "we can replace the propagators by arbitrary operators having the same regularity properties and satisfying the same bounds", and (1.5). It is accepted as Bałaban's statement. It is used quantitatively in case (A) of (4), for RG2's constants on CRE's operators, which are k-independent, and qualitatively in case (B).
- *Decoupling, RG2 pp.3–6.*
  - (1.6) and p.3: the propagators are random-walk sums. Each walk term is localised in X̃₀⁵ ∪ ⋯ ∪ X̃_n⁵, and its kernel vanishes within ⅓M₁ of ∂X_j.
  - p.3: the cubes Δ of a partition σ_k carry parameters s(Δ) (the print's σ_k has cubes of side R₁M₁ ≤ M; its example, and (4) below, use cubes of side M). Each walk term is multiplied by ∏s(Δ) over the cubes meeting its localisation domain; this defines H(s), G̃(s), H₀(s), and "They coincide with the original ones for s = 1".
  - (1.5): D and 𝐀₀ are functionals of the operators.
  - p.4, at s(σ^c) = 0: the kernels "vanish, unless both arguments are in the interior of one component of Y(σ), in fact with distances to the boundary of this component bigger than M₁". 𝐀₀ "is equal to 0 on the neighborhood of Y(σ)^c", and on a component it depends on the propagators and B′ restricted to that component.
  - (1.11): for |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s} and δ₁M ≥ κ_s, H(s)X is analytic in s and bounded by B₀e^{16κ_s}|X|.
  - (1.17)–(1.18), p.6: 𝐇_k(s(Y₀),B′) is analytic in s(Y₀) and B′, with |𝐇_k(s(Y₀),B′)| ≤ 4B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′| "and the same bound for all admissible norms". This holds for |B′| < ε₃, with 4C₂B₀e^{16κ_s}ε₂ ≤ 1 (p.5), 4C₄B₀²e^{32κ_s}ε₃ ≤ 1 and 2B₀e^{16κ_s}ε₃ ≤ ε₂ (pp.5–6), uniformly for (𝐔,𝐉) in a complex domain given by I.(i)–(iii) (p.6, after (1.18)).
  - RG2's example decouples only the cubes disjoint from the interior of □̃⁴ (p.3). Below, all cubes carry parameters.

### (2) The leading word as a function of the fluctuation (derived from (1), with reading R-g)

- *Reading R-g (notation).* In RG1's "U_k′^{u_k^{−1}}", the gauge transformations act on U_k(V′V^{(k)}) with U_{k+1} fixed, as in VAR's "fix the Landau gauge for the configuration U_k(V′V₀)U₀^{−1}". CRE (3.6) and (3.17) print this form for the multi-domain functions: the gauge transformation acts on the product e^{iη𝐇}U_{k+1}. It is also the reading under which RG1 (2.6) "Using the gauge invariance of the action" applies as written: if the transformation acted on the factor U_k′ alone, U_k′U_{k+1} and (U_k′)^{u}U_{k+1} would not be gauge equivalent in general. So: for real A_k on the support, there is a G-valued lattice gauge transformation u with U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})^u = e^{iη𝐇_k(B′)}U_{k+1} bondwise.
- *The identity.* χ_π∘Hol_C is invariant under lattice gauge transformations, since Hol_C(U^u) = u(x₀)Hol_C(U)u(x₀)^{−1} for the base point x₀ of C. So for real A_k on the support,
  f_k(U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})) = dim(π)^{−1}χ_π(Hol_C(e^{iη𝐇_k(B′)}U_{k+1})).
- *Complex A_k and complex backgrounds* (for D(k+1)(iii)). Run the identity theorem jointly in (A_k, a′), with 𝐔 = e^{iηa′}U_{k+1} as in (4) and a′ 𝔤^c-valued (CRE (2.39) complexifies the configuration, U′ = exp iξA′, not the variables that define U_{k+1}). Take a polydisc centred at a real point (real A_k, a′ = 0) that lies inside both the analyticity domain of the left side (VAR (172), VAR p.307 and (181), Prop. 9 [15] for the minimiser, D(k)(iii) for f_k) and RG2's domain I.(i)–(iii) (p.6), on which the right side is analytic by RG2 (1.18). The two sides agree at the real points of the polydisc, a uniqueness set for functions analytic on a polydisc centred at a real point, so they agree on the polydisc, and by the identity theorem on the connected component of the intersection of the two domains that contains it. The gauge transformation is not needed here.
- *At A_k = 0.* 𝐇_k(0) = 0 by (1.18), so f_k(U_k(V^{(k)})) = f_{k+1} := dim(π)^{−1}χ_π(Hol_C(U_{k+1})). This is the leading word of D2.0(3) at step k+1.
  - Where C runs outside Ω_{k+1}, U_{k+1} is a function of the pending variables. CRE p.266: U_{k+1} "is determined by the sequence of domains {Ω_{k+1},Ω_k,…}, and the corresponding sequence of fields {V_{k+1}|Γ_{k+1},V_k|Γ_k,…}". These variables enter f_{k+1} as outer variables, as in D(k).
  - The composition formulas on the pages read are RG1's single-domain (2.3)–(2.6) and CRE's cube-local (3.6) and (3.17). The identity above rests on CRE p.267's transfer of RG1 Sect. 2 to the multi-domain operators in every case, since 𝐇_k is non-local ((1), accepted as Bałaban's statement).

### (3) Transported form and the first-order term (derived)

- *Transport identity.* Let b_1, …, b_P be the fine bonds of C in order from x₀, U a configuration and X a 𝔤^c-valued function of the bonds. Then
  Hol_C(e^{iX}U) = (∏_{i=1}^{P} exp(i Ad(Γ_i)X(b_i)))·Hol_C(U), with Γ_i := U(b_1)⋯U(b_{i−1}) and the product ordered with i increasing to the right.
  - Proof: e^{iX₁}U₁e^{iX₂}U₂⋯ = e^{iX₁}(U₁e^{iX₂}U₁^{−1})U₁U₂⋯, and induction.
  - Orientation. The b_i are oriented along C. If C traverses a lattice bond b backwards, b_i = b̄ with U(b̄) = U(b)^{−1}, and (e^{iX}U)(b̄) = U(b)^{−1}e^{−iX(b)} = e^{iX(b̄)}U(b̄) with X(b̄) := −Ad(U(b)^{−1})X(b). With this convention the identity holds as stated.
- *The first-order term.* Take X = η𝐇_k(B′), with first-order part ηH₀B′ by (1), and B′ = g_kCA_k + O(C₁(ε₁^{RG2})^{−1}(g_k|A_k|)²) by (1). Then the part of f_k(U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})) linear in A_k is
  iηg_k Σ_i dim(π)^{−1}tr[π_*(Ad(Γ_i)(H₀CA_k)(b_i))·π(Hol_C(U_{k+1}))], with Γ_i the U_{k+1}-transports.
  - It is odd in A_k. It is the leading word's first-order fluctuation of D2.0(5)(d).
  - Size. Cauchy's estimate in t for t ↦ 𝐇_k(tB′), |t| < ε₃/|B′|, and (1.18) give |H₀B′| ≤ 4B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′| in the sup norm. With |g_kCA_k| ≤ C₁g_k|A_k| (Cauchy, (1)) and L^kη = 1, the term is at most c_π·4B₀e^{16κ_s}C₁g_k|A_k|_∞ per step-k bond of C, where |A_k|_∞ is the sup norm. This uses (1.18) at s = 1 for CRE's operators (CRE p.267). With RG2's constants it holds at bonds of C in Ω_k, the scale-k set of VAR (190) ((4), *Two cases*), where (190) has j = k; at bonds in older regions (190) carries L^{k−j}, a D2.6 item. CRE (3.7) prints a consistent sup bound < ε_k/10 for the cube-local functions 𝐇_{k,□} on □^∼.
  - Its locality in A_k is that of (4). In older large-field regions (y ∈ Λ_j, j < k), VAR (190) carries the prefactor (L^jη)^{−1} = L^{k−j}; that is case (B) of (4), whose sizes belong to D2.6 (the margin comparison is D2.2(4)(f), (6)(e), under R-dec).

### (4) Localisation along C (derived from (1))

*Setting.* Let σ_k be a partition of the torus into cubes of side M, as in RG2's example. Every cube Δ ∈ σ_k carries a parameter s(Δ). H(s), G̃(s), H₀(s) are defined by RG2 p.3's rule, and 𝐇(s,B′) by (1.2)–(1.4) with these operators ((1.5)). For σ ⊆ σ_k let Y(σ) := ∪_{Δ∈σ}Δ, with RG2's connectedness (p.4: a common (d−1)-dimensional wall). Since no cube is exempt here, RG2's second case of connectedness, the one involving □̃⁴, does not occur.

- **(a)** 𝐇(1,B′) = 𝐇_k(B′) (p.3). Also 𝐇(0,B′) = 0: every walk term meets a cube, so H(0) = G̃(0) = H₀(0) = 0; then 𝐀₀ = 0 by (1.4), and 𝐇 = 0 by (1.2).
- **(b)** Let s(σ^c) = 0. The argument of RG2 p.4 uses the walk localisation and the multiplication rule of p.3, and the locality of C in (1.3) ("D(A′) = C(A′) on the neighborhood of Y(σ)^c"), of V₀, and of B′ in B. By it, 𝐇(s,B′) vanishes on a neighbourhood of Y(σ)^c, and on a component Y of Y(σ) it depends only on s|_Y, B′|_Y and (𝐔,𝐉)|_Y. Call it 𝐇_Y(s|_Y,B′|_Y).
- **(c)** The proofs of (1.11) and (1.18) use (1.7), |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s}, δ₁M ≥ κ_s, the count of at most 2⁴ cubes met by a short walk (p.5), the contraction bound (1.13), Prop. 4 [15] (p.5, with ε₂ for ε₃), and the conditions on ε₂, ε₃ quoted in (1). None of these uses the exempt cubes, so they hold with all cubes carrying parameters. 𝐇_Y is analytic for |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s} and |B′| < ε₃, and applying (1.18) to B′1_Y gives |𝐇_Y(s,B′)| ≤ 4B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′|_Y, where |B′|_Y is the supremum over Y. These are RG2's single-scale constants; where they apply is the case split of the bounds below.

*Expansion.* Let Φ(s) := Hol_C(e^{iη𝐇(s,B′)}U_{k+1}), as π-matrices at x₀. The torus has finitely many cubes, so the fundamental theorem of calculus in each s(Δ) gives
Φ(1) = Σ_{σ⊆σ_k} ∏_{Δ∈σ}∫₀¹ds(Δ)∂_{s(Δ)}Φ(s)|_{s(σ^c)=0}.
- σ = ∅ gives Hol_C(U_{k+1}), by (a).
- At s(σ^c) = 0, by (b), the factor on a fine bond b of C is e^{iη𝐇_Y(b)}U_{k+1}(b) if b lies in a component Y, and U_{k+1}(b) otherwise. A component that contains no fine bond of C gives zero, since Φ does not depend on its parameters. So only the σ whose components all contain bonds of C remain.
- *Arcs and groups.*
  - For such a component Y, let S(Y) be the set of fine bonds of C in Y. Let I(Y) be the arc of C obtained by removing the longest maximal run of bonds not in S(Y); ties go to the run whose first bond comes first from x₀, and I(Y) = C if S(Y) = C.
  - Components whose arcs share a bond are joined into *groups* G: the connected components of the overlap graph. The arc of a group is I(G) := ∪_{Y∈G}I(Y), and distinct groups have disjoint arcs.
- *Insertions.* For a group G whose arc runs from x_G to x_G′, let
  Y_G := [∏_{Y∈G}∏_{Δ⊂Y}∫₀¹ds(Δ)∂_{s(Δ)}] Hol_{I(G)}(e^{iη𝐇(s,B′)}U_{k+1}) at s(Δ) = 0 for every cube Δ outside the components of G,
  a linear map from the fibre at x_G′ to the fibre at x_G, as a transport under 𝐔^u(b) = u(b_−)𝐔(b)u(b_+)^{−1} (orientation of D2.2(4)(a)). Write #(Y) for the number of cubes of Y and #(G) := Σ_{Y∈G}#(Y).
  - Inside a family, the other components' parameters do not change Y_G: a bond of C in a component Y′ of another group G′ lies in S(Y′) ⊆ I(G′), which is disjoint from I(G), and by (b) the factors on the bonds of I(G) depend only on the parameters of the components that contain them.
- **Result.** Put the groups in cyclic order along C, and let U_{(i)} be the U_{k+1}-transport along C from the end of I(G_i) to the start of I(G_{i+1}). Then
  f_k(U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})) = f_{k+1} + Σ_{𝔊≠∅ compatible} dim(π)^{−1}tr(Y_{G_1}U_{(1)}⋯Y_{G_n}U_{(n)}).
  - A family 𝔊 is *compatible* if (0) each of its components is a connected union of cubes containing a fine bond of C, (i) its groups have disjoint arcs, (ii) its components are pairwise disjoint with no common wall, and (iii) each group is connected by arc overlaps.
  - The map σ ↦ (components of Y(σ)) ↦ 𝔊 is a bijection onto the compatible families. Given 𝔊, σ is the set of cubes of its components, and the components of Y(σ) are exactly these, since they are connected and pairwise wall-disjoint.
  - If one group has arc C, the family consists of that group alone and the term is dim(π)^{−1}tr Y_G.
  - This is the class form of D2.0(3) with Q ≡ 1 (hard-core interactions only).
- **Bounds** (case split).
  - *Two cases.* (A) every component of G lies in Ω_k, where (190) has j = j′ = k and RG2's constants of (c) apply; (B) some component of G meets Ω_kᶜ. A component can meet Ω_kᶜ while C lies in Ω_k, and large-field crossings are the main case of the IT-1 note's D1.1, so (B) is generic.
    - *Why Ω_k is the scale-k set of VAR (190).* (190) holds "for x ∈ Δ(y), … y ∈ Λ_j, y′ ∈ Λ_{j′}" (VAR p.308), with VAR's sets defined on p.277: "Λ_j = Ω_j^{(j)}∖Ω_{j+1}^{(j)}, Ω_{k+1} = ∅, or B^j(Λ_j) = Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}", and 𝔅_k = ∪_{j≤k}Λ_j. So VAR's scale-k set is B^k(Λ_k) = Ω_k. RG1 p.265 applies VAR Sect. G at the step k → k+1, and CRE p.267 runs it on CRE's sequence {Ω_j} ((1)), so VAR's Ω_j are CRE's.
    - VAR's Λ_j are not CRE's small-field sets Λ_j of (3.20), which lie inside Ω_j; V3 L122's "the large-fluctuation region R_1, which with Ω_1 determines the filled region Λ_1" is about CRE's Λ_1. Below, VAR's sets are written Λ_j^{VAR} where they occur.
  - *Case (B).* The identity of (2) and the expansion above hold, since they need only the qualitative analyticity of 𝐇(s,B′) in (s,B′), which is CRE p.267 together with RG2 p.3: (1.5), the walk multiplication rule, and the walk expansion of the propagators ((3.107) [13] for RG1's operators; the multi-domain version through CRE p.267, not on the pages read otherwise). The size of Y_G is not derived here: at points of a scale-j region, j < k, the kernels carry the prefactor (L^jη)^{−1} = L^{k−j} of VAR (190), and neither the per-bond bound of (c) nor (1.13)–(1.16) is on the pages read with k-independent constants. It is a D2.6 item: bound L^{k−j} against the walk decay e^{−δ₀d/8}, with d the distance from supp B′ ⊆ B(Γ_{k+1}). (The comparison of the older-layer deformation with the analyticity margins is made in D2.2(4)(f) and (6)(e) under reading R-dec; the sizes of the case (B) insertions stay with D2.6.)
  - *Constants.* Fix c_π with ‖π(e^{iX})‖ ≤ e^{c_π|X|} for X ∈ 𝔤^c; c_CM bounds the length of C in a cube of side M (D2.0(5)(f)). Fix ε_H < ε₃^{RG2} with c_πc_CM·4B₀e^{16κ_s}ε_H ≤ 1 and ε_H ≤ 1 (the last for D2.6 closure §4.2). ε_H depends on π, M, κ_s, Bałaban's constants, and on C only through c_C. *Amendment (D2.6 closure §4.2):* for the older-layer groups of case (B), ε_H is shrunk to c_πc_CM·O(1)·4B₀e^{16κ_s}ε_H ≤ 1 with R-dec⁺'s O(1) ≥ 1; this implies the condition above, and C₁^{RG2}δ_k ≤ ε_H/2 still holds for g_K small. *Amendment (D2.6 closure §4.2):* also ε_H ≤ C₁^{VAR}ε̄₁/(2c_V), c_V ≥ 1 the comparison constant |V − 1|_{VAR} ≤ c_V|V − 1| (G and π only) (VAR Prop. 9's B-range, (172), p.305: hypothesis |V′ − 1| < C₁^{VAR}ε₁, consequence |B′| < 2C₁^{VAR}ε₁, "for ε₁ sufficiently small"; ε̄₁ is VAR Sect. G's threshold, including (169)'s M′ε̄₁ ≤ a₁, and ε₁ := max(the data regularity, 2c_Vε_H/C₁^{VAR}) ≤ ε̄₁, so |V′ − 1|_{VAR} ≤ c_V(e^{|B′|} − 1) ≤ c_V(e^{ε_H} − 1) < 2c_Vε_H ≤ C₁^{VAR}ε₁ (B′ complex on the polydisc, submultiplicative norm, ε_H ≤ 1); (172)'s hypothesis, not its consequence, is the one checked, and a₁ is not a bound on ε₁), a constant condition fixed before the coupling. The shrunk ε_H replaces ε_H everywhere it occurs (T_Y = ε_H/|B′|_Y, the hypotheses O(1)ε_k ≤ ε_H and C₁^{RG2}δ_k ≤ ε_H/2, c_ev, c_M), not only for case (B).
  - *Conditions used*, all checkable:
    - 4C₂B₀e^{16κ_s}ε₂ ≤ 1 (RG2 p.5), 4C₄^{RG2}B₀²e^{32κ_s}ε₃ ≤ 1 and 2B₀e^{16κ_s}ε₃ ≤ ε₂ (pp.5–6), C₁^{RG2}ε₁^{RG2} ≤ ε₃^{RG2} (RG2 p.7), ε_H < ε₃^{RG2}, δ₁M ≥ κ_s (in D2.1(4) the unmarked C₁ and ε₃ are RG2's (1.20) and p.7 constants, distinct from C₁^{VAR}; C₄ is marked RG2's, since VAR's C₄ (p.293) appears in the D2.6 closure §4.2; the unmarked C₂, ε₂ here are RG2's p.5 constants as well);
    - κ_s ≥ 1 (for the Cauchy radius e^{κ_s} − 1 ≥ e^{κ_s−1}), and κ_s > 3 for decay at complex backgrounds;
    - O(1)ε_k < ε₁^{RG2} (for (1.20)) and O(1)ε_k ≤ ε_H for every k ≤ K, with the O(1) of CRE (3.14) (times C₁^{RG2} of RG2 (1.20) in the second); these are smallness conditions on g_K through ε_k = ε(g_k) and g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K (for a fixed g₀ they restrict K); the general-G form of CRE (2.6) is the coupling item of the D2 gaps block (reading R-cpl);
    - g_k|A_k| ≤ ε_H/C₁^{RG2} on the complex A_k-domain ((4), *The hypothesis*), fixed with D2.2 and D2.4;
    - (𝐔,𝐉) in RG2's domain I.(i)–(iii) (p.6), including a bound on ∇a′ as in CRE (2.39);
    - VAR (172) for the configurations B at which 𝓗(B) is used;
    - 4c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 at complex backgrounds: a condition on the radius of D(k+1)(iii), fixed with D2.4;
    - every torus period at least twice the side of C (for the arc-length step of the proof; at step 1 V3 L124's "every coarse period K_μ … satisfies K_μ ≥ 3(n+2)" gives it for C_n of side nL);
    - |𝐇_k| < a₁ (RG1 p.266, for the analyticity of u_k in 𝐇_k), which follows from (1.18) if 4B₀e^{16κ_s}ε₃ ≤ a₁;
    - VAR (7), |(∂V)(p′) − 1| < ε₁^{VAR} on 𝔅_k, for V^{(k)}. This is part of Bałaban's construction as presented: RG1 p.265 applies VAR Sect. G with V₀ = V^{(k)} ("As in Sect. G [15] we write U_k(V′V^{(k)}) = U_k′(V′)U_{k+1}"), VAR Sect. G assumes "V₀ satisfies the condition (7)", and CRE p.267 transfers this to the multi-domain step. CRE p.268's regularity of U_{k+1} on Ω_{k+1} (D2.0(2)) alone does not give it on 𝔅_k.
  - *The hypothesis |B′|_Y ≤ ε_H.*
    - At real configurations. By CRE (3.11) and (3.15), V_k′ = e^{iB′} on B(Γ_{k+1}), so (3.14) gives |B′| < O(1)ε_k there directly; under O(1)ε_k ≤ ε_H this is the hypothesis. No (1.20) is needed.
    - The range of (1.20). g_k|A_k| < ε₁^{RG2} is part of what CRE p.267 transfers ("we perform the scaling transformation A_k′ = g_kA_k. All the applied formulas are the same as in Sect. 2 [I]"); RG2's domain for those formulas is g_k|B| < ε₁^{RG2}. The bound g_k|A_k| < O(1)ε_k for the scaled variable is not on the pages read: RG1 p.267 makes the change of variables B′ = B − hD̃(B) and then the scaling B = g_kB′ without stating it. It is used only in the second-order bound of (1)'s *Derived* item and in the complex A_k-domain of D2.2, where the radius g_k|A_k| ≤ min(ε₁^{RG2}, ε_H/C₁^{RG2}) is part of the domain's definition; the claim below uses |B′| directly.
    - At complex A_k. (1.20) on its complex domain gives |B′| ≤ C₁g_k|A_k|, so the hypothesis holds for g_k|A_k| ≤ ε_H/C₁^{RG2}. This is a radius condition of the complex A_k-domain, fixed with D2.2 and D2.4. The uniformity of (1.20) in (𝐔,𝐉) is RG2's "Gathering together the above statements", read as including the uniformity sentence just before it.
  - *Claim (case (A)).* Let G be a group of case (A), and suppose |B′|_Y ≤ ε_H for every Y ∈ G. Let the background be 𝐔 = e^{iηa′}U_{k+1} with a′ 𝔤^c-valued, as in CRE (2.39), and let C be a square or a′ = 0. Then
    ‖Y_G‖ ≤ ∏_{Y∈G}(|B′|_Y/ε_H)e^{−(κ_s−2−4c_πM‖a′‖_∞)#(Y)}, where ‖a′‖_∞ is the sup over the bonds of I(G).
    - At real backgrounds, a′ = 0 and the exponent is −(κ_s−2)#(Y). For C a square (the target's C, D2.0(5)), the condition 4c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 gives e^{−(κ_s−3)#(Y)}. For general contours the arc length is not controlled by #(Y), and the complex-background bound is not derived.
  - Proof.
    - Replace B′|_Y by t_YB′|_Y for each Y ∈ G. By (c), the integrand is analytic in the s(Δ) for |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s}, and in the t_Y for |t_Y| ≤ T_Y := ε_H/|B′|_Y, where T_Y ≥ 1.
    - At t_Y = 0, 𝐇_Y = 0 on S(Y) for every s. So the arc product does not depend on s|_Y, and the s|_Y-derivatives vanish. Since this holds for each Y separately, the Schwarz lemma in each t_Y gives the factor ∏_YT_Y^{−1}.
    - Cauchy's estimate on circles of radius e^{κ_s} − 1 ≥ e^{κ_s−1} (κ_s ≥ 1) around s(Δ) ∈ [0,1] gives e^{−(κ_s−1)} per cube.
    - The arc product over the polydisc. Write the factor on a bond b of I(G) as e^{iη𝐇_Y(b)}e^{iηa′(b)}U_{k+1}(b) (no 𝐇-factor on bonds in no component) with U_{k+1}(b) unitary. Its norm is at most exp(c_πη(|𝐇_Y(b)| + |a′(b)|)). Summing, the supremum is at most exp(c_πηΣ_{Y∈G}Σ_{b∈S(Y)}|𝐇_Y(s,t_YB′)(b)|)·exp(c_π‖a′‖_∞·length(I(G))). The first factor is ≤ exp(c_πc_CM·4B₀e^{16κ_s}ε_H·#(G)) ≤ e^{#(G)}, since η#S(Y) is the length of C in Y and is at most c_CM#(Y).
    - Arc length for a square C. I(Y) is the smallest arc of C containing S(Y), and Y is a connected union of #(Y) cubes of side M, so S(Y) has ℓ∞-diameter D ≤ M#(Y) (distances on the torus; every period is at least twice the side of C). If S(Y) lies on one side, it lies in a segment of length ≤ D, so length(I(Y)) ≤ D. If D is less than the side of C, S(Y) meets no two opposite sides, so at most two adjacent sides, and every point of it on one of them is within D of their common corner (the ℓ∞-distance of points on two perpendicular sides at distances a, b from the corner is max(a,b)); so length(I(Y)) ≤ 2D. Otherwise length(I(Y)) ≤ length(C) = 4·side ≤ 4D. Hence length(I(G)) ≤ Σ_{Y∈G}length(I(Y)) ≤ 4M#(G).
    - Multiplying the factors gives the claim.
  - Locality. Y_G depends on B′ and (𝐔,𝐉) on the components of G, and on U_{k+1} along I(G).
  - First order. A group with two or more components has no part linear in B′, since each component's factor vanishes at t_Y = 0. For G = {Y}, the linear part is the s|_Y-derivative of iηΣ_{b∈S(Y)}Hol_{I(Y),<b}(U_{k+1})·π_*((H₀(s)B′)(b))·Hol_{I(Y),≥b}(U_{k+1}), which is odd in B′.

### (5) What D2.1 gives, and what it leaves

- *Gives.* The leading word after the step-(k+1) substitution, exactly: f_{k+1} plus compatible families of local insertions, for every family (cases (A) and (B) of (4)).
  - Each insertion is analytic in A_k and in the background, on RG2's domain I.(i)–(iii); in case (B) by CRE p.267's transfer.
  - An insertion of case (A), all components in Ω_k = B^k(Λ_k^{VAR}), has size ∏(|B′|_Y/ε_H)e^{−(κ_s−2−4c_πM‖a′‖_∞)#(Y)}, for the square C, at real and complex backgrounds.
  - The first-order parts are odd.
  - The constants are independent of k, K, the contour size and the torus, and depend on C only through c_C (D(k+1)(iv)).
  - No bound on the whole word is used.
- *Leaves to D2.2.*
  - (i) The old insertions, words and scalar exponent of 𝒲_k, composed with U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)}). The same expansion applies to each closed product once its factors are G-covariant (D2.5).
  - (ii) The parameters s of (4) and the decoupling of the fluctuation covariance (RG2 Sect. 2; IT-1 note §3i(2)) must be merged into one cluster expansion.
  - (iii) The integration itself.
- *Leaves to D2.6* (by components). The size of the insertions of case (B): groups with a component meeting Ω_kᶜ, the older scale sets B^j(Λ_j^{VAR}) = Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}, j < k, whether or not C runs there. At points of a scale-j region the kernels carry L^{k−j} (VAR (190)), to be bounded against the walk decay e^{−δ₀d/8} from supp B′ ⊆ B(Γ_{k+1}).
  - *Decay distance.* A component Y with B′|_Y = 0 contributes zero: 𝐇_Y(s, 0) = 0 by (4)(c), so the arc product does not depend on s|_Y and the s|_Y-derivatives vanish. So every surviving case-(B) component meets both B(Γ_{k+1}) and Ω_kᶜ, and M#(Y) is at least the ℓ∞-distance between them. This is the decay distance D2.6 bounds L^{k−j} against. Where C runs outside Ω_{k+1}, the identity of (2) rests on CRE p.267's transfer of RG1 Sect. 2.
- *Leaves to D2.4.* The radius condition 4c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 of (4), together with the complex-background factor of the transports U_{(i)} between groups, which is the product form of D1(iii)′ (IT-1 note, e^{P(C)ρ₁} with the perimeter).

## D2.2 The small-field map at step k+1 ((0)–(4) reviewed; status in the gaps block)

### (0) What is needed

D(k) → D(k+1) on the small-field side, for k ≤ N − 1, where no R-operation acts (D2.0(3), R-h). Given 𝒲_k on a labelled term of ρ_k, D2.2 has to produce the dressed loop of step k+1 on each term of ρ_{k+1} and show that it has the class form of D2.0(3) at step k+1. Its parts:
- (2) the numerator identity, which defines 𝒲_{k+1};
- (3) the carrier at step k+1, with D2.6's carrier item;
- (4) the composition of 𝒲_k with the step-(k+1) variables: D2.1 for the leading word, the same expansion for the old insertions once they are G-covariant (D2.5);
- (5) one cluster expansion for the localisation of the observable and for the fluctuation measure;
- (6) the integration, the analogue of V3 Lemmas c5-5-2 and c5-5-3, with its output in class D(k+1).

### (1) Quoted (CRE pp.267–271)

- *The step-(k+1) integrand* at fixed Ω_{k+1}, pp.267–268: χ_{k+1}(Ω_{k+1})∫dV_k|_{Ω_{k+1}ᶜ}δ(V̄_kV_{k+1}^{−1})ζ(Ω_{k+1}ᶜ)·T_kexp[A_k(1/g_k², U_{k+1}) + log g_kd(𝔤)|B(Γ_{k+1})*| + log σ₀|B(Γ_{k+1})*| − log z(L⁴−1)|Γ_{k+1}|]·z^{(k)}∫dA_k|_{Ω_{k+1}}χ_k′(Ω_{k+1})exp[−½⟨A_k, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ + 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k, U_{k+1}, A_k) + {(𝐄_k+𝐑_k+𝐁_k)(U_k(exp i[g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k)]V^{(k)})) − (𝐄_k+𝐑_k+𝐁_k)(U_{k+1}) + A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², U_k(…)) − A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², U_{k+1})}] ((3.15)).
- *(3.16), p.268:* on Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1}, 1 = Σ_{R_{k+1}}χ^{(k)}(R_{k+1}ᶜ)χ^{(k)c}(R_{k+1}), with the tests sup_{b∈(□′^{∼2})^{(k)*}}|A_k(b)| < δ_k, resp. ≥ δ_k, on L²M₂R_{k+1}-cubes. On cubes of R_{k+1}ᶜ the small-field function of (3.3) "is equal to 1, because of the bound on A_k introduced by the second" ((3.17)–(3.19), pp.268–269).
- *(3.20)–(3.21), p.269:* Λ_{k+1} = Ω_{k+1}^{∼−2} ∩ (R′^∼_{k+1})ᶜ and S_{k+1} = (Ω_{k+1}∖Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1}) ∪ R_{k+1}; Λ_{k+1} is modified "in the same way as in Sect. 1" (components of Λ_{k+1}ᶜ inside a cube of size 100LMR_{k+1} are replaced by the smallest rectangular parallelepiped containing them); χ(Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, S_{k+1}) = χ^{(k)}(R_{k+1}ᶜ∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ)χ^{(k)c}(R_{k+1})χ_k′(S_{k+1}).
- *The change of variables, p.269:* on the bonds b ∈ S_{k+1}^{(k)*}, (1.22) with 𝐇^{(k)}(b) for 𝐇_{1,Ax}(b) on (R_{k+1}^{∼2}∩S_{k+1})^{(k)*}, the simpler change elsewhere. It turns χ_k′(S_{k+1}) into the same function with V^{(k)} replaced by V^{(k)}_{Λ_{k+1}ᶜ∩Λ_k}; "The other characteristic functions are unchanged"; the action gains V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}, A_k, 𝐇^{(k)}).
- *(3.23)–(3.25), p.270:* T^{(k+1)} := Σ_{S_{k+1}}∫dV_k|_{Ω_{k+1}ᶜ}δ(V̄_kV_{k+1}^{−1})ζ(Ω_{k+1}ᶜ)z^{(k)}∫dA_k|_{Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ}χ(Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, S_{k+1})exp[−½⟨A_k, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ + ½⟨A_k, C*Δ^{(k)}CC^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1})C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩], T_{k+1} = T^{(k+1)}T_k, and
  C^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1}) is the inverse of the Λ_{k+1}-block of C*Δ^{(k)}C. This is the step-(k+1) form of CRE (1.26), p.253 (checked in the transcription, lines 6–11), where dμ_{C^{(0)}(Λ₁)} is the normalised Gaussian measure of ½⟨Λ₁A, C*Δ₁CΛ₁A⟩, as the completed square ½⟨Λ₁ᶜA, C*Δ₁CC^{(0)}(Λ₁)C*Δ₁CΛ₁ᶜA⟩ there shows.
  (Tρ_k)(V_{k+1}) = Σχ_{k+1}(Ω_{k+1})T_{k+1}exp[A_k(1/g_k², U_{k+1}) + E_0^{(k)}]·z^{(k)}∫dA|_{Λ_{k+1}}χ^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1})exp[−½⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}CA⟩ − ⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ − ½⟨A_k, C*Δ^{(k)}CC^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1})C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ + 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k, (A_k, A)) + {…} + V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}, (A_k, A))].
  "Here A_k denotes the fluctuation field on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, and A denotes this field on Λ_{k+1}."
- *(3.26)–(3.27), p.271:* the logarithm of the last integral is written by interpolation in s (multiplying 𝐑_k″) and t (multiplying 𝐁_k, the A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², ·) terms and V^{(k)}), with ⟨·⟩_{s,t} "the expectation value with respect to the probabilistic measure" Z_{s,t}^{−1}dμ_{C^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1})}(A)χ^{(k)}exp[−⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ + 𝐏^{(k)} + {…}_{s,t} + tV^{(k)}(S_{k+1})]. The print has χ_k and S_k in (3.27), read as χ^{(k)} and S_{k+1} as in the display before it; and the display called "(3.26)" on pp.275, 277, 279 carries no printed number (transcriber's notes, p.271).

### (2) The numerator identity at step k+1 (derived, k ≤ N − 1)

- *Linearity.* From (3.1) to (3.25), every operation of CRE pp.264–270 acts on the integrand of a fixed term in one of seven ways (pp.264–267 read in full for this list):
  - (i) inserting a decomposition of unity: (3.2) for P_{k+1} and (3.3) for Q_{k+1}, p.265, and (3.16) for R_{k+1}, p.268;
  - (ii) a pointwise identity on the support of the characteristic functions: (3.8)–(3.9), p.266, "χ_k(□) = 1 for □ ⊂ Ω_{k+1}^∼" and "χ_Ax((Ω_{k+1}^∼)^{(k)}) = 1"; and (3.17)–(3.19), pp.268–269, the small-field function of (3.3) is 1 on the cubes of R_{k+1}ᶜ;
  - (iii) a change of integration variables on the bonds of S_{k+1} (p.269), a bijection ψ_{k+1} of the A_k-variables there;
  - (iv) regrouping the sums: at fixed Ω_{k+1} the resummation over P_{k+1}, Q_{k+1} into ζ(Ω_{k+1}ᶜ), p.267, which "applies only to the characteristic functions and the gauge fixing terms localized in Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}"; then the reindexing R_{k+1} ↦ (Λ_{k+1}, S_{k+1}) of p.270, a bijection since R_{k+1} = S_{k+1}∖(Ω_{k+1}∖Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1}); also the filling of Λ_{k+1}, a function of (Ω_{k+1}, R_{k+1}), and the pointwise regroupings of factors on pp.267, 269, 270;
  - (v) splitting the A_k-integral into the conditional integral over A = A_k|_{Λ_{k+1}} and the outer integral in T^{(k+1)}, with the block form of the quadratic form ((3.23)–(3.25)): the exponent of (3.23) plus that of the last integral of (3.25) is −½⟨A_k + A, C*Δ^{(k)}C(A_k + A)⟩, the two terms with C^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1}) cancelling;
  - (vi) the change of variables of pp.266–267 on B(Γ_{k+1}): the fluctuation field V_k′ = V_k(V^{(k)})^{−1} ((3.11)), A_k′ = (1/i)log V_k′, the expansion "with respect to A_k′", the linearisation of the δ-functions, their removal "using the operator C", and the scaling A_k′ = g_kA_k. Together these give V_k = e^{iB′(A_k)}V^{(k)} with B′ = g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k), and the Jacobian is part of the first exponential of (3.15) and of 𝐏^{(k)} (reading R-x, its 𝐏^{(k)} half, (iii)(b));
  - (vii) the axial gauge fixing of p.265, "as in (1.5), with V_k, ε_k, g_k instead of U, ε_0, g_0", in (P′^∼_{k+1} ∪ Z′^{∼5}_k)ᶜ, followed by the Faddeev–Popov procedure (p.265: "We get an expansion of the form (1.6)"; the procedure itself is on p.247, not reread here).
- *Each commutes with the insertion.* Multiply the integrand by a bounded function F of the integration variables.
  - (i), (ii), (v): directly; (v) by Fubini on the finite torus.
  - (iv): F does not depend on P_{k+1}, Q_{k+1}, and the p.270 regrouping is a reindexing, so F stays inside each term.
  - (vi): F is defined as composed with it, F = 𝒲_k(U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})).
  - (iii): with F composed with ψ_{k+1}. This rests on three inputs. (a) ψ_{k+1} is a bijection between the supports (below). (b) *Reading R-x (exact changes).* p.269 prints: "The action is changed by terms corresponding to (1.24), and by terms obtained by the first order expansion of the action with respect to the perturbative parts of the changes of variables … We denote this new part of the action by V^{(k)}(S_{k+1},A_k,𝐇^{(k)})" ((1.24) not reread here). R-x reads V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}, A_k, 𝐇^{(k)}) as the exact change of the action under ψ_{k+1}, with its Jacobian (first-order Taylor formula with integral remainder), and 𝐏^{(k)} as the exact remainder of the p.267 expansion in A_k′. The 𝐏^{(k)} half is also what (vi)'s "=" uses. This is the reading under which CRE's "=" in (3.25) holds as printed, and the one the IT-1 note adopts at step 1 (gaps block of the IT-1 note). (c) p.269's "The other characteristic functions are unchanged" and "These changes of variables transform the function χ_k′(S_{k+1}) into the same function, but with V^{(k)} replaced by V^{(k)}_{Λ_{k+1}ᶜ∩Λ_k}", accepted as Bałaban's statements. The identity itself does not depend on (c), since ∫G·F = ∫(G∘ψ)(F∘ψ)|det Dψ|; (c) only identifies the result with CRE's printed (3.25) and (3.27). Whether the (3.16) tests of cubes next to S_{k+1} are unchanged under the "simpler change" is not visible from the text read; no counterexample is known.
  - (vii): the Faddeev–Popov step needs the integrand to be invariant under the gauge transformations u of V_k used there, which are 1 at the centres of the scale-(k+1) blocks and act in (P′^∼_{k+1} ∪ Z′^{∼5}_k)ᶜ ⊂ Λ_k. Without F this is CRE p.250's "invariance of all the expressions determining the density with respect to the gauge transformations of the new field" (quote completed from the transcription). For F, put ũ(x) := u(y) for x ∈ B^k(y). The block averages are covariant (RG1 (0.6), "M({u𝐔_jv}) = uM({𝐔_j})v"; REG p.78, "(Ū^u)^j_b = u(b_−)(Ū^j)_bu^{−1}(b_+) for b ⊂ T^{(j)}") and the action is invariant, so U_k(V_k)^ũ minimises under the constraint of V_k^u, as U_k(V_k^u) does. By the uniqueness of the minimiser in its orbit (Bałaban's; RG1 p.265 states it for V^{(k)}: "there exists the exactly one critical point, which is obtained by taking the critical orbit … and choosing the element of the orbit satisfying the axial gauge conditions"), U_k(V_k^u) = (U_k(V_k)^ũ)^r, with r in REG's subgroup (1.14) (r(y) = 1 for y ∈ 𝔅_k) restoring the gauge condition. (Agreement "up to the residual subgroup of (4)(d), compensated as there" doesn't work: that compensation rotates the slot values and changes F. So r = 1 on the blocks that matter is derived.)
    - *r = 1 where ũ = 1 (derived from REG pp.78–79 and RG1 pp.252–253).* REG (1.19), "∏_{b⊂Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}}R(Ū_0^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},b_−}))Ũ′^n_b = 1" with Ũ′^n = (overline{U′U_0})^n(Ū_0^n)^{−1} (1.20), telescopes to Ū^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}) = Ū_0^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}) for U = U′U_0 (for two bonds b₁, b₂: Ū_{b₁}Ū_{0,b₁}^{−1}·Ū_{0,b₁}Ū_{b₂}Ū_{0,b₂}^{−1}Ū_{0,b₁}^{−1} = Ū_{b₁}Ū_{b₂}(Ū_{0,b₁}Ū_{0,b₂})^{−1}). The absolute form (1.15) is the case U_0 = 1. By the covariance, for U^r the condition reads r(x_{n+1})Ū^n(Γ)r(x_n)^{−1} = Ū_0^n(Γ), so r(x_n) = Ū_0^n(Γ)^{−1}r(x_{n+1})Ū^n(Γ): r is fixed down each chain x_j, x_{j−1}, …, x₀ from r(x_j) = 1. The path Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n} joins x_{n+1} to x_n ∈ B(x_{n+1}), and the averages on its bonds use the contours 𝐆(y,x), "shortest contours with the initial point at y, and the final point at x" for x ∈ B(y) (RG1 p.252), inside the two blocks of each bond (RG1 p.253's M(𝐔,c)). So the conditions on a block B^k(y) involve the configuration on B^k(y) only. On a block where ũ = 1, U_k(V_k)^ũ = U_k(V_k) there, which satisfies them, so r = 1 on that block. This holds for any background U_0 that the transformation u leaves unchanged; U_0 = U_{k+1}(V_{k+1}) is unchanged, since u = 1 at the centres of the scale-(k+1) blocks and the averages are covariant. Which gauge condition the step's representative uses is the representative item (D2.2(4)(d): the hierarchical mean axial gauge, RG1 (0.11)'s variables equal to 1 at every level); the argument covers (1.15), (1.19), and that mean condition alike, each being a covariant condition on the averages inside the block.
    - Hence ũr = 1 on every block outside the gauge-fixing region, and R(ũr)ω = ω below. ũ = 1 on every block outside the gauge-fixing region, in particular near Z_k = Λ_kᶜ, which carries the outer fluctuation fields ω: they live on Ω_{j+1}∩Λ_{j+1}ᶜ ⊆ Λ_kᶜ, j < k, since Λ_k ⊆ Ω_k ⊆ Λ_{k−1} (pp.264–265), and at k = 1 on the bonds I∖D(Λ) and J of V3's exterior (IT-1 note §3d(2)(d)). So R(ũr)ω = ω, and Cov(k) gives F(U_k^{ũr}, ω) = F(U_k, ω) for the closed words, that is F(V_k^u) = F(V_k). CRE p.250 prints the same block-constant extension at step 1, "u_0(x)=v(y) for x ∈ B(y), and the field U_0 constructed in (1.6) transforms by u_0".
- *The bijection ψ_{k+1}.* ψ_{k+1} is a bijection between the supports by (6)(a), the step-(k+1) form of V3 Lemma boundary-bijection (V3 L548). Its inputs are Inputs 1–4 of (6)(a), Input 3 being |Z_b − 1| < C_Zδ_k from (3.13). Its conditions are e^{R_k} ≥ 40·O(1)A₀/A₁ and δ_k ≤ δ_G(C_Z), both uniform in k for g_K small by g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K (coupling item, R-cpl₀). It rests on five inputs of the gaps block, none a reading, as (6)(a) lists them: the (k)* convention, R-g_k, and Input 4's three (the k-independent O(1) of p.269, R-cpl₀, and the bound on S_{k+1} for bonds leaving S_{k+1}).
- The nonlinear step, the treatment of the logarithm of the A-integral from the unnumbered display of p.271 on, concerns the normalisation only.
- *The identity.* Apply (i)–(vii) to each term of (3.1) for the numerator N_k^C = Σχ_kT_k[𝒲_kexp A_k] (D2.0(3)) with F := 𝒲_k(U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)}))∘ψ_{k+1}, B′ = g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k) (D2.1(1)). Then
  T N_k^C = Σχ_{k+1}(Ω_{k+1})T_{k+1}[𝒲_{k+1}exp A_{k+1}], with 𝒲_{k+1} := ⟨F⟩_{1,1},
  where exp A_{k+1} is CRE's integrand of (3.25) (A_{k+1} the logarithm, unchanged) and ⟨·⟩_{1,1} is the expectation of (3.27) at s = t = 1: at s = t = 1, (3.27) is the normalised integrand of the last integral of (3.25), with the constant −½⟨A_k, …A_k⟩ cancelled by the normalisation.
  - Z_{1,1} ≠ 0: the last integral of (3.25) equals z^{(k)}·(Gaussian normalisation)·e^{−½⟨A_k,…A_k⟩}·Z_{1,1}, and CRE takes its logarithm in the unnumbered display of p.271; p.279 states that the resulting terms "satisfy all the conditions of the inductive assumption" (accepted with CRE's construction).
  - F is finite on the support of the characteristic functions: |𝒲_k| ≤ |κ^{(k)}|e^{|𝓔_k|}∏_n(1+ν_n^{(k)}) < ∞ on the finite torus, from the class form of D2.0(3). The identity uses no other bound on F.
- *What 𝒲_{k+1} depends on.* V_{k+1} (through U_{k+1}); the outer variables of T_{k+1}, namely those of T_k, A_k on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ and V_k on Ω_{k+1}ᶜ; and the labels. These are D2.0(3)'s outer variables at step k+1 (CRE (2.20)–(2.21)).
- So D2.2's task is to write ⟨F⟩_{1,1} in the class form of D2.0(3) at step k+1, with D(k+1)(ii)–(v).

### (3) The carrier at step k+1 (D2.6's carrier item)

- *(a) The carrier is CRE's Λ_{k+1}.* The observable F is integrated in A = A_k|_{Λ_{k+1}} under ⟨·⟩_{1,1}; A_k on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ is outer. D2.2 adds no label at step k+1. At step 1, V3's labels Λ = Λ₁^fin refine CRE's Λ₁ (IT-1 note D1.0(P)); whether the integration of (6) needs a refinement of Λ_{k+1} is decided in (6). What (6) can use without one: on Λ_{k+1}^∼ the small-field tests of (3.16), and on all of Ω_{k+1} the bound |A_k′| < O(1)ε_k of CRE (3.14), which gives D2.1's condition |B′| ≤ ε_H under O(1)ε_k ≤ ε_H (D2.1(4)).
- *(b) The step-1 domain Λ = Λ₁^fin at the later steps (every line of CRE pp.255–284 containing Λ_j, Z_j or φ_j read in its context, pp.264–267 in full).*
  - *The data.* With (P), the step-1 data of a labelled term are (Ω₁, Λ₁, Λ, R₁, S₁). Ω₁, Λ₁, R₁, S₁ are CRE's, Λ₁ stays an outer label, and Λ = Λ₁^fin ⊆ Λ₁ is made by V3's retreat and plays CRE's Λ₁ at the later steps, with Z₁ = Λᶜ (IT-1 note §3c(4⁗), §3f(2)).
  - *The printed conditions on the domains, at Λ.* p.255: "if a component of Z_j is contained in a cube of the size 100MR_j (in the L^{−j}-lattice), then it is a rectangular parallelepiped". p.256: Ω_j, Λ_j, "which are determined by he j-th renormalization transformation, but not by the R-operation, are unions of MR_j-cubes in the lattice T_{L^{−j}}. They satisfy also other conditions, e.g., the distance between their boundaries is at least equal to 2MR_j, which will be determined inductively by the operations in the k+1-st step" (two conditions printed, others announced without a list; print "he" for "the"). At Λ: Λ is a union of ℓ-cubes, the MR₁-cubes of T_{L^{−1}} (ℓ = LMR₁ fine, D2.0(1)); Λ ⊆ Λ₁ ⊆ Ω₁^{∼−2}, so the distance condition holds (IT-1 note §3c(4′)); and Λ is filled, since the retreat ends with Fill, the iteration of CRE's p.251 modification until nothing changes (V3 L2407, L2410, L2419). P.251 (transcription): components of Λ₁ᶜ "which are contained in cubes of sizes smaller than 100LMR_1" are replaced "by the smallest rectangular parallelepipeds containing them". Filling can merge components; the label indicator still factorises over the ∗-components of Λᶜ (IT-1 note §3c(4⁗)(d), which treats Fill_1 explicitly).
  - *The uses of Λ_j, j ≤ k, on pp.262–284, each with the property it needs and its status at j = 1:*
    1. *𝐄^{(j)}(Λ_j, U_k, z)* with (2.26)–(2.27): z ∈ Λ_j⁰, the sum over X ∈ 𝐃_j with X ⊂ Λ_j, properties (i)–(iv) of p.259. Used in (2.43) (p.263, Ω ⊂ B^j(Λ_j⁰)), on p.276 ("For the term 𝐄^{(j)} it is in fact restricted to the domain Λ_j"), and in the proof of Theorem 2 and the renormalisation, pp.279–284 (cases (I.3.5) for X ⊂ Λ_j; comparison with "(2.26) for Λ_j = L^{−j}Z^d", p.282). Needs: the representation at Λ, and that the terms with X ⊂ Λ do not depend on Λ, the step-1 form of p.278's remark ("do not depend on Λ_{k+1}, and coincide with the corresponding terms arising from" the whole space). At Λ: built by CRE's step-1 construction at Λ (D1.0(P)(i), IT-1 note §3c(4″)), under R-c (status in the gaps block, bullet *R-c*; re-check row 28).
    2. *The coupling function.* (2.24), p.259: 1/g_{j−1}²(x) = 1/g_j²(x) + β_j(g_{j−1})φ_j(x), φ_j ∈ C_0^∞(Λ_j), φ_j = 1 on Λ_j^{∼−1}. Used on p.263 (Ω = B^j(Λ_j⁰), φ = φ_j), in the terms A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², ·) of (3.15) and the unnumbered display of p.271, in p.279's update by (2.24), and on p.283 ("For z ∈ Λ_j⁰ we have h_zφ_j = h_z"). At Λ: φ₁ ∈ C_0^∞(Λ), φ₁ = 1 on Λ^{∼−1}, by CRE (1.27) at Λ (p.253, checked in the transcription: "We renormalize it in the region Λ₁ … φ₁ ∈ C_0^∞(Λ₁), φ₁ = 1 on Λ₁^{∼−1}"), which (P) applies with Λ (IT-1 note §3c(4), "A₁^Λ given by CRE's (1.26)–(1.30) with Λ₁ replaced by Λ"). h_zφ₁ = h_z for z ∈ Λ⁰ needs only that Λ is a union of MR₁-cubes. The j = 1 piece β₁(g₀)(1 − φ₁) of 1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k² vanishes on Λ^{∼−1}, which contains every later Ω_{k+1} ⊆ Ω₂ ⊆ (Z₁′^{∼6})ᶜ (p.265, (3.5)), as for CRE's Λ₁.
    3. *E_k.* p.262: "The constant E_k (depending on {Ω_j}, {Λ_j} also) is obtained by subtracting one-step vacuum energy expressions, generated in small field regions, from the initial constant E". Λ enters through the step-1 subtraction only, the values at U₁ = 1 in Λ (p.253: "We renormalize it in the region Λ₁ subtracting the values at U₁=1", checked in the transcription). At Λ: by (1.26)–(1.30) at Λ, under (P). Its bound (p.264: "E_k, except the terms logarithmic in coupling constants, has the same bound as above, only with a different constant") holds at Λ: at k = 1, Λ^{(1)} ⊆ Ω₁^{(1)} = Γ₁; for k ≥ 2 the bound is CRE's for every Λ₁ satisfying the printed conditions, under R-c (status in the gaps block, bullet *R-c*; re-check row 28) (D1.0(P)(i)), as in (b)'s conclusion, and Λ satisfies them (second bullet of (b)).
    4. *The 𝐁-bound (2.47)–(2.48), p.264.* Λ_k⁰ enters (2.47) in the terms j = n ("this inequality with |Γ_n∖Λ_k⁰| for j = n"). Since Γ_n ∩ Λ_k = ∅ for n < k (Λ_k ⊆ Ω_k ⊆ Ω_{n+1}), (2.48) keeps it only for n = k. So Λ enters only at k = 1: |𝐁₁| ≤ 2B₁|Ω₁^{(1)}∖Λ⁰| (IT-1 note §3c(4⁗)(a), under R-c).
    5. *The label sums* over {Ω_j}, {Λ_j}, {S_j} in (3.1), (3.15) and on p.270. The operations act term by term ((2)). Each step-1 term has the form (2.18) at k = 1 with Λ (D1.0(P)), and the label sum is IT-1 note §3c(4⁗)(d)'s.
    6. *Z_k = Λ_kᶜ, pp.264–265.* It is the input of Z_k′, "the union of all LMR_{k+1}-cubes intersecting the region Z_k", and of the step-(k+1) restrictions built around Z_k′^{∼4}, …, Z_k′^{∼6}. The construction takes any union of cubes, so at k = 1 it applies to Z₁ = Λᶜ.
    7. *p.269, j = k.* The determining set 𝐁(Λ_{k+1}ᶜ∩Λ_k) ∪ 𝐁_{k+1} of (2.14), with 𝐁_k(Λ_k)|_{Ω_{k+1}ᶜ}, the minimiser U_{k+1,Λ_{k+1}ᶜ∩Λ_k}, V^{(k)}_{Λ_{k+1}ᶜ∩Λ_k}, 𝐇^{(k)}, and with them ψ_{k+1} and V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}). At k = 1 they use Λ through 𝐁₁(Λ)|_{Ω₂ᶜ}, which needs Λ to be a union of M₁-cubes (p.261's construction). This holds: CRE's cube partitions are nested, so each ℓ-cube is a union of M₁-cubes (V3 L548, on pp.245–246).
    8. *(3.38)–(3.39), p.274:* Euclidean covariance of the whole-space construction (Ω_j = Λ_j = T_η); no Λ_j.
    9. *p.277.* The last expression in the curly bracket of (3.27), or (3.15), A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², U_k(…)) − A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², U_{k+1}), is localised by "supp(g_k^{−2}(·)−g_k^{−2}) ⊂ Z_k". By (2.24) and the constant recursion of (I.0.20), cited on p.259 ("Here the coupling constants g_{j−1} are defined as in (I.0.20)"; (I.0.20) is RG1's and not reread here; (1.27) at j = 1), with 1/g_0²(·) = 1/g_0², g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2} = Σ_{j≤k}β_j(g_{j−1})(1 − φ_j); p.259 prints the case j = k as "Thus g_j²(x)=g_j² on the last domain". The j-th summand vanishes on Λ_j^{∼−1}. Λ_k ⊆ Ω_{j+1} ⊆ Λ_j^{∼−1} for j < k (item 2's inclusion, Ω_{j+1} ⊆ (Z_j′^{∼6})ᶜ, p.265). So the support lies in (Λ_k^{∼−1})ᶜ, which is what the localisation uses, since Ω_{k+1} ⊆ (Z_k′^{∼6})ᶜ ⊆ Λ_k^{∼−1} (item 2's inclusion at j = k), so (Λ_k^{∼−1})ᶜ ⊆ Ω_{k+1}ᶜ ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, and the terms are localised away from Λ_{k+1}, as "contribute to the boundary terms only" requires; the print's "⊂ Z_k" is not needed. At j = 1 the inclusion is item 2's Ω₂ ⊆ Λ^{∼−1}.
       - *The inclusion (Z_j′^{∼6})ᶜ ⊆ Λ_j^{∼−1}.* By (2.9), second line at n − m = 1, R_j ≤ L(1+g_{j+1}²β′)^{β₀}R_{j+1} < L²R_{j+1} for g small, and the R's are powers of L, so R_j ≤ LR_{j+1}. So an LMR_{j+1}-cube of p.264's partition contains every MR_j-cube it meets (the partitions are compatible, p.264), and (Λ_j^{∼−1})ᶜ ⊆ Z_j′^{∼1}. The same holds in item 2.
    10. *The inductive form on pp.257–261 at j = 1.* Each use is at Λ by the inductive form at k = 1, through (P) (D1.0(P)(i), IT-1 note §3c(4)):
       - (2.19)–(2.22): T_k(Z_k)'s factorisation and C^{(0)}(Λ), with Z₁ = Λᶜ;
       - (2.25): the subtractions;
       - (2.41)–(2.42): the 𝐁^{(1)} localisation X∩Ω₁ ≠ ∅, X∩(Λᶜ)^∼ ≠ ∅, analyticity on Ũ₁ᶜ and the bound B₀e^{−κd₁(X)};
       - p.262's alternative with d_j(X∖Z_j), which is not used.
    - Pages 271–273, 275 and 278 use only Λ_{k+1}, the new step's own domain; p.274 is item 8 and p.277 item 9; pp.280–281 contain no Λ_j, Z_j or φ_j.
  - *𝐑^{(j)}* (p.260 (2.30)–(2.31): X ⊂ Λ_j^{∼−1}, unions of MR_j-cubes; (2.44), p.263) arise from the R-operations, so they go with D2.7.
  - *Conclusion (small-field objects).* Every use of Λ_j, j ≤ k, on CRE pp.262–284 needs either a geometric property that Λ has (unions of MR₁- and M₁-cubes, the 2MR₁ distance, filling) or a step-1 object that (P) builds at Λ by CRE's own formulas, under R-c (status in the gaps block, bullet *R-c*; re-check row 28). So the small-field part of CRE Sect. 3 accepts Λ at every later step. The large-field objects (D1.0(P)(ii), whose j = 1 factor is R-d″(ii)) and the 𝐑-terms are not covered here (until D2.7).
- *(c) Not covered by (b).* The large-field parts of the later steps (LFR I, LFR II: the bounds of the T-operation and Bałaban's induction pp.384–389, with the geometry of (1.76)), the R-operations and 𝐑^{(j)}, and p.256's announced "other conditions", which are not listed. On pp.262–284 those conditions enter only through the properties listed in (b); their content stays with D2.6 (large fields) and D2.7 (the R-operation). D2.6's part: WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §1 (G1)–(G3), §7.2 (the induction pp.384–389), §9.4 (the "other conditions"). If (6) needs no refinement of Λ_{k+1}, no further retreat occurs, and the enlarged (1.84) of IT-1 note §3c(4⁗) is needed for the j = 1 factor only.

### (4) Composition of 𝒲_k with the step-(k+1) variables, with D2.5 (gauge covariance)

*(a) The covariance property Cov(k) (definition).*
- *Gauge transformations* are G-valued functions u on the sites of the fine lattice. They act on fine configurations by 𝐔^u(b) = u(b_−)𝐔(b)u(b_+)^{−1}, and on the outer fluctuation fields ω (the A_j among D2.0(3)'s outer variables) by R(u), (R(u)A)(b) = u(b_−)A(b)u(b_−)^{−1}, as in CRE (1.17), p.250, and (2.41)(iii), p.261 ("the variables 𝐉, A are transformed by the adjoint representation"). The outer block variables V_j enter only through the background: CRE p.266, U_{k+1} "is determined by the sequence of domains {Ω_{k+1},Ω_k,…}, and the corresponding sequence of fields {V_{k+1}|Γ_{k+1},V_k|Γ_k,…}".
- *Cov(k).* Every insertion of 𝒲_k is the value at 𝐔 = U_k of a function Y_W(𝐔, ω) of a fine configuration 𝐔 and of ω, both restricted to a domain X_W containing the interval of W. For k ≥ 2 it is the value at (𝐔, 𝐉) = (U_k, J_k) of a function Y_W(𝐔, 𝐉, ω) (D2.0(3), amendment of D(k)(iii)), and the law below reads Y_W(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)ω) = π(u(x_W))Y_W(𝐔, 𝐉, ω)π(u(x′_W))^{−1}; Cov(1) has no 𝐉. It is analytic on the domain of D(k)(iii), jointly in 𝐔 and ω (D(k)(iii), *Outer fluctuation fields*), and
  Y_W(𝐔^u, R(u)ω) = π(u(x_W)) Y_W(𝐔, ω) π(u(x′_W))^{−1} for every u,
  where x_W is the initial and x′_W the final endpoint of the interval in the orientation of C, as in V3 (c4-11-5). Under 𝐔^u(b) = u(b_−)𝐔(b)u(b_+)^{−1} a transport along an arc maps the fibre at its final point to the fibre at its initial point, and Y_W is read the same way. This is the matrix analogue of CRE (2.27)(i)–(iii) and (2.41)(i)–(iii); for closed words it reduces to CRE's invariance.
- *Consequence.* Every closed word dim(π)^{−1}tr(Y_{W₁}U_{(1)}⋯Y_{W_m}U_{(m)}), with 𝐔-transports U_{(i)} and the leading word as the case m = 0, is invariant under (𝐔, ω) ↦ (𝐔^u, R(u)ω), and for k ≥ 2 under (𝐔, 𝐉, ω) ↦ (𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)ω): the conjugations cancel at each endpoint, and tr is conjugation invariant.
- *The scalar parts.* At step 1 the interactions and the local terms are numbers on each labelled term (IT-1 note §4(iii): "φ and e_X are numbers on each labelled term"). D(k) is read with the same clause: Q_k(𝔅) and 𝓔_k do not depend on V_k, so they pass through the substitution unchanged. This is an output requirement on (6) at step k+1: the field-dependent parts go into the insertions, and the reference and Schur scalars go to D2.3. It is D2.0(3)'s D(k)(v).

*(b) Cov(1) (from V3 and the IT-1 note).*
- The step-1 insertions are Y_W = X_W·(transport of the interval), with X_W = Υ¹_W + a^Λ_W·id (IT-1 note §4). Their extension to 𝐔 = e^{ia′}U₁ is covariant, with a′ ↦ Ad_u a′, on every labelled term (IT-1 note §3e(2)(b): "The formula of each factor is covariant identically in 𝐔"; §3e(4)).
- The coefficient form is V3 (c4-11-5), c_W(U^g; g(o)hg(o)^{−1}) = c_W(U;h). Burnside (V3 Lemma c5-5-7(a)) turns it into the matrix form: for irreducible π the π(h), h ∈ G, span End(V_π), so tr(Xπ(h)) = 0 for all h forces X = 0. This holds for every compact simple G.
- *The exterior inputs.* On uncovered terms the rows of a group that is not good consult V₀, A_e and a_old (IT-1 note §3d(4)). V₀ consists of fine links (the unintegrated original field on Ω₁ᶜ), and A_e, a_old are fluctuation fields. V3's formulas use them through the same operations as the free variables: plaquette actions, the block mean (covariant by RG1 (0.6), V3 L5031), the chart logarithm (conjugation covariant, V3 L88(ii)), Haar measure, and the cross term s⟨a, H_{ξ,IJ}A_e⟩ of (c4-11-1). With U ↦ U^g on links and R_g on fluctuations, the intertwinings of §c4-11-2 (Q_{U^g}R_g = R^{(1)}_gQ_U, h_{U^g} = R_gh_U(R^{(1)}_g)^{−1}) apply term by term. So Cov(1) holds with ω = (A_e, a_old). V₀, the original field on Ω₁ᶜ, is the background there and is part of 𝐔.
- *From the IT-1 note.* The quoted sentence of IT-1 note §3e(2)(b), the exterior-input claim of §3d(4) and the printed formula Y_W = X_W·transport of §4 are used here as stated there.

*(c) The substitution identity (derived from Cov(k) and reading R-g).* For real A_k on the support, let w be the gauge transformation of R-g, U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})^w = e^{iη𝐇_k(B′)}U_{k+1}. By Cov(k),
F = 𝒲_k(e^{iη𝐇_k(B′)}U_{k+1}, R(w)ω)∘ψ_{k+1}.
- *Factors without ω-dependence:* w drops out, as for the leading word (D2.1(2)). On covered terms this covers every factor.
- *Factors with ω-dependence:* w enters through R(w)ω. Its locality is (d).
- *ψ_{k+1}* changes only the A_k on S_{k+1} ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ (p.269). These are outer variables at step k+1, so ψ_{k+1} does not affect the dependence on the integrated field A = A_k|_{Λ_{k+1}}.
- *Complex A_k and complex backgrounds.* The identity theorem applies jointly, as in D2.1(2). The right side is analytic:
  - in the background by D(k)(iii), and in ω by D(k)(iii)'s *Outer fluctuation fields* clause;
  - in w, since w = u_k^{−1}v₁ ((d), route (ii)) is analytic in 𝐇_k for |𝐇_k| < a₁ (RG1 p.266 for u_k; the gaps block's (d′), Steps 7–8, for v₁, Step 8 for the complex backgrounds, where at a general configuration w_tot = c₀(𝐔)ŵ with the B′-dependent ŵ = û_k^{−1}v̂₁, û_k the u_k of the step at the minimiser at 𝐔's averages, and the B′-independent c₀(𝐔) going to (6)(b7)'s covariant form).
  - R(w)ω stays in the ω-domain. (R(w)ω)(b) = Ad_{w(b_−)}ω(b), so |R(w)ω − ω| ≤ (e^{2c_G|log w|} − 1)|ω| bond by bond, with c_G := sup_{X∈𝔤^c}‖ad_X‖/(2|X|), the operator norm on 𝔤^c of the Hermitian extension of the Ad-invariant norm; this holds for complex w and ω (for a product w = ∏_je^{Z_j}, Z_j ∈ 𝔤^c, with Σ_j|Z_j| for |log w|; for the conjugated factors e^{Z_j} = Ad_{P_j^{−1}}e^{Z_j′} of (d)'s v₁ this is at most Σ_j‖Ad_{P_j^{−1}}‖|Z_j′| with ‖Ad_P‖ ≤ e^{2c_G|Im Z(P)|}, P = e^{iZ}, equal to 1 at real backgrounds and 1 + o(1) at the complex backgrounds, the gaps block's (d′), Step 8; at a general complex configuration w is ŵ = û_k^{−1}v̂₁ with û_k the u_k of the step at the minimiser Ū at 𝐔's averages (w_tot = c₀(𝐔)ŵ, the gaps block's (d′), Step 8), the B′-independent outermost factor c₀(𝐔) being absorbed by the covariant form of the ω-clause, (6)(b7) *G^c-valued u*). So, on the layer of ω_j, dist(R(w)ω, real support) < r^{(k)}_{ω,j} per bond when dist(ω, real support) + (e^{2c_G|log w|} − 1)|ω| < r^{(k)}_{ω,j} (the neighbourhood form of D(k)(iii) with its per-layer radii).
  - At k = 1 the real inputs satisfy |ω| ≤ Cr, C absolute ((6)(e), j = 0), in B-units on the support and r_ω^{(1)} = 1 (D(k)(iii)), so this holds for real ω and |𝐇_1| = O(ε_1). For k ≥ 2 the bound on |ω| on the support, the radii r^{(k)}_{ω,j} per component and the form |log w_tot| ≤ (C_w + 3dL^{j−k})θ^{k−j−1}h_k of |log w_tot| on older layers are derived in (6)(e) (the u_k part, C_w) and in the gaps block's (d′), Steps 7–8 (the v₁ part, 3dL^{j−k}; Step 8 reviewed in rounds 43–44, minors applied and not re-reviewed).

*(d) w is block-local in 𝐇_k (derived from the quoted pages).*
- RG1 p.265 builds w from two transformations. The first takes U_k′(V′) "into the axial gauge Ax_k(T^{(k)},U_{k+1})" and is "equal to 1 on T^{(k)}".
