The second, u_k, changes "the axial gauge into the Landau gauge" and "can be expressed in terms of the configuration 𝐇_k(B′) by the formula (106) in [12] with U_1 = exp iη𝐇_k". Here [12] is "Averaging operations for lattice gauge theories", CMP 98 (RG1 p.299).
- *The representative.*
  - VAR p.305: "U_0=U_k(V_0), and if we fix a gauge condition for U_k(V′V_0)U_0^{−1}, then it is an analytic function of B=1/i log V′". RG1 p.265 takes V_0 = V^{(k)}: (2.3) "V^{(k)}=Ū^k_{k+1}=M^k(U_{k+1})", and "U_k(V′V^{(k)})=U_k′(V′)U_{k+1}". So the reference is U_0 = U_k(V^{(k)}) = U_{k+1}.
  - It does not depend on B′: U_{k+1} is determined by {V_{k+1}|Γ_{k+1}, V_k|Γ_k, …} (CRE p.266), B′ lives on B(Γ_{k+1}) (CRE (3.11)), and Γ_k = Ω_k^{(k)}∖Ω_{k+1}^{(k)} (CRE (2.2) at step k+1) lies outside Ω_{k+1}. Reading RG1's single-domain T^{(k)} as 𝔅_k is CRE p.267's transfer ("only the symbols represent different operators, determined by the sequence {Ω_j}").
  - *Which representative.* There are two representatives in play: ρ_k(V_k), at which D(k)'s identity holds with the slot values of the step-k integration, and the step map's representative of U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)}) in Ax_k(𝔅_k, U_{k+1}). They are related by RG1 p.265's first transformation v₁, which depends on B′, because the configuration being gauge-fixed varies with B′. RG1's reason that v₁ is harmless, "This does not change the expressions in (2.1) because this gauge transformation is equal to 1 on T^{(k)}", covers Bałaban's expressions, not the ω-dependent factors, and (6)(b7)'s rotation covers only B′-independent changes. So the choice of representative is not free.
    - *Route (i): state D(k) at VAR Sect. G's axial configuration.* VAR p.305 (Sect. G, "Analyticity and an Expansion of U_k"; [15] of RG1 is VAR): "We take U_0=U_k(V_0) and we consider the pair of configurations U_k(V′V_0), U_0. We repeat the whole procedure of Sects. A–E for this pair. At first we fix the Landau gauge for the configuration U_k(V′V_0)U_0^{−1}=U_1". VAR p.307: "We make the gauge transformation inverse to the one applied previously, i.e. we transform the configuration in the Landau gauge back to the axial gauge. … we obtain a gauge field configuration in the axial gauge, which we denote also by U_k(V′V_0). It is an analytic function of V′ … and for V′ with values in G it coincides with the minimal configuration constructed in the previous sections". VAR p.305 calls U_k(V) "a (multi-valued) function of the average variables V", so this fixes a branch.
    - Put ρ_k^{ax}(V_k) := this U_k(V′V₀), with V₀ = V^{(k)} = M^k(U_{k+1}) and U₀ = U_k(V₀) = U_{k+1} (RG1 (2.3)). Its axial gauge is that of the pair (U_k(V′V₀), U₀), i.e. Ax_k(𝔅_k, U_{k+1}) (REG (1.19) with reference U_{k+1}; multi-domain by CRE p.267's transfer, as above). REG p.79: "The conditions (1.19) determine uniquely an element in each orbit given by the subgroup (1.14)", so ρ_k^{ax} is the step map's representative, and nothing is left to choose within Ax_k(𝔅_k, U_{k+1}). If D(k)'s identity holds at ρ_k^{ax}, with the slot values read in that frame, then v₁ = 1 and (d) stands as written.
    - *What route (i) would need (not available; route (i) is not used).* It needs the step-(k−1) map to deliver D(k) at ρ_k^{ax}. The sources do not give this. RG1 p.265 transforms U_k′(V′) into Ax_k(T^{(k)}, U_{k+1}) "at first", so its U_k(V′V^{(k)}) is not in that gauge in general. Its reference is fixed by "choosing the element of the orbit satisfying the axial gauge conditions 𝐆(V)=0" (p.265), which is the hierarchical mean axial gauge of the gaps block's (d′), not the axial configuration relative to the next reference. At k = 1, V4's U₁ is "in its axial mean gauge" of CRE p.248 (V4 L2148, L4672), not in Ax_1(T^{(1)}, U₂). Re-representing D(k) afterwards by a gauge transformation that depends on V_k is B′-dependent at step k, which is the problem itself.
    - *Route (ii): derived for every k (reviewed in rounds 40–44; the round-44 minors applied and not re-reviewed; gaps block, representative entry).* Put w := u_k^{−1}v₁ in (c), and prove for v₁ the three properties of (d): block locality, analyticity (ρ_k(V_k) is an explicit analytic gauge transform of e^{iη𝐇_k}U_{k+1}), a B′-independent value at B′ = 0, and a k-uniform size bound, |log v₁(B′)| ≤ 2d(1 + o(1))sup_{B^k(y)}|𝐇_k| ≤ 3d sup_{B^k(y)}|𝐇_k| (d(1 + o(1))sup_{B(y)}|𝐇₁| at k = 1), with the older-layer form 3dL^{j−k}θ^{k−j−1}h_k and the multi-domain chains ((d′) of the gaps block: D(k)'s hierarchical mean axial gauge, RG1 (0.5)–(0.11), CMP 98 (97), (131), and the enclosed holonomies). Then build (e)'s w(s) from both factors; (6)(b7)'s rotation absorbs only the B′-independent part. REG (1.19) with (1.20) telescoped gives v₁ = U_{k+1}(Γ^{(k)})^{−1}ρ̄_k(V_k)(Γ^{(k)}) (round 40, (I1)); the derivation is in the gaps block's representative entry, *The size bound* with (d′).
    - So (c)–(e) hold at D(k)'s representative with w = w_tot = u_k^{−1}v₁ and C_w + 3d in place of C_w (C_w + 2d at k = 1); the frame of the slot values under w_tot is (6)(e)'s *The gauge frame of σ_j*.
  - No change of integration variables is made. (A change of the outer integration variables with CRE p.250's invariance of the rest of the integrand doesn't work: p.250 excepts χ_0′(Ω_1) from the invariance under (1.17), and its invariance of χ_0′ is under block-constant transformations, not REG (1.14)'s subgroup.)
  - At the step map's representative ρ_k^{ax}, U_k′(V′) is already in Ax_k(T^{(k)}, U_{k+1}), which is REG (1.19) with reference U_{k+1}. So the first transformation is 1 there; at D(k)'s representative it is v₁, bounded in the gaps block's (d′). RG1 p.265 prints "U_k′^{u_k^{−1}} = exp iη𝐇_k(B′)", so in R-g's notation w = u_k^{−1}; locality, w₀ = 1, |log w| = |log u_k| and analyticity are those of u_k.
- *u_k on a block B^k(y) ⊂ Ω_k.* CMP 98 (106), p.33, gives u(x) for x ∈ B^k(y), y ∈ Ω^{(k)}, along the chain x = x_0 ∈ B(x_1), …, x_k = y. It is a product over j = k−1, …, 0 of the inverse transports R(U_0(Γ^{(j+1)}_{x_{j+1},x}))^{−1} and of the brackets [(average)^{−1}(R̄^j_{0,x_{j+1}}Ū̄^j_1)(Γ_{x_{j+1},x_j})], with the averages (105) over the blocks B(x_{j+1}).
  - *Locality.* Every contour and every average (105) in this product lies in B^k(y), and "the definition of (Ū̄_1^k)_c, c ⊂ Ω^{(k)}, involves only the gauge fields U_{1,b}, U_{0,b} at bonds b ⊂ B^k(c_−) ∪ B^k(c_+)" (p.34). For an order-j average at a scale-j bond c ⊂ Γ_{x_{j+1},x_j}, j < k, p.31's general remark gives the fine bonds of B^j(c_±) ⊂ B^{j+1}(x_{j+1}) ⊂ B^k(y). So u_k on B^k(y) depends only on (𝐇_k, U_{k+1}) on the bonds inside B^k(y).
  - *u_k = 1 there when 𝐇_k vanishes on those bonds.* Then U_1 = 1 there and R(U_0)1 = 1. The one-step average (89), p.31, of V_1 = 1 is 1, since every logarithm in the point averages vanishes and (V̄₁V₀)_c(V̄₀)_c^{−1} = 1. So Ū̄_1^j = 1 by (90)–(91), every bracket of (106) is 1 by (105), and R(·)1 = 1.
  - *Size.* Proposition 4, p.38: Q_j(U_0, ηA) = (1/i)log Ū̄_1^j is analytic in A, and the display on p.38 gives |(L^jη)^{−1}Q_j(U_0, ηA)| ≤ 2α_1 for |A| < α_1 and small parameters. A contour Γ_{x_{j+1},x_j} in B(x_{j+1}) has O(L) scale-j bonds. So the j-th bracket has |log| ≤ O(L)L^jη sup|𝐇_k| = O(1)L^{j+1−k}sup|𝐇_k|, the real transports R(U_0(·))^{−1} preserve |·|, and the sum over j ≤ k−1 is geometric: |log w| ≤ O(1)sup|𝐇_k|. The same holds at complex 𝐇 with |𝐇| < min(a₁, c₄), by the analyticity of Proposition 4, whose own range is c₄.
  - *Proposition 4's hypotheses.* It needs α₀, α₁ ≤ c₄ for U_0 = U_{k+1}: plaquette regularity of U_{k+1} on every block used, which CRE (3.12) gives on Ω_{k+1}; on Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}, j ≤ k, U_{k+1} "coincides with a U_j-type configuration, it has the same regularity properties and bounds" (CRE p.275); and a size condition on 𝐇. At real B′, on Ω_k, |𝐇| ≤ 4B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′| = O(ε_k). On (f)'s polydisc the size is h_k = O(α_{1,k}) ((6)(e)), so the condition is h_k < min(a₁, c₄), which holds for small couplings.
- *Blocks of the older layers.* On a block B^j(y), y ∈ VAR's Λ_j, j < k (VAR p.277: B^j(Λ_j) = Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}), the axial conditions are REG (1.15), (1.19) with the chain of j steps, and REG p.81 uses CMP 98's formulas on this multi-domain lattice: "In [3] we have determined the gauge transformation u in terms of the configuration U_1. We refer the reader especially to the formulas (87), (99), (97), and (105) of that paper" ([3] is CMP 98, REG p.102). For bonds with both endpoints in Λ_j the left side of REG (1.30) "is equal to (Ū̄_1^j)_b by (92) of [3]"; the bonds that cross ∂Λ_j are treated by (97), (99), (87) of [3]. CMP 98 (97), p.32, is v_j(x) = (R_{0,x}U_1)‾(R̄_{0,x}Ū̄_1)‾⋯(R̄^{j−1}_{0,x}Ū̄_1^{j−1})‾, x ∈ Ω^{(j)}, the product of the averages of orders 0, …, j−1, and (99) is (R_{0,x}U_1)‾^{(j)} = v_j(x). REG states the multi-domain statement ("The rest of this paper is devoted to a proof of this statement", p.81) as Bałaban's result.
  - *R-REG81 (printed; not a reading).* On B^j(y), y ∈ VAR's Λ_j, u is CMP 98's (106) with the chain of j steps, the product (97) truncated at order j. This gives the three properties of the k-blocks with k replaced by j: locality in B^j(y) (each factor of (97) is an average over blocks inside B^j(y)), u = 1 where 𝐇 vanishes on the block, and the size bound with L^{i+1−j} per order i < j. REG p.81 prints the source: "In [3] we have determined the gauge transformation u in terms of the configuration U₁. We refer the reader especially to the formulas (87), (99), (97), and (105) of that paper"; these give u = v_j^{−1} on Λ_j with v_j the j-fold product (97) of the averages (105), and (106) is CMP 98's rewriting of the same by (101)–(104). REG (1.29), "(R̄_0u)^j(y)=1 for y∈Λ_j", is the order-j analogue of CMP 98 (86). The bonds crossing ∂Λ_j are case (B) of D2.1(4) and go to D2.6 with it.
- *Result.* On each block B^j(y), j ≤ k, w is a function W_y of (𝐇_k, U_{k+1}) on the bonds inside the block, analytic in 𝐇_k for |𝐇_k| < a₁ (RG1 p.266), with |log W_y| ≤ O(1)sup|𝐇_k| for |𝐇_k| < min(a₁, c₄), and W_y = 1 when 𝐇_k vanishes on the block (w₀ = 1).

*(e) The joint expansion (derived from (c), (d) and D2.1(4)).*
- *Interpolation.* With the parameters s and 𝐇(s,B′) of D2.1(4), put w(s) := (W_y(𝐇(s,B′), U_{k+1}))_y. For one old family 𝔅, let Φ_𝔅(s) be its closed word evaluated at (e^{iη𝐇(s,B′)}U_{k+1}, R(w(s))ω), and for k ≥ 2 at (e^{iη𝐇(s,B′)}U_{k+1}, 𝐉′(U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}, 𝐇(s,B′)), R(w(s))ω), with 𝐉′ of (6)(b4). Q_k(𝔅) is a constant by (a).
  - Φ_𝔅(1) is the word of (c), since 𝐇(1,B′) = 𝐇_k(B′) (D2.1(4)(a)).
  - Φ_𝔅(0) is the word at (U_{k+1}, ω), since 𝐇(0,B′) = 0 and w₀ = 1 ((d)).
  - W_y is given by (106) for every complex 𝐇 with |𝐇| < a₁ (RG1 p.266), and obeys (d)'s size bound for |𝐇| < min(a₁, c₄), so w(s) is defined for every s, also where 𝐇(s,B′) is not the 𝐇 of a configuration. The identity of (c) is used at s = 1 only.
- *Locality at s(σᶜ) = 0.* 𝐇(s,B′) vanishes on a neighbourhood of Y(σ)ᶜ (D2.1(4)(b)). So w(s) = 1 on every block all of whose bonds lie in that neighbourhood, in particular on every block disjoint from Y(σ). A factor then takes its s = 0 value unless its domain X_W, its interval or, for an ω-dependent factor, a block meeting X_W, meets a component of Y(σ). Blocks are the B^j(y), y ∈ VAR's Λ_j, j ≤ k, of (d).
- *Expansion.* The fundamental theorem of calculus in each s(Δ), as in D2.1(4), gives Φ_𝔅(1) = Σ_σ ∏_{Δ∈σ}∫₀¹ds(Δ)∂_{s(Δ)}Φ_𝔅(s)|_{s(σᶜ)=0}. A component on which Φ_𝔅 does not depend gives zero.
  - Call a component Y *active* for 𝔅 if it contains a fine bond of C, or meets X_W for some W ∈ 𝔅, or meets a block meeting X_W for an ω-dependent W ∈ 𝔅.
  - Only the σ whose components are all active remain.
- *Arcs and new groups.* For an active Y let S′(Y) be the fine bonds of C in Y together with the intervals of the W ∈ 𝔅 for which Y meets X_W (or, ω-dependent W, a block meeting X_W). Let I(Y) be the smallest arc containing S′(Y) by D2.1(4)'s rule, then enlarged at each end to the end of any old interval that it cuts. Old intervals are pairwise disjoint (old compatibility), so the enlargement stops after at most one interval at each end, and every old interval lies inside I(Y) or is disjoint from it.
  - The new groups G are the connected components of the overlap graph of the arcs I(Y), with I(G) := ∪_{Y∈G}I(Y).
  - The new insertion of G is Y_G := [∏_{Y∈G}∏_{Δ⊂Y}∫₀¹ds(Δ)∂_{s(Δ)}] of the ordered product along I(G) of the old insertions with intervals in I(G) and the transports of e^{iη𝐇(s,B′)}U_{k+1} along the pieces of C between them, at s = 0 off the components of G. Its endpoints are on C, at the ends of I(G).
  - An old insertion whose interval lies in no new arc stays, evaluated at (U_{k+1}, ω). Since w₀ = 1, it is an insertion of step k+1 with the same group, domain and interval.
- *Result.* For real A_k on the support,
  F = κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}[f_{k+1} + Σ_{𝔊≠∅}dim(π)^{−1}tr(Y_{G₁}U_{(1)}⋯) + Σ_{𝔅≠∅}Q_k(𝔅)Σ_{𝔊 compatible with 𝔅}dim(π)^{−1}tr(word of (𝔅,𝔊))]∘ψ_{k+1}.
  - The first sum is D2.1(4)'s.
  - In the second, the word of (𝔅,𝔊) is the product along C, in cyclic order, of the new insertions Y_G, the untouched old insertions and the U_{k+1}-transports between them.
  - 𝔊 is *compatible with 𝔅* if each of its components is a connected union of cubes (D2.1(4)'s clause (0)), its components are active for 𝔅 and pairwise disjoint without a common wall, its arcs are disjoint, and each group is connected by arc overlaps.
  - The map σ ↦ 𝔊 is a bijection onto the families compatible with 𝔅, as in D2.1(4).
  - For complex A_k and backgrounds the same formula holds, by (c).
- *Locality.* Y_G depends on B′ and (𝐔,𝐉) on the components of G, on ω on the domains of its old insertions, on U_{k+1} along I(G), and on the blocks of (d) meeting these domains. This is D2.2(0)'s "localisation of the observable", in the form (5) needs.
- *Gauge covariance of the new pieces (closed in (6)(b), not re-reviewed).* B′ transforms by R(v) for gauge transformations v of V_{k+1} (RG1 p.265: "A gauge transformation v of V induces the gauge transformation v of V^{(k)} and the transformation B′ → R(v)B′"). Each factor of Y_G is then covariant in (U_{k+1}, ω) if 𝐇(s,·) is built from covariant operators. That RG2's walk expansion has this property is not printed on the pages read (RG2 pp.3–4). It is checked with Cov(k+1) in (6); (g) lists what it needs.

*(f) The analyticity domain of the old factors (with reading R-m).*
- The bounds of (e), by the Schwarz lemma and Cauchy's estimate as in D2.1(4), need every old factor to be analytic at e^{iη𝐇(s,tB′)}e^{iηa′}U_{k+1} for |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s}, |t_Y| ≤ T_Y and a′ in the radius of D(k+1)(iii), and, for ω-dependent factors, with ω replaced by R(w(s,t))ω. That stays in the ω-domain by (c) with sup|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ min(β,1)α_{1,k}/2 in place of sup|𝐇_k| on Ω_k, and on older layers with (6)(e)'s decaying bound; at k = 1 since α_{1,1}r → 0, and for k ≥ 2 by (6)(e)'s propagation; on Ω₁∖Ω₂ the propagation uses the base points of (6)(e) *The inclusion* (O3, closed: derived at every k, reviewed in rounds 40–41 and 45), at every k. The leading word needs no condition, since a character of a holonomy is entire in the background.
- Write the point as e^{iηA″}X₁ with the real centre X₁ := e^{iη𝐇_k(B′)}U_{k+1} = U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})^w, which lies in the support up to the gauge transformation w. By BCH and (1.18), |A″| ≤ 4B₀e^{16κ_s}(T_Y|B′|_Y + sup|B′|) + |a′| + O(·)², with sup|B′| for the term 𝐇_k(B′), since 𝐇_k is not local.
- D(k)(iii) asks |A″| below the step-k radius of CRE (2.39), and the step-(k+1) radius is smaller:
  - on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, n < k, by β2^{−(k+1−n)}α_{1,n}L^{k−n} (in units of |A″|; the phase margin per fine bond is this times η = L^{−k});
  - on Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} by (β/2)α_{1,k};
  - on Ω_{k+1} by α_{1,k} − α_{1,k+1}/L (the step-(k+1) radius there is that of n = k+1). This is ≥ α_{1,k}/2 when α_{1,k+1} ≤ (L/2)α_{1,k}, and that holds. CRE (2.6), first line, with n = k+1, m = k (reading R-cpl₀), gives g_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)g_k. If g_{k+1} ≥ g_k, then α_{1,k+1}/α_{1,k} ≤ g_{k+1}/g_k ≤ 1+β₀ ≤ L/2 for L ≥ 2(1+β₀), since (log g^{−2})^{q₁} decreases as g grows. If g_{k+1} < g_k, then α_{1,k+1} < α_{1,k}, since g(log g^{−2})^{q₁} increases in g once log g^{−2} > 2q₁.
  So the condition is 4B₀e^{16κ_s}(T_Y|B′|_Y + sup|B′|) < margin, for a′ within the step-(k+1) radius: the margin is already the step-k radius minus the step-(k+1) radius, and |a′| is below the latter. B′ vanishes off B(Γ_{k+1}) and |B′| < O(1)ε_k there at real configurations (CRE (3.11), (3.14), (3.15); D2.1(4), *The hypothesis*). On Ω_k, with T_Y ≥ 1, it holds when 8B₀e^{16κ_s}T_YO(1)ε_k < min(β, 1)α_{1,k}/2.
- *R-m (Bałaban's, accepted).* CRE p.277 states the same requirement for the boundary terms 𝐁_k and asserts it: "we have to use a part of the analyticity domains of terms in 𝐁_k for the function 𝐇_k. This is the reason for putting the powers of 1/2 in the conditions (2.36)–(2.39). The analyticity domains become smaller after each step, but the difference is very small and exponentially decreasing in the number of steps. The localization of the fluctuation field, and the exponential decay of the minimizing function 𝐇_k imply that this difference is still much greater than bounds on this function" (the older layers use the decay, (*The older regions*) below). Accepted as Bałaban's statement. It applies to the old factors because D(k)(iii) is posed on the same domains (D2.0(3), complex part of (2.39)).
  - *What R-m entails.* On Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} the margin (β/2)α_{1,k} is the power-of-1/2 difference, and there is no decay to use there. So R-m's "much greater than bounds on this function" is the inequality 8B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)ε_k < (β/2)α_{1,k}.
  - On Ω_{k+1} R-m does not apply, since the margin α_{1,k} − α_{1,k+1}/L is not a power-of-1/2 difference. But that margin is ≥ α_{1,k}/2 ≥ (β/2)α_{1,k}, as β ≤ 1, so the same inequality closes it.
  - So the expansion of (e) needs, and R-m gives, 8B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)ε_k < min(β,1)α_{1,k}/2. Under (R-m′), q₁ > p₀ (accepted provisionally), the factor 2 and (6)(e)'s complex-data form with the constant 32C₁^{RG2} below both hold for g_K small, so neither is read into R-m; only the excluded case q₁ = p₀ would need them as constant conditions. Without it already Φ_𝔅(0), and the Cauchy estimates in s(Δ) at t_Y = 1, would leave the domain.
  - With ε_k = g_kA₀(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀} (CRE (2.4), p.255) and α_{1,k} = g_kC₁(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁} ((2.28), p.259: "q_0, q_1 are integers greater than 1, C_0, C_1 are sufficiently large positive numbers"), the inequality is q₁ > p₀ for g_K small (g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K for k ≤ K, CRE (2.6) with R-cpl₀), or q₁ = p₀ with C₁/A₀ > 16B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)/min(β,1). (6)(e)'s complex data need the stronger C₁/A₀ > 32C₁^{RG2}B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)/min(β,1) in the equality case. Recorded with R-m: V3 L3968's "p_0 ≥ 5𝔯" (at CRE p.246) and CRE p.259's "C_0, C_1 are sufficiently large positive numbers".
  - No printed comparison of q₁ with p₀ was found: q₁ occurs only on CRE p.259 and LFR II p.368, and p₀ is not fixed on CRE p.255 (search of the CRE, RG1, RG2, LFR I, LFR II and UV3 transcriptions). The entailment is recorded with R-m (gaps block); the stronger (R-m′) of D2.4(12)(f) is accepted by the user provisionally. A printed q₁ < p₀ would contradict R-m and would be reported as a counterexample.
  - *T_Y.* T_Y is taken as large as the margin allows. By the inequality T_Y ≥ 1, and 1/T_Y = max(|B′|_Y/ε_H, 16B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′|_Y/(min(β,1)α_{1,k})) ≤ 1. This is the factor per component in D2.4's count.
- *The older regions.* On Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, n < k, the margin in the units of |A″| is β2^{−(k+1−n)}α_{1,n}L^{k−n} = (β/2)α_{1,n}(L/2)^{k−n}. CRE (2.6), first line, gives g_k ≤ (1+β₀)(k−n)^{1/2}g_n, and (2.7), first line (both with reading R-cpl₀), (log g_k^{−2})^{q₁} ≤ (1+β₀)(log g_n^{−2})^{q₁}; so α_{1,n} ≥ α_{1,k}/((1+β₀)²(k−n)^{1/2}), and the margin is ≥ (β/2)α_{1,k}(L/2)^m/((1+β₀)²√m), m = k−n ≥ 1.
  - The deformation there is not bounded by the Ω_k inequality: the inequality of (f) bounds it on Ω_k only, as D2.1(3), D2.1(4) case (B) and (6)(e) say. (The Ω_k inequality in the sup norm, without decay, doesn't work on the older layers.)
  - The decay closes it. On Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, sup|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ O(1)(Lθ)^{m}h_k with θ := e^{−δ₀RM₁/32}, by VAR (190), PRT2's weighted distance with VAR (1), and, on the polydisc, reading R-dec (labelled; R-adm has no decay form, RG2 (1.18) gives none), as derived in (6)(e) (*Older layers*: the layer of ω_j is Ω_{j+1}∖Ω_{j+2}, so k−j−1 = m). Against the margin it suffices that O(1)(1+β₀)²√m(2θ)^mh_k < (β/2)α_{1,k}. Since h_k ≤ min(β,1)α_{1,k}/2 by R-m's inequality in its complex-data form ((6)(e)), and √m x^m ≤ x for 0 < x ≤ 1/2, m ≥ 1, this holds for all m ≥ 1 once 2θ ≤ 1/2 and 2θ·O(1)(1+β₀)² < 1. That is so for g_K small, since R ~ R_j is large uniformly in j ((6)(e)).
  - So the older-layer margin rests on the decay, as CRE p.277 says (the sentence on the exponential decay of 𝐇_k in R-m's quote above), and on reading R-dec. No condition L ≥ 3 or (1+β₀)² ≤ 3/2 is needed for it. The gradient part follows R-dec and R-adm as in (6)(e); the curvature and 𝐉 parts on the older layers are in (6)(e), *The curvature conditions*, *Older layers*. Case (B) of D2.1(4) goes to D2.6 for the sizes of its insertions; for this margin see (6)(e).
  - The bounds sup|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ min(β,1)α_{1,k}/2 of (f) and |𝐇| ≤ 4B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′| of (d) are on Ω_k.
- *Carrying 𝐉 (k ≥ 2).* For k ≥ 2 the old factors are functions Y_W(𝐔, 𝐉, ω) (D2.0(3), amendment of D(k)(iii)). At the deformed background the 𝐉-argument is carried as Bałaban carries it for 𝐄^{(j)}:
  - *Sources.* RG1 (3.10)–(3.13), p.272, RG1 p.273, RG2 p.2 and PBG (3.42), p.397, are quoted below.
  - RG1 (3.10)–(3.13), p.272. 𝐉′ = D^{ξ*}_{e^{iξ𝐀}𝐔}ξ^{−2}π Im∂e^{iξ𝐀}𝐔 is expanded by (3.11). For 𝐀 in the Landau gauge, the operator D*D is replaced by Δ_𝐔^ξ − P₁ + (lower order, local operator) (p.272: "We use the fact that 𝐇_j satisfies the Landau gauge condition RD*𝐇_j=0"). The old term becomes the function (3.13) of (𝐔, 𝐉, 𝐀), with second argument (L^{j−1}η)³𝐉 + Δ_𝐔^ξ𝐀 − P₁𝐀 + 𝐅₁(𝐔,𝐀). It is analytic for 𝐀 satisfying (3.14): |𝐀|, |P₁𝐀|, |∇_𝐔^ξ𝐀|, |Δ_𝐔^ξ𝐀| < α₂ on X.
  - RG2 p.2 states this structure for every old term: "there exists a function 𝐄(X,𝐔,𝐉,𝐀) analytic and localized to X in 𝐔, 𝐉, 𝐀, such that a given term is obtained substituting a proper nonlocal expression in the place of 𝐀". So Ỹ_W(𝐔, 𝐉, 𝐀, ω) := Y_W at the (3.13)-substituted arguments is local in 𝐀 on X_W's neighbourhood. At 𝐀 = 𝐇_k(B′), which is in the Landau gauge, it is the old factor at the true configuration ((3.11)–(3.12)). At 𝐀 = 0 it is Y_W(𝐔, 𝐉, ω).
  - The s-expansion of (e) substitutes 𝐀 = 𝐇(s, tB′), with (𝐔, 𝐉) in D(k+1)(iii)'s domain. At s ≠ 1, 𝐇(s, tB′) need not be in the Landau gauge, and nothing requires it: Φ_𝔅(s) is a value of the analytic function Ỹ_W, equal to the true word at s = 1 and to the word at (𝐔, 𝐉, ω) at s = 0.
  - The condition. (3.14) for 𝐀 = 𝐇(s, tB′), with α₂ replaced by the 𝐉-margin β2^{−(k+1−n)}α_{0,n}(L^nξ)^{−3} between the step-k and step-(k+1) 𝐉-bounds of CRE (2.36), in the units of (3.13). At s = 1 RG1 p.273 asserts it: "We assume also that ε₁ is so small that 𝐇_j(B(t)) satisfies (3.14) with 1/3α₂ on the right-hand side". For the s-localised 𝐇(s, ·) the |P₁𝐀| and |Δ_𝐔𝐀| bounds are RG2 (1.18)'s "same bound for all admissible norms" with reading R-adm: PBG Theorem 3.1, (3.42), p.397, lists |(Δ_UG′(U)λ)(x)| among its norms, with weight 1 against (L^jη)² for |G′λ|. RG1 p.272 promises such bounds: "for terms in this expansion we will have good bounds, including bounds for the covariant Laplace operator". So R-adm is extended to the Laplacian and P₁ norms (gaps block). The resulting smallness condition on ε_k is of the kind of (f)'s, and its sizes go to D2.4.

*(g) What (4) gives, and what it leaves.*
- *Gives.* F is written exactly, on every labelled term, as f_{k+1} plus compatible families of local insertions at step k+1. These are new groups, built from s-components and the old insertions they touch, together with the untouched old insertions. The scalar parts are unchanged. Covered and uncovered terms are treated alike, and the gauge transformation is made explicit and block-local (w = u_k^{−1}, w₀ = 1). D2.5's composition part, for all compact simple G: Cov(1) holds; Cov(k) ⇒ (c)–(e) for the factors without ω-dependence; for the ω-dependent factors with D(k)(iii)'s ω-clause, the representative of (d), and the bound on |ω| of (c), whose propagation is (6)'s.
- *Leaves to (5).* The localised pieces of (e) and the fluctuation measure (3.27) must go into one cluster expansion, with the s of (e) and the covariance decoupling of RG2 Sect. 2 (IT-1 note §3i(2)).
- *Leaves to (6).*
  - Cov(k+1): the integrated insertions must be values at (𝐔, 𝐉) = (U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}) of covariant functions of (𝐔, 𝐉, ω′), where ω′ is ω together with the slot variable σ_k of A_k on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ. The construction is RG1 p.272 after (3.12), "we replace J_{k+1} by the variable 𝐉, and in the remaining expressions we replace U_{k+1} by the new variable 𝐔", and p.273 for the operators.
  - This needs every ingredient of (6) to be built covariantly from (𝐔, 𝐉): the measure (3.27), the s-localised operators, and the transports. Done in (6)(b).
  - The scalar parts of the output must not depend on V_{k+1} ((a)).
- *Leaves to D2.4.* The sizes of the new insertions: the factor per component 1/T_Y of (f) from the Schwarz lemma, times the old insertion's own weight, against the count. Also the radius condition 4c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 of D2.1(4), now along the arcs I(G) that contain old intervals; after the merging of (6)(d) the constant is 20.
- *Leaves to D2.6.* Case (B): components meeting Ω_kᶜ, the bonds crossing ∂Λ_j in (d), and the sizes of the ω-dependent factors. For these R(w)ω is complex for complex B′, with |R(w)ω − ω| ≤ (e^{2c_G|log w|} − 1)|ω| ((c)); the bound on |ω| on the support at k ≥ 2 is derived in (6)(e). The older-layer margin of (f) rests on (6)(e)'s decaying bound on 𝐇(s,tB′).

### (5) The joint cluster expansion (derived from RG2 (2.1)–(2.12))

*(a) Reduction to tensor expectations.* By (4)(e), F is a finite sum (finite torus) of terms c·T, with c = κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}Q_k(𝔅) a number (D(k)(v)) and T = dim(π)^{−1}tr(X₁U₁X₂U₂⋯X_mU_m) a closed word.
- The X_i are the new insertions Y_G and the untouched old insertions; the U_i are U_{k+1}-transports. Only the Y_G depend on the integrated field A = A_k|_{Λ_{k+1}}, through B′. Let J be the set of these slots. Y_{G_i} depends on A only on D_i := the union of the components of G_i (D2.1(4)(b), (4)(e) *Locality*).
- In matrix entries, T = dim(π)^{−1}Σ_{α,β}∏_i(X_i)_{α_iβ_i}(U_i)_{β_iα_{i+1}} (α_{m+1} := α₁) is multilinear in the X_i. So ⟨T⟩_{1,1} is the contraction, with the A-independent factors, of the tensor expectation ⟨𝒳⟩_{1,1}, 𝒳 := ⊗_{i∈J}X_i, a function of A with values in ⊗_{i∈J}End(V_π). If J = ∅ (the leading word f_{k+1} and the words of untouched old insertions only), ⟨T⟩ = T.
- ⟨𝒳⟩_{1,1} = I(𝒳)/I(1), with I(·) the last integral of CRE (3.25) with the observable inserted, at fixed outer variables ((2)).

*(b) Source of the expansion.* RG2 Sect. 2 (pp.11–14) expands the fluctuation integral of RG1 (2.13) ("Thus we have prepared the integral in (I.2.12), or (I.2.13), to the last step in our construction", p.11), with the action localised as Σ_{Y∈𝐃_k}𝐕_k(Y,B) (RG2 Lemma 2, (1.41)). For the measure of CRE (3.27) Bałaban's own statement is CRE pp.275–279 (read in full in the transcription):
- p.275: "Let us consider now the expectation values in the formulas (3.26), (3.28), (3.37). To construct their cluster expansions we repeat all the considerations of Sect. 3.7 [I]" (so printed), with the multi-scale cover {□}, "which is a cover of Ω_1" (p.275), built from "cubes □, which are unions of 2^d neighboring cubes from π_j", and its partition of unity.
- p.276 localises "the actions defining the measures (3.27), (3.29)": "The action in (3.29) differs only by the additional dependence on the field tA_k, but this was already taken into acount [so printed] in the above considerations. The action in (3.27) contains more expressions": 𝐑_k as 𝐄_k; 𝐁_k by (I.3.6)–(3.7), localised over Y₀ ∈ 𝐃_k meeting Λ_{k+1}. p.277: these terms "can be estimated as in (2.42), but with the additional factor O(ε_k) arising from the bound of the fluctuation field"; the A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², ·) terms are localised in Z_k; V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}) likewise.
- p.277: the normaliser of the last expectation value in (3.37) "is expanded in exactly the same way as in Sect. 7 [I]" and "represented as exp Ẽ^{(k+1)}(Λ_{k+1})", given by (I.7.12) with terms (I.7.13). The numerator with an inserted localised term is (3.44), with one marked domain Z₀; "for n>0 the factors H(Z_i) are the same as in the expansion of the denominator". p.278: the sum over the polymers not meeting Z₀ along a cube or a wall "is exponentiated, and the exponent is represented by (I.7.12), with the additional restriction on the polymers Z_i" ((3.45)–(3.46)).
- p.279: "The other expectation values in (3.26), (3.28) are treated in exactly the same way. We have discussed already the localized expansions for the actions defining the corresponding measures, and for the integrated expressions. Having these expansions we proceed with the cluster expansions, and with the operations described above in (3.44)–(3.47)."
- The first expectation value of the display called (3.26) is ⟨·⟩_{s=1,t}, t ∈ [0,1], under (3.27), whose source term −⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ carries no t ((1)). At t = 1 it is ⟨·⟩_{1,1}. So the localised action of (3.27) at s = t = 1, the exponentiation of Z_{1,1} and the exponentiation of the restricted gases are Bałaban's construction as presented, accepted as such.
- *R-vac (Bałaban's, accepted).* (i) "treated in exactly the same way" (p.279) includes the convergence of the cluster expansions of Z_{1,t} and of the gases restricted to polymers compatible with given domains, for every t ∈ [0,1] and with the source term at full strength. On the supplied pp.276–279 Bałaban's statements on that term are p.276's sentence "The action in (3.29) differs only by the additional dependence on the field tA_k, but this was already taken into acount [so printed] in the above considerations", with the analyticity space (3.43), and p.279's "Similarly, the first expectation value in (3.26) is included into this boundary term, too", which names ⟨·⟩_{s=1,t} under (3.27) directly. The field A_k there can be of size O(ε_k/g_k) (p.267: |A_k′| < O(1)ε_k, A_k′ = g_kA_k). No step uses a locality of the support of C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k. No counterexample is known, so the statement is used as Bałaban's. In (i), "convergence" means absolute convergence. (ii) Bałaban's "sufficiently small" (RG2 (2.12)) is used in the Kotecký–Preiss form Σ_{Z′≁Z}|H(Z′)|e^{a(Z′)+d(Z′)} ≤ a(Z) for every polymer Z and every single cube Z = {□}, with a(Z) = c|Z| and d(Z) = 9 log2·|Z|, |Z| the number of cubes; then Σ_{𝒞≁X}|φ^T(𝒞)|e^{d(𝒞)} ≤ c|X| for every union X of cubes, d(𝒞) := Σ_jd(Z_j) (a cluster ≁ X has a member ≁ {□} for a cube □ of X, since a wall in a union of closed cubes is a face of one of them). The weights d are needed for the bounds of (d) and in (6)(c). (ii) is derived in D2.4(2), (e)–(g), with c = (9/64)e^{−4κ} for κ ≥ κ_vac, and is not a reading of its own. The derivation needs (i) in a bound form, R-vac(i′) of D2.4(2) (Bałaban's, accepted): the activities of the gas of (3.27) with the source term satisfy (2.38) with Bałaban's constants; and the repair R-L3 of D2.4(1) (derived).
- *R-I7 (identified by content): "Sect. 7 [I]" is RG2 Sect. 2.* CRE's [I] is RG1 (CMP 109) "and references therein" (CRE p.285); "references therein" concerns RG1's bibliography (CRE p.244: "The numbers here mean the numbers in the references to [I]"), not RG2. CRE p.285 also lists "[II] Bałaban, T.: Renormalization group approach to lattice gauge field theories. II. Cluster expansions. Commun. Math. Phys. 116, 1–22 (1988)", i.e. RG2, so R-I7 has to explain why CRE cites RG2 material under [I]: the explanation is that CRE cites the preprint of [I], which contained RG2's material as its Sects. 6–7 (the Sect. 7 evidence is CRE's (I.7.n) citations and RG2 pp.1–2, 15; the Bałaban project's key fact "[I] is the preprint B134", BALABAN_CLOSURES.md, is the same identification). Only the content match below is used. CRE's "Sect. 7 [I]" and "(I.7.1)"–"(I.7.13)" (CRE pp.277–278, the only pages of pp.243–285 with these citations) match RG2 Sect. 2 under (7.n) ↔ RG2 (2.n). For "Sect. 6 [I]": (I.6.41) ↔ RG2 (1.41), and (I.6.34) ↔ RG2 (1.34) by the form of the analyticity space (CRE (3.43) has the form of RG2 (1.34), U^c_{k+1}(Y,(1+β)α₀,(1+β)α₁,α₀)). The lemma numbers do not follow the equation rule: "Lemmas 5, 6 [I]" are identified with RG2's lemmas of Sect. 1; Lemma 1 (p.9) and Lemma 2 (p.11) are both read. **Identified by content (the identity itself is not printed):** Lemmas 5, 6 [I] are the two lemmas of RG2 Sect. 1, Lemmas 1 and 2 in order. CRE p.276 says the terms of (I.6.41) satisfy "exactly all the conditions of those lemmas", with the analyticity space (I.6.34) replaced by (3.43); RG2 (1.34) is in the statement of Lemma 1 (p.9), (1.41) in that of Lemma 2 (p.11, analytic "on the space (1.34)"), and RG2 Sect. 1 has no other lemma. Only this content is used, so the numbering is immaterial. (A count from RG1's last lemma (Lemma 4, p.280) doesn't work: the preprint's lemma counter is not printed.) "7 is RG2 Lemma 3" is a consequence of the offset only, and unused. Content: Lemma 1 localises the difference E_k(U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})) − E_k(U_k(V^{(k)})) ((1.33)); Lemma 2 localises 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k, U_{k+1}, B) together with the bracketed terms of (1.41), as V_k = ½⟨Q(Y,B)B,B⟩ + V″_k ((1.42)). The evidence: RG2 (2.1) is the Mayer expansion, (2.11)–(2.13) the exponentiated expansion, (1.41) the localisation of Lemma 2, and RG2 p.15 prints "|(7.14)|" for the bound of (2.14) (transcriber's note there). RG2's introduction: "This expansion is described in Sect. 2". The identity is not printed as such.
- So (c)–(d) run RG2 (2.1)–(2.12) on the measure (3.27) at s = t = 1, with CRE's localised action (pp.276–277) for Σ𝐕_k(Y,B), CRE's cover (pp.275–276) and χ^{(k)} of CRE (3.16), p.268.

*(c) The expansion with marked supports (derived).* Run RG2 (2.1)–(2.11) on I(𝒳):
- (2.1) Mayer expansion of exp Σ_Y𝐕_k(Y): unchanged, with 𝒳 multiplying the integrand.
- (2.2) Y₀ := ∪_{Y∈𝐃}Y ∪ D_J^#. Here D_J^# is the smallest union of cubes of the partition π_k (CRE p.278: "Z̄_0^c is the union of all cubes from π_k, such that they do not intersect Z_0, or intersect it along a two-dimensional edge at most") that contains in its interior every bond on which some Y_{G_i}, i ∈ J, depends. It is unique, being the intersection of all such unions (p.276's cover {□} overlaps and has several sizes, so a smallest union of its cubes need not be unique). This is the convention RG2 p.12 uses for P ("bonds of P have to be contained in the interior of Z_0"). Z₀ is the smallest localisation domain containing Y₀ and P. D_J^# is a union of such cubes, so Z₀ is formed as in RG2. The enlargement from ∪D_i to D_J^# costs a bounded factor per cube in D2.4's count.
  - D2.4(2) counts "π_{k+1}-cubes" of RG2's 𝐃_{k+1}. *CRE p.278's π_k (transcriptions read).* The families are different: π_j is the cubes "of a size M" in the scale of T_{L^{−j}} (RG1 p.257), of side ML^jη in CRE's η-scale (CRE p.275), and a π_{k+1}-cube is "of the size LM in the scale corresponding to the lattice T_η" (RG2 p.13), the union of L⁴ cubes of π_k (RG2 p.19 sums over "(L+2)^4 cubes □′ from π_k"). P.278 uses π_k only to formulate the condition that "the domains Z_i do not intersect Z_0 along a cube, or a wall of a cube" as Z_i ⊂ Z̄_0^c. Z_0, …, Z_n "satisfy all the conditions described in Sect. 7 [I] in connection with the formula (7.11)" (p.277), so under R-I7 they are unions of π_{k+1}-cubes. For such unions the two formulations agree. The π_k-lattice refines the π_{k+1}-lattice (both have side a power of L and nested centres), so Z_i ⊂ Z̄_0^c if and only if every π_k-subcube q of every π_{k+1}-cube Q of Z_i meets Z_0 in a set of dimension ≤ 2. Q ∩ Z_0 is Q or a union of faces of Q, since Q and Z_0 are made of cubes of one lattice, and each face of Q of dimension ≥ 3 is a union of faces of subcubes of the same dimension. So this holds if and only if Q ∩ Z_0 has dimension ≤ 2, which is the condition stated with π_{k+1}-cubes. So the index does not change the condition, and D2.4(2)'s count in π_{k+1}-cubes is the count of RG2's polymers. D_J^#, a union of π_k-cubes, enters only through Z₀, its π_{k+1}-hull with P.
- (2.3) the decomposition of χ on Y₀^{c*}. CRE's χ^{(k)} is a product of sup-tests at δ_k over cubes (p.268, (3.16): χ({sup_{b∈(□′^{∼2})^{(k)*}}|A_k(b)| < δ_k})), and each test is on single-bond values |A_k(b)|, so it is a product of per-bond indicators. (2.3)'s decomposition then applies bond by bond, with CRE's thresholds in place of RG2's ε_1/g_k.
- (2.4)–(2.6) conditioning on Z₀ᶜ: the integrand F(Z₀,B)𝒳 is localised in Z₀ with respect to B, which is all that (2.5) uses. 𝒳 is a function of the conditioned Z₀-variable B, since D_J^# ⊂ Z₀, and the X-variables enter only through Γ_k. So (2.5)–(2.6) hold with 𝒳 inside G.
- (2.8) s-localisation of C^{(k)}(Z₀), (C^{(k)})^{1/2}, Δ_k: unchanged. 𝒳 carries no s-parameter, so H(Z,Z₀) now also contains 𝒳, and its external-field dependence is localised in Z together with the arcs and blocks on which the Y_{G_i} depend ((4)(e) *Locality*).
- (2.9)–(2.10) factorisation. RG2 states (2.10) for the components of Z ("From the definition of H(Z) it is also clear"). With 𝒳 it holds over *linked components*: call two components of Z linked if some D_i meets both (a group with several components gives a disconnected D_i). The Mayer factors, the χ-factors, the conditioned Gaussian integrals at s(σᶜ) = 0 and the factors of 𝒳 all split over linked components, by the same argument. A linked component with no slot of J has the vacuum activity H(Z) of (2.11). One with slot set J(M) ≠ ∅ has a *marked activity* H̃(M) with values in ⊗_{i∈J(M)}End(V_π).
- (2.11) Hence I(𝒳) = Σ_𝔐 (⊗_{M∈𝔐}H̃(M))·Ξ(𝔐), where 𝔐 runs over the families of pairwise compatible (ζ = 1) marked linked polymers whose slot sets partition J, and Ξ(𝔐) := Σ ∏H(Z) over the families of vacuum polymers compatible with each other and with every M ∈ 𝔐. The tensor product is over slots, so the order of the factors does not matter. I(1) = Ξ := Ξ(∅).

*(d) The ratio (Bałaban's exponentiation formula, RG2 (2.12)).* Under R-vac, which gives the smallness that RG2 (2.12) uses ("If the activities H(Z) … are sufficiently small, then the polymer expansion can be exponentiated according to the well-known formula"),
Ξ(𝔐)/Ξ = exp(−Σ_{𝒞≁𝔐}φ^T(𝒞)), φ^T(Z₁,…,Z_n) := (1/n!)ρ^T(Z₁,…,Z_n)H(Z₁)⋯H(Z_n),
the sum over the ordered vacuum clusters 𝒞 = (Z₁,…,Z_n) of which some Z_j is incompatible with some M ∈ 𝔐. This is the same formula, applied to Ξ and to the gas of vacuum polymers compatible with 𝔐, whose cluster sum is that of Ξ minus the clusters incompatible with 𝔐. The restricted gas has the activities H(Z)·1[Z compatible with 𝔐], so the Kotecký–Preiss condition of R-vac(ii) passes to it; for one marked domain this is CRE p.278's restricted exponentiation.
- *Per-polymer form (exact).* For a cluster 𝒞 let S_𝔐(𝒞) := {M ∈ 𝔐 : 𝒞 ≁ M} and s := |S_𝔐(𝒞)|. Put
  - Ψ(M) := Σ_{𝒞≁M}φ^T(𝒞), all clusters incompatible with M;
  - Ψ_≥(T) := Σ_{𝒞≁M for every M∈T}φ^T(𝒞), for T ⊆ 𝔐;
  - Q_{k+1}(𝔐) := exp(Σ_{T⊆𝔐,|T|≥2}(−1)^{|T|}Ψ_≥(T)).
  Then Ξ(𝔐)/Ξ = ∏_{M∈𝔐}e^{−Ψ(M)}·Q_{k+1}(𝔐). Proof: a cluster with s ≥ 1 appears s times in Σ_MΨ(M) and once in Σ_{𝒞≁𝔐}, and Σ_{T⊆S_𝔐(𝒞),|T|≥2}(−1)^{|T|} = (1−1)^s − 1 + s = s − 1. So both sides have the exponent −Σ_{𝒞≁𝔐}φ^T, and Q_{k+1}(𝔐) = exp(Σ_𝒞(s−1)_+φ^T(𝒞)). The rearrangement is absolutely convergent: a cluster lies in 2^s − 1 − s of the Ψ_≥(T) with |T| ≥ 2, and s ≤ 9|supp 𝒞| ≤ 9Σ_j|Z_j|, because the M are pairwise compatible and each M ∈ S_𝔐(𝒞) contains a cube of supp 𝒞 or one of its wall-neighbours, at most 9 per cube in d = 4 (ζ = 0 for a shared wall, RG2 p.14), the cubes and walls being those of π_k ((c)). On the finite torus s ≤ |𝔐| < ∞, so the rearrangement converges absolutely whenever Σ_{𝒞≁𝔐}|φ^T(𝒞)| < ∞, which is R-vac(i): the identity uses R-vac(i) only. R-vac(ii), with 2^s ≤ e^{d(𝒞)}, gives the bounds uniform in 𝔐.
  - Ψ(M) depends on M alone, and Ψ_≥(T) on T alone (with the fields). So Q_{k+1} is a product of genuine |T|-body factors, which is the form (6)(c) needs. (Grouping by "exactly S" doesn't work: it makes the self-energy of M depend on the other members of 𝔐.)
- The self-energy e^{−Ψ(M)} is put into the marked activity: Ĥ(M) := e^{−Ψ(M)}H̃(M).

*(e) Result (k ≤ N, real outer variables independent of the integrated field A).*
⟨𝒳⟩_{1,1} = Σ_𝔐 Q_{k+1}(𝔐)·⊗_{M∈𝔐}Ĥ(M), so ⟨T⟩_{1,1} = Σ_𝔐 Q_{k+1}(𝔐)·dim(π)^{−1}(contraction of ⊗_MĤ(M) with the A-independent factors of T).
- Each M is a new *group* at step k+1, possibly with several intervals of C (one per slot in J(M)); its insertion Ĥ(M) is a tensor over its slots and depends on M alone. The hard core is ζ, the soft interaction is Q_{k+1}. This is the class form of D2.0(3) with these changes, settled in (6):
  - insertions may be multi-interval tensors: (6)(d);
  - Q_{k+1}, the self-energies e^{−Ψ(M)} and the H̃(M) depend on U_{k+1} through the vacuum activities, and on the outer fluctuation field A_k on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, through −⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩, 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k, (A_k, A)) and V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}, (A_k, A)) in (3.27). In the slot variables this is the dependence on σ_k ((6)(b6)), and the reference extraction of (6)(c) is taken at ω′ = 0;
  - the marked polymers have no hard core with the supports of the untouched old insertions, which take no part in the expansion: (6)(d)'s second rule merges each untouched old insertion whose domain is incompatible with a constituent of a group into that group, an exact regrouping.
- |Q_{k+1}(𝔐)| ≤ exp(Σ_𝒞(s−1)_+|φ^T(𝒞)|) ≤ ∏_{M}exp(Σ_{𝒞≁M}|φ^T(𝒞)|) and |Ψ(M)| ≤ Σ_{𝒞≁M}|φ^T(𝒞)|. Under R-vac(ii) at the single-cube test sets the exponent is at most c|M| ((b); D2.4(2)(g); the weaker a(M⁺) ≤ 9c|M| also holds). Whether c|M| ≤ νσ(M)/16, the form of D2.0(3)'s norms, is D2.4's count.
- *Exactness.* (c) is an identity on the finite torus. (d) uses the absolute convergence of the vacuum cluster expansion: Bałaban's construction as presented (CRE pp.275–279), under R-vac(i); R-vac(ii) is used for the bounds only. No bound on 𝒳 is used beyond boundedness on the support (as in (2)).

*(f) What (5) leaves.*
- *To (6):* (i) D(k+1)(v) needs the scalars as numbers given the labels, but Q_{k+1}(𝔐) and e^{−Ψ(M)} are functions of U_{k+1} and of the outer field. Either a reference extraction at a flat background (V3's route at step 1, with the difference as a field-dependent insertion of deviation size), or a revision of D(k)(v) to gauge-invariant local functions, composed at the next step as Bałaban composes 𝐄^{(k+1)}(X). Done in (6)(c) by the extraction. (ii) Multi-interval tensor insertions: either D2.0(3) is widened to tensors over several intervals (the class form allows it if words are contractions), or the slots of one M are merged along C. Done in (6)(d) by merging, with the hard-core rule for untouched old insertions. (iii) Cov(k+1) for Ĥ(M), Ψ_≥ and Q_{k+1}. RG2 Lemma 2's last statement gives the gauge invariance of the potentials 𝐕_k(Y) only (it concerns "the functions 𝐕_k(Y,𝐔,𝐉,B), and both terms in (1.42)"). The invariance of the activities H(Z), and so of φ^T, Ψ, Ψ_≥ and Q_{k+1}, needs in addition the covariance of C^{(k)}(Z₀,s), (C^{(k)})^{1/2}(s), Δ_k(s) and of the cutoffs, and with the outer field the transformation acts on σ_k too. Done in (6)(b1)–(b6). (iv) The extension to complex outer variables, which the class needs for analyticity and Cov, while (e) is stated for real ones: (6)(e).
- *To D2.4:* the sizes of Ĥ(M): the D2.1/D2.2(4) factor per component, times the conditioned Gaussian expectation, against the count; the enlargement of D_J to D_J^# ((c)); c|M| ≤ νσ(M)/16; and that Bałaban's bounds give R-vac(ii) with the weight d(Z) = 9 log2·|Z| (derived in D2.4(2)).
- *To D2.6:* marked polymers that meet Λ_{k+1}ᶜ or older regions, and vacuum polymers that do, where the conditioning domain and the propagators are those of CRE's multi-domain construction. *Closed in WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §2.1–2.4 (species rule; ω′-groups O(b_k) in the collar; boundary-type potentials, reading R-𝐁; the vacuum gas by CRE p.277's Kotecký–Preiss statement, accepted) and §4 (kind (γ)); status in closure §8.*
- *Status of (b)'s inputs:* R-vac Bałaban's, accepted; R-I7 and "Lemmas 5, 6 [I]" identified by content.

### (6) The integration: output in class D(k+1)

*(0) What (6) does.* Input: the identity of (2), 𝒲_{k+1} = ⟨F⟩_{1,1} with F composed with ψ_{k+1}, and (5)(e), ⟨T⟩_{1,1} = Σ_𝔐Q_{k+1}(𝔐)·dim(π)^{−1}(contraction of ⊗_MĤ(M)). Output: the class form of D2.0(3) at step k+1 on every labelled term of ρ_{k+1}. (6) writes the structural parts: (a) the bijection ψ_{k+1} used in (2)(iii); (b) Cov(k+1); (c) the scalar parts, D(k+1)(v); (d) multi-interval insertions; (e) D(k+1)(iii), with the ω-clause. The sizes, D(k+1)(ii), (ii′) and the norms, are D2.4's count and D2.6's.

*(a) The bijection ψ_{k+1} (derived; the step-(k+1) form of V3 Lemma boundary-bijection, V3 L548).*
- *The map.* For b ∈ S_{k+1}^{(k)*}, ψ_b is p.269's change of variables: g_kA_k(b) = log(e^{ig_kA_k^{new}(b)}e^{−iω_b𝐇^{(k)}(b)}), with ω_b := g(A_k^{new}(b)) for b ∈ (R_{k+1}^{∼2}∩S_{k+1})^{(k)*} and ω_b := 1 on the other bonds of S_{k+1}^{(k)*} (p.269: "For bonds in (R_{k+1}^{∼2}∩S_{k+1})^{(k)*} we take the change of variables given by the formula (1.22), with 𝐇^{(k)}(b) instead of 𝐇_{1,Ax}(b). For the remaining bonds we take the simpler change of variables with the function g(A_k′(b)) replaced by 1"). log is the chart logarithm. g is CRE p.252's function at (g_k, δ_k): 0 ≤ g ≤ 1, g = 0 on |A| ≤ (4/3)g_k^{−1}δ_k, g = 1 where |e^{ig_kA} − 1| ≥ (5/3)δ_k. One such g with |Dg| ≤ 6g_k/δ_k is fixed by V3's construction (V3 L542) with (g₀, δ₀) replaced by (g_k, δ_k).
  - *R-g_k (derived).* p.269 says only "(1.22), with 𝐇^{(k)}(b) instead of 𝐇_{1,Ax}(b)", and does not restate g's parameters. (g_k, δ_k) follows from the index shift: the variable is A_k′ = g_kA_k (p.267) and the threshold of the step-k tests is δ_k ((3.16)). It is used only through |Dg| ≤ 6g_k/δ_k and the two support conditions.
- *The star sets.* X^{(k)*} is CRE p.247's X* = X∖{b₀(c) : c ∈ X^{(1)}} at step k+1: the bonds of the scale-k lattice with at least one endpoint in X, minus the bonds b₀(c) for c with at least one endpoint in X^{(k+1)} (p.247: "with at least one end-point belonging to □′^{∼2}, except the bonds b₀(c) for c, such that at least one end-point belongs to (□′^{∼2})^{(1)}"). CRE Sect. 3 writes (k)* in (3.3) and (3.16) without restating the definition. RG1 pp.267–268 nearly force the reading: "We perform the same operation as in the first step, namely using the δ-functions δ(Q̃B′) we eliminate the variables B′(b₀(c)), c ∈ T^{(k+1)}" (quoted as printed), so there is no variable A_k(b₀(c)) for p.269's "each bond variable A_k(b), b ∈ S_{k+1}^{(k)*}" to act on. p.269's statement that the changes "transform the function χ_k′(S_{k+1}) into the same function" presupposes it too: on a bond b₀(c) the test would involve B′(b₀(c)), a function of all the free variables of the corridor, and no bondwise change could act on it in this way. So the convention is forced by the print (status in the gaps block: printed, CRE p.247 "More generally, for a set X").
- *Input 1, the bond form (derived).* For b ∈ S_{k+1}^{(k)*}, V_k(b) = e^{ig_kA_k(b)}V^{(k)}(b).
  - The step-k field is e^{iB′}V^{(k)} on B(Γ_{k+1}), with B′ = g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k) (D2.1(1)).
  - RG1 p.267: "The function hB is equal to 0 everywhere, except the set {b₀(c): c ∈ T^{(k+1)}}". RG1 pp.267–268: "namely using the δ-functions δ(Q̃B′) we eliminate the variables B′(b₀(c)), c ∈ T^{(k+1)}. Denoting the remaining variables by B we have B′ = CB". So (CA_k)(b) = A_k(b) and (hD̃)(b) = 0 off the bonds b₀(c). CRE p.267 transfers this to the multi-domain operators ("exactly the same as … Sect. 2 [I]", D2.1(1)).
  - A bond b of S_{k+1}^{(k)*} is not a b₀(c). If b = b₀(c), b lies in the corridor {b : b_− ∈ B(c_−), b_+ ∈ B(c_+)} (RG1 p.267), so its endpoint in S_{k+1} lies in the block of an endpoint of c. That block lies in S_{k+1}, since the cube partitions are nested (p.265: the "partition is compatible with all the other partitions"; V3 L548 at step 1). So c has an endpoint in S_{k+1}^{(k+1)}, which b ∈ S_{k+1}^{(k)*} excludes.
  - *Which bonds carry A_k.* p.267 integrates dA_k|_{Ω_{k+1}} and dV_k|_{Ω_{k+1}ᶜ} separately. Input 1 and ψ_b are claimed for the bonds of S_{k+1}^{(k)*} that carry an A_k variable; on the others the variable is V_k, which p.269's change ("each bond variable A_k(b)") does not touch.
- *Input 2, the tests (quoted).* For b ∈ S_{k+1}^{(k)*}, let □′(b) ⊂ S_{k+1} be an L²M₂R_{k+1}-cube containing an endpoint of b. It exists: S_{k+1} = (Ω_{k+1}∖Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1}) ∪ R_{k+1} is a union of such cubes, the layer by the nesting and R_{k+1} by (3.16). Then b ⊂ □′(b)^∼ ⊆ □′(b)^{∼2}. On the support, the factor χ_k′(□′(b)) of χ_k′(S_{k+1}) in (3.21) is 1. By (3.3), p.265, that is |V_k(b′)(V^{(k)}_{□′(b)}(b′))^{−1} − 1| < 2δ_k for b′ ∈ (□′(b)^{∼2})^{(k)*}. At b′ = b, with Input 1, this reads |e^{ig_kA_k(b)}Z_b − 1| < 2δ_k, where Z_b := V^{(k)}(b)(V^{(k)}_{□′(b)}(b))^{−1}.
- *Input 3, the size of Z_b (quoted).* (3.13), p.267, is stated for every cube □′ ⊂ Ω_{k+1}: |V^{(k)} − V^{(k)}_{□′}| < (4dL)²(1+β₀)B₃ε_k on □′^∼. Hence |Z_b − 1| < C_Zδ_k, with C_Z := (4dL)²(1+β₀)B₃A₀/A₁, since δ_k = (A₁/A₀)ε_k (p.265; confirmed by (3.19), p.268, "O(1)44d²B₃²(1+β₀)ε_k exp(−R_k) = O(1)44d²B₃²(1+β₀)(A₀/A₁)exp(−R_k)δ_k") and the factors are unitary.
  - At step 1, V3 had |Z_b − 1| < 2δ₀ from (B1). The input here is weaker. C_Z exceeds 2 when A₁/A₀ is small. C_Z depends on d, L, B₃, β₀ and A₀/A₁ only, so it is fixed before the coupling.
- *Input 4, the size of 𝐇^{(k)} (quoted, with the reading of (2)).* p.269: 𝐇^{(k)} is "bounded on the set S_{k+1} by O(1)ε_k exp(−R_k)". So |𝐇^{(k)}(b)| ≤ O(1)(A₀/A₁)e^{−R_k}δ_k ≤ δ_k/40 once e^{R_k} ≥ 40·O(1)A₀/A₁. This holds for small g_k, uniformly in k by the coupling item (g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K), with this O(1) independent of k.
  - Three inputs enter here (none a reading; gaps block). The k-independence of the O(1): Bałaban's "absolute constant O(1)" (RG1 pp.255, 279; RG2 p.8), with the explicit constant of (3.19) and p.269's "with similar bounds". The coupling item g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K, R-cpl₀ of the gaps block (CRE p.255; for simple G derived from Cor. 8.2). And "bounded on the set S_{k+1}" applied to the bonds of S_{k+1}^{(k)*} with an endpoint outside S_{k+1}, as V3 L570 reads p.251 at step 1 (Bałaban's, accepted; re-check row 33).
- *The constants.* r_y := (π/2)(2 + C_Z), r_K := r_y + 1 and r_F := (π/2)(r_K + 1/40). The condition on the coupling is δ_k ≤ δ_G(C_Z) := min(log(3/2)/(√N r_F), r_G/(√N(r_K + 1/40))), with r_G and N as in V3's lemma. It holds for g_K small, since δ_k → 0 uniformly in k (coupling item). At C_Z = 2 these constants are comparable to V3's (r_y = 2π ≈ 6.28 for V3's 6.3).
- *The lemma.* Put 𝒯_b := {A ∈ 𝔤 : h(g_kA) < ϱ_G, |e^{ig_kA}Z_b − 1| < 2δ_k}. Under Inputs 1–4 and δ_k ≤ δ_G(C_Z), V3's statements (a)–(c) hold for every b ∈ S_{k+1}^{(k)*}. So Ψ_{k+1} maps the domain of the new variables bijectively onto that of the old ones, DΨ_{k+1} is invertible there, and the Jacobian is ∏_b|det Dψ_b|. The domain of the new variables is defined as at V3 L567: the preimage of the old domain, with the new variables restricted to ∏_b𝒲_b ("the inclusion ⊆ being the definition").
- *The proof is V3's, with these changes.*
  - (a) Use Input 2 at the actual A_k, and the chart condition from (3.14), p.267: |V_k′ − 1| < 2δ_k + (4dL)²(1+β₀)B₃ε_k = (2 + C_Z)δ_k ≤ r_G/√N, so V_k′ lies in the chart domain and |A_k′(b)| ≤ r_yδ_k < ϱ_G, under δ_k ≤ δ_G(C_Z) (A_k′ = g_kA_k on free bonds, by Input 1). No further coupling condition enters.
  - (b) |e^{iy} − 1| < (2 + C_Z)δ_k, so |y| ≤ r_yδ_k. Take K := {|x| ≤ r_Kδ_k}.
    - K lies in the chart ball and e^{ix}W(x) in the chart domain, since (r_K + 1/40)δ_k ≤ r_G/√N.
    - |F(x)| ≤ r_Fδ_k, and ‖DR‖ ≤ ½ on {|X| ≤ r_Fδ_k}, since e^{√N r_Fδ_k} − 1 ≤ ½. So |F(x) − x| ≤ 2|𝐇^{(k)}(b)| ≤ δ_k/20.
    - Hence T(x) := x − F(x) + y maps K into itself, |T(x)| ≤ (r_y + 1/20)δ_k < r_Kδ_k. Brouwer's theorem gives a fixed point.
  - (c) |e^{ix} − 1| < (2 + C_Z + 1/40)δ_k, so |x| ≤ (π/2)(2 + C_Z + 1/40)δ_k ≤ r_Kδ_k, and both points lie in K.
    - The injectivity estimate |e^{ix} − e^{ix′}| ≤ (6/δ_k)|x − x′|·(δ_k/40) = (3/20)|x − x′| < ½|x − x′| does not involve C_Z.
    - The same holds for the differential.
  - V3's remark that the proof depends on the tolerance of Z_b only through these constants is what is used here.
- *What it closes.* Input (a) of (2)(iii), and the OPEN item "ψ_{k+1} bijection" of the gaps block. The inputs it rests on are the (k)* convention above (printed), R-g_k (derived), and Input 4's three (the k-independent O(1), printed; R-cpl₀; the bound on S_{k+1} for bonds leaving S_{k+1}, Bałaban's); none is a reading. The bijection uses only r_G, ϱ_G = π/√N and the √N comparisons of norms, so it holds for every compact simple G ⊂ U(N) (V3 L557).

*(b) Cov(k+1) (derived; reviewed: correct and sufficient within its scope).*
- *Slot variables.* The outer variables of step k+1 are ω′ := (ω, σ_k). Here σ_k is the old field A_k on the outer bonds of Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, called the *slot variable* of ω_k. After (2)(iii) the integration variables on S_{k+1}^{(k)*} are the new variables A_k^{new}, and the slot variable receives σ_k(b) = ψ_b(A_k^{new}(b); 𝐇^{(k)}(b)), with ψ_b of (a). On the other outer bonds σ_k = A_k. Every insertion is a function of the slot variables; ψ_{k+1} only says which values they receive. So D(k+1)'s analyticity in the outer fields (D(k+1)(iii), *Outer fluctuation fields*) is analyticity in σ, and A_k^{new} is never complexified. g in ψ_b is not analytic in A_k^{new}, and this does not matter.
- *Statement.* Every insertion Ĥ(M) of (5)(e) is the value at (𝐔, 𝐉) = (U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}) of a function Ĥ(M)(𝐔, 𝐉, ω′), analytic jointly (by (e), not by (b)). It transforms slot by slot: Ĥ(M)(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)ω′) = (⊗_{i∈J(M)}π(u(x_i)))Ĥ(M)(𝐔, 𝐉, ω′)(⊗_{i∈J(M)}π(u(x′_i)))^{−1}, with x_i, x′_i the endpoints of the arc of slot i. Q_{k+1}(𝔐) and the self-energies e^{−Ψ(M)} are invariant. Here u is any G-valued gauge transformation of the fine lattice. It acts on a field on the scale-j lattice through its values at the sites of that lattice, (R(u)A)(x,x′) = R(u(x))A(x,x′) (PBG (3.28)). So R(u) acts pointwise and is orthogonal.
  - At real configurations 𝐉 = J_{k+1} = J(U_{k+1}), and J(U^u) = R(u)J(U) (PBG p.395, after (3.30)). So nothing changes there. 𝐉 is a variable for RG1 p.272's reason: bounds for D*D "do not hold", "and this is the reason why we do not formulate second order regularity conditions, as in (1.9) [14], (2) [15], but we replace the expression J_j by the new variable 𝐉".
  - ψ_{k+1}'s covariance is not used and is not claimed; for local u it is not available: 𝐇^{(k)} is built from the minimiser V^{(k)}_{Λ^c∩Λ_k}, which is gauge fixed (VAR p.277, "unique up to gauge transformations"; the axial gauge Ax). CRE p.250 keeps the invariance of the density under gauge transformations of the new field apart from the invariance of the effective action under (1.17), and CRE (3.17)–(3.18) show that the two minimisers differ by u_{k+1,□′} and a small field exp iL^{−1}η𝐇_{k+1,□′}, with 𝐇_{k+1,□′} "bounded by … ≤ 44d²B₃²(1+β₀)exp(−R_k)ε_k on □′^{∼2}" (CRE p.268, transcription read).
- *The functions of (𝐔, 𝐉).* RG1 p.272, after (3.12): "we replace J_{k+1} by the variable 𝐉, and in the remaining expressions we replace U_{k+1} by the new variable 𝐔". RG1 p.273, for the operators: "we replace the configurations U_{k+1}, J_{k+1} occurring in operators there by the configurations 𝐔, 𝐉". So every operator of (4)–(5) that depends on (U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}) is taken at (𝐔, 𝐉). The covariance is checked on these operators in (b1)–(b4).
- *(b1) The operators, and RG2's s-localised operators.*
  - PBG (3.30)–(3.34), pp.395–396, for every gauge transformation u: Δ^η(U^u) = R(u)Δ^η(U)R(u^{−1}), the same for Δ_U^η, G′, R, Δ_a and G; and (Q_j′(U^u)R(u)λ)(y) = R(u(y))(Q_j′(U)λ)(y). For the gauge-field averaging operators Q_j(U): "inspecting the definitions … we can see that the equalities (3.32) hold again" (p.396).
  - Restriction to a set (X ↦ ΩXΩ) and multiplication by a scalar function commute with R(u), since R(u) acts pointwise. So local inverses of these operators on cubes and domains are covariant too.
  - The walk terms. PBG p.412: "Each term in this expansion is a product of factors". Its examples are K(h′_□)G′_□h′_□, h_□C_□h_□ and □̃Q′[G′_{□₀}, h′²_{□₀}]G′_{□₀}Q′*h_□C_□h_□ (pp.412–413), the last with a leading characteristic function. Each factor is built from these local operators, the scalar partitions of unity h_□, h′_□ and characteristic functions; K(·) and C_□ are not defined on pp.412–413 and their definitions are not read here; their covariance is used as Bałaban's (listed in (b8)). So every walk term T_ω satisfies T_ω(𝐔^u) = R(u)_{target}T_ω(𝐔)R(u)^{−1}_{source}, where target and source are the lattices on which T_ω takes and receives values ("act between different scales", p.413). So does every sum Σ_ωc_ωT_ω with scalar coefficients.
    - This is printed for PBG's expansion (3.98) (Theorem 3.9). For the other expansions, of G, G′, H, H₀, G̃, Δ_k, C^{(k)}(Z₀) and G̃₃(x) of RG2 p.13, the factor lists are Bałaban's and are not read here. They are used as Bałaban's, with RG2 p.11, "the operations in this section preserve the gauge invariance", and RG2 p.21, "By Lemma 2, and the transformation properties of the operators in (2.14) with respect to gauge transformations, e.g. see (3.28)–(3.34) [13], the expressions (2.14) are gauge invariant with respect to all G-valued transformations" (listed in (b8)). PBG p.408's "all the results of these theorems are gauge invariant" concerns the bounds of PBG Theorems 3.1–3.3 and is not cited for this.
  - The s-parameters. RG2 p.3: "for a random walk ω localized in X̃_0^5∪X̃_1^5∪…∪X̃_n^5 we take the {Δ_1,…,Δ_m} of all cubes from σ_0 which intersect this localization domain, and we multiply the term in (1.6) corresponding to ω by s(Δ_1)⋯s(Δ_m)"; p.13: "The parameters s(Δ), Δ∈σ_0, are introduced into the operators as before" (both read in the transcription). (C^{(k)})^{1/2} is the integral (2.7) of the resolvents (xI + C*Δ_kC)^{−1} and the powers (C*Δ_kC)^n. The resolvent is expanded by (3.185) of [13], with the added term −½x‖χ*(QA + D̄μ(QA))‖². χ* is "the characteristic function of the set of bonds T_1^{(k)}∖{b₀(c): c ∈ T^{(k+1)}}" (p.13), a scalar multiplier on bonds. Q and D̄ are not identified on p.13; the transcription notes "printed with a bar, D̄; (1.19) prints D̃", and RG2 (1.19) is B′ = g_kCB − hD̃(g_kCB). If they are Q̃ and D̃ of RG1 p.267, they are covariant by the C/D̃ bullet below; their identification and covariance are listed in (b8). μ is defined in [13] and is not read here; its covariance is used as Bałaban's ((b8)).
  - Hence, for every complex s, H(s), G̃(s), H₀(s), C^{(k)}(Z₀, s), (C^{(k)})^{1/2}(s) and Δ_k(s) are covariant, and Γ_k(Z₀, s), a scalar, is invariant. This is what RG2 p.21 calls "the transformation properties of the operators in (2.14) with respect to gauge transformations, e.g. see (3.28)–(3.34) [13]". It closes the OPEN item "covariance of RG2's s-localised operators".
  - *The operator C and B′.* RG1 p.268: "C is the operator determined by the configuration V^{(k)}". V^{(k)} = M^k(𝐔) transforms by u at the scale-k sites: M({uU_jv}) = uM({U_j})v for unitary site gauges with no smallness restriction (V3 L5031; V3 L84–92, Lemma (ii)). Q̃ is the linear part of the block constraint at V^{(k)}, and h is "an inverse of a coefficient at the variable B′(b₀(c)) in (Q̃B′)(c)" (RG1 p.267).
    - Q̃ and h (one line each). Differentiating the covariance of M at e^{iB′}V gives Q̃(V^u)R(u) = Ad(u(c_−))Q̃(V), with Q̃ based at c_− as in V3 L88(ii). Hence h_{V^u}(c) = Ad(u(b₀(c)_−))h_V(c)Ad(u(c_−))^{−1}.
    - C is fixed by Q̃CB = 0 with (CB)(b) = B(b) on the free bonds ((a), Input 1). b₀(c) enters only the constraint of c (V3 L84 (i)), and its coefficient there is invertible (RG1 p.267: "h is uniquely defined by these conditions"). R(u) preserves the free bonds, so R(u)C(𝐔)R(u^{−1}) satisfies the same two conditions at 𝐔^u, and uniqueness gives C(𝐔^u) = R(u)C(𝐔)R(u^{−1}).
    - D̃. Its equation is LQ̃B′ + C̃(B′) = LQ̃B (RG1 p.267), with C̃ the nonlinear part of the block constraint. C̃ is covariant as Q̃ is, so R(u)D̃ solves the equation at (V^u, R(u)B). The set in which RG1 p.267's "exactly one solution" holds is a ball in the sup norm of the pointwise matrix norm. RG1 p.267 proves it "following the proofs in the above mentioned papers", which are "Sect. C [15], Sect. E [14]" (p.267; [15] is VAR, [14] is REG, RG1 p.299). In VAR, (51), p.285, poses the fixed-point problem on |A′| < ε₃(L^jη)^{−1} on Ω_j, X = 0 on Λ₀ and |X| < ε₃/B₀ on 𝔅_k, and p.286 finds "exactly one fixed point". Every unitarily invariant matrix norm (the operator norm, or the tr XY norm of PBG pp.391–392) has |uXu^{−1}| = |X| for u ∈ U(N), also for complex X. So the ball is invariant, the transformed fixed-point map is R(u)TR(u)^{−1}, and uniqueness gives D̃(V^u, R(u)B) = R(u)D̃(V, B). The same follows from the power-series recursion, whose terms (RG1 p.267: "D̃(B) has an expansion beginning with quadratic terms, and D̃^{(2)}(B)=C̃^{(2)}(B)") are covariant term by term. The data domain is invariant: RG1 p.263, "The spaces U_j^c(X,α_0,α_1) are, by the definition, gauge invariant also"; RG1 p.276's "so that the configurations after the transformations belong to proper spaces also" is a proviso on G^c-valued u and is not needed for G-valued u.
    - So B′(𝐔^u, R(u)A_k) = R(u)B′(𝐔, A_k).
    - Δ^{(k)}: RG1 (2.11) is ⟨H₁hC̃^{(2)}(B′), J⟩ − ½𝐆^{(2)}(B′) − ½⟨H₁B′, Δ₁H₁B′⟩ = −½⟨B′, Δ^{(k)}B′⟩, "see the definition (3.156) [13] with the δ-function gauge fixing term replaced by the exponential one". The first and third terms are invariant by PBG (3.30) (for J), by the average (for h and C̃^{(2)}) and by the walk terms above (for H₁ and Δ₁). 𝐆 (CRE (1.7)) is a gauge-fixing term and is not gauge invariant, so no invariance of 𝐆^{(2)} is claimed. What is used is the covariance of Δ^{(k)}(𝐔) itself in the transported form of [13] (3.156), which is RG2 p.21's "transformation properties of the operators in (2.14) with respect to gauge transformations"; it is used as Bałaban's ((b8)). So C^{(k)}(𝐔) = (C*Δ^{(k)}C)^{−1}, its restrictions and C^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1}) are covariant.
- *(b2) 𝐇(s,·) (derived).*
  - RG2 (1.2)–(1.4): 𝐇_k(B′) = H₀B′ + 𝐀₀ − HD(H₀B′ + 𝐀₀), with D(A′) = C(A′ − HD(A′)) and 𝐀₀ + G̃(δV/δA′)(H₀B′ + 𝐀₀) = 0. These equations "are determined by local, analytic functions, and nonlocal propagators H, H₀, G̃" (RG2 p.2).
  - The local function C of (1.3) is the nonlinear part of the logarithmic average: VAR p.285, "Q_j(ηA) = L^jηQ_jA + C_j(L^jηA), |C_j(L^jηA)| ≤ C₂(L^jη)²|A|²", with C(A′) = C_j(L^jηA′) on Λ_j (VAR p.286). Q_j(U, ηA) = (1/i)log of the average (CMP98 Proposition 4, (4)(d)), and its linear part Q_j, are covariant by V3 L5031 and the conjugation equivariance of the chart logarithm (V3 L88(ii)). So C is covariant.
  - V is VAR (80), built from J, Δ_π, H, D and the higher-order part V₀ of the action. J is covariant by PBG (3.30). Δ_π is PBG (3.119), p.419: ⟨A, Δ_πA⟩ = ⟨A − DG′RD*A, Δ(A − DG′RD*A)⟩, covariant by PBG (3.30)–(3.34). The action is invariant by PBG (3.29). V₀ is the sum of the homogeneous orders ≥ 3 in A of the invariant action, and PBG p.395's expansion ("expanding both sides of the above equality in A we get a sequence of equalities between homogeneous polynomials of the same order") gives the invariance of each order, hence of V₀. So V(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉; R(u)A′) = V(𝐔, 𝐉; A′), and δV/δA′ is covariant.
  - *Uniqueness on invariant balls.* RG2 (1.13)–(1.14) solve (1.3) by contraction on {X : |X| < 4C₂ε₂²} for |A′| < ε₂. RG2 (1.15)–(1.16) solve (1.4) on balls in max{|𝐀|, |∇𝐀|} for |B′| < ε₃. These are balls in sup norms of unitarily invariant pointwise norms, and |∇_{𝐔^u}R(u)𝐀| = |R(u)∇_𝐔𝐀| = |∇_𝐔𝐀| since ∇_{𝐔^u}R(u) = R(u)∇_𝐔. So the balls are invariant under R(u), also for complex 𝐀, and so is the data domain (RG1 p.263, the spaces U_j^c are "by the definition, gauge invariant").
  - With (b1) at the same s, R(u)D and R(u)𝐀₀ solve the equations at (𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)B′), and they lie in the invariant balls. The transformed fixed-point maps are R(u)TR(u)^{−1}, so uniqueness gives 𝐇(s; 𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)B′) = R(u)𝐇(s; 𝐔, 𝐉, B′) for every s in RG2's domain. At s = 1 this is the covariance that RG1 p.276 uses ("in the function 𝐇_k(B′) the variables 𝐉, B are replaced by R(u_{k+1}^{−1})R(v^{−1})𝐉, R(u_{k+1}^{−1})R(v^{−1})B"); p.276 asserts the invariance of (3.25)–(3.26), not this covariance by itself.
- *(b3) w(s) (derived from CMP98 (85)–(106)).* W_y(R(u)𝐇, 𝐔^u) = u·W_y(𝐇, 𝐔)·u^{−1} pointwise, for every complex 𝐇 with |𝐇| < a₁.
  - Here U₀ = 𝐔 and U₁ = e^{iη𝐇}, so U₁(b) ↦ u(b_−)U₁(b)u(b_−)^{−1}: U₁ is based at the initial point of each bond.
  - The one-step average (89), p.31, (R̄_{0,c_−}V₁)^{−1}(V̄₁V₀)_c(V̄₀)_c^{−1}R̄_{0,c}(R̄_{0,c_+}V₁), is based at c_−. Under (V₀, V₁) ↦ (V₀^u, u(·_−)V₁u(·_−)^{−1}), (V̄₁V₀)_c(V̄₀)_c^{−1} is conjugated by u(c_−), since both averages are covariant (V3 L5031; V3 L84–92, Lemma (ii)). The point averages R̄_{0,x}V₁, averages over a block of the quantities transported to x ((85); (105) for the logarithmic mean), are conjugated by u(x), since the chart logarithm and exp are conjugation equivariant. The middle transport R̄_{0,c} is the adjoint action of (V̄₀)_c, and (V̄₀)_c ↦ u(c_−)(V̄₀)_cu(c_+)^{−1}, so it carries conjugation by u(c_+) of R̄_{0,c_+}V₁ to conjugation by u(c_−). So all four factors of (89) are conjugated by u(c_−).
  - By induction through (90)–(91), with V₀ = Ū₀^j covariant, every Ū̄₁^j is based at its initial point in the same way.
  - In (106) each bracket [(…)^{−1}(R̄^j_{0,x_{j+1}}Ū̄^j_1)(Γ_{x_{j+1},x_j})] is conjugated by u(x_{j+1}). R(U₀^u(Γ_{x_{j+1},x}))^{−1} carries conjugation by u(x_{j+1}) to conjugation by u(x). CMP98's definition of R is not read here; the property used is printed on PBG p.395 for the same notation, R(U^u(Γ_{y,x})) = R(u(y))R(U(Γ_{y,x}))R(u^{−1}(x)). So each factor of (106), and the product, is conjugated by u(x).
  - The same holds at the block centres, where CMP98 (87) gives the value w(y) = (R̄_{0,y}U₁)^{−1} (CMP98 writes u(y); renamed here to keep it apart from the gauge transformation u). It is a point average at y, so it is conjugated by u(y). So w need not be 1 there, and no such property is used.
  - *Scope.* (b3) covers the blocks on which (106), or its j-step form of (4)(d), gives w. The bonds crossing ∂Λ_j (VAR's Λ_j) are case (B) of D2.1(4); their covariance goes to D2.6 with them.
- *(b4) The new insertions before integration.* By (4)(e), Y_G is the ordered product along I(G) of old insertions at (e^{iη𝐇(s,B′)}𝐔, 𝐉′, R(w(s))ω) and of transports of e^{iη𝐇(s,B′)}𝐔, differentiated in s and taken at s = 0 off G. Here 𝐇(s, B′) = 𝐇(s; 𝐔, 𝐉, B′), and for k ≥ 2 𝐉′ = 𝐉′(𝐔, 𝐉, 𝐀) at 𝐀 = 𝐇(s, B′) is (4)(f)'s second argument of (3.13); at k = 1 there is no 𝐉′.
  - *The covariance of 𝐉′ (derived).*
    - RG1 p.272 (transcription read): (3.10) substitutes 𝐉′ = J̃(e^{iξ𝐀}U_{k+1}), with J̃(U) := D_U^{ξ*}ξ^{−2}π Im∂U; (3.11) is J̃(e^{iξ𝐀}U) = J̃(U) + D_U^{ξ*}D_U^ξ𝐀 + 𝐅(U, 𝐀) "for any regular configuration U", with 𝐅 "a local operator depending on U, ∂U, 𝐀, ∇_U^ξ𝐀 only"; then "we replace the operator D*D by D*D+DRD*=D*D+DD*−DPD*=Δ_U^ξ−P₁+(lower order, local operator)"; (3.12) is J̃(U_{k+1}) = (L^{j−1}η)³J_{k+1}; and J_{k+1} is replaced by 𝐉 and U_{k+1} by 𝐔.
    - So (3.13)'s second argument is (L^{j−1}η)³𝐉 + (D_𝐔^*D_𝐔 + D_𝐔R(𝐔)D_𝐔^*)𝐀 + 𝐅(𝐔, 𝐀), with 𝐅₁(𝐔, 𝐀) := 𝐅(𝐔, 𝐀) + (D*D + DRD* − Δ_𝐔^ξ + P₁)𝐀, the lower-order operator absorbed. 𝐅₁ is not defined in the print; this is the only reading under which (3.13) follows from (3.10)–(3.12) as printed (reading R-F₁, listed in (b8)). P₁ is not needed in this form.
    - The covariance of 𝐉′ does not depend on R-F₁: under the other conceivable reading, with D*D not replaced, the second argument is (L^{j−1}η)³𝐉 + D*D𝐀 + 𝐅, which is covariant by the same bullets. So R-F₁ only identifies (3.13)'s second argument.
    - J̃ is covariant (derived directly; (3.12) is printed at U_{k+1} only). At G-valued U, ∂U^u(p) = u(x_p)∂U(p)u(x_p)^{−1}, with x_p the base point of p; Im and the projection onto 𝔤 commute with unitary conjugation; D_{U^u}R(u) = R(u)D_U and R(u) is orthogonal, so D_{U^u}^*R(u) = R(u)D_U^*. Hence J̃(U^u) = R(u)J̃(U). (The D of D*D maps bonds to plaquettes and the D of DRD* maps sites to bonds; both are covariant by the transport law below.) J̃ is a rational function of the entries of the U(b) and U(b)^{−1} (on G, X* = X^{−1}), and (3.10) uses it at the complex configuration e^{iξ𝐀}U_{k+1}, so it is taken in this rational form. The law is a rational identity in the entries that holds on G-valued configurations, which are Zariski dense in the G^c-valued ones (a compact real form is Zariski dense in its complexification). So it holds at every G^c-valued configuration where J̃ is defined. This rational form is Bałaban's continuation, by uniqueness of holomorphic continuation off the totally real G-valued configurations. Compactness and connectedness suffice; simplicity is not used.
    - D_𝐔 and R(𝐔) are covariant: R by PBG (3.33), "R(U^u)=R(u)R(U)R(u^{−1})"; D_𝐔 because it is built from the transports, R(U^u(Γ_{y,x})) = R(u(y))R(U(Γ_{y,x}))R(u^{−1}(x)) (PBG p.395), so D_{𝐔^u}R(u) = R(u)D_𝐔, and D_𝐔^* likewise. They extend to complex 𝐔 by the same Zariski-density argument, since each is a rational function of the entries (R through the finite-volume inverse G′, by Cramer's rule). For the other operators of (b1) at complex 𝐔: walk-series sums, (C^{(k)})^{1/2} and the fixed points D̃, D, 𝐀₀ are not rational. Zariski density is applied to each rational building block (finite-volume and local inverses, each walk factor, each resolvent of RG2 (2.7)), and the law passes to the convergent sums and integrals; for the fixed points, (b1)–(b2)'s uniqueness on invariant balls is used, which does not use real 𝐔.
    - e^{iξR(u)𝐀}𝐔^u = (e^{iξ𝐀}𝐔)^u (as in the next bullet). So 𝐅(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐀) = J̃((e^{iξ𝐀}𝐔)^u) − J̃(𝐔^u) − D_{𝐔^u}^*D_{𝐔^u}R(u)𝐀 = R(u)𝐅(𝐔, 𝐀), and hence 𝐉′(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)𝐀) = R(u)𝐉′(𝐔, 𝐉, 𝐀) for every G-valued u.
  - By (b2), e^{iη𝐇(s; 𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)B′)}𝐔^u = (e^{iη𝐇(s; 𝐔, 𝐉, B′)}𝐔)^u. By the bullet above at 𝐀 = 𝐇(s; 𝐔, 𝐉, B′), the 𝐉-argument of the old insertions becomes R(u)𝐉′. By (b3), R(w′(s))R(u)ω = R(u)R(w(s))ω, with w′(s) the transform at the transformed data.
  - Cov(k) for the old insertions and the transport law then give Y_G(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)B′, R(u)ω) = π(u(x_G))Y_G(𝐔, 𝐉, B′, ω)π(u(x′_G))^{−1}, for every s, so also after the s-integrals. Derivatives and integrals in s commute with a fixed conjugation. The untouched old insertions are Cov(k)'s.
  - The transformed arguments stay in Cov(k)'s domain: R(u) is bondwise isometric and real, so it maps the polydisc of radius r around a real support point x onto the polydisc of radius r around R(u)x, which satisfies the same bondwise bounds (D(k)(iii)'s neighbourhood form), and the spaces of the background are gauge invariant, RG1 p.263: "The spaces U_j^c(X,α_0,α_1) are, by the definition, gauge invariant also" (unions of G^c-orbits, p.262; this replaces the appeal to RG1 p.276, whose "so that the configurations after the transformations belong to proper spaces also" is a proviso on G^c-valued u). |R(u)𝐉| = |𝐉| and |R(u)𝐉′| = |𝐉′|, since R(u) is orthogonal pointwise. For (3.14) at the transformed data: |R(u)𝐀| = |𝐀|, |∇_{𝐔^u}R(u)𝐀| = |∇_𝐔𝐀| ((b2)), |Δ_{𝐔^u}R(u)𝐀| = |Δ_𝐔𝐀| (PBG (3.31)), and |P₁(𝐔^u)R(u)𝐀| = |P₁(𝐔)𝐀| needs P₁ covariant. P₁ is not defined in RG1 (it occurs on pp.272, 273, 281 only). The printed identity D*D + DRD* = D*D + DD* − DPD* prints R = 1 − P, so P is covariant by PBG (3.33), and then P₁ = Δ_U^ξ − D*D − DD* + DPD* + L, with L the printed "(lower order, local operator)". So P₁ is covariant if L is; L is not specified in the print, and its covariance is used as Bałaban's ((b8)). PBG's P₁(A) of (3.76), p.405, is a different object (the A-expansion remainder of DRD*) and is not this P₁.
- *(b5) The integration (derived; the argument of RG2 p.21 with insertions).* A term of the marked activity H̃(M) is RG2's (2.14) with the factors of 𝒳 over J(M) inserted. In it make the changes of variables B ↦ R(u)B and X ↦ R(u)X in dμ_{C^{(k)}(Z₀,s)}(B) and dμ₀(X). Their Jacobian is 1, since R(u) is orthogonal. (Notation: RG2's localisation parameters are written s(Δ), t(Y) here, to keep them apart from the slot variable σ_k. At complex parameters dμ_{C^{(k)}(Z₀,s)} is not a measure; the parameters enter after B = (C^{(k)})^{1/2}(s)X (RG2 (2.6)–(2.8)), so the change X ↦ R(u)X in the Gaussian dμ₀ suffices, with (C^{(k)})^{1/2}(s) covariant by (b1).)
  - The Gaussian densities and Γ_k(Z₀, σ) are invariant by (b1); the pushforward of μ_{C(𝐔)} under R(u) is μ_{R(u)C(𝐔)R(u)^{−1}} = μ_{C(𝐔^u)}. The χ-factors are invariant, since they are bondwise functions of |B(b)| and |R(u)B| = |B| (the form of the χ in RG2 (2.14) is used as Bałaban's). The localised terms 𝐑_k, 𝐁_k and A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², ·) of CRE pp.276–277 are invariant ((b6)). The 𝐕_k(Y) are invariant by RG2 Lemma 2's last statement (p.11: "The functions 𝐕_k(Y,𝐔,𝐉,B) … are gauge invariant with respect to the simultaneous gauge transformations (I.3.29)"). (I.3.29) is 𝐔 → 𝐔^u, 𝐉 → R(u)𝐉, B → R(u)B (RG1 p.276), so 𝐉 is transformed with the rest. 𝒳 is covariant slot by slot by (b4).
  - The Cauchy integrals in s(Δ), t(Y) do not involve u. So H̃(M) is covariant slot by slot, and the vacuum activities H(Z) are invariant, which is RG2 p.21's statement.
  - Ψ(M), Ψ_≥(T) and Q_{k+1}(𝔐) are functions of the H(Z) and of supports fixed by the labels ((5)(d)), so they are invariant (the argument applies verbatim to Ψ(M) and Ψ_≥(T)). Ĥ(M) = e^{−Ψ(M)}H̃(M) is covariant.
- *(b6) The new outer field.* The record of (3.25)–(3.27) above and (5)(e) have σ_k entering also through 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k, (A_k, A)) and V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}, (A_k, A), 𝐇^{(k)}), and V^{(k)} depends on A and on the non-covariant 𝐇^{(k)}. In the slot variables these are handled by reading R-x ((2)(iii)(b)): the exponent of (3.25) satisfies
  [old exponent at (A_k^{new}, A)] + V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}, (A_k^{new}, A), 𝐇^{(k)}) = [old exponent at (σ_k, A)] + log|det Dψ_{k+1}(A_k^{new})|,
  with "old exponent" the exponent before p.269's change of variables. So V^{(k)} is not a channel of its own, and σ_k enters through the old exponent at (σ_k, A), in three channels, each covariant under (𝐔, 𝐉, A, σ_k) ↦ (𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)A, R(u)σ_k):
  - (The channels are disjoint: the curly bracket of (3.25) is split into its 𝐄_k-part, which goes to the third channel with 𝐏^{(k)}, and its 𝐑_k, 𝐁_k and A(·) parts, which form the first channel. B′ in the observable is not part of the exponent.)
  - The 𝐑_k, 𝐁_k and A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², ·) parts of the bracket, at the configuration U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)}): gauge invariant (the Wilson action, PBG (3.29); CRE (2.27), (2.41), used as Bałaban's; CRE pp.276–277 localise them), with B′ covariant by (b1).
  - the cross term −⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}Cσ_k⟩ of (3.27), invariant: ⟨R(u)A, R(u)C*Δ^{(k)}Cσ_k⟩ = ⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}Cσ_k⟩ by (b1) (CRE (3.27) is used as printed in CRE).
  - 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k, (σ_k, A)) + {𝐄_k-part} = Σ_{Y∈𝐃_k}𝐕_k(Y, U_{k+1}, (σ_k, A)), RG2 (1.41) (RG2's {⋯} is RG1's 𝐄_k-bracket) (Lemma 2, transcription read), with B = (σ_k, A) and CRE's localised action for Σ𝐕_k ((5)(b)). It is invariant by Lemma 2's last statement: "The functions 𝐕_k(Y,𝐔,𝐉,B), and both terms in (1.42), are gauge invariant with respect to the simultaneous gauge transformations (I.3.29)".
  - The Jacobian depends on A_k^{new} and 𝐇^{(k)} only, not on A (𝐇^{(k)} is built from the minimiser V^{(k)}_{Λ_{k+1}ᶜ∩Λ_k}). So it factors out of the A-integral, cancels in ⟨·⟩_{1,1}, and stays in Bałaban's density of the outer variables; it is part of no insertion. The same holds for the quadratic form in σ_k alone, which is A-independent.
  - ψ_{k+1} gives the values of σ_k and is not part of the function. So R(u) acts on ω′ = (ω, σ_k) jointly, as Cov(k+1) states. (6)(e)'s *V^{(j)}* bullet, which treats V^{(j)} as a σ_j-dependence at frozen θ, is the same exponent written in σ_j.
  - The slot values on the support. σ_k = A_k, the old variable, so its bound on the support is CRE (3.14) directly ((e)). At later steps the background on the layer of σ_j can be complex, and then σ_j(b) = ψ_b(A_j^{new}(b); 𝐇^{(j)}(b)) is complex through 𝐇^{(j)}, by at most 2|𝐇^{(j)}(b)|/g_j in the sense of (a)'s proof step (b). CRE p.269 bounds 𝐇^{(j)} by O(1)ε_je^{−R_j} on S_{j+1}, as an analytic function of the background. Its size and its change at the complex backgrounds of later steps join D2.4's complex-data sizes, and (e)'s radius propagation must include it (OPEN, D2.4).
- *(b7) Representatives.* (Within Ax_{k+1}(𝔅_{k+1}, U_{k+2}) the representative is unique, REG p.79; CRE p.250's invariance is not used.) Let u be a G-valued gauge transformation fixing every block variable and independent of the next step's integration variable. By Cov(k+1), each closed word satisfies word(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, σ) = word(𝐔, 𝐉, R(u)^{−1}σ). So passing from 𝐔 to 𝐔^u rotates the slot values by the real R(u)^{−1}. R(u) is real and orthogonal, so |Re σ| and |Im σ| are kept bond by bond, and the rotated values satisfy the same support bounds. D(k+1) states its clauses for slot values satisfying those bounds, so it holds at 𝐔^u. The change of representative that actually occurs, v₁, is not of this kind (*Scope* below). No integration variable is changed, so no Jacobian and no invariance of the rest of the integrand is used. The w of (4)(c) at the next step acts on the slot values in the same way, R(w)σ, with the radius bound of (4)(c) and (e) for complex w. *Scope.* This covers changes of representative by a u that does not depend on the next step's integration variable. RG1 p.265's first transformation v₁ = U_{k+1}(Γ^{(k)})^{−1}ρ̄_k(V_k)(Γ^{(k)}) depends on B′ exactly when ρ_k's hierarchical transports do; D(k)'s representative is in the hierarchical mean axial gauge, not axial relative to a B′-independent reference ((4)(d), (d′) Step 1), so v₁ depends on B′ and goes through (4)(c)–(e) with w_tot = u_k^{−1}v₁ (see (4)(d), *Which representative*, route (ii): derived for every k, reviewed in rounds 40–44; the round-44 minors applied and not re-reviewed). At a configuration 𝐔 of D(k+1)(iii)'s polydisc that is not the step-k output at its own data (a real point other than the centre, or a general complex point), v₁ = c₀(𝐔)v̂₁ with c₀(𝐔) the B′-independent mismatch between 𝐔 and the minimiser at its averages ((d′), Step 8): c₀ is of this bullet's kind. *G^c-valued u.* For a G^c-valued u fixing the block variables and independent of the integration variable, Cov(k+1) holds by analytic continuation: each closed word is analytic in u, the identity holds on G, and G is a totally real uniqueness set in G^c (the real points of a polydisc).
