The rotation R(u)^{−1} is then complex and does not preserve the polydisc around the real support, so D(k+1)'s ω-clause at 𝐔^u is the clause at 𝐔 in the rotated slot values: it holds for σ with R(u)^{−1}σ in the polydisc (word(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, σ) = word(𝐔, 𝐉, R(u)^{−1}σ), and the clause at 𝐔 needs its slot value R(u)^{−1}σ in the polydisc); u must lie in the connected domain of analyticity of the words, which c₀'s size |log c₀| = O(dι) gives ((d′), Step 8). This is the covariant form of the ω-clause at complex configurations (D(k)(iii), *The ω-clause at complex configurations*), which reduces to the plain polydisc at real u. At the next step the same holds with B″: the rotation R(v₁(B″))^{−1}σ depends on the integration variable and goes through (4)(e)'s interpolation of R(w_tot(s))σ (the gaps block's representative entry, *The size bound*).
- *(b8) What it closes.* (4)(e)'s "Gauge covariance of the new pieces (OPEN until (6))" and (5)(f)(iii), in the slot variables. The inputs are quoted (PBG, RG1, RG2, CMP98, VAR) or derived above. Used as Bałaban's printed results, with no proof printed or read here:
  - RG1 p.267's "exactly one solution" for D̃, proved there by reference to VAR Sect. C and REG Sect. E; the invariance of its ball is derived above from VAR (51);
  - PBG p.396's "inspecting the definitions … the equalities (3.32) hold again", for the gauge-field averages;
  - the contraction and uniqueness of RG2 (1.13)–(1.16);
  - RG2 p.13's representation via (3.185) of [13], with G̃₃(x), which "has the same properties as G̃₂", and the covariance of μ there;
  - the walk-term structure of the expansions other than PBG (3.98), with RG2 p.11's "the operations in this section preserve the gauge invariance" and RG2 p.21;
  - the covariance of Δ^{(k)}(𝐔) in the transported form of [13] (3.156) (RG2 p.21) and the bondwise form of the χ in RG2 (2.14);
  - the definitions and covariance of K(·) and C_□ in PBG's walk factors (pp.412–413, not defined there); the covariance of the unspecified lower-order local operator L in RG1 p.272's identity, which is what P₁'s covariance reduces to; VAR (51), p.285, and PBG (3.119), p.419, and VAR's V₀, quoted from the transcriptions;
  - RG2 Lemma 2's last statement, for 𝐕_k;
  - reading R-F₁ ((b4), 𝐅₁ of RG1 (3.13) is 𝐅 of (3.11) plus the absorbed lower-order operator) and the covariance of P₁ (RG1 p.272, not defined there), for the |P₁𝐀| clause of (3.14); reading R-x, D2.2(2)(iii)(b), for (b6)'s exponent; the invariance of 𝐑_k, 𝐁_k and A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², ·), CRE (2.27), (2.41) and the Wilson action, as Bałaban's; Q, D̄ and μ of RG2 p.13's added term in [13]'s (3.185), not identified or read here.
  - Not used any more: CRE p.250's invariance of the rest of the integrand, and the gauge covariance of the minimiser V^{(k)}_{Λ^c∩Λ_k}.

*(c) The scalar parts, D(k+1)(v) (decided: reference extraction; (c) and (d) derived).*
- *Where field dependence enters.* κ^{(k)}, 𝓔_k and Q_k(𝔅) are numbers by D(k)(v), and they pass through (2)–(5) unchanged. ⟨·⟩_{1,1} is normalised, and its normaliser is CRE's ((2)), so (2)–(5) create no new scalar exponent. The self-energies e^{−Ψ(M)} are inside Ĥ(M), and insertions may depend on the field. So the only field-dependent scalar is Q_{k+1}(𝔐) = exp(Σ_{T⊆𝔐,|T|≥2}(−1)^{|T|}Ψ_≥(T; 𝐔, 𝐉, ω′)) at (𝐔, 𝐉) = (U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}) (RG2's activities are functions of (𝐔, 𝐉), p.15). This is the per-polymer form of (5)(d).
- *Decision.* Reference extraction at the flat reference (𝐔, ω′) = (1, 0), with the labels of the term. This is V3's route at step 1 (q°, E° at U°₁ = id, V3 L986). D(k)(v) then stands as written, and (4)–(5) stay as they are. The alternative, field-dependent scalars composed at the next step as Bałaban composes 𝐄^{(k+1)}(X), would put Mayer expansions of the old interactions into (4)–(5) at every step, and D2.3 would still need numbers for δm and z. So it is not taken.
- *The extraction (exact).* Put Ψ°_≥(T) := Ψ_≥(T; 𝐔 = 1, 𝐉 = 0, ω′ = 0) and δΨ_≥(T) := Ψ_≥(T; 𝐔, 𝐉, ω′) − Ψ°_≥(T), and likewise Ψ°(M), δΨ(M). 𝐉 = 0 is the current of the flat background, J(1) = 0. The vacuum expansion of (5)(d) converges at (1, 0, 0): (𝐔, 𝐉) = (1, 0) satisfies (2.34)–(2.39) trivially, Bałaban's bounds are uniform on the space (RG2 p.6: "all the bounds above are uniform on the domain"; p.15, the activity estimated as an analytic function on U^c_{k+1}(X, α₀, α₁)), and ω′ = 0 removes the coupling term of (3.27). R-vac(ii) is applied at the reference point (1, 0, 0); by RG2 p.6's uniformity this is the same count as at the true background, derived in D2.4(2). So Ψ°_≥(T) is defined, and it is a number given the labels and the supports of the M ∈ T; it depends on T alone.
  - The self-energies are split the same way: e^{−Ψ(M)} = e^{−Ψ°(M)}e^{−δΨ(M)}, with e^{−Ψ°(M)} a number given the labels and the support of M. So Ĥ(M) = e^{−Ψ°(M)}Ĥ′(M), Ĥ′(M) := (1 + (e^{−δΨ(M)} − 1))H̃(M); e^{−Ψ°(M)} stays in Ĥ(M), a number given the labels with |e^{−Ψ°(M)}| ≤ e^{c#(M)}, so it enters c_𝒢 = Y_𝒢(1, 0) of D2.3(2), the activities of the gas from which D2.3 extracts δm_{k+1} and z_{k+1}; Ĥ′(M) carries e^{−δΨ(M)}. *Localisation (exact).* δΨ(M) and δΨ_≥(T) are field-dependent (they vanish at (1, 0, 0) and are not constant), and unexpanded they depend on the fields on every cluster incompatible with M. With y_𝒞 := φ^T(𝒞; 𝐔, 𝐉, ω′) − φ^T(𝒞; 1, 0, 0), δΨ(M) = Σ_{𝒞≁M}y_𝒞 and δΨ_≥(T) = Σ_{𝒞≁every M∈T}y_𝒞, so e^{−δΨ(M)} = Σ_𝒦∏_{𝒞∈𝒦}(e^{−y_𝒞} − 1) and e^{ε_TδΨ_≥(T)} − 1 = Σ_{𝒦≠∅}∏_{𝒞∈𝒦}(e^{ε_Ty_𝒞} − 1), over the finite sets 𝒦 of such clusters (the set of clusters is infinite, D2.3(3); the expansion is exact since Σ_{𝒞≁M}|e^{±y_𝒞} − 1| ≤ Σ_{𝒞≁M}u_𝒞 ≤ 3c#(M) by D2.4(2)(g) in its sup form, u_𝒞 as in D2.4(12)(c)). The expansion is done before grouping: for fixed 𝒮 and fixed chosen sets 𝒦, the groups of (d), Ĥ(G) and Y_𝒢 are defined term by term. φ^T(𝒞) depends on the external fields only on supp 𝒞 (RG2 p.14: "The function H(Z) is localized in the interior of Z with respect to the external gauge fields"; 𝐉 is an independent variable of Cov(k), l.622, on which H(Z) depends only on its dependence set, RG2 p.5, D2.3(4)(Z-c); its ω′-dependence is D2.6's, below), so each term is a function of the fields on M's domain and ∪_{𝒞∈𝒦}supp 𝒞, the locality Cov(k+1) asks of an insertion; y_𝒞 is invariant by (b5). The clusters of the chosen sets are the *vacuum links*, with domain supp 𝒞: those of e^{−δΨ(M)} at M (T = {M}), those of e^{ε_TδΨ_≥(T)} − 1 at the first M of T ((d)); a cluster chosen by several of these factors is one link domain, with factor ∏ over the choosing factors, carried at each M_T of a choosing factor (D2.4(12)(c)'s pairs (T, 𝒞)). They enter Y_𝒢 as field-dependent factors; the vacuum occurrences of Q° and the old occurrences of Q_k(𝔅) are numbers given the labels.
  - Then, with ε_T := (−1)^{|T|}, Q_{k+1}(𝔐) = exp(Σ_Tε_TΨ°_≥(T))·∏_{T}(1 + (e^{ε_TδΨ_≥(T)} − 1)) = exp(Σ_Tε_TΨ°_≥(T))·Σ_𝒮∏_{T∈𝒮}(e^{ε_TδΨ_≥(T)} − 1), over the sets 𝒮 of subsets T ⊆ 𝔐 with |T| ≥ 2. The product is finite on the finite torus.
  - For fixed 𝒮, call two M linked if some T ∈ 𝒮 contains both. The classes of the generated equivalence relation are the *scalar-linked groups* 𝔊(𝒮). The factor ∏_{T∈𝒮}(e^{ε_TδΨ_≥(T)} − 1) splits over them, each factor depending on its T alone.
  - Q° := exp(Σ_Tε_TΨ°_≥(T)) = exp(Σ_𝒞(s_𝔐(𝒞) − 1)_+φ^T(𝒞; 1, 0, 0)) is the whole flat factor, a number given the labels; it is D2.3(3)'s vacuum part, expanded there in vacuum occurrences, and it is not split over the groups.
  - So ⟨𝒳⟩_{1,1} = Σ_𝔐Σ_𝒮 Q°·⊗_{G∈𝔊(𝒮)}Ĥ(G), with Ĥ(G) := ∏_{T∈𝒮,T⊆G}(e^{ε_TδΨ_≥(T)} − 1)·⊗_{M∈G}Ĥ(M), a tensor over the slots of G that depends on G alone.
- *Properties.* Q° is a number given the labels, so D(k+1)(v) holds. δΨ_≥(T) and δΨ(M) are invariant by (b5), so Ĥ(G) is covariant slot by slot, and Cov(k+1) is kept. δΨ_≥(T) is analytic where the vacuum expansion converges locally uniformly (Weierstrass), and it vanishes at (1, 0, 0).
- *Left to D2.4 and D2.6.* D2.6's part (the clusters meeting Λ_{k+1}ᶜ or depending on ω′, as att(k+1, coll)): WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §2.4 (status in closure §8).
  - The multiplicity: a cluster 𝒞 enters 2^s − 1 − s of the Ψ_≥(T), s = |S_𝔐(𝒞)| ≤ 9|supp 𝒞| ((5)(d)). So Σ_{|T|≥2}|Ψ°_≥(T)| and Σ_{|T|≥2}|δΨ_≥(T)| are bounded with the weight 2^s ≤ e^{d(𝒞)} of R-vac(ii).
  - The sizes: |e^{ε_TδΨ_≥(T)} − 1| through |δΨ_≥(T)| ≤ Σ_{𝒞≁every M∈T}|φ^T(𝒞; 𝐔, 𝐉, ω′) − φ^T(𝒞; 1, 0, 0)|, and |δΨ(M)| likewise over 𝒞 ≁ M. Each term must carry a deviation factor, since the species dev is bounded by C_χρ_kδ*_n (D2.0(3)), and that holds for every cluster size. Route, for a cluster of linear size ℓ on its support:
    - Choose a gauge on the support in which the field of 𝐔 is at most a constant times ℓδ, with δ the local curvature size (the form of CRE (2.38)). Interpolate along the real path from 1 to 𝐔 in that gauge, with 𝐉 along it, and use Cauchy's estimate in the transverse complex direction. The radius is the analyticity radius α over the path velocity, so |φ^T(𝐔) − φ^T(1)| ≤ C(ℓδ/α)sup|φ^T| while ℓδ ≤ cα.
    - For ℓδ > cα, use |φ^T(𝐔)| + |φ^T(1)| ≤ 2sup|φ^T| with sup|φ^T| ≤ Ce^{−κℓ} and e^{−κℓ} ≤ (2δ/(κcα))e^{−κℓ/2}.
    - Both bounds carry the factor δ. With q₁ = p₀ the threshold α/ε is a constant, so the second case includes clusters of fixed size, and the second bound is needed. The gauge on the support is CRE (2.38)'s local gauge; that it exists at the needed size on the cluster support is part of D2.4. That item also covers: the real points e^{ixA}, x ∈ [0,1], of the path must lie in the real part of the space ((2.34)–(2.35) and (2.38)), with 𝐉 = J(e^{ixA}) obeying (2.36)–(2.37); and the gauge must bound the scale-weighted gradient (L^nξ)²|∇A| too, since (2.39)'s gradient condition limits the transverse radius.
  - The bound |Q°| ≤ ∏_Ge^{θ(G)}, θ(G) := Σ_{M∈G}Σ_{𝒞≁M}e^{d(𝒞)}|φ^T(𝒞; 1, 0, 0)|, from the multiplicity bound; under R-vac(ii) θ(G) ≤ c|G|, as in (5)(e), with |G| := Σ_{M∈G}|M|, the number of cubes.
  - The species: dev when the clusters lie in the small-field region and depend on 𝐔 only; att(k+1, ·) when they meet Λ_{k+1}ᶜ or depend on ω′ (D2.6: WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §2.1–2.2; status in closure §8).
  - D2.3 extracts δm_{k+1} and z_{k+1} from the reference gas, whose activities c_𝒢 contain the e^{−Ψ°(M)} and whose interaction contains Q°'s occurrences.

*(d) Multi-slot insertions: merging along C (decided; derived; the linked-hull arc rule accepted by the user).*
- *The issue.* Ĥ(G) is a tensor over the slots of G, one per group of (4)(e) that G contains. D(k)'s words have one insertion in End(V_π) per group, on one interval (IT-1 note §4: "insertions Y_W ∈ End(V_π), one per group"). Two ways out were listed in (5)(f)(ii). Merging is taken, since it keeps the class of D2.0(3) unchanged.
- *The arc hull (the linked hull).* For a slot i, let I_i be the arc of its group in (4)(e), and for a marked polymer M let I(M) be the smallest arc of C containing ∪_{i∈J(M)}I_i, by D2.1(4)'s rule. The *pieces* of a group are the arcs I(M) of its marked polymers, the intervals of its untouched old insertions and the arcs J_{W′} of the members of its old occurrences. The piece of every constituent of a marked polymer's vertex (M itself, each touched W ∈ 𝔅(M), each cluster assigned to M) is I(M): a touched interval is not a separate piece, it lies in I(M) by (4)(e). Two pieces are *joined* when a rule merges their constituents directly: a scalar link T is assigned to its first M (in a fixed order), and a vacuum occurrence 𝒞 to the first M of S_𝔐(𝒞), so that the assignment depends on T, not on the unexpanded clusters of e^{x_T} − 1, and I(M) is joined to the piece of each other constituent that the link or occurrence contains; a constituent domain is incompatible with an old insertion's domain, touched or not (second rule; the pair is formed between the two constituents' pieces, I(M) for a touched W ∈ 𝔅(M)); a vacuum link at M is incompatible with a marked polymer M′ ≠ M or a vacuum link at M′ ≠ M (vacuum-link rule; the pair is between I(M) and I(M′), fixed data independent of the merge order); a member of an old occurrence hits an insertion, or two members of one occurrence are incompatible (third rule). The *linked hull* of a class of pieces under the joined pairs is the union of its pieces and, for each joined pair of disjoint pieces, the shorter of the two arcs of C between them (when the two are equal, the one in C's orientation from the piece that comes first in a fixed order of the pieces, e.g. by their first bond along C from the base point), enlarged at each end to the end of any old interval that it cuts, as in (4)(e); it is an arc, or C (a group with arc C is alone in its family and its term is dim(π)^{−1}tr Y_G, l.488). The arc I(G) of a group is the union of the linked hulls of its classes. It is connected: the groups of 𝔊(𝒮) are scalar-link components ((c)), whose marked polymers are joined through their links, and every other membership comes from a joined pair, an arc overlap, the hull rule below or a touched insertion inside I(M). Merge the groups of 𝔊(𝒮) whose arcs overlap, the merged arc being the union, and repeat until the arcs are pairwise disjoint. The untouched old insertions whose intervals lie inside an arc join the group of that arc. (The smallest arc containing the pieces doesn't work: an insertion that it brings in, inside a long gap, is linked to no vertex of D2.4(12)(c). The linked hull lies in the union of D2.4(12)(c)'s windows, which is what the tree bound there needs; Y_𝒢, the class form and Cov(k+1) below use only an arc containing the pieces, with the arcs of distinct groups disjoint.)
  - *Second rule.* Also merge into a group every old insertion W, touched or not, whose domain X_W is incompatible with a constituent domain of the group; a touched W ∈ 𝔅(M) of another group carries that group with it. *Vacuum-link rule.* Merge two groups when a vacuum link of one ((c) *Localisation*, domain supp 𝒞) is incompatible with a marked polymer or a vacuum link of the other: the links are field-dependent factors of Y_𝒢, and the M are compatible with each other ((5)(c)) but not with the clusters of other M (a 𝒞 ≁ M′ with M′ ∈ S_𝔐(𝒞)∖T is allowed). Old insertions against vacuum links are the second rule's case (a vacuum link is a constituent of its M-vertex), and the members of old occurrences are treated as old insertions of their group for the second rule (third rule). A marked polymer's support contains Z₀ ⊇ Y₀ and Z ⊇ Z̃₀ ((5)), which are larger than the s-components, and (4)(e) only guarantees that no s-component meets X_W. Merging is a union of classes: a W already in a group by the arc rule carries its group with it, so the two groups merge. After each merge, recompute the arc as the union of the linked hulls, with the enlargement, and repeat both rules to a fixed point. The process is monotone and stops on the finite torus, and its result does not depend on the order of the merges: the pieces, the gap of each joined pair and its enlargement are fixed, and every rule only adds pieces, pairs or members, so the fixed point is the least structure closed under the rules. The words are unchanged, since it regroups an ordered product along C.
  - Call the results the *step-(k+1) groups* 𝒢.
- *The insertion.* For 𝒢 with arc I(𝒢) = [x, x′], let Y_𝒢 ∈ End(V_π) be the contraction along I(𝒢), in the order of C, of the tensors Ĥ(G), G ⊂ 𝒢, with the untouched old insertions in I(𝒢) and the U_{k+1}-transports between consecutive slots. By multilinearity, dim(π)^{−1}(contraction of ⊗Ĥ(M) with the A-independent factors of T) = dim(π)^{−1}tr(Y_{𝒢₁}U_{(1)}⋯Y_{𝒢_r}U_{(r)}), with the 𝒢's in cyclic order and U_{(i)} the transports between their arcs. This is exact.
- *Class form.* The families {𝒢} have disjoint intervals, and their constituent domains are pairwise compatible: the M are compatible by (5)(c), the old insertions by D(k), a new constituent with an old insertion, touched or not, by the second rule, and a vacuum link with a marked polymer or a vacuum link of another group by the vacuum-link rule. A touched old insertion W ∈ 𝔅(M) is not inside D_J^# (D_J^# is the B-dependence set of the slots, (2.2) above, and Y_G depends on B through B′ and 𝐇 on the components only, so X_W ⊂ D_J^# fails in general); its domain X_W is subject to the second rule like every old insertion's, and it is counted inside M's vertex in D2.4(12)(c). Inside one 𝒢 the constituents are linked by construction, so the condition of IT-1 note §4 is read across distinct 𝒢's, with the union of its constituent domains as the domain of 𝒢: its marked polymers' domains, its vacuum links' supports and its old insertions' domains, touched or not, which with the bonds of I(𝒢) (the U_{k+1}-transports) is the set on which Y_𝒢 depends ((c) *Localisation*); the vacuum and old occurrences' factors are numbers given the labels and enter Y_𝒢 as scalars (D2.3(3)'s hard core still counts their members' domains). So they are compatible families in the sense of IT-1 note §4 (V3 L967, quoted in the IT-1 note: "all their constituent domains … mutually compatible and their intervals are disjoint"). Cov(k+1) holds for Y_𝒢 with the endpoints x, x′, by (b) and the transport law. Distinct 𝒢's interact only by the hard core of D2.3(3) (third rule below); the parts of Q° and of Q_k(𝔅) inside one 𝒢 are numbers and go into Y_𝒢 (D2.3(3)).
  - *Third rule (amendment from D2.3(3); not reviewed).* Q° and the old interactions Q_k(𝔅) are expanded in cluster occurrences, the members hit by one occurrence join one group, and the arc hull is taken over the arcs J_Γ of the old occurrences as well. Then distinct step-(k+1) groups interact only by the hard core of D2.3(3).
- *Cost (D2.4).*
  - Norms. At real backgrounds, in the projective norm on ⊗End(V_π), contraction with unitary transports does not raise the norm. In the operator norm on V_π^{⊗m} a contraction can cost up to dim(π)^{m−1}: the swap Σ_{ij}E_{ij}⊗E_{ji} has norm 1 and contracts to dim(π)·1. So D2.4 uses the projective norm. At complex backgrounds the transports along I(𝒢) add exp(c_π‖a′‖_∞·length(I(𝒢))), which D2.4(12)(d) pays from the vertices' shares (see *Arcs*).
  - Arcs. The merging enlarges arcs: the arc of 𝒢 can exceed the spatial diameter of its supports. For the squares C_n the shorter arc between two points of C is at most 2 times their distance in the maximum norm, with every period at least twice the side (D2.1(4)). For the hull of a set the constant is 4: length(I) ≤ 4·(ℓ∞-diameter of the supports), as in D2.1(4)'s proof, and the four midpoints of the sides attain 3. (A merged arc is not at most the sum of its constituents' arcs, since a second-rule merge also covers the gap between W's interval and I(𝒢).) The smallest-arc bound length ≤ 4·diam_ℓ∞ serves the arcs I(M) and those of (4)(e) (with 20c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 for the latter, D2.4(11)(c)). For the linked hull, the shorter arc of a joined pair is at most twice the ℓ∞-distance of the pieces, so I(𝒢) lies in the union of D2.4(12)(c)'s windows and length(I(𝒢)) ≤ Σ_v|win(v)| over its vertices (D2.4(12)(c)'s covering lemma), which D2.4(12)(d) pays with the decay of the activities. For other contours this chord–arc constant enters.
- *What it closes.* (5)(f)(ii), with D(k+1)'s groups, intervals and insertions in the form of D2.0(3).

*(e) D(k+1)(iii): the background domain and the ω-clause (derived, with reading R-adm).*
- *The background domain must change at k + 1 ≥ 2.*
  - Ĥ(M) contains the covariances and propagators of (5) at the complex background 𝐔. RG2 p.15 gives their analyticity only "on the space of configurations (𝐔,𝐉) satisfying the conditions I.(i)–(iii) on the domain Z, with constants α₀′, α₁′ much bigger than α₀, α₁". Those conditions bound gradients as well.
  - D(k)(iii)'s sup-only domain does not control gradients. On Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} it allows |A′| < αL^{k−n} in step-k field units, hence |∇^ξA′| ≤ 2ξ^{−1}|A′| < 2αL^{2k−n}, so (L^nξ)²|∇^ξA′| < 2L^nα. That exceeds CRE's bound α by the factor 2L^n, which is not bounded in n.
  - So D(k+1)(iii), k + 1 ≥ 2, is posed on CRE's domain at 𝐔 = e^{iηa′}U_{k+1}: the full complex part of Ũ^c_{k+1}, i.e. (2.34) for 𝐔 and (2.36) for 𝐉 per plaquette and bond, (2.38) per cube, and (2.39) with both the sup condition and the gradient condition (L^nξ)²|∇^ξ_UA′| < (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(k+1−n)}))α_{1,n}, posed on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} for n = 1, …, k and on Ω_{k+1} for n = k+1 (CRE poses none on Ω₀∖Ω₁); and (2.35), (2.37) for the averaged configurations built on a domain X, posed for each factor that uses them on that factor's own domain: Bałaban's X ∈ 𝐃_k (CRE p.261), and the domain Z of RG2 p.15's covariance operators for Ĥ(M) ((6)(c); l.917). RG2 p.15's domain then contains it by definition.
    - The reason (2.39) alone does not suffice: for 𝐔 = e^{iξA′}U, |∂𝐔 − 1| ≤ |∂U − 1| + C(ξ²|∇_UA′| + ξ²|A′|²), so (2.39) at radius α_{1,n} allows curvature up to Cα_{1,n}ξ²(L^nξ)^{−2}, within (2.34)'s α_{0,n}ξ²(L^nξ)^{−2} only if α_{1,n} ≲ α_{0,n}, and CRE (2.28) fixes no relation between (q₀, C₀) and (q₁, C₁).
    - The real background. U_{k+1} on the support lies in the real part of the space: on Ω_{k+1} by CRE p.268 ("The field U_{k+1} satisfies the regularity condition |∂U_{k+1} − 1| < 2B₃ε_{k+1}(L⁻¹η)² on Ω_{k+1}"), and on Ω_{k+1}ᶜ by the conditions of the earlier steps: reading R-bg (gaps block l.183; CRE p.276 l.10–12, "regularity properties of the configuration U_{k+1} on a neighborhood of Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, and these are the same as for U_n-configurations"; p.277 l.3–8, "on the domain Ω_{k+1}^c there is no regularity improvement", the powers of 1/2 as the room for the shifts; Bałaban's, accepted; derived quantitatively for the curvature and gradient conditions at (6)(e), *The base point on Ω₁∖Ω₂*, *For k ≥ 2* and *Every older layer*, through R-dec from the top-layer bounds; not reviewed). R-m proper (the complex margins of (2.36)–(2.39)) is not this statement (round 41).
      - *Derived instead of assumed.* On Ω_{k+1}, (2.34) at j = n = k+1 has the factor 1: |∂U − 1| < α_{0,k+1}ξ²(L^{k+1}ξ)^{−2} (p.261). p.268 gives |∂U_{k+1} − 1| < 2B₃ε_{k+1}(L^{−1}η)². Both weights are the scale-(k+1) plaquette weight. *Unit check (transcriptions read).* CRE p.276: "In all bounds connected with such a cube we have to replace η=L^{−k} by L^{−n}", so η = L^{−k} and (L^{−1}η)² = L^{−2(k+1)}. (2.34) for X ∈ 𝐃_{k+1} is in the scale ξ = L^{−(k+1)} (p.256: "the lattice T_ξ, ξ=L^{−j}"), and at n = k+1, ξ²(L^{k+1}ξ)^{−2} = ξ² = L^{−2(k+1)}. The weights are equal, so membership needs 2B₃ε_{k+1} < α_{0,k+1}, i.e. (with ε_{k+1} = g_{k+1}A₀(log g_{k+1}^{−2})^{p₀}, α_{0,k+1} = g_{k+1}C₀(log g_{k+1}^{−2})^{q₀}) q₀ > p₀ for g_K small, or q₀ = p₀ with C₀/A₀ > 2B₃; this is the same kind of entailment as q₀ ≥ p₀ below, which it bases. Condition (2.38) for U_{k+1} on Ω_{k+1} (closed, SW closure §6(6d)): VAR Thm 1 for U_{k+1} with the global ε_{k+1} gives (2.38) on cubes of size CM directly, under B₃ε_{k+1} ≤ 2Bα_{0,k+1}, the membership entailment above (at the root k = 1 the (7)-constant of the U₂ problem is *The base point on Ω₁∖Ω₂*'s ε^V = 2(1+β₀)²ε₁ ≤ 2(1+β₀)⁴ε₂ by (2.8), so there the condition carries the factor 2(1+β₀)⁴); no (LPG′) needed. On Ω_{k+1}ᶜ the conditions are those of the earlier steps, by reading R-bg (p.276 l.10–12, p.277 l.3–8; gaps block l.183); p.277 names (2.36)–(2.39), not (2.34)–(2.35), and p.276 speaks of U_{k+1}'s regularity on the older layers being that of the U_n-configurations, so on Ω_{k+1}ᶜ the curvature conditions rest on the earlier steps' (2.34)–(2.35) at their own margins; on the layer n = 1 the root of that reading is the step 1 → 2, V4's, where the real parts of (2.34) and (2.38) are derived for U₂ by VAR Theorem 1 in (6)(e), *The base point on Ω₁∖Ω₂* (reviewed in rounds 40 and 41, the round-41 minors not re-reviewed); for k ≥ 2 and for every older layer the quantitative form is the response chain of (6)(e), *For k ≥ 2* and *Every older layer* (derived, not reviewed).
  - This is weaker than the sup-only form, which stays at k = 1 (D1(iii)′, from V3). D1(iii)′ implies the new form at k = 1, since its domain contains CRE's.
  - *The consumer still gets what it needs.* (4)(f) at the next step evaluates the old factors at A″ = 𝐇(s,tB″) + a″, with a″ in the new domain. So (4)(f)'s margin inequality is needed in the gradient norm too.
    - RG2 (1.18) bounds 𝐇_k(s(Y₀),B′) by 4B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′| "and the same bound for all admissible norms". RG2 p.5 bounds the operator H(s(Y₀)) of the same formula "in all norms of Theorems 3.1.-3.10 [13]" ([13] is read as PBG). PBG Theorem 3.1, (3.42), p.397, bounds |G′λ| and |∇_UG′λ| by B₀(L^jη)² and B₀L^jη on Δ(y), y ∈ Λ_j. That is the weighting of CRE (2.39), (L^nξ)² for the gradient against L^nξ for the field.
    - *R-adm (printed; not a reading).* The "admissible norms" of RG2 (1.18) are the norms of PBG Theorems 3.1–3.10: RG2 p.5 bounds H(s(Y₀))X "by B₀e^{16κ₁}|X| in all norms of Theorems 3.1.–3.10 [13]", and PBG (3.42), in Theorem 3.1, bounds |G′λ|, |∇_UG′λ|, |G′∇_U^*λ|, |Δ_UG′λ| by B₀[(L^jη)², L^jη, L^jη, 1]e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)}|λ|, i.e. the gradient with one extra factor (L^jη)^{−1}, the relative weighting of (2.39). (1.15) holds on the ball max{|𝐀|, |∇𝐀|} < 4C₄(B₀e^{16κ₁})³ε₃², and "holds for all admissible norms" keeps the map in that ball; its ∇ is unweighted (PBG (3.39)).
    - With R-adm, the gradient part of A″ obeys the same bound as its sup part, up to the change of the covariant derivative between the centres U_{k+1} and X₁ of (4)(f). That change is (Ad(e^{iξ𝐇}) − 1)(·)/ξ, of size O(|𝐇||a″|), so (L^nξ)² times it is ≤ (L^nξ|𝐇|)(L^nξ|a″|), and it joins the O(·)² of (4)(f). So (4)(f)'s condition carries over unchanged to the gradient norm. D2.0(3)'s D(k)(iii) is amended accordingly (note added there).
    - *The curvature conditions (2.34)–(2.35).* At the evaluation points e^{iηA″}X₁ the change of curvature against e^{iηa″}U_{k+1} is ≤ C(L^nξ)^{−2}ξ²[(L^nξ)²|∇𝐇| + (L^nξ|𝐇|)² + (L^nξ|𝐇|)(L^nξ|a″|)], with 𝐇 = 𝐇(s, tB′). It must stay below the curvature margin β2^{−(k+1−n)}α_{0,n}ξ²(L^nξ)^{−2}. At t = 1 this is an inequality of R-m's type with α_{0,n} in place of α_{1,n}: 8B₀e^{16κ_s}C·O(1)ε_k < (β/2)α_{0,k}, i.e. q₀ > p₀, or q₀ = p₀ with C₀/A₀ large. CRE's powers of 1/2 appear in (2.34)–(2.37) as in (2.39), and R-m's statement on p.277 covers the 𝐉-bounds (2.36)–(2.37), which carry α_{0,n}. On the polydisc T_Y is taken so small that the deformation also respects this margin: 1/T_Y ≥ C′B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′|_Y/(min(β,1)α_{0,k}) is added to the maximum of (4)(f). The entailment q₀ ≥ p₀ joins R-m's (gaps block).
      - *Older layers.* The check above is at n = k. On Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, n < k, m := k−n ≥ 1, the bound on the deformation in the units of |A″| is O(1)L^mθ^m times its Ω_k-size, by R-dec in the sup and the scale-weighted gradient norm. With h_k^{(0)} := 4min(β,1)α_{0,k}/C′ + 8C₁^{RG2}B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)ε_k, the bound on the polydisc under the T_Y condition above, the curvature part needs O(1)(2θ)^m h_k^{(0)} < (β/2)α_{0,n}. CRE (2.6)–(2.7), first lines (for every p; reading R-cpl₀), give α_{0,n} ≥ α_{0,k}/((1+β₀)²√m). The n = k inequality in its complex-data form gives h_k^{(0)} ≤ min(β,1)α_{0,k}/2 for C′ ≥ 16. With √m x^m ≤ x for x ≤ 1/2, the condition holds for all m ≥ 1 once 2θ ≤ 1/2 and 2θ·O(1)(1+β₀)² < 1, as for the sup part in (4)(f). The 𝐉-bounds (2.36)–(2.37) are treated the same way at the 𝐉-margin of (4)(f), *Carrying 𝐉*, with R-dec covering its Laplacian and P₁ parts (the extension of R-adm there is part of R-dec). The basis on the older layers is CRE p.277 (R-m) with R-dec.
- *Analyticity of the new insertions (derived from (4)(f) and RG2 p.15).* For real U_{k+1} on the support and a′ in the new domain at step k+1:
  - the old factors are analytic at e^{iη(𝐇(s,tB′) + a′)}U_{k+1} by (4)(f), in the sup and the gradient norm;
  - the transports and the leading word are entire in the background;
  - the Gaussian parts and the 𝐕_k(Y) are analytic on Bałaban's domain by RG2 p.15, which contains the new one by the definition above;
  - the characteristic functions do not depend on a′. They depend on the fluctuation field (CRE p.268: "We want to have characteristic functions depending on the fluctuation field only", (3.16)) or on the real step-(k+1) configuration (the (3.3) factors that remain), and the latter are among the term's indicators, kept as factors.
  - Ĥ(M), and with it Y_𝒢 of (d), is then analytic in a′ by Morera's theorem in each variable under the integrals, and jointly by Hartogs' theorem. This needs: an integrable majorant for the parameter integrals, locally uniform in a′ (RG2 p.15's second measure is complex, and the large-field B is unbounded, so a uniform bound in B is not available); locally uniform convergence of the cluster series inside Ĥ(M), Ψ(M) and the vacuum sums, with Weierstrass' theorem. Both are part of D2.4's sizes at complex data (D(k+1)(ii) with δ*^{(k+1),30} + a*^{(k+1)}). Ĥ(M) is G-covariant by (b).
- *The ω-clause: where ω lives (quoted).* At step k the components of ω are ω₀, V3's exterior fields on the bonds I∖D(Λ) and J (IT-1 note L786–790) of Ω₁∩Λᶜ, and the slot variables ω_j := σ_j, the old A_j on Ω_{j+1}∩Λ_{j+1}ᶜ ((b)), for 1 ≤ j ≤ k−1. Since Ω_{j+2} ⊆ Λ_{j+1} (CRE pp.264–265), ω_j lies in the layer Ω_{j+1}∖Ω_{j+2}. That layer is a union of scale-(j+1) blocks: VAR p.277, "B^j(Λ_j)=Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}", with VAR's Λ_j := Ω_j^{(j)}∖Ω_{j+1}^{(j)}, not CRE's. The new component ω_k := A_k on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ lies in the layer Ω_{k+1}∖Ω_{k+2}.
- *|ω| on the support (derived; closes the OPEN item "|ω| bound at k ≥ 2").*
  - At step j+1, CRE (3.14), p.267, gives |A_j′| < O(1)ε_j on Ω_{j+1} on the support of the step-(j+1) characteristic functions of (3.3) ("|V_k′−1|<O(1)ε_k on Ω_{k+1}… |A_k′|<O(1)ε_k also"). These are kept as factors in T_{j+1}. CRE p.268 removes a (3.3) factor only on cubes □′ ⊂ R_{j+1}ᶜ, where it "is equal to 1, because of the bound on A_k introduced by the second", the (3.16) factor. So the support is unchanged.
  - By (a), Input 1, A_j′ = g_jA_j on the free bonds, so |A_j| < O(1)ε_j/g_j = O(1)A₀(log g_j^{−2})^{p₀}. This bounds the slot variable σ_j = A_j of (b) directly, which is the bound the ω-clause uses. After ψ_{j+1} on S_{j+1}, |g_jA_j^{new} − g_jA_j| ≤ 2|𝐇^{(j)}| ≤ δ_j/20 by (a)'s proof step (b), and δ_j = (A₁/A₀)ε_j (Input 4). So also |A_j^{new}| ≤ O(1)ε_j/g_j on the support.
  - At j = 0. IT-1 note §3d(2)(d) gives |a_old| < r on I∖D(Λ) and |A_e(b)| < r on J for exterior data in the support of the collar indicators, with r = A₁(log g₀^{−2})^{p₀} (V3 L122, L3966–3973; IT-1 note L1149 does not print r). ω₀ is bounded on all of Ω₁∩Λᶜ by the step-1 small-field test on Ω₁: |e^{ig₀A(b)}Z_b − 1| < 2δ₀ with |Z_b − 1| < 2δ₀ from (B1) (V3 L559, L570) gives |e^{ig₀A(b)} − 1| < 4δ₀, so |g₀A(b)| ≤ 2πδ₀. Since δ₀/g₀ = r, this is |B(b)| ≤ Cr with an absolute C, with the conversion between phases and B-units of IT-1 note §3d(2)(d). The j = 0 condition below holds with C in place of 2.
- *The size of w on the layer of ω_j (derived from (4)(d)'s computation).* On a block B^n(y), n ≤ k, the chain of (106) has n steps. By (4)(d), *Size*, the j′-th bracket has |log| ≤ O(L)L^{j′}η sup|𝐇| with η = L^{−k}, and the real transports preserve |·|. The sum over j′ < n gives |log W_y| ≤ O(1)L^nη·sup_{B^n(y)}|𝐇| = O(1)L^{n−k}sup_{B^n(y)}|𝐇|, with 𝐇 in fine units as in (4)(d). At complex backgrounds the chain's transports are G^c-valued and the conjugations cost the factor e^{4c_Gdι(1 + o(1))} = 1 + o(1) on the constant ((4)(d)'s (d′), Step 8 (ii)–(iii); derived, not reviewed).
  - *The complex data.* The ω-dependent old factors are evaluated at R(w(s,t))ω on the whole polydisc of (4)(e)–(f), |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s}, |t_Y| ≤ T_Y, not only at t = 1. There sup|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ 4B₀e^{16κ_s}(max_YT_Y|B′|_Y + sup|B′|) ≤ min(β,1)α_{1,k}/4 + 8C₁^{RG2}B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)ε_k =: h_k, by 1/T_Y ≥ 16B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′|_Y/(min(β,1)α_{1,k}) ((4)(f)). By R-m's inequality h_k ≤ α_{1,k}/2. So the size is O(α_{1,k}), not O(ε_k).
  - *The adjacent layer, j = k−1.* There n = k, the layer Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} is in the scale-k region, and RG2 (1.18) bounds 𝐇 by h_k directly. So |log w| ≤ C_wh_k.
  - *Older layers, j ≤ k−2.* The sites lie in Ω_{j+1}∖Ω_{j+2} ⊆ Ω_kᶜ, where no k-independent sup bound of 𝐇 holds: VAR (190), p.308, bounds δ𝓗_μ(B,x)/δB_ν(y′) by O(1)(L^jη)^{−1}(L^{j′}η)^{−d}exp(−⅛δ₀d(y,y′)) for x ∈ Δ(y), y ∈ Λ_j, y′ ∈ Λ_{j′}, and the prefactor (L^{j+1}η)^{−1} = L^{k−j−1} cancels the chain's gain L^{j+1−k}. The decay replaces it:
    - d is PRT2's weighted distance (2.46), p.231. PBG cites it as "(2.36) in [4]", [4] = PRT2; the equation numbers differ, and which printed display PBG means is not resolved against PRT2 here: d(y,y′) = inf_Γ Σ_m(L^mη)^{−1}|Γ∩B^m(Λ_m)|, each layer's length in its own block units.
    - VAR (1), p.277: (L^mη)^{−1}dist(Ω_mᶜ, Ω_{m+1}) > RM₁. A contour from B(Γ_{k+1}) ⊆ Ω_{k+1} to the layer Ω_{j+1}∖Ω_{j+2} crosses Ω_m∖Ω_{m+1} for m = j+2, …, k, each with weighted length > RM₁. So d > (k−j−1)RM₁.
    - Integrating the derivative along tB′ (𝓗(0) = 0) and keeping half the decay for the sum over y′ gives, at s = 1, sup_{B^{j+1}(y)}|𝐇| ≤ O(1)L^{k−j−1}e^{−δ₀(k−j−1)RM₁/16}h_k.
    - *At the complex data.* VAR (190) is printed for VAR's 𝓗 at s = 1. (For 𝐇(s, tB′) on the polydisc, RG2 (1.18)'s "same bound for all admissible norms" under R-adm with PBG (3.42)'s decay doesn't give the same kernel bound: RG2 (1.18) and p.5's bound "B_0e^{16κ_1}|X| in all norms of Theorems 3.1.-3.10 [13]" are norm bounds obtained by spending the walk decay against e^{mκ₁} at complex s, and PBG (3.42)'s decay is an operator bound, not one of p.397's norms.)
      - *Single scale (derived from RG2 p.5).* RG2 bounds H(s(Y₀))X by (1.11), "B_0|X|sup_ω M_1^{−1/2|ω|}exp(−δ_0d(ω))e^{mκ_1}", and for m > 2⁴, "δ_0d(ω) ≥ δ_1mM". If δ₁M ≥ 2κ₁ (twice RG2's δ₁M ≥ κ₁; a condition on M, fixed before the coupling), then e^{−δ₀d(ω)}e^{mκ₁} ≤ e^{−δ₀d(ω)/2} for m > 2⁴, and for m ≤ 2⁴ the factor is at most e^{16κ₁}e^{−δ₀d(ω)}. So RG2's single-scale H(s) keeps half the decay of the walks at complex s. The walk bound (1.7) behind (1.11) is not read.
      - *R-dec (Bałaban's statement, accepted under the accepted-claims rule; the rate computed from the print).* On CRE's multi-domain lattice, 𝐇(s, tB′) on the polydisc obeys VAR (190)'s kernel bound with the rate halved, in the sup and in the scale-weighted gradient norm. Bałaban states the multi-domain extension: CRE p.267 ("Their properties are essentially the same as before, because of the localization of the field A_k") and p.277, which asserts the comparison made here: "The localization of the fluctuation field, and the exponential decay of the minimizing function 𝐇_k imply that this difference is still much greater than bounds on this function". The rate is computed from printed bounds: VAR (190) (R-v, printed, VAR Prop. 9) and RG2 p.5's argument above, which keeps half the walk decay at complex s. The gradient part uses R-adm (printed).
      - With R-dec, sup_{B^{j+1}(y)}|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ O(1)L^{k−j−1}θ^{k−j−1}h_k on the polydisc, with θ := e^{−δ₀RM₁/32}. So |log w| ≤ C_wθ^{k−j−1}h_k on ω_j's layer.
    - CRE's domains satisfy VAR (1) with R of the size of R_{j}: the layers are built from a fixed number of layers of LMR_{j+1}-cubes around the large-field regions (CRE p.265: "four layers of the LMR_{k+1}-cubes, i.e., we take the domain Z_k′^{∼4}", then further single layers and "two layers of such cubes"), and R_j ≥ (log g_j^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5)) is large uniformly in j for g_K small.
  - So |log w| ≤ C_wθ^{k−j−1}h_k at the sites of ω_j, j ≤ k−1, with θ := e^{−δ₀RM₁/32} and C_w independent of j, k; for j ≤ k−2 this rests on reading R-dec. Without R-dec the supplied pages give it only for j = k−1.
- *The radii.* Fix r_{ω,j} when ω_j is created: r_{ω,0} := 1 in V3's B-units (D(1)(iii)), and r_{ω,j} := g_j^{1/2} for j ≥ 1 (amendment, D2.6 closure §2.2 *Complex σ_k*; for the user's review). (The radius c_ωε_j/g_j doesn't work: the source phase per localisation domain is of order r_kr_{ω,k} ~ (log g_k^{−2})^{2p₀} ≫ 1, and χ^{(k)} blocks a complex mean shift.) At g_j^{1/2} the per-cube Kotecký–Preiss sum of the localised source terms x_X (closure 2.2 (2), the random-walk expansion of K) is ≤ C_KB₀e^{32a}A₁g_k^{1/2}(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀} → 0. The radius is used only as a domain (no Cauchy estimate in ω). The membership below holds since g_j|σ_j| ≤ O(1)ε_j + g_j^{3/2} ≤ 2O(1)ε_j for small g; *Propagation* is re-checked below, and the slot-value move in the closure (§2.2 (c)).
  - In slot variables (b), the insertions depend on ω_j = σ_j, the old A_j, and not on A_j^{new}. So the cutoff g in ψ_{j+1} never enters their analyticity (in the slot form ψ only gives the values). At j = 0 the same convention holds with ω₀ = Ψ_ξA′ and the cutoff values ω_b frozen (amendment, D(k)(iii) above, D2.6 closure §6.1).
  - At step j+1, σ_j enters through the configuration U_j(exp i[g_jCσ_j − hD̃(g_jCσ_j)]V^{(j)}) of CRE (3.15) (the old factors and the terms 𝐄, 𝐑, 𝐁, A(1/g_j²(·) − 1/g_j², ·)), through the quadratic form ⟨A_j, C*Δ^{(j)}CA_j⟩ of (3.15), and through 𝐏^{(j)}(g_j, U_{j+1}, A_j) of (3.15) and (3.27). The cross terms of the quadratic form between A_j|_{Λ_{j+1}} and σ_j are linear in σ_j, hence entire. The analyticity of 𝐏^{(j)} in the outer field A_j = σ_j is printed nowhere (CRE (3.43) complexifies the integration field only); it is derived in WILSON_IT1_O6_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md (26 Oct 2026; reviewed in round 46, it1-o6-20261026-r1, serious findings repaired, not re-reviewed; D2.6 closure §8's O6): every σ_j-dependence of the actions of (3.27) and (3.29), apart from the entire quadratic forms, passes through the composite field B′ = g_jC(σ_j, A) − hD̃(g_jC(σ_j, A)), which RG2 §1 treats as a variable field (RG2 p.6: "in the preceding considerations B′ was a variable field"), so the analyticity in (σ_j, A) jointly is composition with RG2 (1.19)–(1.20) on R-m's domain 𝒫_j^m = {g_j|B| < r_{α,j}/C₁^{RG2}}, r_{α,j} = min(β,1)α_{1,j}/(16B₀e^{16κ_s}) (the bracket terms 𝐄, 𝐑, 𝐁, A(·) are analytic in the shift only on CRE's g-scaled spaces (2.28), (2.34)–(2.39), R-m, p.277; 𝐏^{(j)}'s terms on the absolute domain e^{16κ₁}|B′| ≤ a₁), uniformly over the term's (𝐔, 𝐉)-space, and the bound is the real-support bound with the fluctuation factor and the coefficients at most doubled and the quadratic forms' values by at most 8 (g_j sup|B| ≤ c₃ε_j + g_j^{3/2} + 6h_j ≤ 2c₃ε_j on the ω-polydiscs, h_j = max(c_He^{−R_j}ε_j, S_H)); the membership of the consumers' points in 𝒫_j^m is l.959's computation, a smallness condition on g_K under (R-m′) q₁ > p₀, or with q₁ = p₀ the constant condition C₁/A₀ > 32C₁^{RG2}B₀e^{16κ_s}c₃/min(β,1) (l.702); (3.43) is Bałaban's analyticity space in the integration field, a polylog radius of the same kind as R-m's, both containing the support (O6 closure §7); V^{(j)}'s action part by a Cauchy estimate at half R-m's radius in the direction of the change of variables, and its Gaussian-form part −⟨K(σ_j, A) + ½Kδ, δ⟩, K = C*Δ^{(j)}C, bounded through K's random-walk expansion with S_{j+1} separated from Λ_{j+1} by (3.20)'s layer (O6 closure §6); conditions (C1)–(C5) there, smallness conditions on g_K given (R-m′).
    - *V^{(j)}.* p.269: the change of variables on S_{j+1} changes the action "by terms corresponding to (1.24), and by terms obtained by the first order expansion of the action with respect to the perturbative parts of the changes of variables. We denote this new part of the action by V^{(k)}(S_{k+1},A_k,𝐇^{(k)}). All terms of the previous action contribute to it", and p.271 puts V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}) inside ⟨·⟩_{s=1,t} and tV^{(k)}(S_k) into the measure (3.27). So V^{(j)} depends on the integrated field, and its dependence on σ_j (the S_{j+1}-bonds are bonds of σ_j) enters Ĥ(M) through the measure.
    - On (R_{j+1}^{∼2}∩S_{j+1})^{(j)*} the change of variables is (1.22), "A(b) = (1/ig_0)log exp ig_0A′(b)exp(−ig(A′(b))𝐇_{1,Ax}(b))" (p.251) with 𝐇^{(j)}(b) in place of 𝐇_{1,Ax}(b), and the Jacobian is (1.24) (p.252). Both carry the cutoff values g(A_j′(b)), and (1.24) also their derivatives. These are frozen as real factors θ_b := g(A_j′(b)), θ′_b := g′(A_j′(b)) at the real configuration and kept with the indicators, as for the cutoff in the slot form. At frozen θ, A_j′(b) = (1/ig_j)log(exp(ig_jσ_j(b))exp(iθ_b𝐇^{(j)}(b))) is analytic in σ_j for g_j|σ_j| small, and V^{(j)} is the first-order term of the action in the perturbation together with the (1.24) terms. The change of variables is analytic in σ_j at frozen θ, but V^{(j)} is the first-order term of the whole action, 𝐏^{(j)} included, so its analyticity in σ_j is derived with O6 (WILSON_IT1_O6_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md §6; reviewed in round 46, repaired, not re-reviewed: a directional derivative of the analytic action of l.956 at points of R-m's domain 𝒫_j^m, bounded by a Cauchy estimate at radius r_{α,j}/(2C₁^{RG2}g_j), plus the entire Gaussian-form part bounded through K's random-walk expansion). Its size on S_{j+1} is O(|𝐇^{(j)}|) ≤ O(1)ε_je^{−R_j} at real backgrounds (p.269: 𝐇^{(k)} is "bounded on the set S_{k+1} by O(1)ε_kexp(−R_k)") and ≤ S_H (l.222) at the complex backgrounds of D(j+1)(iii). V^{(j)} is in the list of the σ_j-dependences.
  - In the first, B′ = g_jCσ_j − hD̃(g_jCσ_j) is RG2 (1.19), with |B′| ≤ C₁^{RG2}g_j|B| for g_j|B| < ε₁^{RG2} (RG2 (1.20): "where C_1 is an absolute constant", written C₁^{RG2} here to keep it apart from CRE's C₁ in α_{1,k}). Fix c_ω ≤ O(1), the O(1) of CRE (3.14). For |σ_j − σ_{j,0}| < r_{ω,j} around a real σ_{j,0} in the support, g_j|B| < 2O(1)ε_j, so |B′| ≤ 2C₁^{RG2}O(1)ε_j. So D2.1(4)'s conditions, (4)(f)'s margin condition and R-m's entailment hold with O(1) replaced by 2C₁^{RG2}O(1). These are smallness conditions on g_K, and R-m's alternative q₁ = p₀ becomes C₁/A₀ > 32C₁^{RG2}B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)/min(β,1).
  - D(m)(iii)'s ω-clause, m ≥ j+1 (polydiscs): joint analyticity in σ_j on the polydiscs |σ_j − σ_{j,0}| < r^{(m)}_{ω,j} := r_{ω,j}(1/2 + 2^{−(m−j)}) per bond, around real σ_{j,0} in the support. A tube |Im σ_j| < r would not control the real shift of R(w)σ off the real support. At k = 1 this matches the row domain, the polydisc 𝒰_c. So r^{(j+1)}_{ω,j} = r_{ω,j} at creation (r^{(1)}_{ω,0} = 1 = r_ω^{(1)}), decreasing to r_{ω,j}/2. These are the powers of 1/2 of CRE (2.36)–(2.39), used in the same way.
- *Propagation.* At the step k → k+1, k ≥ j+1, (4)(c) needs |σ_j − σ_{j,0}| + (e^{2c_G|log w|} − 1)(|σ_{j,0}| + |σ_j − σ_{j,0}|) < r^{(k)}_{ω,j} whenever |σ_j − σ_{j,0}| < r^{(k+1)}_{ω,j}. The two radii differ by r_{ω,j}2^{−(k+1−j)}. With e^{x} − 1 ≤ 2x for x ≤ 1 and |log w| ≤ C_wθ^{k−j−1}h_k, it suffices that 4c_GC_wθ^{k−j−1}h_k(|σ_{j,0}|/r_{ω,j} + 1) ≤ 2^{−(k+1−j)}, that is
  16c_GC_w(2θ)^{k−j−1}h_k(|σ_{j,0}|/r_{ω,j} + 1) ≤ 1.
  *At r_{ω,j} = g_j^{1/2} (D2.6 closure §2.2 (b)):* |σ_{j,0}|/r_{ω,j} ≤ O(1)r_jg_j^{−1/2}, and CRE (2.6) gives g_j ≥ g_k(1 + β′(k−j))^{−1/2} for g_k ≤ 1, so h_kr_jg_j^{−1/2} ≤ Cg_k^{1/2}(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁+p₀}(1 + β′(k−j))^{1/4}(1 + log(1+β′(k−j)))^{p₀}, which (2θ)^{k−j−1} makes small uniformly in k, K for g_K small.
  (The slot values at complex backgrounds are bounded by a total move inside the smallest polydisc, the bullet below, so no per-step reservation is needed. The factor (|σ_{j,0}| + |σ_j − σ_{j,0}|) ≤ |σ_{j,0}| + r_{ω,j} covers any point of the polydisc, the slot values included.)
  - θ = e^{−δ₀RM₁/32} ≤ 1/4 once RM₁ ≥ 64 log 2/δ₀. This holds for g_K small, since the layer widths are of the size of R_j and R_j ≥ (log g_j^{−2})^r (above), uniformly by CRE (2.5) and g_j ≤ (1+β₀)g_K. Then (2θ)^{k−j−1} ≤ 2^{−(k−j−1)}. (Reviewed: the Propagation bullet is correct for j ≥ 1 and j = 0, given R-dec, R-m and R-cpl₀.)
  - *j ≥ 1:* the condition is the bullet *At r_{ω,j} = g_j^{1/2}* above.
  - *j = 0.* |σ_{0,0}|/r_{ω,0} ≤ Cr = CA₁(log g₀^{−2})^{p₀}, and r is not bounded by a power of log g_k^{−2} alone. CRE (2.6) with n = k, m = 0 gives 1/g₀² ≤ 1/g_k² + β′k, so log g₀^{−2} ≤ log g_k^{−2} + log(1 + β′k) for g_k ≤ 1. Hence α_{1,k}r2^{−(k−1)} ≤ 2^{p₀}C₁A₁g_k(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁}[(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀} + sup_{k≥1}2^{−(k−1)}(log(1+β′k))^{p₀}]. The sup is finite, and the bracket times g_k(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁} is small for g_K small, uniformly in k and K. So the j = 0 condition holds.
  - The layer decay θ^{k−j−1} carries the propagation; the chain's gain L^{j+1−k} is cancelled on older layers by VAR (190)'s prefactor. With the decay, L ≥ 3 is not needed here. D2 keeps L ≥ 3, which V3's L > 11 odd implies anyway (V3 L3968).
  - *Constraint for D2.4.* If D2.4 uses D2.1(4)'s complex A_k-domain g_k|A_k| ≤ ε_H/C₁^{RG2} while ω-dependent factors are present, sup|𝐇| reaches 4B₀e^{16κ_s}ε_H, a fixed constant, and the j = 0 condition fails (ε_H r → ∞ as g₀ → 0). That radius must then be cut to g_k|A_k| ≤ cα_{1,k}/C₁^{RG2}.
  - *The slot values at complex backgrounds (a total-move bound; the inclusion derived into R-𝐇's domain 𝒰_j; reduced to R-𝐇, R-𝐇₀; the base points on Ω₁∖Ω₂ derived below at the root k = 1, reviewed in rounds 40 and 41 with the round-41 minors not re-reviewed, for k ≥ 2 by reading R-bg).* At the complex backgrounds of later steps the slot values σ_j(b) = ψ_b(A_j^{new}(b); 𝐇^{(j)}(b)) move with 𝐇^{(j)}, which is "an analytic function of the background field U_{k+1} restricted to Λ_{k+1}^c∩Λ_k" (CRE p.269, (3.22), at k = j; p.269 states no domain of analyticity); A_j^{new} and the frozen cutoff values θ_b stay real (θ_b, not ω_b, to keep them apart from the outer fields ω). The move is bounded in total, not per step: the polydiscs r^{(m)}_{ω,j} = r_{ω,j}(1/2 + 2^{−(m−j)}) of D(m)(iii)'s ω-clause all contain the polydisc of radius r_{ω,j}/2 about the real slot value, and the rotations R(w) map r^{(k+1)} into r^{(k)} (*Propagation*), so it suffices that every slot value at every complex background reached lies within r_{ω,j}/4 of its real value.
    - *R-𝐇 (analyticity and bound derived below from VAR Sect. G, Prop. 9 and CMP 98 Prop. 3; reviewed, this form not re-reviewed).* Let Y_j be the support of p.269's determining set of 𝐇^{(j)} (𝔅_{j+1}(Λ_{j+1}^c)|_{Ω_{j+1}} ∪ 𝔅_j(Λ_j)|_{Ω_{j+1}^c}), that is Λ_{j+1}ᶜ∩Λ_j as on p.269, which meets the layers n = j+1 and n = j. Let 𝒰_j be the set of 𝐔 on Y_j satisfying (2.34), (2.38) and (2.39) of Ũ^c_{j+1}(Y_j, α̃₀, α̃₁) on Y_j. On 𝒰_j the analytic extension of 𝐇^{(j)} of (3.22) is bounded on S_{j+1} by S_H := C_H(α_{0,j+1} + α_{1,j+1})e^{−R_j} (= C_H(1 + C₁/C₀)α_{0,j+1}e^{−R_j} under q₀ = q₁, SW closure §2). The derivation below uses only α_{i,n} → 0 uniformly as g_K → 0, so a fixed factor on the α's of 𝒰_j would only enter C_H. 𝐇^{(j)} is a function of the background U_{k+1} only (p.269), so the 𝐉-conditions (2.36) are vacuous for it. (2.35) and (2.37) constrain configurations built on a domain X, U_{p,X}(M^·(𝐔)) and J_{p,X}(M^·(𝐔)), and are left out of 𝒰_j: the bound below does not use them (the argument bound runs on 𝐔 and its averages, REG (1.65)).
      - *What is printed, and what is not.* Printed (Bałaban's): 𝐇^{(j)} is "an analytic function of the background field U_{k+1} restricted to Λ_{k+1}^c∩Λ_k, bounded on the set S_{k+1} by O(1)ε_k exp(−R_k)" (p.269, at k = j; no domain is stated); it is obtained from (3.17) "with similar bounds"; VAR Prop. 9 (p.309): 𝓗 "is an analytic function of B, and also of the external gauge field configuration U satisfying the regularity conditions (3.35)–(3.38) [5], or (1.7)–(1.9) [6]", and "its functional derivative (182) satisfies the inequalities (190)", and p.309: "These functions and operators can be extended to arbitrary, regular configurations U … They are analytic functions of these configurations, having the properties and bounds as described above"; PBG (3.37)–(3.38) is the complex class U′U, |A′| < α₁(L^jη)^{−1}; CRE p.261 calls Ũ^c "a more detailed and precise form, suitable for our inductive constructions, of the regularity conditions for background fields, introduced in [13–15]". Derived from these: since 𝓗(0) = 0, 𝓗(B) = ∫₀¹Σ_{y′}(δ𝓗/δB(y′))(tB)·B(y′)dt, so by (190) |𝓗(x)| ≤ O(1)(L^jη)^{−1}e^{−δ₀D/16}sup|B|, D the distance from x to supp B (half the rate of (190)'s e^{−δ₀d/8} pays the sum over y′, which is ≤ O(1)(16/δ₀)^d; the factor (L^{j′}η)^{−d} of (190) is the density of a functional derivative with respect to the scalar product with volume element (L^{j′}η)^d, and with that element in the y′-sum it cancels; read so, as for the propagators whose bounds (190) is said to copy, p.307); this has the form of p.268's bound B₃exp(−δLM₂R_{k+1}) times the bound on the argument (a paraphrase, not a quotation), at complex regular U, with δ₀/16 in place of p.268's δ (*Constants* for the comparison with e^{−R_j}). *The argument bound at complex 𝐔 (derived; not printed).* p.268 bounds the argument (1/i)log[M^·(U_{k+1})(M^·(Q*_{k+1}V_{k+1}))^{−1}] of 𝐇_{k+1,□′} by 44d²B₃ε_{k+1} from the real regularity |∂U_{k+1} − 1| < 2B₃ε_{k+1}(L^{−1}η)². The computation behind this "hence" is REG Lemma 1 (pp.79–80), iterated over the scales as in REG (1.65) (p.87; CRE p.247 cites the same lemma for the analogous step-1 bound, "by the same reasoning as in the proof of Lemma 1 [14]"). In the axial gauge, a link of the ratio is bounded by the plaquette deviations summed along block paths of at most (d−1)(L−1) bonds, plus the deviation of the block averages. *The gauge (derived).* This is for 𝐔 = U′U whose real base U is in the axial gauge of CRE p.248 relative to the layer's blocks (p.247: U_{1,□′} is taken in the axial gauge; REG Lemma 1's hypothesis (1.24)); 𝒰_j carries no gauge condition and is G-invariant (its conditions are), and at a point not in that relation the argument is O(1). Reduction: for a G-valued v on Y_j the axial averages are covariant, M^·(𝐔^v) = M^·(𝐔)^{v̄} with v̄ the restriction of v to the block centres of the level in question (CMP 98 (93), "for an arbitrary gauge transformation v", one level; iterated over the levels; the transports obey R(U^u(Γ_{y,x})) = R(u(y))R(U(Γ_{y,x}))R(u^{−1}(x)), PBG p.395 before (3.32)), and the minimisers obey U_k(V^υ) = U_k(V)^{ῡ} with ῡ the block-constant extension of the data transformation υ, "constant on blocks B^j(y), y ∈ Λ_j, and equal to υ(y)" (VAR (181), p.307, extended to G^c-valued V and υ by analyticity); the reference data, a lift of the top data, transform by the lift of v̄_top, block-constant on the top blocks (Q^{s*}(V^t) = (Q^{s*}V)^{t̂} by (1.3)). So the j-th averages of both minimisers at a bond b ∈ S_{j+1}^{(j)*} transform by block-constant transformations, which at b₋, b₊, away from the lower-level collar strips, both equal v(y_top(b±)), the value at the centre y_top(b±) of the top-level block of the region of Y_j containing b± (the (j+1)-block on Ω_{j+1}; the j-block on Λ_j∩Ω_{j+1}ᶜ, which a bond of S_{j+1}^{(j)*} leaving Ω_{j+1} reaches, and where both data families have top level j); in e^{i𝐇^{(j)}(b)} = V^{(j)}(b)(V^{(j)}_{Y_j}(b))^{−1} the factors at b₊ cancel and 𝐇^{(j)}(𝐔^v)(b) = v(y_top(b₋))𝐇^{(j)}(𝐔)(b)v(y_top(b₋))^{−1}, of the same modulus, the norm on 𝔤^c being Ad(G)-invariant (with v = 1 at the top block centres of both regions, as the axial gauge allows, it is equality). Every real U has an axial representative U^v with v G-valued (REG p.79: the conditions (1.19) fix one point in each orbit), and 𝐔^v = e^{iξvA′v^{−1}}U^v with |vA′v^{−1}| = |A′|; so 𝐔^v ∈ 𝒰_j is in the axial relation, and the bound at 𝐔^v is the bound at 𝐔. Its inputs at 𝐔 ∈ 𝒰_j: the curvature of 𝐔 and of its averages Ū^m at every level m, which is all REG (1.65) uses (p.87: CMP 98 Prop. 2 gives |∂Ū^m − 1| < 2α₀L^{2m}η² from |∂U − 1| < α₀η², and Lemma 1 is applied to these averages level by level; Lemma 1 is local, p.80, so it applies on the lower-level collar strips of CRE p.256 too, where the averages are smoother); for 𝐔 this is (2.34) with α_{0,n} at the weight where p.268 has 2B₃ε_{k+1} (*Unit check*), and p.268's own input is likewise the regularity of the background U_{k+1} alone; and the deviation of the averages, which CMP 98 bounds at complex data: Proposition 3 (p.36), posed for A ∈ 𝔤^c over a real V₀ with (109) (p.34), makes Q(V₀, A) = (1/i)log of the average analytic in A with Q = L(Q(V₀)A) + C(V₀,A), |(Q(V₀)A)_c| < e^{O(1)L²α₀}α₁ by (126) and |C| < C₁^{CMP}(Lα₁)² by (123), composed over the scales by CMP 98 (127)–(129) (each composition < 2α₁), so by (2.39) the deviation is O(α_{1,n}). REG's proof uses unitarity only in |XY − 1| ≤ |X − 1| + |Y − 1| and in the isometry of the conjugations R(·). For G^c-valued factors, |∏(1 + x_i) − 1| ≤ e^{Σ|x_i|} − 1, and over a block path of at most dL links each with phase below α_{1,n} by (2.39), ‖R(X)‖ ≤ ‖X‖‖X^{−1}‖ ≤ e^{2dLα_{1,n}} (R(X)Y = XYX^{−1}, CMP 98 (56), and submultiplicativity). So the bound holds with a factor 1 + O(d²α_{0,n} + dLα_{1,n}) ≤ 2, and the argument is O(α_{0,j+1} + α_{1,j+1}). *From the minimiser function to 𝐇^{(j)} at complex 𝐔 (derived; not printed).* The mean-value step bounds the minimiser function 𝓗 (p.268's 𝐇_{k+1,□′} of (3.17)); 𝐇^{(j)} of (3.22) is the logarithm of the ratio of j-th averages, reached on p.268 through (3.18) and CMP 98 (106)–(108), (159)–(163) at real data. At complex data: by (3.18), with p.269's replacement, e^{i𝐇^{(j)}(b)} = Φ_b(h) with h = L^{−1}η𝓗, Φ_b(h) = u(h)^{−1}(b₋)·exp iQ̃_j(h)·(R̄^ju(h))(b₊), as in (3.18). Here u = u_{j+1,□′} is the transformation from the Landau to the axial frame of the configuration e^{iη𝓗}U_{j+1,□′}, an analytic function of 𝓗 (VAR p.307: "This transformation is an analytic function of 𝓗"), with u(0) = 1, the reference being in the axial frame already; by CMP 98 (106) it is a product over the levels of conjugated ratios of the averages along the contours Γ_{x_{l+1},x_l}, so it depends on h only within the top-level blocks containing b₋, b₊. Q̃_j(h) depends on h only on the bonds of the blocks of b (CMP 98 p.34: the average at c involves only the bonds in B^k(c_−) ∪ B^k(c_+)). Let B_b be the bonds of these blocks; its diameter is O(L) in the units of (190). At X = 0 the data on the determining set agree, so the two minimisers agree ((3.17), first line), 𝓗 = 0 and the ratio is 1: (1/i)log Φ_b(0) = 0 at every background, real or complex. *Scale.* CMP 98 (127)–(129) are posed for U₁ = e^{iηA} with |A| < α₁, a field bound: after n averaging steps the phase is < 2α₁L^nη, so in phases a level-n average multiplies the input phase by up to 2L^n. With n_b the number of levels from 𝓗's lattice to b's lattice (n_b ≤ j: (3.18) takes the j-th average, and (106) uses the averages of the levels up to j along contours inside the (j+1)-blocks), (1/i)log Φ_b is a composition of CMP 98's averaged logarithms (Prop. 3 with (127)–(129), A ∈ 𝔤^c over the real base U of 𝐔 = e^{iξA′}U) and of the factors of u(h), each a near-unit analytic function of these averages (on the polydisc the phases of these averages are ≤ 2L^{n_b}ρ_b = c₃/2, so ‖R(u^{±1})‖ ≤ e^{O(dL)c₃} ≤ 2 once c₃ ≤ c/(dL); Prop. 3's composition condition 8C₁^{CMP}α₁ ≤ 1/3 ((129), (131)) is posed on the input field bound, which is c₃/4 in level-n_b units; with the safe upper bound c₃/2 it is c₃ ≤ 1/(12C₁^{CMP}); and the principal logarithm in (1/i)log Φ_b is defined while C_{d,L}c₃ < 1: three smallness conditions on c₃, met by taking c₃ smaller, which leaves the Schwarz factor M_b/ρ_b = C_{d,L}L^{n_b} unchanged and only tightens the g_K-threshold sup_{B_b}|h| < ρ_b). Hence (1/i)log Φ_b is analytic on the polydisc |h|_{B_b} < ρ_b := (c₃/4)L^{−n_b}, with modulus ≤ M_b := C_{d,L}ρ_bL^{n_b} (the averages' 2L^{n_b}ρ_b plus u's phases, O(dL) such terms per level). Schwarz's lemma (applied to λ ↦ (1/i)log Φ_b(λh/|h|_{B_b}), |λ| < ρ_b) gives |𝐇^{(j)}(b)| ≤ (M_b/ρ_b)|h|_{B_b} = C_{d,L}L^{n_b} sup_{b′∈B_b}|L^{−1}η𝓗(b′)|; sup_{B_b}|h| ≪ ρ_b by the mean-value step. The factor L^{n_b} is the ratio of b's length to the length of 𝓗's bonds, and it cancels against the field normalisation (L^{j_x}η)^{−1} of the mean-value step, j_x ∈ {j, j+1} the layer at the point, since n_b ≤ j ≤ j_x and L^{n_b}·L^{−1}η·(L^{j_x}η)^{−1} ≤ 1 in these units; so |𝐇^{(j)}(b)| ≤ C_{d,L}e^{O(L)δ₀}O(1)e^{−δ₀D/16}sup|B|, which is S_H on S_{j+1} (the e^{O(L)δ₀} from B_b's diameter). At real data this is p.268's form: (3.19) bounds the j-th average ratio by O(1) times the bound on 𝐇 itself. CRE p.277's "the exponential decay of the minimizing function 𝐇_k imply that this difference is still much greater than bounds on this function" concerns RG1's 𝐇_k, not 𝐇^{(j)}, and is not used. *Analyticity on 𝒰_j (derived).* 𝐇^{(j)} depends on 𝐔 only through the data M^·(𝐔) on its determining set D_j (by (3.17), first line, with p.269's replacement: V^{(j)} and V^{(j)}_{Y_j} are the j-th averages of the minimisers with these data; off minimisers F∘M is the definition of the extension, the convention of *Which function is deformed*), so the evaluation point's own third-order behaviour never enters. At 𝐔 = e^{iξA′}U, M^·(𝐔) = V′V₀ with V₀ = M^·(U) real, satisfying VAR (7) by the real part of (2.34) and CMP 98's averages, and V′ analytic in A′ with |(1/i)log V′| < 2α_{1,n} at the scale weight (CMP 98 Prop. 3, (127)–(129)). So V′ satisfies VAR (172), |V′ − 1| < C₁^{VAR}ε₁, with ε₁ taken as the larger of V₀'s curvature bound in (7) and 3α_{1,n}/C₁^{VAR} (|V′ − 1| ≤ e^{|B′|} − 1 ≤ (3/2)|B′| for |B′| < 2α_{1,n} ≤ 1/2), which is ≤ a₁ for g_K small. VAR Sect. G (pp.305–307): U_k(V′V₀) is "an analytic function of V′, for V′ with values in a small neighborhood of the identity in G^c", and U_k(V′V₀)U_k(V₀)^{−1} in the Landau gauge is e^{iη𝓗(B)}, 𝓗 satisfying (19)–(21) at ε₂ = B₅ε₁ ((173)). The reference data are the lift of the top data: Q^{s*} acts bondwise (CRE (1.3): (Q^{s*}V)(b) is V(c) on the bonds b between B(c₋) and B(c₊) of the block bond c, and 1 inside the blocks), so Q^{s*}(V′V₀) = Q^{s*}(V′)Q^{s*}(V₀), and the averages of a lift are the lift, M^m(Q^{s*}V) = Q^{s*}_{top→m}V at every level m (the averaging loops of CMP 98 (110), (113), p.34, Γ_{c,x} ∪ (−c): the contours inside the blocks carry 1, the crossing between the blocks carries V(c) and −c carries V(c)^{−1}, so every loop is 1 and the average at c is V(c); by induction over the levels); hence the reference data at V′V₀ are V″D_ref(V₀) with V″ = Q^{s*}(V′) exactly, analytic in A′ with |(1/i)log V″| < 2α_{1,n}, and (127)–(129) are not needed for this concentrated input; the same applies to the reference minimiser. The j-th averages of both minimisers are analytic by CMP 98 Prop. 3 over the real bases U_k(V₀), applied in the Landau frame, where the ratio is e^{iη𝓗} with 𝓗 field-small by VAR (19) (in the axial frame it is only phase-small, REG p.80), and carried to the axial frame by u, analytic (above). The logarithm of their ratio is analytic. So 𝐇^{(j)} is analytic in A′ on 𝒰_j, in fact wherever the real part satisfies (2.34) and |A′| is below the fixed constants of CMP 98 Prop. 3 and VAR (172); no gradient or third-order condition on A′ is used. *Complex external fields in the mean-value step.* There the external configuration is the reference minimiser at complex data, U₀ := U_k(V″V₀′) = e^{iη𝓗}U_k(V₀′) (Landau frame of VAR Sect. G). Two links make the mean-value step's function the one bounded: (i) U_k(XV″V₀′)U₀^{−1} in the Landau gauge is e^{iη𝓗(X;U₀)} at complex V″ too, since both sides are analytic in V″ near the real slice (p.307; Prop. 9's analyticity in the external U composed with V″ ↦ U₀) and agree there (Prop. 9 at the real data V″V₀′), so they agree by the identity theorem; (ii) the axial→Landau passage for U₀ is a G^c-valued transformation u_L, under which the operators and 𝓗 conjugate by PBG (3.33)–(3.34) (G′, R, Δ_a, G at U^u are R(u)(·)R(u^{−1}); extended to G^c-valued u by analyticity, as VAR p.307 does for (181)), covered by p.309's extension to arbitrary regular U ("All the above considerations and properties are valid for this function also", the considerations including p.307's transformation), with conjugation cost ‖R(u_L^{±1})‖ ≤ 2 (derived): u_L is the tree-gauge transformation to the axial representative, unique in the orbit (REG p.79, (1.19)), fixed level by level by CMP 98 (77), (87) from the block-path transports of the averages of U₀ relative to those of U_k(V₀′); at level n a path has ≤ d(L−1) block bonds, whose averaged phases are ≤ 2L^nη sup|𝓗| ≤ 2ε₂L^{n−j} by (127)–(131) and VAR (19), so the paths contribute ≤ Σ_{n<j}d(L−1)2ε₂L^{n−j} ≤ 2dε₂ to the phase of u_L, the inverse block averages (105) of each level's factor in (106) another ≤ 2dε₂(1 + 4dε₂) ((105) is the exponential of the average of log T_x, T_x a product of ≤ d(L−1) G^c-valued near-unit factors with summed phase s < 2dε₂, so |T_x − 1| ≤ e^s − 1 and |log T_x| ≤ −log(2 − e^s) ≤ s(1 + 2s) for s ≤ 1/4), total ≤ 4dε₂ + 8d²ε₂², and ‖R(u_L^{±1})‖ ≤ e^{8dε₂ + 16d²ε₂²} ≤ 2 for ε₂ ≤ (log 2)/(10d) (ε₂ = B₅ε₁ → 0 with g_K); u_L is analytic in 𝓗 as a finite product of the averages (Prop. 3), which is p.307's statement. It is the construction of u(h) above, applied to U₀. Its real part satisfies PBG (3.35)–(3.36) with α₀ = B₃ε₁ by VAR Thm 1 (9)–(10) (|A|, |∇A|, |∂*∂A| < B₃Mε₁(L^jη)^{−1,−2,−3}), and its complex part 𝓗 satisfies PBG (3.37)–(3.38) with α₁ = B₅ε₁ by VAR (19) (|A|, |∇A|, |D*DA| < ε₂(L^jη)^{−1,−2,−3}). So it lies in PBG's complex class (3.35)–(3.38), where VAR p.309 and Prop. 9 print the analyticity of 𝓗 and the bounds (190). The bound is derived (not re-reviewed).
      - *Constants.* C_H = C(d, L, G, δ₀, B₃, B₅, β₀, C₀^{CRE}, C₁^{CRE}, C₁^{CMP}, c₃) with (190)'s O(1): δ₀ through the y′-sum, CMP 98's C₁^{CMP} and c₃ through Prop. 3, B₅ through VAR (173), G through the norm on 𝔤^c, CRE's C₀ and C₁ (p.259) through the layer-n = j and α₁/α₀ comparisons, L also through the factor C_{d,L}L^{n_b} of the analytic bound before its cancellation. p.269's O(1) is not an input of this route. The decay: the argument is supported in p.268's boundary layer of width 2LM₁ along ∂Λ_{j+1}, and S_{j+1} lies beyond p.269's layer of LMR_{j+1}-cubes, so the distance from S_{j+1} to that support is ≥ LMR_{j+1} − 2LM₁ ≥ ½LMR_{j+1} in p.268's units, in which the (j+1)-blocks have side L (CRE p.246: "cubes of the size LMR₁ … For the more natural scale L^{−1} these are … MR₁-cubes"; p.256: Ω_j are unions of MR_j-cubes of T_{L^{−j}}), since M ≥ 4M₁ and R_{j+1} ≥ 1 (CRE p.245: "M will be chosen much larger than M₂", M₂ > M₁). Y_j's second boundary component, the collar strips along ∂Λ_j (layer n = j), carries argument too; it is farther from S_{j+1} ⊂ Ω_{j+1}: by CRE (3.5) with p.265's B^{j+1}(P¹_{j+1}) = (P′^∼_{j+1})ᶜ∩(Z_j′^{∼5})ᶜ, Z_j = Λ_jᶜ, Ω_{j+1} is separated from Λ_jᶜ by eight layers of LMR_{j+1}-cubes, so the first component's bound covers it. (190)'s distance is that of [3] = PRT2 (2.46) (p.231; in the local set), which counts the blocks of the layer's own lattice: on Ω_{j+1} these are (j+1)-blocks, so the separation is ≥ ½MR_{j+1} in its units (a path through the layer n = j counts j-blocks, L per (j+1)-block, so it is longer there), and at rate δ₀/16 the decay factor is e^{−(δ₀/32)MR_{j+1}} up to O(1). By CRE (2.9), R_j ≤ (L+1)R_{j+1}, so e^{−(δ₀/32)MR_{j+1}} ≤ e^{−R_j} once (δ₀/32)M ≥ L+1: a condition on M, which Bałaban chooses last, after δ₀, L and M₂ (at j = 0 the condition δM ≥ L+1 below). For comparison, p.268 prints B₃exp(−δLM₂R_{k+1})44d²B₃ε_{k+1} ≤ 44d²B₃²(1+β₀)exp(−R_k)ε_k with its own δ. On the layer n = j Ũ^c_{j+1}'s curvature bound is (1−β/2)α_{0,j} at the scale-j weight, and α_{0,j} ≤ (1+β₀)²α_{0,j+1} (CRE (2.6), (2.7), (2.28)), absorbed into C_H, as is the (1+β). The ratio α_{0,j+1}/ε_j ≤ (C₀/A₀)(1+β₀)²(log g_j^{−2})^{q₀−p₀} (CRE (2.4), (2.6), (2.7), (2.28), p.259). No counterexample is known.
    - *Which function is deformed.* As for all of Bałaban's old terms (RG1 (3.10): 𝐄^{(j)}(X, exp iξ𝐀U_{k+1})), the slot value at a step-k background 𝐔 is 𝐔 ↦ 𝐇^{(j)}(𝐔|_{D_j}), 𝐇^{(j)} evaluated at the restriction of the current fine background to its determining set D_j := 𝔅_{j+1}(Λ_{j+1}^c)|_{Ω_{j+1}} ∪ 𝔅_j(Λ_j)|_{Ω_{j+1}^c} (p.269; the convention of CRE (2.41)(i)–(ii)). D_j meets two layers: Λ_{j+1}ᶜ∩Ω_{j+1} ⊆ Ω_{j+1}∖Ω_{j+2} (by Ω_{j+2} ⊆ Λ_{j+1}), the layer n = j+1, and Λ_j∩Ω_{j+1}ᶜ ⊆ Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}, the layer n = j. Here U is the real point of the step's real data, in the same frame as 𝐔 (the frame is D2.2(4)(d)'s, derived at every k by (d′), reviewed in rounds 42–44). At the real configuration it is the real slot value, by D2.0(5)(c)(α) at the steps j+1, …, k (U_i(V^{(i)}) = U_{i+1}); this covers the slot values in insertions left untouched at every step, which (4)(e) evaluates at (U_{i+1}, ω). *Merged insertions.* Inside a group merged at a step i ∈ [j+1, k−1], 𝐇^{(j)} was evaluated at that step's evaluation point 𝐔_i = e^{iη(𝐇_i(s,tB′) + a″)}U_{i+1}, and at later steps it is evaluated at such composite points, not at 𝐔|_{D_j} of step k. Each is a step-i evaluation point whose base lies in D(i+1)(iii)'s domain, so (4)(f)'s chain condition at step i places it in Ũ^c_i, and *The inclusion* below with i in place of k places its restriction in R-𝐇's domain. So every point at which 𝐇^{(j)} is ever evaluated, at any step i ≥ j+1, is covered, and *The total move* stands unchanged. A slot value is a substituted value, not an insertion of D(k): its analyticity comes from R-𝐇 and the inclusion below, not from D(k)(iii). The slot value depends on 𝐔 on both layers n = j and n = j+1.
    - *The inclusion (derived; reviewed, this form not re-reviewed).* For k ≥ max(j+1, 2), D(k)(iii)'s domain is CRE's complex space Ũ^c_k (amended in this item, *What it closes*), and every evaluation point of the step k → k+1, 𝐔 = e^{iη(𝐇_k(s,tB′) + a″)}U_{k+1} with a″ in D(k+1)(iii)'s domain, lies in it by (4)(f)'s chain condition, the same condition every old factor needs. Claim: every evaluation point 𝐔 satisfies 𝐔|_{Y_j} ∈ 𝒰_j, R-𝐇's domain. *No choice of X.* Y_j = Λ_{j+1}ᶜ∩Λ_j is the whole collar of every step-(j+1) large-field component, so it lies in no single old term's localisation domain. It need not: D(k)(iii)'s domain and (4)(f)'s margins are posed per bond and per cube on each Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} (the loop's factors are not localised in Bałaban's X ∈ 𝐃_k, D(k)(iii)), so the pointwise conditions and (2.38) hold on every bond and cube and restrict to Y_j directly; (2.35) and (2.37) are not in 𝒰_j. Check, condition by condition of CRE p.261: on a layer n ≤ j+1, (2.34) (curvature, α_{0,n}) and (2.39) (A′ and its gradient, α_{1,n}) are pointwise and carry, for the space of index i, the factor (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(i−n)})), which is nonincreasing in i (read on p.261); so Ũ^c_k's conditions on the layers n = j+1 (factor (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(k−j−1)})) ≤ 1, Ũ^c_{j+1}'s top layer) and n = j (factor (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(k−j)})) ≤ 1 − β/2, Ũ^c_{j+1}'s) imply Ũ^c_{j+1}'s. (2.38) is the condition on the real configuration, BCMα_{0,n} with no index factor, on cubes of size CML^nξ, or CM inside the top domain, the same physical size in both spaces. The cube families match on Y_j: Ũ^c_{j+1}'s top domain uses all CM-cubes in Ω_{j+1}, Ũ^c_k's layer n = j+1 the cubes □ ⊂ Ω_{j+1}∖Ω_{j+3} with □∩Ω_{j+2}ᶜ ≠ ∅; on Y_j these agree, since a CM-cube meeting Ω_{j+2}ᶜ avoids Ω_{j+3} when its diameter √d·CM is below dist(Ω_{j+2}ᶜ, Ω_{j+3}). VAR (1)'s RM₁ is not the right gap here (with R = R₁ it is below √d·CM, M being chosen last); CRE p.256 gives the boundaries of consecutive Ω's at distance ≥ 2MR_i in the units of the layer's own lattice ("the distance between their boundaries is at least equal to 2MR_j"), so dist(Ω_{j+2}ᶜ, Ω_{j+3}) ≥ 2MR_{j+2}L in step-(j+1) units, and M cancels: the condition is √d·C < 2LR_{j+2}, implied by L > √d·C/2 since R_{j+2} ≥ 1. This is a lower bound on L given (2.38)'s cube constant C, which p.261 does not fix (only B is called absolute); with C fixed before L, as a constant of the space's definition, it holds for L ≥ L₀ = 5000 when C < 5000; the layer n = j and n = j−1 families (the latter reaches into Y_j∩Ω_j) coincide, with no index factor. So every evaluation point lies in 𝒰_j on Y_j, and |𝐇^{(j)}(𝐔|_{Y_j})| ≤ S_H there, by R-𝐇. At k = 1 (only j = 0, below) D(1)(iii) is V3's sup-only domain, not contained in Ũ^c_1; there only the points reached at the step 1 → 2 count, and they lie in Ũ^c_1 on D₀ by (4)(f) at k = 1 (the evaluation points e^{iη(𝐇_1(s,tB′) + a″)}U_2), with the gradient condition from (6)(e)'s *The consumer still gets what it needs* via R-adm, the curvature condition (2.34) from its check, both written for general k and so at k = 1, and the base-point conditions for U₂ on D₀ from (6)(e)'s *The base point on Ω₁∖Ω₂* (on Ω₂ from *Derived instead of assumed*).
      - *The base point on Ω₁∖Ω₂ (derived at every k: the root k = 1 reviewed in rounds 40 and 41, the round-41 minors applied and not re-reviewed; k ≥ 2 by the response chain of *For k ≥ 2* below, derived and reviewed in round 45, it1-d2-base-20261026-r3, no fatal or serious finding, minors m1–m7 applied and not re-reviewed).* The cited item covers U_{k+1} on Ω_{k+1} only. D₀ := Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ, the set on which U₁(Ω₁∩Λ₁^c, V), and hence 𝐇_{1,Ax}'s background, depends (p.251 states this for U₁(Ω₁∩Λ₁^c, V); for 𝐇_{1,Ax} it says "depends on its argument restricted to a small neighborhood of ∂Λ₁"; D₀ as 𝐇_{1,Ax}'s background set is inferred, as on p.269), lies in Ω₁∖Ω₂, since Ω₂ ⊆ Λ₁. On Ω₁∖Ω₂ CRE p.277 says only that there is "no regularity improvement" of U_{k+1}, and CRE (2.17) at k = 1 bounds the local minimisers U_{1,□}(V₁), not U_{k+1}; V4's lem:c2-plaquette-deviation covers only plaquettes within 3R_c of I. What gives the real part of (2.34) at n = 1 and (2.38) for U₂ on all of Ω₁∖Ω₂, collar included, at the root k = 1, is VAR Theorem 1 (p.279) for U₂ itself, the route by which Bałaban obtains U₁'s existence ("By Theorem 1 [15]", p.248) and U_{k+1}'s regularity (p.267 (3.12): "The restrictions on the fields V_k, V_{k+1} on Ω_{k+1}^∼, and Theorem 1 from [15] imply, that U_{k+1} satisfies the following regularity condition"; "[15]" is the transcription's correction of the printed "[16]"; p.268). For k ≥ 2 the layer n = 1 is an older layer of the step k → k+1, on which U_{k+1}'s membership is reading R-bg (gaps block l.183; CRE p.276 l.10–12: the regularity of U_{k+1} "on a neighborhood of Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}" is "the same as for U_n-configurations", p.277 l.3–8: "no regularity improvement", the powers of 1/2 as the room for the shifts; Bałaban's, accepted; not R-m, which concerns the complex margins of (2.36)–(2.39); the quantitative form, bounding the responses to the data changes on Ω_{m+1}, m = 2, …, k, on the layer n = 1 by R-dec's decay against the root's margin, is *For k ≥ 2* below, so the reading is no longer used on the layer n = 1; round 41 S-A); the root on the layer n = 1 is this item at k = 1. Withdrawn (round 40 F1): the claim that the global Theorem 1 gives the layer n = 1 at every k with a k-independent ε^V. Its hypothesis (7) takes one ε for all layers, so the constant certifiable from the characteristic functions is 2(1+β₀)max_{n≤k+1}ε_n, which CRE (2.8) (p.256; ε_n ≤ (1+β₀)(n−m)^{1/2}ε_m for m < n only, while R-cpl₀ bounds g_m by g_n for m ≤ n) bounds only by 2(1+β₀)²√k ε₁, so B₃ε^V_k < (1−β)α_{0,1} cannot be verified this way when g₁ ≪ g_K; a localisation of Theorem 1 to layers 0–2 at general k is not written (its inner data, the lift of V_n for n ≥ 3 or U_{k+1} on Ω₃, carry ε_n or the a priori ε^V_k, not O(ε₁)). *The problem at k = 1.* U₂ is the critical point of the action with U = V₀ on Ω₁ᶜ and the determining conditions of 𝔅₂ on Ω₁ (the minimal-orbit map (2.12), p.256, with p.266 (3.10); (2.1)–(2.3) on p.255; CRE (1.12) is the step 0 → 1 problem for U₁). Only plaquettes meeting Ω₁ involve free variables, so it is VAR's problem (5)–(6) on Ω₀^{VAR} := Ω₁^∼ with Λ₀^{VAR} = Ω₁^∼∖Ω₁ carrying the fixed data V₀, Λ₁^{VAR} = Ω₁∖Ω₂ carrying V = V₁ and Λ₂^{VAR} = Ω₂ carrying V₂ (p.279: "B^j(Λ_j) ∪ B^{j+1}(Λ_{j+1}) = Ω_j∖Ω_{j+2}"); VAR (1) holds (dist(Ω₁^{∼c}, Ω₁) is one layer of Ω₁'s cubes and dist(Ω₁ᶜ, Ω₂) ≥ 2LMR₁η by (1.20), both above R₁^VM₁ in the layer's units); VAR counts the plaquettes with at least one corner in Ω₀^{VAR}, and the fine plaquettes reaching one spacing beyond Ω₁^∼ are covered by p.248's χ₀ on the cubes □ ⊂ Ω₁^∼, which test p ⊂ □^∼; the omitted action terms are constants in the free variables, so the critical points are those of (2.12), and U₂ is the one in Theorem 1's class (6) by reading R-reg (V4 (H4): "regular" in (2.12) is membership in VAR's class (6) at any radius allowed by Theorem 1), Theorem 1's uniqueness of the critical orbit in (6) for B₃ε^V ≤ ε₀ ≤ a₀ identifying the orbits, with the criticality-and-uniqueness argument of V4 sec:c3-9-1 (prop:c3-9-1, which treats U₁ at the step 0 → 1) as the template at the step 1 → 2; Bałaban's identification of U_{k+1} with Theorem 1's minimal configuration is his (p.248, p.267), accepted. The 𝐑-operation changes the characteristic functions (p.258); by R-h (l.326–327) its basic step acts on no component of the step map for k ≤ N, and that no other part of 𝐑 acts for k ≤ N is D2.7's check (l.326); on the terms of the form (2.21) the support carries the printed characteristic functions, and any other 𝐑-modified term is left to D2.7. *Hypothesis (7) on the support.* (7) asks |(∂V)(p′) − 1| < ε^V for every block plaquette of every layer, mixed plaquettes at a layer boundary taken with the averages V̄ (p.278). Layer n = 1: on the support χ₁(Ω₁) of (2.17) at k = 1 holds (V4 keeps it outside the A_e integral in eq:native-source-explicit-outer-operation and reads it in the measurability paragraph of sec:branch-data and in the proof of lem:c2-plaquette-deviation: χ₁(□′) = 1{V ∈ 𝒱_ax(□′)}·1{sup_{p⊂□′^∼}|U_{1,□′}(V)(∂p) − id| < ε₁L^{−2}} for every cube □′ of Ω₁'s decomposition); V = M(U_{1,□′}) on the block bonds of □′^∼ by 𝐁₁(□′^{∼4})'s conditions, so CMP 98 Prop. 2 (|∂Ū − 1| < 2α₀L²η² from |∂U − 1| < α₀η², read in l.972) gives |∂V(p¹) − 1| < 2ε₁ for the block plaquettes p¹ of □′^∼, hence on Ω₁. The mixed plaquettes at ∂Ω₁ are block plaquettes whose fine-side bonds carry V̄₀; δ(V̄₀V^{−1}) ((1.11), (1.14)–(1.15), (2.21)) identifies V̄₀ with V on those block bonds, so they are block plaquettes of V near ∂Ω₁ inside Ω₁^∼, bounded by the same 2ε₁ (the cubes □′^∼ reach them); Prop. 2 on V₀, which gives only 2L²ε₀, is not needed. Layer n = 0 (Ω₁^∼∖Ω₁): "On the set Ω₁^∼ we have only the small field characteristic functions" and χ₀ "localized in Ω₁^∼ are equal to 1" (p.248; V4 (B1)(i), (B3)(i)), so |∂V₀ − 1| < ε₀ on the fine plaquettes there, with ε₀ ≤ (1+β₀)²ε₁ by (2.8) at (m, n) = (0, 1). Layer n = 2 (the top layer Ω₂ at k = 1): χ₂(Ω₂) of (2.17) and the determining conditions 𝐁₂ on Ω₂^∼ (p.267, "The restrictions on the fields V_k, V_{k+1} on Ω_{k+1}^∼"), so the block plaquettes of V₂ and the mixed plaquettes at ∂Ω₂ (block plaquettes of V̄₁ = V₂ by the δ at level 2) are bounded through Prop. 2 as on layer 1 by 2ε₂ ≤ 2(1+β₀)ε₁ ((2.8) at (1, 2)). So (7) holds with ε^V := 2(1+β₀)²ε₁, and ε^V ≤ a₁ for g_K small. *Conclusion (8): (2.34).* The minimal configuration lies in 𝔘₂({Ω_j}, B₃ε^V), that is (p.278 (2)) |∂U₂ − 1| < B₃ε^Vη²(L^nη)^{−2} on every layer n, in particular on Ω₁∖Ω₂ with the scale-1 weight η²(Lη)^{−2} = L^{−2} (η = ξ the fine spacing, as in *Unit check*). (2.34) at n = 1 for the space of index j ≥ 1 asks (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(j−1)}))α_{0,1}L^{−2} ≥ (1−β)α_{0,1}L^{−2}, so membership needs B₃ε^V < (1−β)α_{0,1}: as in *Derived instead of assumed*, q₀ > p₀ for g_K small, or q₀ = p₀ with C₀/A₀ > 2(1+β₀)²B₃/(1−β). B₃ depends on d and L only (p.279; V4 (H4)). *Conclusions (9)–(10): (2.38).* Write R₁^V for VAR's fixed constant R₁ of (1) (p.277), distinct from CRE's scale R₁. Theorem 1 gives, for every cube □ of its class of side 2M^VL^jη (M^V a multiple of R₁^VM₁, M^V ≤ M(ε^V) = R₁^VM₁a₁/ε^V, with □̃ of side (2M^V + 4R₁^VM₁)L^jη inside Ω_j∖Ω_{j+2} and not inside Ω_{j+1}∖Ω_{j+2}), a gauge u on a neighbourhood of □ with U^{u^{−1}} = e^{iηA}, |A| < B₃M^Vε^V(L^jη)^{−1}, |∇A| < B₃M^Vε^V(L^jη)^{−2}. (2.38) at n = 1 asks this on the cubes □ ⊂ Ω₁∖Ω₃, □∩Ω₂ᶜ ≠ ∅, of side CMLξ, with Lξ|A|, (Lξ)²|∇A| < BCMα_{0,1}; at k = 1, Ω₃ = ∅. Covering: take M^V₁ := the least multiple of R₁^VM₁ with 2M^V₁ ≥ CM + 2R₁^VM₁ (j = 1) and M^V₀ := L(M^V₁ + 2R₁^VM₁) (j = 0); both ≤ M(ε^V) for g_K small. A (2.38)-cube □ at distance d from Ω₁ᶜ lies in a j = 1 cube □¹ (side 2M^V₁Lη ≥ CMLη + 2R₁^VM₁Lη) with □̃¹ ⊂ Ω₁ whenever d ≥ 4R₁^VM₁Lη + (2M^V₁ − CM)Lη =: d₁ (place □¹ ⊇ □ away from Ω₁ᶜ, with the alignment slack of one block in the extra 2R₁^VM₁Lη); □̃¹ ⊂ Ω₁∖Ω₃ = Ω₁ and □̃¹ ⊄ Ω₂∖Ω₃ = Ω₂ since □ meets Ω₂ᶜ (for general k the disjointness from Ω₃ would come from Ω₃ ⊆ Λ₂ ⊆ Ω₂^{∼−2}, (3.5), (1.20)). For d < d₁, □ lies in a j = 0 cube □⁰ of side 2M^V₀η ≥ 2M^V₁Lη + 4R₁^VM₁Lη, placed towards Ω₁ᶜ so that dist(□⁰, Ω₁ᶜ) ≤ max(0, d − (2M^V₀ − CML)η) < 2R₁^VM₁η (d − (2M^V₀ − CML)η < 0 by d < d₁ = 2M^V₀η − CMLη, and the alignment costs at most M₁η), that is □̃⁰ meets Ω₁ᶜ, and □̃⁰ ⊂ Ω₁^∼∖Ω₂ since dist(Ω₂, Ω₁ᶜ) ≥ 2LMR₁η exceeds □̃⁰'s diameter (Ω₂ ⊆ Λ₁ ⊆ Ω₁^{∼−2} by (1.20), with Λ₁ = Ω₁^{∼−2}∩(R₁′^∼)ᶜ on p.251; the comparison is 2R₁ > √d(C + 9R₁^VM₁/M), true for g small since R₁ ≥ (log g₁^{−2})^r); so □⁰ is in the j = 0 class. On □ ⊆ □⁰ the j = 0 bound reads Lη|A| < B₃M^V₀Lε^V and (Lη)²|∇A| < B₃M^V₀L²ε^V, on □ ⊆ □¹ the j = 1 bound Lη|A| < B₃M^V₁ε^V, (Lη)²|∇A| < B₃M^V₁ε^V; both are ≤ BCMα_{0,1} for g_K small, since M^V₀, M^V₁, L, B₃ are fixed and ε^V/α_{0,1} → 0 when q₀ > p₀, while for q₀ = p₀ the binding case (j = 0, ∇A) needs C₀/A₀ > 2L³(1+β₀)²B₃(CM/2 + 4R₁^VM₁)/(BCM) (M^V₁ ≤ CM/2 + 2R₁^VM₁; B is (2.38)'s absolute constant: under q₀ > p₀ any B > 0 works, under q₀ = p₀ it enters this condition); the gauge u is defined on a neighbourhood of □ ⊇ □∩X. This covers the collar Ω₁∖Ω₁^{∼−1} (the j = 0 cubes) without a separate argument. *What is Bałaban's, what is derived.* Theorem 1 and its application to U_{k+1} are Bałaban's (VAR; CRE pp.248, 267–268); the check of (7) on the support from the printed characteristic functions, the constant ε^V, the cube covering, and the restriction to the problem on Ω₁^∼ (reading R-reg with V4 sec:c3-9-1) are derived. The result: U₂|_{Ω₁∖Ω₂} satisfies the real parts of (2.34) and (2.38) at n = 1, so at k = 1 the j = 0 base point on D₀ and the j = 1 base points on the layer n = 1 lie in R-𝐇₀'s and R-𝐇's domains; for k ≥ 2 U_{k+1}'s membership on the layer n = 1 is *For k ≥ 2* below, with this item as its root, and the layer-2 part of 𝒰₁'s domain (Ω₂∖Ω₃ at k ≥ 2; at k = 1 l.922's p.268 item) is *Every older layer* below. *For k ≥ 2 (the response chain; derived; reviewed in round 45, minors applied).* Notation: U_m := U_{𝔅_m}(V₀|Γ₀, …, V_{m−1}|Γ_{m−1}, V_m) is CRE's minimal configuration at the step m → m+1 before the fluctuation integral, a function of the step-m variables with V_m on all of Ω_m (p.256 (2.12); p.266 (3.10)); U₂ is the root's configuration above. At the step m → m+1, (3.10)–(3.11) give U_{m+1} = U_{𝔅_m}(V₀|Γ₀, …, V_{m−1}|Γ_{m−1}, V_m|Γ_m, V^{(m)}|Ω_{m+1}) with V^{(m)} = M^m(U_{m+1}) ("This minimum is given by V^{(k)} = M^k(U_{k+1}), where the minimal configuration U_{k+1} is determined by the sequence of domains {Ω_{k+1}, Ω_k, …}, and the corresponding sequence of fields {V_{k+1}|Γ_{k+1}, V_k|Γ_k, …}") and V_m = e^{iA′_m}V^{(m)} on Ω_{m+1}, |A′_m| < c_Aε_m on the support by (3.14) (c_A := the constant of |A′_m| < c_Aε_m, the supremum over m of (3.14)'s 2δ_m/ε_m + (4dL)²(1+β₀)B₃ times the logarithm's factor 1 + o(1) in A′_m = (1/i)log V′_m, finite since (3.14) reads "= O(1)ε_k"; round 45 m7).
