So U_m and U_{m+1} are the minimal configurations of one determining set 𝔅_m at level-m data differing by A′_m on Ω_{m+1} only, and VAR Prop. 9's response function (the map's 𝐇_m, Step 4 of (d′) with B′ = A′_m) gives U_m = (e^{iη𝐇_m(A′_m)}U_{m+1})^{g_m} for a gauge transformation g_m (the minimal orbit; p.309, 𝓗 at the base U_{m+1}, which obeys PBG (3.35)–(3.38) as at l.972 and lies in VAR's class with the crude constant ε^V_m := max(2(1+β₀)max_{i≤m+1}O(L²)ε_i, (3/2)c_Aε_m/C₁^{VAR}) ≤ a₁ by the global Theorem 1 for U_{m+1} ((7) is monotone in its constant), its hypothesis (7) from p.256's regularity of V_i on Γ_i and (3.12) on Ω_{m+1}; the crude constant enters Prop. 9's hypotheses only, not (190)'s bound, whose constants are uniform). Prop. 9's domain is VAR (172), p.305, |V′ − 1| < C₁^{VAR}ε^V_m, and V′ = e^{iA′_m} lies in it by the choice of ε^V_m, as at l.972, so the path t ↦ e^{itA′_m}V^{(m)} needs no splitting (round 45 m1); Bałaban applies (190) to sources of size ε_m directly on p.266 ((3.6)–(3.7)). *The response on the layer n = 1.* The source A′_m is supported on Ω_{m+1} ⊆ Ω₃, and the walk from Ω_{m+1} to the layer n = 1 crosses the layers 2, …, m, of widths ≥ 2MR_i in level-i cubes (p.256; Ω_{i+1} ⊆ Λ_i by (2.1)). R-dec (l.219; at the real base with (190)'s full rate) gives on the blocks B¹(y) of the layer n = 1: sup|𝐇_m(A′_m)|, (Lη)|∇_{U_{m+1}}𝐇_m(A′_m)| ≤ c_RL^{m−1}∏_{i=2}^{m}θ_i·c_Aε_m, with θ_i := e^{−δ₀R_iM₁/32} the factor of the layer i (R-dec read per layer, as part of the reading: each layer i crossed contributes e^{−δ₀R_iM₁/32}, by VAR (1) in the layer's own units (L^iη)^{−1} and the additivity across layers of (190)'s distance d(y, y′), whose definition VAR p.288 takes from PRT2 [3]; the layer widths are of the size of R_i, l.965; the per-layer form is load-bearing, since with the least R alone θ is controlled by g_K only and Θ/α_{0,1} → 0 is not uniform in K when g₁ ≪ g_K; round 45 m2) and c_R R-dec's constant (the letters chosen apart from CMP 98's c₃, c₄ of l.661–662 and l.972–973). With η = L^{−m} at the step m, the bond change on the layer n = 1 is η|𝐇_m| ≤ c_Ac_RL^{−1}∏_{i=2}^{m}θ_i·ε_m, and the plaquette change is ≤ 2η²|∇𝐇_m| + O(η²|𝐇_m|² + η|𝐇_m||∂U − 1|) ≤ 2c_Ac_RL^{−2}∏_{i=2}^{m}θ_i·ε_m(1 + o(1)) (the plaquette identity: e^{iηX}U around p equals the product of the four transports of e^{iηX(b)} along p times U(∂p), the exponents summing to η² times the covariant curl of X up to the holonomy defects, which carry |∂U − 1|). *The chain.* For any choice of the fluctuation variables A′_m in the supports of the steps m = 2, …, k (such a choice exists wherever the step-(k+1) term is non-zero, and the root applies to U₂ at step-2 data with χ₂(Ω₂) = 1; round 45 m5), U_{k+1} = (e^{iηX_{k+1}}U₂)^{g} with e^{iηX_{k+1}} the product of the conjugates of e^{−iη𝐇_m(A′_m)}, m = 2, …, k (each g_m conjugates the earlier exponents and preserves their sizes), so on the layer n = 1, in the sup and the scale-weighted covariant gradient, |X_{k+1}| ≤ (1 + o(1))Σ_{m=2}^{k}|𝐇_m| by BCH, giving η|X_{k+1}| ≤ L^{−1}Δ_{k+1}(1 + o(1)) and 2η²|∇X_{k+1}| ≤ 2L^{−2}Δ_{k+1}(1 + o(1)) with Δ_{k+1} := Σ_{m=2}^{k}c_Ac_Rε_m∏_{i=2}^{m}θ_i ≤ c_Ac_Rε_*θ₂Σ_{m≥2}∏_{i=3}^{m}θ_i ≤ 2c_Ac_Rε_*θ₂ =: Θ, where ε_* := max_{m≤K}ε_m (≤ (1+β₀)ε(g_K), since g_m ≤ (1+β₀)g_K by (2.6) and ε(g) increases for small g; m7) and θ_i ≤ ½ (l.965). *The size of Θ.* R₂ ≥ (log g₂^{−2})^r with r ≥ 2 printed (CRE p.245, "R₀ ≥ (log g₀^{−2})^r, r ≥ 2"; the same r on p.246 and in (2.5)), and (2.7)'s second line at p = 2, (n, m) = (2, 1), gives (log g₁^{−2})² ≤ (1+β₀)(log g₂^{−2})², so R₂ ≥ (1+β₀)^{−1}(log g₁^{−2})² and θ₂ ≤ exp(−δ₀M₁(log g₁^{−2})²/(32(1+β₀))) = o(g₁^N) for every N; so Θ ≤ 2c_Ac_Rε_*θ₂ and Θ/g₁ → 0 with no condition on δ₀M₁ (round 45 m3; with r = 1 only, θ₂ ≤ g₁^{δ₀M₁/(16(1+β₀))} and δ₀M₁ > 16(1+β₀) would be needed). *Conclusion.* (2.34) at n = 1 for every index j: |∂U_{k+1} − 1| < (B₃ε^V + 2Θ(1 + o(1)))L^{−2} < (1−β)α_{0,1}L^{−2} once 2Θ(1 + o(1)) < (1−β)α_{0,1} − B₃ε^V; the right side is ≥ ½(1−β)α_{0,1} for g_K small under q₀ > p₀, and ≥ ((1−β) − 2(1+β₀)²B₃A₀/C₀)α_{0,1} > 0 under q₀ = p₀ with the root's strict condition; Θ/α_{0,1} ≤ 2c_Ac_Rε_*θ₂/(C₀g₁) → 0, so the condition holds for g_K small. (2.38) at n = 1: the cubes □ ⊂ Ω₁∖Ω₃ meeting Ω₂ᶜ of side CMLξ are the root's cubes (Ω₃ ⊆ Λ₂ ⊆ Ω₂^{∼−2}, at distance ≥ 2MR₂L² > CML from Ω₂ᶜ; their parts inside Ω₂, at most √d·CMLξ deep, carry the decay θ₂^{1−O(C/(MR₂))} and the smaller field normalisation (L²η)^{−1}, so the bounds below hold there as well, m4), the gauge is the root's u composed with the orbit's g, and in it Lξ|A| ≤ B₃M^V₀Lε^V + Θ(1 + o(1)) and (Lξ)²|∇^ξA| ≤ B₃M^V₀L²ε^V + Θ(1 + o(1)) (the j = 0 cubes, the binding case; the j = 1 cubes the same with M^V₁ and without the L-powers; the covariant gradient of Ad_uX_{k+1} in the gauge u is Ad_u of the covariant gradient of X_{k+1} up to the commutator with ξA_root, of size |ξA_root||X_{k+1}|), both < BCMα_{0,1} once Θ(1 + o(1)) < BCMα_{0,1} − B₃M^V₀L²ε^V, which holds for g_K small by the same comparison (under q₀ = p₀ the root's strict condition C₀/A₀ > 2L³(1+β₀)²B₃(CM/2 + 4R₁^VM₁)/(BCM) leaves the room). *Within D(k).* The quantity carried by the induction is Δ_k (Δ₂ = 0, Δ_{k+1} = Δ_k + c_Ac_Rε_k∏_{i=2}^{k}θ_i ≤ Θ): D(k)'s real base point U_k satisfies the layer-1 conditions in the root's gauge with the excess Δ_k, and the step adds the step-k term. *Every older layer (the same chain; derived; reviewed in round 45 with the response chain, minors applied).* For the layer n ≥ 2 at the step k → k+1, k ≥ n, the root is U_n on Ω_n ⊇ Γ_n: p.268's |∂U_n − 1| < 2B₃ε_n(L^{−1}η)² (the top-layer bound at the step n−1 → n, l.921, Bałaban's; the weight is L^{−2n}) and the top-layer (2.38) of l.922 (SW closure §6(6d)); the steps m = n, …, k add the responses to A′_m on Ω_{m+1}, the step n with no decay (R-dec at zero layers crossed: sup|𝐇_n|, (L^nη)|∇𝐇_n| ≤ c_Rc_Aε_n on the blocks B^n(y), consistent with (6)(e) l.942), the later ones with ∏_{i=n+1}^{m}θ_i; so on the layer n the excess is Δ^{(n)}_{k+1} := Σ_{m=n}^{k}c_Ac_Rε_m∏_{i=n+1}^{m}θ_i ≤ c_Ac_Rε_n + 2c_Ac_Rε_*θ_{n+1}, with the weights L^{−n} (bond angles) and L^{−2n} (plaquettes and angle differences; the (2.38)-cubes' parts inside Ω_{n+1} as in m4), and θ_{n+1} ≤ exp(−δ₀M₁(log g_n^{−2})²/(32(1+β₀))) by (2.5) with r ≥ 2 and (2.7)'s second line at p = 2 (m3). Conditions: 2B₃ε_n + 2c_Ac_Rε_n(1 + o(1)) + 4c_Ac_Rε_*θ_{n+1} < (1−β)α_{0,n} for (2.34), and l.922's top-layer (2.38) constant plus Δ^{(n)}_{k+1}(1 + o(1)) < BCMα_{0,n}; under q₀ > p₀ both hold for g_K small (ε_n/α_{0,n} = (A₀/C₀)(log g_n^{−2})^{p₀−q₀}), under q₀ = p₀ they need C₀/A₀ > (2B₃ + 2c_Ac_R)/(1−β) for (2.34) and C₀/A₀ > (c_{SW} + c_Ac_R)/(BCM) for (2.38), c_{SW} the top-layer constant of l.922 (SW closure §6(6d); the global (7)-constant of the U_n problem is ≤ O(L²)(1+β₀)ε_n, since ε(g) increases for small g and g_i ≤ (1+β₀)g_n for i < n by (2.6)) (m6). With this, reading R-bg carries no curvature or gradient condition of the real base on any layer; p.268's top-layer bound, l.922's top-layer (2.38), and R-dec carry them.
    - *The total move.* Since both the complex and the real value of 𝐇^{(j)}(·|_{D_j}) are bounded by S_H, |Δ𝐇^{(j)}| := |𝐇^{(j)}(𝐔|_{Y_j}) − 𝐇^{(j)}(U|_{Y_j})| ≤ 2S_H (Y_j the support of D_j, as in R-𝐇 and the inclusion) at every evaluation point 𝐔 of every step k ≥ j+1, a″ included. No decay in k − j is needed, because the move is charged once against the fixed cushion r_{ω,j}/4, not per step. (A per-step increment from P₀ = e^{iηa″}U_{k+1} with radius (β/2)α_{1,j+1}, would still need the slot value at P₀ inside the step-(k+1) polydisc, which is this total bound again.)
    - *From 𝐇^{(j)} to σ_j.* g_jσ_j(b) = (1/i)log(e^{ig_jA_j^{new}(b)}e^{−iθ_b𝐇^{(j)}(b)}) ((a)), θ_b ∈ [0,1]. By BCH, D_X(1/i)log(e^{iy}e^{−iX}) = −I + O(|y| + |X|), so its norm is ≤ 2 for |y| + |X| ≤ c₂, an absolute constant, at complex X as well (the chart logarithm is analytic). Here |y| = g_j|A_j^{new}(b)| ≤ r_Kδ_j on the support ((6)(a)(c)) and |X| ≤ S_H, both ≪ c₂ for g_K small; c₂ depends on the norm on 𝔤^c, that is on G and its embedding. The segment between the real and the complex X lies in that ball (convex), so |Δσ_j| ≤ (2/g_j)|Δ𝐇^{(j)}| ≤ 4S_H/g_j and |Im σ_j| ≤ |Δσ_j| (σ_j is real at the real background).
    - *The condition (at r_{ω,j} = g_j^{1/2}; D2.6 closure §2.2 (c)).* |Δσ_j| ≤ 4S_H/g_j ≤ r_{ω,j}/4 = g_j^{1/2}/4 holds if 16C_H(α_{0,j+1} + α_{1,j+1})g_j^{−3/2}e^{−R_j} ≤ 1, true for g_K small since α_{i,j+1} ≤ (1+β₀)O(1)g_j(log g_j^{−2})^{q_i}, i = 0, 1, and R_j ≥ (log g_j^{−2})^r with r > 1 (CRE (2.4)–(2.7), (2.28)), uniformly in j and k, by g_j ≤ (1+β₀)g_K. So the condition is a smallness condition on g_K, as the others of *Propagation*; θ ≤ 1/4 suffices there.
    - *j = 0.* The same with 𝐇_{1,Ax} of CRE (1.22) in place of 𝐇^{(j)} and r_{ω,0} = 1 in V3's B-units. p.251 prints, at the real configuration, "The dependence is analytic, and the function 𝐇_{1,Ax} has the exponential decay property … (190) … for the axial gauge too", with the bound O(1)B₃e^{−δMR₁}ε₁ on □′ ⊂ S₁. S_H's e^{−R₀} needs δMR₁ ≥ R₀, which holds by (2.9) once δM ≥ L+1; otherwise S_H keeps e^{−δMR₁}, and the j = 0 smallness below holds either way. The bound S_H on 𝐇_{1,Ax} at complex backgrounds is R-𝐇₀: the same derivation as R-𝐇's (p.251 writes 𝐇_{1,Ax} with the argument (1/i)log[M^·(U₁)(M^·(Q₁^{s*}V))^{−1}], the structure of p.268), with the axial gauge in place of the Landau gauge: p.251 states that the dependence of 𝐇_{1,Ax} on its argument "is analytic, and the function 𝐇_{1,Ax} has the exponential decay property … (190) … for the axial gauge too" (Bałaban's, accepted, at real external U); at complex external U it is derived as the j = 0 case of *From the minimiser function to 𝐇^{(j)}*: by (1.21), e^{iL^{−1}𝐇_{1,Ax}(b)} is the ratio of the two axial-gauge minimisers on the unit lattice, u(h)^{−1}(b₋)·e^{iη𝓗(b)}U^L(b)·u(h)(b₊)u(0)(b₊)^{−1}·U^L(b)^{−1}u(0)(b₋), with U^L the reference minimiser in the Landau frame, 𝓗 its Landau-frame deviation (analytic with (190) at complex regular U, VAR p.309 and Prop. 9) and u the Landau→axial transformation (REG (1.19), CMP 98 (106); analytic in 𝓗, VAR p.307; block-local, ‖R(u^{±1})‖ ≤ 2, as in l.972); no average is taken (n_b = 0), so Schwarz's lemma gives |𝐇_{1,Ax}(b)| ≤ C_{d,L}sup_{B_b}|η𝓗| and (190)'s decay with the factor e^{O(L)δ₀}; p.251's propagator criterion is not used at complex data (derived; not reviewed); (190) is posed on VAR (172)'s complex domain, the axial configuration is an analytic function of 𝓗, hence of B (VAR p.307), and the analyticity in the data is derived as for R-𝐇 (re-check row 35), at the points reached: for k ≥ 2 the points of Ũ^c_k, by the inclusion above; for k = 1 the points e^{iη(𝐇_1(s,tB′) + a″)}U_2 reached at the step 1 → 2, not all of D(1)(iii). The phase → B conversion is |ΔB| ≤ 4S_H/(g₀C_U), C_U V3's unit constant, and the condition 4S_H/(g₀C_U) ≤ 1/4 is 16C_H(α_{0,1} + α_{1,1})e^{−R₀} ≤ g₀C_U, true for g₀ small since α_{i,1}/g₀ = O((log g₀^{−2})^{q_i}·g₁/g₀) and e^{−R₀} decays faster than any power of log g₀^{−2}.
    - *Inputs used (with D2.4's (2.38)/(LPG′) base-point item; REG Lemma 1 with CMP 98 Prop. 3, (56), (106), (127)–(129) at complex data, VAR Thm 1 ((7), (9)–(10)), Sect. G ((172)–(173), (19), p.307), (190), p.309 and Prop. 9, PBG (3.35)–(3.38), CRE (1.3), REG (1.65), derived in R-𝐇 and *The inclusion* (re-check row 35); p.251's axial transfer at real external U, Bałaban's, and at complex external U derived as the j = 0 case of R-𝐇's passage (p.309's 𝓗 with the block-local u), not reviewed; the base points on Ω₁∖Ω₂: derived at the root k = 1 by VAR Theorem 1 in *The base point on Ω₁∖Ω₂*, reviewed in rounds 40 and 41 with the round-41 minors not re-reviewed, for k ≥ 2 by reading R-bg).* R-𝐇 (and R-𝐇₀ at j = 0; R-𝐇₀'s domain is 𝒰₀ on D₀ = Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ, the set inferred for 𝐇_{1,Ax}'s background (p.251); U_{k+1}|_{D₀} lies in its real part by *The base point on Ω₁∖Ω₂* at k = 1 and by reading R-bg for k ≥ 2), R-dec, R-m, R-cpl₀, R-adm (through *Propagation*), (4)(f)'s chain condition that the evaluation points lie in Ũ^c_k (at every step i ≥ j+1, for the merged insertions), D2.0(5)(c)(α) for the real-point identification, CRE (2.9) with the condition (δ₀/32)M ≥ L+1 for the decay (*Constants*), and Input 4's bound for bonds of S_{j+1}^{(j)*} that leave S_{j+1} (Bałaban's, p.269, at real data; re-check row 33; at complex data the decay derivation of R-𝐇 covers those bonds directly, at one bond less distance).
    - *What it does not cover.* Bonds crossing the layer boundaries (next bullet, D2.6). *The gauge frame of σ_j (derived; reviewed in rounds 40–41, repairs not re-reviewed; its input |log w| from (4)(d) is derived for every k, reviewed in rounds 40–44, the round-44 minors applied and not re-reviewed).* σ_j is defined in the frame of the configuration U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)}) before the step's gauge transformation w = u_k^{−1}v₁ ((4)(d), w_tot), and the real centre σ_{j,0} must be read in the same frame; the two frames differ by R(w). How 𝐇^{(j)} transforms is R-𝐇's law (l.972, *The gauge*): 𝐇^{(j)}(𝐔^v)(b) = v(y_top(b₋))𝐇^{(j)}(𝐔)(b)v(y_top(b₋))^{−1} for G-valued v, y_top(b₋) the centre of the top-level block of the layer containing b₋, extended to G^c-valued v by VAR (181)'s analyticity; so under the frame change the value conjugates by an element g with |log g| ≤ |log w|, and ‖Ad_g‖ ≤ e^{2c_G|log w|}. The frames reached on a layer n = j compose over the steps m = j+1, …, k, so the relevant size is the cumulative Σ_m|log w_m| ≤ Σ_m C_wθ^{m−j−1}h_m ≤ C_wh̄/(1−θ) for u_m's shares ((4)(c), (6)(e)), with h̄ the value of l.942's h_k at the coupling (1+β₀)g_K (h_k is increasing in g_k, and g_m ≤ (1+β₀)g_K for every m; h_m ≤ h_{j+1} would need a monotonicity CRE (2.6) does not give; round 41), plus v₁'s shares from (4)(d), each ≤ 3dL^{j−m}θ^{m−j−1}h_m on the layer n = j at the step m ((d′), Step 7), so Σ_m|log w_m| ≤ (C_w + 3d)h̄/(1−θ); the condition used below is Σ_m|log w_m| ≤ (log 2)/(4c_G), so that e^{4c_G Σ|log w_m|} ≤ 2 and e^{2c_G Σ|log w_m|} ≤ √2. Hence |𝐇^{(j)}| ≤ 2S_H in every frame reached, and *The total move* needs no frame: the move is bounded by the sizes, |Δ𝐇^{(j)}| ≤ |𝐇^{(j)}(𝐔)| + |𝐇^{(j)}(U)| ≤ 3S_H, |Δσ_j| ≤ 6S_H/g_j before the frame discrepancy. The slot's own rotation law in Cov(k) is Ad_{w(b₋)} at the level-j site b₋ (the law of the fields ω, (4)(c)), while 𝐇^{(j)} conjugates at y_top(b₋); for b on the layer n = j the two sites coincide, and for b inside a (j+1)-block of Ω_{j+1} (the bonds of S_{j+1}) they differ by Ad_{w(b₋)w(y_top(b₋))^{−1}}, a rotation with |log| ≤ 2|log w|. The discrepancy acts on the value 𝐇^{(j)}(b)/g_j that the slot is built from, not on σ_j (σ_j can vanish while 𝐇^{(j)}(b) does not; round 40 S2), so the slot value read in either frame differs from the other by at most |σ_j′ − Ad_{w(b₋)}σ_j| ≤ 2θ_b e^{2c_G|log w|}(e^{4c_G|log w|} − 1)|𝐇^{(j)}(b)|/g_j, with 𝐇^{(j)}(b) in the frame before the rotation (|𝐇^{(j)}| ≤ S_H there), |log w| the cumulative Σ_m|log w_m|, the factor e^{2c_G|log w|} = ‖Ad_{w(b₋)}‖ for G^c-valued w (round 41), θ_b the frozen cutoff value g(A_j′(b)) ∈ [0, 1] of (6)(a) (not the decay ratio θ of l.943), and 2 the BCH derivative bound; under the condition above this is ≤ 2√2(e^{4c_GΣ|log w_m|} − 1)S_H/g_j ≤ 4S_H/g_j; charged to the total-move cushion, |Δσ_j| ≤ 10S_H/g_j, and *The condition* reads 40C_H(α_{0,j+1} + α_{1,j+1})g_j^{−3/2}e^{−R_j} ≤ 1, a smallness condition on g_K as before. For (6)(b7)'s u, G-valued and B′-independent (REG (1.14)'s residual gauge transformations are those equal to 1 on 𝔅_k, not block-constant ones), the discrepancy is a real rotation that carries the real centre with the value, at no cost. The real support set is Ad_G-invariant, so for G-valued rotations the real centre and the complex value rotate together and the polydisc condition is frame-independent; for G^c-valued w the centre's displacement is l.961's term, which (4)(c) budgets. With (4)(d)'s bound on v₁'s shares this item is derived at every k; the frame derivation itself does not depend on k.
- *Bonds crossing the layer boundaries.* The bonds of (4)(d)'s blocks that cross ∂Λ_j (VAR's Λ_j) are case (B) of D2.1(4) and stay with D2.6, as in (4)(d). *Closed in WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §4.4 (status in closure §8).*
- *What it closes (derived given its inputs; reviewed, this form not re-reviewed).* The slot values at complex backgrounds are reduced to R-𝐇 and R-𝐇₀. Their analyticity on 𝒰_j is derived (*Analyticity on 𝒰_j*: 𝐇^{(j)} depends on 𝐔 only through its data, and VAR Sect. G with CMP 98 Prop. 3 and CRE (1.3) gives analyticity in the complex data; the complex external fields of the mean-value step lie in PBG's class (3.35)–(3.38) by VAR Thm 1 (9)–(10) and VAR (19), with the frame links by the identity theorem and p.309; re-check row 35), and their bound S_H is derived in R-𝐇 (*The argument bound at complex 𝐔*: REG Lemma 1 with CMP 98 Prop. 3, (56), (127)–(129), on 𝐔 and its averages as in REG (1.65), so (2.35) and (2.37) are not used; *From the minimiser function to 𝐇^{(j)}*: Schwarz's lemma on CMP 98's averages and u(h), with the factor L^{n_b} cancelled by (190)'s field normalisation; the decay under (δ₀/32)M ≥ L+1), and the evaluation points lie in 𝒰_j by *The inclusion*. The gauge frame is D2.2(4)(d)'s, derived at every k by (d′) and reviewed in rounds 42–44, and the base points on Ω₁∖Ω₂, rooted at k = 1 and reaching every k, are derived (bullet *The inclusion*; k = 1 reviewed in rounds 40–41, k ≥ 2 in round 45). This covers (4)(c)'s bound on |ω| for k ≥ 2 and (4)(g)'s ω-propagation item, given R-dec, R-m, R-cpl₀, R-adm and (4)(f)'s chain condition besides R-𝐇 and R-𝐇₀. It amends D(k)(iii)'s domain for k ≥ 2 to CRE's full complex space: the pointwise conditions (2.34), (2.36), (2.39) and the per-cube (2.38) are global, posed on every bond and cube of each Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, while (2.35) and (2.37) are posed on the domains X of the terms that use them (CRE p.261), not on Y_j. It adds R-adm (printed), R-dec for the older layers (Bałaban's, accepted; without R-dec the propagation is closed for j = k−1 only), and the entailment q₀ ≥ p₀ of the curvature margin. The sizes at complex data are D2.4's.

*(f) What (6) leaves.*
- *To D2.4:* the sizes of the new groups, D(k+1)(ii) and (ii′), with the norms at complex data of (e), in the projective norm (d); the per-component factor T_Y of (4)(f), with the curvature-margin term of (e); the chord–arc cost of the merging in (d), constant 4; the majorants and locally uniform convergence that Morera's and Hartogs' theorems need in (e); the deviation factor for every cluster size in (c), with the local gauge on the cluster support; the slot values at complex backgrounds (reduced in (e) to R-𝐇, R-𝐇₀ with the unprinted links of (e), *What it closes*, as a total move; gauge frame derived, rounds 42–44; base points on Ω₁∖Ω₂ derived at every k, rounds 40–41 and 45); the cut of D2.1(4)'s complex radius when ω-dependent factors are present ((e)).
- *To D2.6:* the species of the new insertions, and the marked polymers that meet Λ_{k+1}ᶜ ((5)(f)); case (B) of D2.1(4), the bonds that cross the layer boundaries. *Closed in WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §2 (species), §4.1–4.3 (case (B): the polydisc radius is not reduced on older layers, VAR (190)'s L^{k−j} is inside R-dec's (Lθ)^{k−j}; parity on old layers by CRE p.276), §4.4 (crossing bonds: covariance by the transport law, size by §5.4); status in closure §8. The gauge frame of σ_j stays OPEN there; derived in (6)(e) on 26 Oct (round 40), given (4)(d)'s input.*
- *To D2.3:* the extraction of δm and z from the flat parts Ψ° and the reference scalars of (c).
- *Λ_{k+1}.* (6) uses CRE's Λ_{k+1} (p.269) as printed, with ω_k on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ. No refinement of Λ_{k+1} is needed for (a)–(e): (a) works bond by bond on S_{k+1} ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, (b)–(d) are identities, and (e) uses only Ω_{j+2} ⊆ Λ_{j+1} (CRE pp.264–265) and VAR (1).
- *L.* D2.2(4)(f) needs L ≥ 2(1+β₀). D2 takes L ≥ 3, which V3's L > 11 odd (V3 L3968) implies. With V3's L ≥ 13 the first condition holds for β₀ ≤ 11/2; at L = 3 it would need β₀ ≤ 1/2, which CRE allows. D2.2(4)(f)'s older-layer margin does not need (1+β₀)² ≤ 3/2: it rests on (6)(e)'s decay, with the condition 2θ·O(1)(1+β₀)² < 1, which holds for g_K small. D2.4(6) needs (1+β₀)⁸ < L, i.e. β₀ < 3^{1/8} − 1 ≈ 0.147 at L = 3 and β₀ < 13^{1/8} − 1 ≈ 0.378 at V3's L ≥ 13.

## D2.3 Exact absorption of δm_{k+1} and z_{k+1} (reviewed: no fatal finding; repairs not re-reviewed)

### (0) What is needed

- *Input.* D2.2's output on a labelled term ℓ of ρ_{k+1}: ⟨𝒲_k⟩_{1,1} = κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}·Σ_𝔉 Q(𝔉)·dim(π)^{−1}tr(Y_{𝒢₁}U_{(1)}⋯Y_{𝒢_r}U_{(r)}), the sum over the families 𝔉 of step-(k+1) groups of (6)(d), with f_{k+1} for 𝔉 = ∅. The insertions are Y_𝒢(𝐔, ω′) at 𝐔 = U_{k+1}, covariant by (6)(b), with flat scalars Ψ° by (6)(c).
- *Output.* The form of D(k+1):
  - (i) κ^{(k+1)} = κ^{(k)}κ_{k+1}, κ_{k+1} = e^{−δm_{k+1}P_k(C)}z_{k+1}⁴, with δm_{k+1} and z_{k+1} independent of the contour size, the torus and ℓ;
  - (v) the scalar parts are numbers given ℓ;
  - the insertions that remain vanish at the flat reference, or are attached to ℓ's data (D2.6).
- *Constraint (D2.0(5)(a), (b)).* No remainder proportional to P_k(C) may be left. The corner remainders must be summable over k uniformly in K.

### (1) Quoted

- *Step 1 (V4, WILSON_FIRST_RG_STEP_V4.tex at hash d463c554b76f; every passage below has the same text in V3 at a520a59f363d, typography aside).*
  - *Reference data,* L1130: the same regions, cubes and cutoffs, with U°₁ = id, V°₀ = id, A°_e = 0; "These data need not be a branch of the history sum." The lemmas used "apply to U = id directly".
  - *Schur,* Lemma schur, L1055–1064. At the identity background a constant gauge transformation fixes the data. So the group matrix Υ = w°(W)∫π(P°_W(a))d(⊗ν̂°)(a) satisfies Υ = π(u)Υπ(u)^{−1} for all u ∈ G, hence Υ = a_W·𝟙, and S°_{ξ,C} = Σ_{𝔅 compatible}∏_{W∈𝔅}a_W (eq:part1-reference-gas).
  - *Joint extraction,* Prop. joint-extraction, L1070–1075. With the joint defect R_ξ(𝔅) := Σ_{𝒮⊆𝔅}(−1)^{|𝔅∖𝒮|}(∏_{W∈𝔅∖𝒮}a_W)A_ξ(𝒮), F_ξ(0) = S°_{ξ,C}f_ξ(0) + Σ_{𝔅≠∅}S°^{[𝔅]}_{ξ,C}R_ξ(𝔅). At the reference data every nonempty defect vanishes (derived; the cited lines end at the statement): by Lemma schur A°(𝒮) = ∏_{W∈𝒮}a_W, so R°(𝔅) = ∏_{W∈𝔅}a_W·Σ_{𝒮⊆𝔅}(−1)^{|𝔅∖𝒮|} = 0 for 𝔅 ≠ ∅.
  - *Perimeter,* eq:marginal-full-perimeter (L1595): κ_v = Σ_{Γ: 0∈J_Γ}Φ_v(Γ)/|J_Γ| on the infinite straight line, δm_v = −κ_v/(Lε), with |κ_v| ≤ Cτ² from the rooted tail eq:marginal-cluster-tail (L1574). κ_v is kept per orientation v; D2 uses only the average δm^σ = −(4Lε)^{−1}Σ_{v∈𝒱_□}κ_v (eq:marginal-square-mass, L1649; so D2.0(3)'s δm_1 = εδm^σ = −(4L)^{−1}Σ_{v∈𝒱_□}κ_v per step-0 bond) over C's sides (D2.3(5) *Independence*; V4's Borel axial choice and chart choices are not checked for permutation invariance, RTR_PROOF_PASS, *Hypercubic covariance*). eq:marginal-line-endpoints (L1612) is the exact endpoint identity for a finite segment.
  - *Corners,* eq:corner-local-extraction (L1702): ζ_{v,w} = Σ_{j∈ℤ}(a_{v,w}(j) − k_{v,w}(j)) on the infinite bent path, with |a_{v,w}(j) − k_{v,w}(j)| ≤ Cτ²e^{−c|j|} (eq:corner-localisation-bound, L1710). eq:corner-mixed-cluster-formula (L1720) shows that ζ contains the straight-edge endpoint deficits.
  - *Square,* eq:corner-square-extraction-bound (L1763): |F_{□,∞}(n) − nΣ_vκ_v − Σ_{(v,w)∈𝒞_□}ζ_{v,w}| ≤ Cτ²(n+1)e^{−cn}, with z_c := exp(¼Σζ_{v,w}) (eq:corner-square-factor, L1772).
  - *Iteration,* Conjecture iteration, L3532, and Remark next-step (3)(e). There the torus and corner part E°_{T,n} "at most Cτ²(2+1/c)" is "one source of the term (k+2)τ²". Cτ²(2+1/c) bounds the torus part, by 4ne^{−cι_T} ≤ 1 from (H3), and the corner part, by (n+1)e^{−cn} ≤ 1+1/c (V4 L210); the decay in n is not used.
- *The partitions (Bałaban).*
  - RG2 p.3: "a regular partition σ_k of the space T, in the scale corresponding to the lattice T_η, into cubes Δ of the size R₁M₁. The number R₁ is a power of L".
  - RG2 p.13: "π_{k+1}, i.e. cubes of the size LM in the scale corresponding to the lattice T_η".
  - CRE p.245: "a partition into cubes of the size MR₀, where R₀ is the smallest power of L such that R₀ ≥ (log g₀^{−2})^r, r ≥ 2, and M = L^m is sufficiently large. The second is a partition into cubes of the size LM₂R₀, where M₂ = L^{m₂}".
  - PBG p.408: "We take these numbers as powers of L" (R, M).
  - CRE p.256: the domains Ω_j, Λ_j "which are determined by he j-th renormalization transformation, but not by the R-operation, are unions of MR_j-cubes in the lattice T_{L^{−j}}" (print "he" for "the"). At step k+1 the small-field tests of CRE (3.16) are on L²M₂R_{k+1}-cubes (D2.2(1)).
  - *R-tr (translation covariance).* Every operation of Bałaban's step (averages, minimisers, propagators, cutoffs, s-localisation, activities) is defined from the lattice, the partitions and the fields alone, so a lattice translation that preserves all the partitions maps each object to the object of the translated data. The prints define the operations this way; no page states the covariance of the localised 𝐄-activities (for the R-terms CRE (2.32), p.260, under MR_j-partition-preserving translations). Derived on ℓ₀ by RTR_PROOF_PASS_2026_10_23.md (printed covariance RG1 p.263/p.269, CRE (2.29), (3.39); the localised activities from the definitions); the free choices by our convention, with the PBG p.411 tie averaged with equal weights over the tied cubes (only translations are used below; the average keeps the free choices hypercubic symmetric, but the step itself is not shown hypercubic covariant, l.186).
  - Nesting: each cube of a coarser partition is a union of cubes of each finer one, the L-blocks included (V4 L612, V3 L548 as used in D2.2(6)(a)).
  - CRE (2.9), p.256, for n > m: R_n ≤ LR_m and R_m ≤ L(1+g_n²β′(n−m))^{β₀}R_n ≤ (L+1)(n−m)^{β₀}R_n.

### (2) The flat value of a group is a scalar (derived)

- *The reference point.* On a labelled term ℓ it is (𝐔, 𝐉, ω′) = (1, 0, 0) with ℓ's regions, as in (6)(c) ("𝐔 = 1, 𝐉 = 0, ω′ = 0") and V4 L1130. Below, (1, 0) abbreviates it. Write ℓ₀ for the trivial-label term: Ω_j = Λ_j = T for every j ≤ k+1, no large-field regions, hence no outer variables. On ℓ₀ the reference point is a real configuration in the support of every indicator, so D2.2's identities hold there. On other terms it need not lie in the support, and the insertions are taken there through the reference clause.
- *Reference clause (derived; added to D(k)(iii)).* The background-dependent factors of D(k) on ℓ are defined and analytic also at 𝐔 = e^{iη_kA′}·1, A′ in D(k)(iii)'s domain, jointly with ω in its radii around ω = 0.
  - At k = 1 this is V4 L1130: the step-1 lemmas "apply to U = id directly", on every branch.
  - At step k+1 it is (6)(e)'s analyticity argument with U_{k+1} = 1:
    - (4)(f) at U_{k+1} = 1 uses the clause at step k;
    - the transports and the leading word are entire;
    - the Gaussian parts and 𝐕_k(Y) are analytic on Bałaban's domain, given by the conditions I.(i)–(iii) (RG2 pp.6, 15; "I" is RG1). RG1 p.262 states them: (i) 𝐔 = U′U with |∂U − 1| < α₀ξ² (1.11) and, on cubes of size O(1)LM, a gauge with U^u = exp iξA, |A|, |∇^ξA| < O(1)LMBα₀ (1.12); (ii) U′ = exp iξA′, |A′|, |∇_U^ξA′| < α₁ (1.13); (iii) |∂𝐔 − 1| < α₀ξ², |𝐉| < γ₀ (1.14). At (𝐔, 𝐉) = (1, 0) take U = U′ = 1, u = 1, A = A′ = 0: every inequality holds strictly, and it holds for e^{iη_kA′}·1 with A′ in D(k)(iii)'s domain, whose bounds are those of (1.13) with smaller constants (derived);
    - the large-field objects in the insertions on ℓ ≠ ℓ₀ are defined at the flat point (derived): RG1 p.260, for regular V "there exists exactly one regular, critical orbit" of the action, "and it is a minimal orbit". At V = 1 the configuration 1 is a minimum (the action is ≥ 0 and vanishes there) and satisfies the constraint, so the orbit is that of 1. Hence the minimisers V^{(k)}_{Λᶜ∩Λ_k} are 1 in the axial gauge, 𝐇^{(k)} = 0, and the slot values ψ_{k+1}(A_j^{new}; 𝐇^{(j)}) are taken at 𝐇^{(j)} = 0 and ω′ = 0. That these are defined there is the statement; their sizes are D2.4's;
    - the characteristic functions do not depend on a′.
  - The bounds at the reference point are part of D2.4's sizes at complex data.
- *Schur.* Let 𝒢 be a step-(k+1) group with arc I(𝒢) from x to x′. Take u ≡ g constant in Cov(k+1) ((6)(b), (d)). Then 1^g = 1 and R(g)0 = 0, and the labels are gauge invariant. So Y_𝒢(1, 0) = π(g)Y_𝒢(1, 0)π(g)^{−1} for all g ∈ G.
  - π is irreducible (V3 (H1): χ = dim(π)^{−1}tr π). By Schur's lemma, Y_𝒢(1, 0) = c_𝒢𝟙 with c_𝒢 = c_𝒢(ℓ) ∈ ℂ, a number given ℓ.
  - This is V3's Lemma schur at step k+1. V3 applies Schur to the integrated reference group matrix; here the integral is already inside Y_𝒢 ((5)(c)).
  - Old groups that stand alone in 𝔉 (the untouched old insertions of (5)(a)) are covered by Cov(k) in the same way. For a whole-contour group, x = x′ is the base point and the argument is the same.
- *The split.* Let T_𝐔(I) := π of the ordered product of the 𝐔-transports along the arc I, in the orientation of C. Put
  Y^d_𝒢(𝐔, ω′) := Y_𝒢(𝐔, ω′) − c_𝒢T_𝐔(I(𝒢)).
  - T_𝐔(I(𝒢)) transforms as π(u(x))·π(u(x′))^{−1}, so Y^d_𝒢 is covariant with the endpoints of Y_𝒢. T_𝐔 is entire, so Y^d_𝒢 is analytic on Y_𝒢's domain. T_1 = 𝟙, so Y^d_𝒢(1, 0) = 0.
  - The split is made in the formulas, as D2.0(3)'s *Species* requires, not by the sizes.
  - The flat parts of D2.0(5)(d) (the scalar parts of pair contractions of old insertions at flat U_{k+1}) are in c_𝒢. Their background-dependent remainder, of deviation size, is in Y^d_𝒢. This is the division that D2.0(5)(a) asks for.

### (3) The exact factorisation (derived)

- *The interactions.* (A subset form Φ(S) doesn't work: from step 2 on its terms with S inside one 𝒢 depend on C outside I(𝒢), since a cluster of the step-k reference gas that hits an old group can contain groups anywhere on C.) Both interactions are kept in cluster form and expanded into the groups, as V4 does with its vacuum clusters at step 1 (eq:c-cluster-expansion, eq:part1-group-weight, L1101–L1108).
  - *Old part.* By D(k)'s interaction clause in cluster form (note in D2.0(3)), Q_k(𝔅) = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits 𝔅}φ_k(Γ)), over the clusters Γ of the step-k reference group gas that are incompatible with some member of 𝔅. The φ_k(Γ) are numbers given ℓ, and they depend on C only through J_Γ, the traversals in the arcs of Γ's groups. At k = 1 this is IT-1 note §4's Q(𝔅) = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits 𝔅}φ(Γ)). At step k+1 it is derived in the last bullet of this (3).
  - *Vacuum part.* Q° = Q_{k+1}(𝔐) of D2.2(5)(d) at the reference point, exp(Σ_𝒞(s_𝔐(𝒞) − 1)_+φ^T(𝒞)), over the vacuum clusters 𝒞 that are incompatible with at least two marked polymers. It does not involve C.
  - *Expansion in occurrences.* Expand the old part as the exponential series Σ_{m≥0}((−1)^m/m!)Σ_{Γ₁,…,Γ_m each hits 𝔅}∏_jφ_k(Γ_j), and the vacuum part as ∏_𝒞(1 + (e^{(s_𝔐(𝒞)−1)φ^T(𝒞)} − 1)), over the 𝒞 with s_𝔐(𝒞) ≥ 2. Both converge absolutely: Σ_{Γ hits W}|φ_k(Γ)| ≤ θ(W) by D(k)'s norms, Σ_{𝒞≁M}|φ^T(𝒞)| ≤ 9c#M by D2.4(2)(g), and |e^x − 1| ≤ |x|e^{|x|}. On the finite torus these are infinite sums, since clusters may repeat polymers, so the rearrangements below need this absolute convergence.
  - *Third merging rule (amends D2.2(6)(d)).* In each term, the old groups hit by one old occurrence, and the marked polymers hit by one vacuum occurrence with factor e^{(s−1)φ^T} − 1, join one group. The arc hull is taken over the arcs of the members and over J_Γ for every old occurrence Γ; vacuum occurrences add no arc. Then D2.2(6)(d)'s first two rules are repeated to a fixed point. Each occurrence lies in exactly one group 𝒢. Its factor goes into Y_𝒢 as a number, with (−1)^{m_𝒢}/m_𝒢! for the m_𝒢 old occurrences of 𝒢: the m! of the series splits into the m!/∏_𝒢m_𝒢! interleavings, as in eq:part1-group-weight.
  - *Hard core.* Two groups are compatible when their arcs are disjoint, their constituent domains are compatible, and no occurrence of one hits a member of the other. The terms of the expansion are then in bijection with the families of pairwise compatible groups with their occurrences. So the step-(k+1) reference group gas is a pure hard-core gas: Q(𝔉) = 1, and every interaction is inside a group.
- *Multilinear expansion.* Put Y_𝒢 = c_𝒢T_𝐔(I(𝒢)) + Y^d_𝒢 into each word and expand:
  dim(π)^{−1}tr(Y_{𝒢₁}U_{(1)}⋯Y_{𝒢_r}U_{(r)}) = Σ_{D⊆𝔉}(∏_{𝒢∈𝔉∖D}c_𝒢)·w^d(D).
  - w^d(D) is dim(π)^{−1}tr of the word with Y^d_𝒢 on the arcs of D and the 𝐔-transports elsewhere, and w^d(∅) = f_{k+1}. The transports of the scalar terms join the neighbouring U_{(i)}, so w^d(D) is the class word of the family D.
  - This is V3's joint defect. Boolean inversion of A(𝔉) = Σ_{D⊆𝔉}(∏_{𝔉∖D}c_𝒢)R(D) has the unique solution R(D) = w^d(D). So at step k+1 the joint defect is the word of the deviation parts. As in V3, it vanishes at the reference point for D ≠ ∅.
- *Resummation.* Σ_𝔉Q(𝔉)Σ_{D⊆𝔉}(∏_{𝔉∖D}c_𝒢)w^d(D) = Σ_Dw^d(D)·S°^{[D]}(ℓ), where
  S°^{[D]}(ℓ) := Σ_𝔈 Q(D ∪ 𝔈)∏_{𝒢∈𝔈}c_𝒢 = Σ_𝔈∏_{𝒢∈𝔈}c_𝒢,
  the sum over the families 𝔈 disjoint from D for which D ∪ 𝔈 is a family (pairwise compatible groups), with Q = 1 by the hard core above. Put S°(ℓ) := S°^{[∅]}(ℓ).
  - The interchange needs Σ_𝔉∏_{𝒢∈𝔉}(|c_𝒢| + ‖Y^d_𝒢‖) < ∞ at the evaluation point, over the families with their occurrences. Without the occurrences the family sum is finite on the finite torus; with them it is an infinite sum, and the absolute convergence is D2.4's, as V3's proof of Prop. joint-extraction does with its β_W.
- *Result (exact).* Where S°(ℓ) ≠ 0,
  ⟨𝒲_k⟩_{1,1} = κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}S°(ℓ)·[f_{k+1} + Σ_{D≠∅}Q^{out}_{k+1}(D)·w^d(D)], Q^{out}_{k+1}(D) := S°^{[D]}(ℓ)/S°(ℓ).
  - The name Q^{out} keeps it apart from D2.2(5)(d)'s vacuum interaction Q_{k+1}(𝔐). Q^{out}_{k+1} is D(k+1)'s Q_{k+1} in D2.0(3)'s display.
  - Q^{out}_{k+1}(D) is a number given ℓ, so D(k+1)(v) holds for it.
  - The insertions at step k+1 are the Y^d_𝒢. They vanish at the reference point.
- *S°(ℓ₀) is the flat reference integral.* At (1, 0) every Y^d_𝒢 vanishes and f_{k+1} = 1, so the bracket is 1 and ⟨𝒲_k⟩_{1,1}(1, 0) = κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}S°(ℓ). On ℓ₀ the reference point is a configuration of the term. So
  𝒲°_{k+1}(C; T) := S°(ℓ₀) = ⟨f_k + Σ_{𝔅≠∅}Q_k(𝔅)word_k(𝔅)⟩_{1,1} at U_{k+1} = 1,
  the expectation of D(k)'s bracket under the flat step-(k+1) fluctuation measure of CRE (3.25), with its small-field cutoffs. It is the step-(k+1) analogue of V3's 𝒲°_T(C) = N^σ_{C,T}/D^σ_T (eq:marginal-literal-ratio, V4 L1362), the bare loop W_χ(C; ·) under the flat first-step measure.
- *The gas and its logarithm.* S°(ℓ) is the partition function of the *reference group gas*: polymers 𝒢 (with their occurrences) with activities c_𝒢 and the hard core of the *Hard core* bullet. There are no other interactions.
  - Under D2.4's Kotecký–Preiss condition its cluster series converges. Then S°(ℓ) = exp(Σ_ΓΦ°(Γ; ℓ)) ≠ 0, the sum over the clusters Γ of groups, Φ°(Γ) the Ursell function times ∏c_𝒢 (FV (5.11)). log S° denotes this series.
  - Every group has an arc on C (its slots, or the old group's arc), and the vacuum clusters were divided out in (5)(d). So every cluster Γ has a nonempty set J_Γ of traversals of C in the arcs of its groups.
- *The interactions at step k+1 have the cluster form.* S°^{[D]} is the partition function of the gas with the activities c_𝒢·1[𝒢 compatible with every member of D], so the Kotecký–Preiss condition passes to it (as in D2.2(5)(d)), and its cluster series is that of S° without the clusters incompatible with D. Hence
  Q^{out}_{k+1}(D) = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits D}Φ°(Γ; ℓ)),
  the sum over the clusters incompatible with some member of D. This is D(k+1)'s interaction clause in the cluster form of (3)'s *Old part*, with φ_{k+1} := Φ°, and the old occurrences of the next step are these clusters. The bound Σ_{Γ hits 𝒢}|Φ°(Γ)| ≤ θ_{k+1}(𝒢) is FV (5.11) under D2.4's condition (D2.4(3), θ_ref).

### (4) Periodicity, contour locality and the infinite lattice (derived, except the OPEN items of the stopping scale, and the inputs of contour locality from (T′), i.e. from (S-w))

- *The period.* Let Π_{k+1} be the largest cube side, in step-k bonds, of the partitions and cube families used at the steps 1, …, k+1: those of (1), the MR_j-cubes of which the step-j domains are unions (CRE p.256), and the L²M₂R_j-cubes of the tests (3.16).
  - The largest side is a power of L: every candidate except RG2's R₁M₁ is a power of L by (1), and R₁M₁ ≤ M (no transcribed text says M₁ is a power of L). By the nesting every side divides the largest, and every one is invariant under the translations by Π_{k+1} step-k bonds along each axis. The torus periods are multiples of Π_{k+1}, since the partitions are regular partitions of T.
  - *Size.* At step j+1 the cubes are, in step-j units: R₁M₁ ≤ M (RG2 p.3); LM ≤ MR_j and LM₂R_j ≤ MR_j (M₂ < M, both powers of L, and R_j ≥ L for small g); the step-(j+1) domain cubes MR_{j+1} of scale j+1, which are LMR_{j+1} ≤ L²MR_j step-j bonds (R_{j+1} ≤ LR_j, CRE (2.9)); and L²M₂R_{j+1} ≤ LMR_{j+1} ≤ L²MR_j. In step-k units the largest is at most L^{j−k}L²MR_j. By CRE (2.9) with n = k, m = j, this is ≤ (L+1)(k−j)^{β₀}L^{j−k}L²MR_k ≤ L²MR_k for k > j, since (L+1)m^{β₀}L^{−m} ≤ 1 + 1/L for m ≥ 1, β₀ ≤ 1, and the side is a power of L; for j = k it is L²MR_k. So Π_{k+1} ≤ L²MR_k.
- *Invariance on ℓ₀ (derived from R-tr, itself derived on ℓ₀ by RTR_PROOF_PASS_2026_10_23.md, with our convention for the choices the prints leave free).* On ℓ₀ every object of the steps 1, …, k+1 is defined from the lattice, the partitions, the flat field and, for the insertions, their groups and occurrences, the arcs of C. A translation τ by a multiple of Π_{k+1} step-k bonds preserves the first three, and by R-tr ((1)) each object is carried to the object of the translated data. So c_{τ𝒢} = c_𝒢 and Φ°(τΓ) = Φ°(Γ) whenever τ maps the arcs of 𝒢 (of the groups of Γ) onto arcs of C of the same shape, by the next bullet.
- *Contour locality (derived from D2.1(4), D2.2(4)(e), (5)(c) and (3)).* For a group 𝒢 with a short arc (fewer than P_k(C)/2 step-k traversals), Y_𝒢 depends on C only through the bonds of C in I(𝒢).
  - Y_𝒢 is the contraction along I(𝒢) of the Ĥ(G), whose slots lie in I(𝒢), with the old insertions in I(𝒢) and the transports along I(𝒢) ((6)(d)), times the factors of its occurrences ((3)). The vacuum activities, the vacuum occurrences and Ψ° do not involve C. An old occurrence Γ has weight φ_k(Γ), which depends on C only through J_Γ ⊆ I(𝒢) (the third merging rule). The old insertions and the old cluster weights satisfy the same statement, by induction from step 1: there V3's group words are products along their intervals, the contour enters through the contact sets (eq:part1-contact-set, V4 L1097), and V3's cluster weights φ(Γ) are Ursell functions times activities of groups with arcs in J_Γ. The hard core between groups depends on C only through their arcs.
  - (The statement without the occurrences doesn't work from step 2 on: with a subset form Φ(S), Y_𝒢 contains cluster terms from all of C.)
  - The hull rule depends on the length of C, so whole-contour groups are excluded. They have spatial extent at least P_k(C)/2 step-k traversals in contour distance and are paid by the tail bound (T′) of (5) (from (S-w)): a whole-contour group has a network of diameter ≥ P_k(C)/4 in step-k bonds (its arc of ≥ P_k(C)/2 = 2s_k traversals along the square contains a whole side, whose endpoints are s_k = P_k(C)/4 apart, and the arc I(𝒢) lies in the support X(𝒢)), so the clusters containing one are ≤ C_S′𝔞_{k+1}e^{−c_dP_k(C)/4} per traversal.
- *Placement of C.* C is placed with a corner at the origin, taken at the centre of a cube of the coarsest partition. The partitions are grids with a common corner and sides powers of L (RG1 p.251, p.257, RG2 p.3), and L is odd, so this point is a site of every T^{(k)}, k ≤ K, and the centre of a cube of every partition; the period points (the origin plus multiples of Π_{k+1}) are cube centres of the partitions whose side divides Π_{k+1}, and every lattice and partition is invariant under the hypercubic group about the origin (the step's objects are not shown to be, D2.3(5) *Independence*). The bare expectation is translation invariant, so this is no restriction. C is a union of step-K bonds, with side s_k := ℓ_CL^{K−k} in step-k bonds.
  - Call step k+1 *commensurate* for C if Π_{k+1} divides s_k. Then the four corners lie on the period lattice and each side is a union of n_k := s_k/Π_{k+1} period cells.
  - Since Π_{k+1} is a power of L and s_k is ℓ_C times a power of L, step k+1 is commensurate whenever Π_{k+1} ≤ L^{K−k}. By Π_{k+1} ≤ L²MR_k and CRE (2.9) with n = K−1, m = k, R_k ≤ (L+1)(K−1−k)^{β₀}R_{K−1}. So a step that is not commensurate has L^{K−k} < L²(L+1)M(K−k)^{β₀}R_{K−1}, that is K − k ≤ a*, with a* depending on L, M, β₀ and R_{K−1} only. R_{K−1} is fixed by g_{K−1}, which CRE (2.6) compares with g_K. So at most a* steps are not commensurate, and a* does not grow with K at fixed g_K (void under the admissible sides).
  - *The admissible sides (for the user's review).* Let L^{q*} be the least power of L with L^{q*} ≥ sup_{k<K}Π_{k+1}L^{k−K}, the largest period in step-K units. By Π_{k+1} ≤ L²MR_k and CRE (2.9), Π_{k+1}L^{k−K} ≤ LMR_{K−1} at k = K−1 and ≤ (L+1)MR_{K−1} for k < K−1, so L^{q*} ≤ L²MR_{K−1}. ℓ_C is restricted to the multiples of L^{q*}. Then Π_{k+1} divides s_k = ℓ_CL^{K−k} at every step (Π_{k+1} is a power of L dividing L^{q*}L^{K−k}), every step is commensurate, and (6)(b) is void.
    - This is the kind of restriction D2.0(1) already makes (C a union of step-K bonds). It restricts no square when the scale at which the steps stop may depend on the loop, as far as the next bullet checks (its OPEN items stand). A square on the L-adic lattices has side pL^{−q} in units of a scale u. Stopped at uL^{−j}, its side is pL^{j−q} step bonds (ℓ_C = pL^{j−q}, the side), a multiple of L^{q*} once j ≥ q + q*. Here q* is fixed by the coupling at the stopping scale through CRE (2.5), (2.7); it grows at most like β₀log_L j (next bullet), and j ≥ q + q*(j) has solutions. With the stopping scale held fixed, it does restrict the sides to multiples of L^{q*}. That D2's consumers allow a loop-dependent stopping scale is checked in the next bullet.
    - *The stopping scale per loop (reviewed: correct and sufficient for "Partly derived").* Let u be the unit scale, ε = uL^{−K} the fine spacing (K steps from ε to u), and C a square of physical side pL^{−q}u with integers p ≥ 1, q ≥ 0. For j ≥ q put K′ := K − j, the *stopping scale* uL^{−j}: the side of C is ℓ_C = pL^{j−q} step-K′ bonds, P_{K′}(C) = 4ℓ_C (ℓ_C is the side throughout D2.3), and C is a union of step-k bonds for every k ≤ K′. Four checks.
      - (i) *j(C) exists and does not depend on ε, given the coupling prescription.* Along the sequence ε = uL^{−K} → 0 the unit-scale couplings g_K are prescribed with g_min := inf_K g_K > 0 and γ := sup_K g_K small (for instance g_K = ḡ for all K). That a bare-coupling sequence realises this is the coupling item of the gaps block: the first assertion of RG I Thm 2 (p.259: for g small there is g₀(ε, g) with g_k ∈ ]0, γ_B] and g_K = g), printed for SU(2) and Bałaban's presented-as-true statement for general G by RG I p.260 ("restriction to the group SU(2) is superficial"), accepted, proof not printed (re-check row 26); for simple G proved in the Bałaban project (ONE_LOOP_PROOFS.md Corollary 8.2, RG1_Thm2_REP). (0.31) is not used here, and γ below is not RG I's γ, written γ_B. It is not D2's, and without it j(C) is not K-independent (as g_K → 0, j(C) → ∞: the forcing is the lower bound L^{q*(K′)} ≥ Π_{K′}L^{−1} ≥ LM₂R_{K′}, with R_{K′} ≥ (log g_{K′}^{−2})^r → ∞ since g_{K′} ≤ (1+β₀)g_K). The admissible-sides bullet at the final step K′ defines q*(K′) with L^{q*(K′)} ≤ L²MR_{K′−1}. CRE (2.5) makes R_{K′−1} the least power of L above (log g_{K′−1}^{−2})^r, so R_{K′−1} ≤ L(log g_{K′−1}^{−2})^r, and CRE (2.7) with n = K, m = K′−1 (n − m = j+1) gives (log g_{K′−1}^{−2})^r ≤ (1+g_K²β′(j+1))^{β₀}(log g_K^{−2})^r ((2.7) is printed for positive integers p (p.256, first line); p₀ is a positive integer (p.244); at p = r it follows from p = ⌈r⌉ by raising to r/⌈r⌉ ≤ 1; (2.6)–(2.7) at general G: R-cpl₀). So q*(K′) ≤ q̄(j) := ⌈log_L(L³M(1+γ²β′(j+1))^{β₀}(log g_min^{−2})^r)⌉, which grows like β₀log_L j, while j − q grows like j. Hence the least j ≥ q with j ≥ q + q̄(j) exists; call it j(C). It depends on q, L, M, β₀, β′, r, g_min and γ only: not on ε, K, p or the torus. For K′ = K − j(C) the side pL^{j(C)−q} is a multiple of L^{q*(K′)}, so every step k+1 ≤ K′ is commensurate and (6)(b) is void. The construction needs K′ ≥ 1, i.e. K > j(C), true for ε small. For two loops (V4's two-loop theorem) translation puts only one loop's corner at the origin; the other's corners must be period points too, so take the least j ≥ max(q₁, q₂, q₁₂) + q̄(j), with q₁₂ the least q such that the displacement between the two loops' corners lies in L^{−q}uℤ⁴.
      - (ii) *D2 stopped at K′.* The induction D(1) → ⋯ → D(K′) uses the step map only. D2 is written for k ≤ N, N the number of LFR I p.177's condition (ii); N = N_k ≤ O(1)(log g_k^{−2})^ν with (1/2)ν ≤ p₀ − p₁ − 1 (LFR I p.198 after (1.94); p.192 N₀ = O(log g_k^{−2}); LFR II p.384 "N = R_j"), a function of g_k and so not a constant: at fixed k it grows like (log K)^ν with K, and the R-free initial segment {k : k ≤ N_k} has length O((log K)^ν) (WILSON_IT1_D27_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md item 1, replacing the earlier gloss "not growing with K"); for K′ > N_{K′}, which holds for all large K, D(K′) and the inputs at step K′−1 are D2.7's, derived in that note's item 15 given the reading R-far″, its round-52 findings (it1-d27-20261027-r3) repaired and not re-reviewed (l.342; the round-48 review killed items 4–9's accounting, replaced by item 15). With that proviso, the places identified so far where the final step enters, each checked with K′ in place of K: D2.0(1) (C a union of step-K′ bonds, by the corner placement and pL^{j(C)−q} ∈ ℤ, which is not a unit-scale condition since C is not a union of step-K bonds unless L^q divides p; torus periods multiples of L^{K′}, implied by the unit-scale torus condition); this bullet's q* (at K′, by (i)); (7)'s τ̄² := sup_{k<K′}τ_k² with g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K by CRE (2.6)'s last inequality with n = K, m = k (at general G this rests on the accepted Bałaban statement R-cpl₀, as the coupling prescription does; for simple G it is proved in the Bałaban project: ONE_LOOP_PROOFS.md Corollary 8.2's last clause, x_l − C ≤ x_k for k ≤ l ≤ K with x = g^{−2}, gives g_k² ≤ g_K²/(1 − Cg_K²)), so C_Στ̄² ≤ Cg_K²(log g_K^{−2})^{6p₀} with (1+β₀)² absorbed into C; (7)'s torus sum with N^{(k)}_min ≍ (T_ph/u)L^{K′−k+j(C)} (N^{(k)}_min, s_k, and P_k are physical lengths in step-k units, the same in the stopped run as in the K-step run); D2.3(4)(Z-d)'s torus condition (TC), reduced there to the coarsest torus through R_K and ι_{T,K}: at K′ it reads ι_{T,K′} ≥ 16C_DL⁴M(L+1)(1+γ²β′j(C))^{β₀}(log g_min^{−2})^r by CRE (2.7), implied by the unit-scale condition since ι_{T,K′} = L^{j(C)}ι_{T,K} and L^{j(C)} ≥ L^{q̄(j(C))} ≥ (1+γ²β′j(C))^{β₀}, and (Z-g)'s lcm then runs over k ≤ K′ (added after round 45); the deviation insertions summed at the stopping scale (D2.0(5)(c), checked with K′ in place of K, not reviewed: its per-step statements are the step map's and do not see K; its K-dependent constants are the coupling sup, g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K for k < K′ by CRE (2.6)'s last inequality as before, so the feedback recursion's condition g_K ≤ g(L) is unchanged, and the torus count in (ε)'s wrap term, N^{(j)}_min ≍ (T_ph/u)L^{K′−j+j(C)}, so the sum over j ≤ K′ is at most 2C(sup_jρ̂_j)e^{−c′(T_ph/u)L^{j(C)}/M} and the condition T_ph ≥ CM of (ε) reads (T_ph/u)L^{j(C)} ≥ CM, weaker than at K); ONE MORE STEP (7)(d), for K′ ≤ N only (its own scope; for K′ > N D2.7's), with CRE p.268 at step K′−1, where ε_{K′} ≤ (1+β₀)(1+g_K²β′j(C))^{β₀}ε_K, which is CRE (2.8)'s second line (p.256, printed; also from (2.4), (2.6), (2.7)), so its bound is O(pL^{j(C)−q}(1+g_K²β′j(C))^{β₀}ε_K/L), with constants independent of K, and its two smallness conditions are conditions on g_K for the given loop. They hold for small couplings: j(C) is least and q̄ is nondecreasing, so j(C) − q ≤ q̄(j(C)) and ℓ_C = pL^{j(C)−q} ≤ pL⁴M(1+γ²β′(j(C)+1))^{β₀}(log g_min^{−2})^r; with ε_{K′} ≤ (1+β₀)(1+γ²β′j(C))^{β₀}ε_K and ε_K = g_KA₀(log g_K^{−2})^{p₀}, ℓ_Cε_{K′} = O(p·γ(log g_min^{−2})^{r+p₀}(1+γ²β′(j(C)+1))^{2β₀}), and j(C) = O(log log g_min^{−2}) at fixed q, so ℓ_Cε_{K′} → 0 as γ → 0 with γ/g_min bounded (for instance g_K = ḡ → 0). D2.3(8)'s assembly and κ^{(K′)} (the perimeter coefficients of the steps 1, …, K′ and the corner factors) are the same formulas with K′. Every bound stated for k ≤ K in terms of g_K ((7)'s amplitude 𝔞̄ = max(τ̄², sup_kρ_k·pl) in its ρ part, (d)'s factor (1+β₀)⁸L²g_K², and the conditions "for every k ≤ K, g_K small" of D2.2 and D2.4) restricts to k ≤ K′ by the same g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K and the monotonicity of τ(g), ρ(g) for small g (round 45). Reviewed in round 45 (it1-d2-stop-20261026-r1): no fatal or serious finding; the minors applied here and at l.193 (l.499 already read R-cpl₀), not re-reviewed. Not checked, OPEN: D2.5–D2.7 at K′ (not written), explicitly D2.0(5)(f)'s large-field induction (Bałaban pp.384–389 rerun) and the inputs of the error bounds of (iii), which may use Bałaban's steps beyond K′; until they are written the claim that nothing uses the last step to the unit scale is not made.
      - (iii) *The final-scale expectation.* The identity E_ε[W(C)] = κ^{(K′)}⟨𝒲_{K′}⟩_{K′} + errors holds with ⟨·⟩_{K′} the expectation against Bałaban's step-K′ density, since D(K′) is the statement that the fine expectation equals the step-K′ integral of the dressed loop: stopping performs no further integral, and the identity does not use the remaining j(C) steps of Bałaban's construction; whether the bounds on the errors use them is the OPEN item of (ii). The identity holds on every labelled term on which D(K′) holds; for K′ ≤ N, on the large-field terms D(K′) is D2.6's (derived, WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md, with its §8 items); for K′ > N, on every labelled term, ℓ₀ included, D(K′) is D2.7's, derived in WILSON_IT1_D27_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md item 15.8 given the reading R-far″ and LFR II Theorem 1, its round-52 findings (it1-d27-20261027-r3) repaired and not re-reviewed (l.342). CRE Thm 2's (2.43)–(2.46) hold at k = K′ with the same E₁, R₁ (p.263: "independent of j, k, Ω, {Ω_j}, {Λ_j}, T"), for regular U_{K′}, under Theorem 1's assumptions, with (2.46) for κ₀ ≥ 7 and g small (p.263). The torus condition is on the periods in step-K′ units.
      - (iv) *The K → ∞ statement.* The continuum limit is ε = uL^{−K} → 0 at fixed u, fixed C and fixed physical torus periods T_ph, with the prescription of (i), so j(C) is fixed and K′ = K − j(C) → ∞. Four parts. (a) On ℓ₀ the integration variable is V_{K′} on the lattice of spacing uL^{−j(C)}, independent of ε (ℓ₀ has no outer variables, D2.3(2)). (b) On ℓ₀ the needed statement is the convergence there of the dressed-loop expectation ⟨𝒲_{K−j(C)}⟩ on ℓ₀ with κ^{(K′)} divided out: along subsequences it converges to a weak limit point of the stopping-scale effective measures tested against the fixed coarse loop (the continuum note WILSON_IT1_CONTINUUM_LIMIT_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md, items (3.5), (8), derived given D(K′) on every labelled term); along the full sequence it is the comparison of two runs (WHAT THE CONSTRUCTION IS; OPEN, the continuum note's item (9), not in D2), with no uniformity in j(C) required. κ^{(K′)} is a K-dependent number (P_{k−1}(C) = 4ℓ_CL^{K′−k+1} and the couplings depend on ε), not an object on that lattice, divided out exactly (the continuum note's item (6)). (c) On labelled terms with large-field regions the step-K′ variables include V_j|Γ_j for j < K′ (CRE (2.2), p.255; ONE MORE STEP (7)(c)), on lattices of spacing uL^{j−K} that depend on ε; already at step 1 V4's E_h runs over the exterior fine field V₀. These terms are D2.6's and the final-scale step's; their total relative weight is 1 − w_{ℓ₀} ≤ 1, and the continuum note's item (7) reduces the bound δ_LF ≤ 4N_s⁴(MR_{K′})^{−4}e^{−(3/2)p₀(g_{K′})} to the exact quotient identity, LFR II's normalised operations (1.70)–(1.99), and one residual quotient Q_ℓ, the small-field integral of the term's completed form over the enlarged large-field region against the ℓ₀ term's, which no print bounds (the bound (Q), not derived); their far part is cancelled by the R-operation given D2.7 (the note's item (4.4)). CRE p.244's first-step per-plaquette bound gives no positive power of ε, against ≍ ε^{−4} fine plaquettes of the fixed torus, so the large-field terms away from C are not small by counting; their normalisation against the partition function is the quotient identity of the note's item (7) with the bound (Q), and their cancellation against the partition function is the R-operation's own, given D2.7 (item (4.4)). (d) K-uniform bounds on the errors do not give convergence along the full sequence; relative to κ^{(K′)} the errors are K-uniformly bounded and converge along the subsequences of (b) (the note's items (5), (6), (8)); their convergence along the full sequence is (b)'s OPEN item, not a separate one.
      - *Result.* Derived: the existence and K-independence of j(C) under the coupling prescription of (i), and the places of (ii) identified so far at K′, for K′ ≤ N, so the admissible sides restrict no square on the L-adic lattices as far as those places are concerned. The coupling threshold of (ii) depends on the loop (on p and q). The coupling prescription of (i) is Bałaban's statement at general G (RG I Thm 2's first assertion with p.260), accepted, its proof not printed; for simple G proved in the Bałaban project (ONE_LOOP_PROOFS.md Corollary 8.2). D2.7 at K′ > N: derived in WILSON_IT1_D27_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md item 15 given the reading R-far″, its round-52 findings (it1-d27-20261027-r3) repaired and not re-reviewed (l.342; round 48 killed items 8, 9 and 11's accounting, replaced by item 15; (5)(c)(α) and (3)(b) derived). The continuum limit ((iv)(c)–(d)): WILSON_IT1_CONTINUUM_LIMIT_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md (26 Oct; reviewed in round 48, it1-cont-20261026-r1: no fatal, five serious repaired, not re-reviewed) fixes the stopping lattice K-independently and reduces the limit to the Cauchy property of the whole renormalised expectation W_ren = E_ε[W(C)]/κ^{(K′)} between the K-run and the (K+1)-run on the common stopping lattice; that property, the relative-weight inequality bounding the large-field terms against the small-field term (its costs (a)–(e) at the note's (4.3)), the convergence of g_{K−i}, and κ^{(K′)} remain OPEN there, as (iv)(c)–(d) state.
    - Without the restriction, the steps with Π_{k+1} ∤ s_k leave a remainder of order τ_k²s_k from the two sides off the origin ((6)(b)).
- *(Z) Torus consistency (D2.4 item; added to D(k) as clause (vi), at flat data on ℓ₀).* For a cluster Γ of the reference gas on ℓ₀ whose groups have short arcs on a straight line or on a bent path, the weight Φ°(Γ; T) is defined on every torus T of the sequence (H3) in which Γ embeds. *Embeds*: the dependence set of Γ (the union of its groups' X̃⁵-neighbourhoods, PBG Thm 3.10, with the π_{k+1}-cubes of RG2 p.13, the supports of its occurrences and its arcs, the dependence sets of the old insertions contracted into its groups, and the L²M₂R_{k+1}-cubes of the cutoffs of CRE (3.16) that enter its activities) has diameter < ι_T/4, in step-(k+1) block units. R_D is the radius of a neighbourhood of the network that contains the dependence set, in those units (computed in (Z-g): R_D^{(k)} ≤ 6L²MR_k step-k bonds, R_D = R_D^{(k)}/L step-(k+1) units). Its contributions: X̃⁵-layers of M₁-cubes, O(M/L); π_{k+1}-cubes, M; the old insertions' own margins, O(ML^{j−k−1}) for one made at step j; and the cutoff cubes, L²M₂R_{k+1} in the T_η scale, which contain R_{k+1} ≍ (log g_{k+1}^{−2})^r and so are not O(M) uniformly in the coupling. The item: these weights have limits Φ°^∞(Γ) as the periods grow, at the rate Σ_{Γ: b∈J_Γ}|Φ°(Γ; T) − Φ°^∞(Γ)| ≤ C_Z^{(k+1)}𝔞_{k+1}e^{−c^Zι_{T,k+1}}, 𝔞_{k+1} := max(τ_k², ρ_{k+1}·pl) (constants of (Z-f)), the sum over the clusters rooted at a traversal b. In this sum a cluster that does not embed in T enters with |Φ°(Γ; T)| on T and with |Φ°^∞(Γ)| on ℤ⁴ (Φ°(Γ; T) := 0 for a ℤ⁴-cluster that does not embed in T); (Z-f) proves the bound in this form.
  - *V4 line numbers in this item:* all "V4 L<n>" below refer to V4 at hash b35c60e537b8.
  - At step 1 this is V4's periodisation argument (V4 L1736, with the image bound eq:bulk-image-error, V4 L3893, which bounds the free covariance entries K_T against K_∞ only). The rate is V4's |F_{C,T} − F_{C,∞}(n)| ≤ Cτ²(4n)e^{−cι_T} (eq:regional-square-periodic-comparison, V4 L2198), one of the three parts of E°_{T,n} listed at V4 L210. That is a total bound over the square; its proof is per root ("Summing over 4n roots gives", V4 L2197), and the rooted form for the insertions as functions of U₁ is (Z-h).
  - At step k+1 it needs the same for Bałaban's flat activities H(Z), which the s-parameters localise (RG2 p.13: σ₀ is the family of cubes of π_{k+1} "disjoint with the interior of Z̃₀, or with the interior of Z₀′"), and for the step-k objects by (vi) at step k. Status: *Status* below.
  - *R-loc (sibling of R-tr).* The walk pieces R_α(X) of PBG (3.107) and RG2 (1.6), and the local operators they are built from (G_□, G′_{□̃²} with the boundary conditions outside □̃⁵, Q_j, D, D*, and the pieces of C^{(k)} and (C^{(k)})^{1/2}, (3.183)–(3.186), PBG p.432, RG2 (2.7) with G̃₃(x)), are defined on an arbitrary torus of (H3) by the same local formulas: on T′ ⊃ T with the partitions and walk-domain families lifted from T, a piece whose dependence set embeds in T is the same function of the configuration on that set on T and on T′. Status: the single-torus restriction is printed (PBG p.394, Thm 3.10, RG2 p.3, p.14); the cross-torus identity is derived (each elementary operator restricted to its domain is a finite matrix with the local entries (3.23)–(3.24), (3.87), (3.90), (3.105); on an isometric non-wrapping copy of the domain it is the same matrix, and compositions and inverses, such as C_□ = (Q′G′_□²Q′*)^{−1}, inherit this); the lifted-partition and walk-domain convention is ours, a choice the prints leave free (RG2 p.13: "can be chosen to a large extent arbitrarily"; PBG p.415: "for an arbitrary partition"); accepted by the user and merged into R-tr's convention: every free choice (cube partitions, partitions of unity, walk-domain families, the Λ-modification, the PBG p.411 tie average) is made by a translation-covariant geometric rule, so on tori whose periods are multiples of the grid sides T′'s choices are the lifts of T's. Families defined relative to a polymer (σ₀ around Z̃₀ or Z₀′, RG2 p.13) are not free choices: they are determined by the polymer and the grid, so for an embedding polymer they agree locally, on Z̃, with the lifts of T's. The CRE (3.16) cutoff cubes, of side L²M₂R_{k+1}, are among the cubes whose sides divide Π_{k+1} (D2.3(1), *Size*: L²M₂R_{j+1} ≤ LMR_{j+1}, all powers of L), and the admissible tori have periods that are multiples of Π_{k+1}; so their partition on T′ is the lift of T's, with no separate lift rule. The activities and insertions are not part of R-loc; their identity is derived from it in (Z-c). Evidence: PBG Theorem 3.10 (a walk term "depends on configuration U restricted to X̃₀⁵ ∪ ⋯ ∪ X̃_n⁵"), PBG pp.415–416 (p.415: "G′_{□̃₀²} with the boundary conditions outside □̃₀⁵"; that every piece has this form is part of the reading). The user can check it against PBG (3.87), (3.90), (3.98), (3.105), (3.183)–(3.186) and RG2 (2.1)–(2.11). Non-local objects (the minimiser, whose restriction is local only up to e^{−cR}, (LPG′)) are not covered. Units: ι_T, |x − y| below are in step-(k+1) block units (RG2's unit lattice at step k+1). RG2 measures (1.7)'s δ₀ per unit of the T_η scale, in which π_{k+1}-cubes have side LM; a step-(k+1) block unit is at least one T_η unit, so using δ₀ per step-(k+1) unit gives a weaker decay and is safe. The cubes of (3.16) are the L²M₂R_{k+1}-cubes of D2.3(1). No transcribed text says M₁ is a power of L (not used below). (H3) is read as a nested sequence of tori (each torus' periods multiples of the previous one's); for two tori of (H3) that are not nested the comparison goes through their least-common-multiple torus, which must itself be admissible: its periods are multiples of Π_{k+1} (they are multiples of both tori's periods), and (S-w) must hold on it, which is part of its torus-uniform statement (settled in (Z-g); the tail (T′) follows from (S-w) on each torus).
  - *(Z-a) Periodisation (derived; a remark, not used below; it needs the decay stated in it, and it does not cover the restricted or s-localised operators).* Let T ⊂ T′ be two tori of (H3), the periods of T′ multiples of those of T, ι_T the shortest period of T. Call an operator on T′ *T-periodic* if its kernel satisfies P(x+m, y+m) = P(x,y) for the translations m of T, and *decaying* if |P(x,y)| ≤ Be^{−δ|x−y|}. For such P put per(P)(x,y) := Σ_{n∈T′/T}P(x, y+n), an operator on T. per is linear, per(P₁P₂) = per(P₁)per(P₂) (write Σ_z over T′ as Σ_{z mod T}Σ_m and use the periodicity of P₂ in both arguments; absolute convergence by the decay), per(1) = 1, so per(P^{−1}) = per(P)^{−1} when both inverses are decaying, and per commutes with the resolvent integral and the series of RG2 (2.7). If A is a finite-range T-periodic operator, per(A) is its restriction to T-periodic functions, i.e. the same operator written on T. Bałaban's operators at a T-periodic background 𝐔̃ on T′ (the lift of 𝐔 on T) are given by the same formulas as on T (a reading of the R-loc kind for the full operators, not used below), as compositions, inverses, resolvents and square roots of finite-range T-periodic operators, with decaying kernels uniformly in the torus: PBG Theorem 3.15, (3.187), |C^{(k)}(Λ;y,y′)| ≤ B₀e^{−δ₀|y−y′|} "with the constants B₀, δ₀ depending on d and L only", and (3.108) for the G-type operators, "The constant O(1) depends on d and L only", both for regular backgrounds. Hence every such operator satisfies P_T = per(P_{T′}), and for |x − y| ≤ ι_T/2
    |P_T(x,y) − P_{T′}(x,y)| = |Σ_{n≠0}P_{T′}(x, y+n)| ≤ B₀′e^{−δ₀ι_T/2},
    B₀′ depending on d, L and δ₀ (the images y + n, n ≠ 0, lie at distance ≥ ι_T/2 from x, and the image sum converges geometrically).
  - *(Z-b) The s-localised propagators (derived from PBG (3.107)–(3.108) and RG2 p.3; a remark at T-periodic data, not used below: Bałaban's activity on T′ uses T′'s own σ₀ around one lift (RG2 p.13), not the periodic lift of s, so (Z-b) does not compare the objects that enter the activities).* P(s) is the walk sum (1.6) with the s-weights of RG2 p.3. By PBG Theorem 3.10, a walk term "depends on configuration U restricted to X̃₀⁵ ∪ X̃₁⁵ ∪ ⋯ ∪ X̃_n⁵", and its local pieces are local inverses on cubes with boundary conditions (PBG pp.415–416), so at a T-periodic background the pieces of a walk whose domain embeds in T are the same on T and on T′. The s-family on T′ is the lift of T's: s(Δ̃) := s(Δ) for every lift Δ̃ of a cube Δ of T. A walk on T′ from x of diameter < ι_T/2 projects to exactly one walk on T from x with the same pieces (by R-loc) and the same s-weight (the cubes it meets are the lifts of the cubes its projection meets, and no two lifts of one T-cube are met); conversely each walk on T from x of diameter < ι_T/2 has exactly one lift from x, ending at the one image of its endpoint within distance ι_T/2 of x. Therefore P_T(s)(x,y) − P_{T′}(s)(x,y), for |x − y| ≤ ι_T/2, is (i) − (ii), with (i) the walks on T from x to y of diameter ≥ ι_T/2 and (ii) the walks on T′ from x to y of diameter ≥ ι_T/2 (P_{T′}(s)(x,y) has no walks ending at y + n, n ≠ 0); each has d(ω) ≥ ι_T/2. By (1.7) with |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s} and RG2 p.5's argument (δ₀d(ω) ≥ δ₁mM ≥ 2mκ_s, i.e. δ₁M ≥ 2κ_s, absorbs the s-weights of long walks; short walks carry at most e^{16κ_s}), each of the two sums is at most B₀e^{16κ_s}e^{−δ₀ι_T/4}. So, on the s-domain and analytically in s, for |x − y| ≤ ι_T/2:
    |P_T(s)(x,y) − P_{T′}(s)(x,y)| ≤ 2B₀e^{16κ_s}e^{−δ₀ι_T/4}.
  - *(Z-c) The activities are exactly local (derived given R-loc).* (A reading R-rep, an interpolation of the propagators with bounds "at most doubled", doesn't work: it goes beyond RG2 p.3 l.3–4 and p.17 l.12–20 and is not uniform in |Z|.) For a polymer Z (or marked polymer M, or group 𝒢) whose X̃⁵-neighbourhood (PBG Thm 3.10's dependence set) embeds in T, H(Z) (RG2 (2.11)), H̃(M), Ψ(M), Ĥ(M) (D2.2(5)(c)–(d)) and the new c_𝒢 (D2.3(2)) are functions of: the s-localised propagators (RG2 (2.8)–(2.10)), taken at s with s(Y₀ᶜ) = 0 and |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ₁} on Y₀∖□̃⁴ (RG2 pp.4–5: (1.10) and its Cauchy estimates use only such s), at which the surviving walks lie in Y₀ and "this term depends on 𝐔, 𝐉 restricted to Y₀ ∪ □₀"; the cutoffs, Mayer factors and Gaussian integrals over its variables; the insertions Y_G, functions of H(s), G̃(s), H₀(s) restricted to their components and of B′ (RG2 p.4–5: "all propagators and the function B are restricted to Y₀"); and the carried old insertions and old cluster weights ((Z-e)). The dependence statements above are RG2 Sect. 1's (Y₀, □̃⁴, cubes of size M). For the Gaussian integrals of RG2 Sect. 2 the supplied evidence is p.13 ("We localize it again by the method used in Sect. 1, introducing the parameters s and differentiating", with π_{k+1}-cubes and σ₀ relative to Z̃₀, Z₀′) and p.14 after (2.9): "The function H(Z) is localized in the interior of Z with respect to the external gauge fields". By R-loc the walk pieces and local operators entering them are the same functions on T and on T′ (with lifted partitions and walk-domain families), and the cutoffs, Mayer factors and Gaussian integrals are composed from them by the same formulas. Hence, for the lift Z̃ of Z to T′ and backgrounds (𝐔, 𝐉) on T, (𝐔′, 𝐉′) on T′ that coincide under the covering on Z's dependence set (𝐉 included: p.5 says "𝐔, 𝐉"),
    H_T(Z)(𝐔, 𝐉) = H_{T′}(Z̃)(𝐔′, 𝐉′) exactly for the vacuum activities, given (vac)_k below, and H̃(M), Ψ(M), Ĥ(M) and c_𝒢 at flat data on ℓ₀ are the same functionals of the configuration on the dependence set and of the carried old factors on both tori
    (the Mayer factors are geometric, defined by cubes and walls (RG2 p.14); the cutoffs are CRE (3.16)'s tests on L²M₂R_{k+1}-cubes, cube-local, and their cubes belong to the dependence set; flat data on ℓ₀ at every scale).
    - *The steps written out.* (i) The s-family: H_{T′}(Z̃) is built with T′'s own σ₀ around one lift (RG2 p.13); the cubes of T′'s σ₀ outside Z̃ carry s = 0 in the term, and those of Z̃∖Z̃₀′ are the lifts of those of Z∖Z₀′, so the surviving walks are the lifts of T's (the same holds for Sect. 1's σ₀ in (Z-e)(a)). (ii) The solutions D(H(s), A′) and 𝐀₀, restricted to the component, are the unique fixed points of the same restricted equations (pp.4–5: "all propagators and the function B are restricted to Y₀"). (iii) The Gaussian integrals: in (2.5) the measures dμ_{C^{(k)}} and dμ_{C^{(k)}(Z₀)} are normalised, and after B′ = (C^{(k)})^{1/2}X, (2.6)'s dμ₀(X) is the product over bonds of normalised standard Gaussians (p.12); the integrand depends on X only on Z (p.17, "∫dμ₀(X)|_Z"), and C^{(k)}(Z₀), (C^{(k)})^{1/2} enter through their walk expansions (p.13: "discussed in [13] for all operators determining Δ_k, and for C^{(k)}(Z₀)", and (2.7)), whose pieces R-loc covers. So no global normalisation constant enters H(Z). (iv) 𝐉: p.14 says "external gauge fields"; 𝐉 = J(𝐔) is local (R-J, RG1 (1.8), (3.12)). What is used: H(Z) depends on 𝐉 only through 𝐉 restricted to its dependence set, by RG2 p.5 ("this term depends on 𝐔, 𝐉 restricted to Y₀ ∪ □₀") for the Sect. 1 pieces and p.14 for H(Z).
    - *(vac)_j, the inherited vacuum objects.* H(Z) also contains the step-k vacuum activities: it is a sum "over Z₀, 𝐃, P" (RG2 p.14), its integrand is built from "the last term in (I.3.34), or an arbitrary expression obtained by the transformations of one of the terms" (p.3), p.17's bound carries "∏_{Y∈𝐃}2E₀ε₁…exp(−(1−3δ)κd_k(Y))", and (2.26) contains g_k. So exactness needs (vac)_j: for every X ∈ 𝐃_j that embeds in T, 𝐄^{(j)}_T(X) = 𝐄^{(j)}_{T′}(X̃) as analytic functions of the full argument (𝐔, 𝐉, 𝐀) restricted to X's dependence set (RG2 p.3 (1.8) evaluates 𝐄(□₀, 𝐔, 𝐀) at 𝐀 = (tζ̃ + t_□ζ)𝐇_k(…)), and g_j is the same on T and T′; the extracted constants agree per polymer, and the global ones cancel in the normalised state. *Embeds* for X ∈ 𝐃_j: the R_D^{(j)}-neighbourhood of X has diameter < ι_{T,j}/4, so the covering T′ → T is injective on it and X has lifts X̃, unique up to deck translations of T′ over T.
      - *Couplings (printed).* RG1 p.264: Π_{j+1} is taken in the "limit of these functions as T^{(j+1)} ↗ Z^d. This limit exists by the localized representation (1.7). The function β_{j+1}(g_j) is defined by" (1.22). So the β-functions are ℤ^d quantities, and with g_K prescribed (D2.3(4)(i)) the g_j of (0.20) are the same on every torus. The coupling-constant subtraction −β_{j+1}(g_j)A(U_k) (RG1 p.261) is a local term with a torus-independent coefficient.
      - *No finite-torus residual (derived from RG1 (0.19)–(0.20), (1.3), (1.6), (1.20), p.264).* The torus functions Π^T_{j+1} differ from their ℤ^d limit, so one might expect the torus action to keep (Π^T_{j+1} − Π_{j+1})·A(U). It does not. In (1.6), A_k = −(1/g_k²)A(U_k) + Σ_{j<k}{−β_{j+1}(g_j)A(U_k) + [𝐄^{(j+1)}(g_j,U_k) − 𝐄^{(j+1)}(g_j,1)]}, and (0.20), "1/g_k² = 1/g_{k+1}² + β_{k+1}(g_k)", gives Σ_{j<k}β_{j+1}(g_j) = 1/g_0² − 1/g_k². So the Wilson terms sum to −(1/g_0²)A(U_k): the β-terms rewrite the Wilson coefficient through the numbers g_j and are never subtracted from 𝐄^{(j+1)}. In (1.6), 𝐄^{(j+1)} is the full torus function of the step-j integration (p.261: "a result of an integration in the j-th step, after a subtraction of the previous action evaluated at U_j"), localised by (1.7). Π^T_{j+1} is only its second derivative at B = 0 ((1.20)), used to read off β_{j+1} in the ℤ^d limit (p.264). So the torus part of Π^T_{j+1} is the second derivative of Σ_X𝐄^{(j+1)}_T(X) over the X that do not embed in T (by (vac)_{j+1} for the embedding ones). Those X sit in the non-embedding polymers of (Z-d) and are bounded there; nothing is left for the embedding ones. The g_j enter the next step as numbers ((0.19): the block term −(1/g_k²)Σ[1 − Re tr U(y,x)], χ_k, the field scaling), the same on T and T′.
      - *Step (derived).* Given (vac)_k, the new activities H(Z) for embedding Z are the same functionals on T and T′ by (i)–(iv) and R-loc, with the inherited 𝐄^{(k)}(Y), Y ⊂ Z, equal by (vac)_k. Then 𝐄^{(k+1)}(X) of (2.13) is a sum over families Z₁, …, Z_n ⊂ X with ρ^T geometric (ζ is defined by cubes and walls, p.14), so it is the same on T and T′ for embedding X. Its constant part 𝐄^{(k+1)}(X, 1) is a local number of the same kind; the subtracted global constants (E_k, p.262 of CRE; RG1's log Z^{(j)}(1)) are field-independent and cancel in the normalised state.
      - *Base (exact).* (vac)_0 holds: the bare action is the Wilson action, the same local function on every torus, and 𝐄^{(0)} = 0. The step-1 vacuum is RG1's: V4 L357, "In RG1's small-field construction, with no large-field regions, … Write 𝐄^{(1)} for the first-step effective interaction, including the Gaussian normalisation", and V4 L147, "those factors remain inside the construction of N₁ and ρ₁". V4's periodised free carrier K_T (V4 L3881–3898) belongs to the conditional integral with the Wilson insertion, whose base is (Z-h). (vac)_1 is not taken from V4's constructions: L357 identifies the step-1 vacuum with RG1's 𝐄^{(1)}, Bałaban's localised function, so (vac)_1 is the *Step* above at k = 0 from the exact (vac)_0. V4's own vacuum clusters use "the infinite-lattice covariance, not a periodically repeated finite-volume covariance" (V4 L3898) and are counted in (vi)_1's base ((Z-h)), not in (vac)_1. So (vac)_j holds for every j on ℓ₀, given R-loc's convention, the *Step* ((i)–(iv), with (iii)'s normalisation from RG2 p.12, (2.5)–(2.6)) and the *No finite-torus residual* bullet. The T–T′ difference of H̃(M), Ψ(M), Ĥ(M) and c_𝒢 is the difference of the carried old factors only, which (Z-e)–(Z-f) bound. No difference bound and no Schwarz step is needed for the new parts. (RG2 p.17's α₅ is Bałaban's remainder size, not an allowance for arbitrary perturbations of the quadratic form.)
  - *(Z-d) The cluster weights (the rooted sum is closed in (Z-f)).* Φ°(Γ) = (Ursell coefficient of Γ)·∏_{𝒢∈Γ}c_𝒢, and the Ursell coefficient depends only on the incompatibility graph, which is the same on T and T′ for clusters whose dependence sets embed in T (the hard core is geometric, D2.3(3)). A group's diameter, and the network of a cluster, include the supports of the vacuum occurrences merged into it (D2.3(3)). For such clusters, by (Z-c), Φ°_T(Γ) − Φ°_{T′}(Γ̃) comes only from the old objects inside the groups ((Z-e)). Telescoping over them, one factor at a time replaced by its difference, the rooted sum of these differences is bounded by (vi)_k in the summed form of (Z-e), through D2.4(13)(c) (|c_𝒢| ≤ S_𝒢 on both tori is (S)). The count is a Kotecký–Preiss root sum in which the telescoping produces one term per old factor. For the old occurrences of a group, m_𝒢 telescoped terms carry 1/m_𝒢!, and m/m! = 1/(m−1)!, so the factorial absorbs them and no weight is needed. For the old insertions D2.4(13)(c) sums over marked trees, so no factor n_ins(𝒢) (the number of old insertions in 𝒢) is produced and no part of a° is reserved for one; the bound a(𝒢) ≥ a_min·n_ins(𝒢) is recorded with the root sum (D2.4(13)(b)). It follows from the size count: the old insertions of one step-(k+1) group lie on I(𝒢) (D2.3(4), *Y_𝒢*) and come from one compatible step-k family, whose arcs are disjoint (D2.3(3), *Hard core*), each of at least one step-k traversal; I(𝒢) has L·n_{k+1}(𝒢) step-k traversals, so n_ins(𝒢) ≤ L·n_{k+1}(𝒢) ≤ LMσ_{k+1}(𝒢) (σ_{k+1} = #_{k+1} + n_{k+1}/M, D2.4(12)(a)), and a° = (ν/32)σ_{k+1} gives a_min = ν/(32LM). (The unit of arc-disjointness in the step-k hard core is the step-k traversal, D2.3(3); that the carried groups and the step-1 base keep it is part of (S) and of (vi)_1's base, (Z-h).) The group-level constant is G = 2, the marked-vertex count of D2.4(13)(c), with C_KP = C_S′ and the amplitude ρ_{k+1}·pl. The clusters whose dependence sets do not embed in T have dependence diameter ≥ ι_T/4, so network extent ≥ ι_T/4 − 2R_D ≥ ι_T/8 once ι_T ≥ 16R_D (R_D is a neighbourhood radius, so the diameter loss is up to 2R_D), and are at most C_S′ρ_{k+1}·pl·e^{−νι_{T,k+1}/(512M)} on each torus, by the a°-weight of the reference gas ((Z-f)). Hence, once ι_T ≥ 16R_D,
    Σ_{Γ: b∈J_Γ}|Φ°(Γ; T) − Φ°(Γ̃; T′)| ≤ C_Z^{(k+1)}ρ_{k+1}·pl·e^{−c^Zι_{T,k+1}}, with C_Z^{(k+1)} and c^Z as in (Z-f),
    for T ⊂ T′, where the old-object term uses ι_{T,k} = Lι_{T,k+1} = (L−1)ι_{T,k+1} + ι_{T,k+1}. *The branch ι_T < 16R_D is excluded by a torus condition.* (A factor e^{16R_Dc^Z} in C_Z doesn't work: it grows like e^{c(log g_K^{−2})^r}, since R_D contains the cutoff cubes L²M₂R_{k+1}, and breaks (7)'s smallness.) (TC): ι_{T,k} ≥ 16L·R_D^{(k)} at every step k ≤ K, both in step-k units (this implies ι_{T,k+1} ≥ 16R_D in step-(k+1) units, since ι_{T,k} = Lι_{T,k+1}). With R_D^{(k)} ≤ C_DL²MR_k, C_D = 6 ((Z-g)), (TC) at every k follows from the single condition on the coarsest torus
    ι_{T,K} ≥ 16C_DL⁴M(L+1)(log g_min^{−2})^r:
    by CRE (2.5) (p.255: "R_j is the smallest number of the form L^r such, that R_j ≥ (log g_j^{−2})^r") R_K ≤ L(log g_K^{−2})^r ≤ L(log g_min^{−2})^r under the prescription g_K ≥ g_min (D2.3(4)(i)); by CRE (2.9) R_k ≤ (L+1)(K−k)^{β₀}R_K; and ι_{T,k} = L^{K−k}ι_{T,K} with L^{K−k} ≥ (K−k)^{β₀} (β₀ ≤ 1). It bounds the unit-scale torus from below by a constant independent of K, ε and the step, compatible with the tori of (H3) (which grow) and with (5)(g)'s T_ph ≫ ℓ_C, of which it is a quantified part. Under (TC) the trivial branch never occurs, and C_Z^{(k+1)} carries no R_D-dependent factor. C_Z is the absolute constant of this rooted sum only; the per-object relative constant is C_rel of (Z-e). Uniformity in k: by D2.4(13)(c) the recursion has a tail term from the non-embedding vacuum clusters inside the marked polymers' vacuum factors, C_rel^{(j+1)} ≤ ¼e^{−c(L−1)ι_{T,j+1}}C_rel^{(j)} + C⁎, c := min(c₁, 1/(32M)), so C_rel^{(k)} ≤ max(C_rel^{(1)}, 2C⁎) uniformly in k and in the torus. C_Z^{(1)} and C_rel^{(1)} are V4's ((Z-h)). The weights form a Cauchy sequence along the nested (H3), so the limits Φ°^∞(Γ) exist with the same rate, passed per root with Fatou ((Z-f), *Limits*).
  - *(Z-e) The old objects and the induction (the carry k → k+1 is D2.4(13)(c), the base is (Z-h); (O) is stated per position, as D2.4(13)(c) delivers it, see D(k)(vi) in D2.0(3)).* The new activities contain the carried old insertions Y_W(𝐔) as factors, evaluated at the step-(k+1) background 𝐔 = e^{iη𝐇_k(s)(B′)}·1 inside the fluctuation integral, not at 1, and the old occurrences as the numbers φ_k(Γ_old; T) (D2.3(3)'s merging rules; φ_k are numbers given ℓ, D(k)(v), not functions of (𝐔, 𝐉)). That background is not T-periodic on T′: 𝐀₀ "is equal to 0 on the neighborhood of Y(σ)ᶜ" (RG2 p.4), so the deformation lives on one lift of Y₀ only, and a clause at periodic lifts does not apply. The clause (vi)_k is therefore stated in local-agreement form, summed per traversal (a per-object clause carries the factor n_old(W), unbounded since occurrences are counted with multiplicity m_𝒢 and 1/m_𝒢!, so no W-independent constant follows). For a torus T′ ⊃ T of (H3) and a pair (𝐔, 𝐉) on T, (𝐔′, 𝐉′) on T′ in D(k)(iii)'s domains (𝐉 for k ≥ 2), ΔY_W := Y_W^T(𝐔,𝐉) − Y_W^{T′}(𝐔′,𝐉′) for an insertion W made at a step ≤ k that embeds in T, the configurations coinciding under the covering on the ι_{T,k}/4-neighbourhood of a lift of W's dependence set, and Δφ_k(Γ) := φ_k(Γ; T) − φ_k(Γ̃; T′). Then (vi)_k states, on ℓ₀ (no attached species, so D(k)(ii) is the right comparison), per position with the full (ii-w) weight, as D2.4(13)(c) uses and delivers it: (I) at every step-k traversal p, ε^{diff}_k(p) := Σ_{W at p, embeds}e^{θ_k(W) + ν_rσ_k(W) + ν_e·ext_k(W)}ℓ_{X_W}|ΔY_W| + Σ_{𝒢 at p, embeds}e^{(ν/16 + ν_r)σ_k(𝒢) + ν_e·ext_k(𝒢)}ℓ_{X(𝒢)}|Δc_𝒢| ≤ C_rel^{(k)}e^{−c_kι_{T,k}}·(D(k)(ii)'s right side in its (ii-w) form at p, the larger of its values on T and T′, since δ*_n's radii R_t^{(k)} = C_boxM(t+1) exceed the agreement radius for large t; ρ_k·pl_k at the reference point), the 𝒢 the groups made at step k; and (O) at every position p, Σ_{Γ at p, embeds}|Δφ_k(Γ)|e^{d(Γ)} ≤ 2e^{2c_sh+1}C_pc′C_rel^{(k)}e^{−c_kι_{T,k}}ρ_k·pl_k, with D2.4(13)(c)'s d(Γ) = Σ_{𝒢∈Γ}(ν_rσ_k(𝒢) + ν_e·ext_k(𝒢) + 2c_sh + 1 + log C_pc′) (the cluster level of D2.4(13)(c) at step k, from (I)'s group part). (I) is the inductive clause at traversals; at π_k-cubes the sums are derived at each step by D2.4(12)(e)'s box reduction (D2.4(13)(c)), since a per-cube clause carries a factor ~L².
