Carrying: the new parts of a step-(k+1) object are exact by (Z-c); its old factors (insertions inside the fluctuation integral, occurrences as numbers) are telescoped, one replaced by its difference, and the activity is multilinear in each factor inside the integral; the sums over the new objects containing n are then bounded by D2.4(13)(c)'s marked tree sums (D2.4(12)(f)'s rooted count with one vertex marked by its difference; a sum over marked trees, so no factor n_ins(W⁺) arises and no part of a° is reserved for it). This gives (I), (O) at k+1 with C_rel^{(k+1)} ≤ ¼e^{−c(L−1)ι_{T,k+1}}C_rel^{(k)} + C⁎ (D2.4(13)(c); C⁎ the tail term from the non-embedding vacuum clusters in the marked polymers' vacuum factors: e^{−Ψ°(M)}, δΨ(M) and the links' x_T sum over all vacuum clusters incompatible with M) and c_{k+1} ≤ c_k, using ι_{T,k} = Lι_{T,k+1}; the occurrences' telescoped terms are absorbed by 1/m_𝒢!. The deviation part: (I) at k+1 is relative to D(k+1)(ii)'s right side, whose dev term vanishes at (1, 0); ΔY^d_{W⁺} is the difference of two functions vanishing at (1, 0), analytic on the common domain, and is bounded relative to the deviation by the Cauchy step of D2.4(4) applied to it, as D2.4(13)(c) does at every step; the rooted cluster sum is then (Z-f)'s. The geometric part of the carry, for a new object W⁺ whose dependence set D⁺ has diameter < ι_{T,k+1}/4 and configurations coinciding on the ι_{T,k+1}/4-neighbourhood of a lift D̃⁺: (a) on the lift Ỹ₀ the deformations e^{iη𝐇_k(s)(B′)} on T and T′ agree, by RG2 pp.4–5 (the deformation is restricted to Y₀) and R-loc, with the integration variables identified by the covering; (b) on T′ the deformation is 0 off Ỹ₀ (p.4), and a point whose projection lies in Y₀ but which is not in Ỹ₀ lies on another lift of Y₀, at distance ≥ ι_T − diam D⁺ > 3ι_T/4 from W̃, outside the neighbourhood N(W̃) of radius ι_{T,k}/4 step-k units = ι_{T,k+1}/4 step-(k+1) units; (c) N(W̃) ⊂ N(D̃⁺), since W ⊂ D⁺ and the radii are the same physical length. So (vi)_k's hypothesis holds at the inner backgrounds for the old insertions of W⁺, given that these backgrounds lie in D(k)(iii)'s domain (D2.2(4)(f), (6)(e)) and 𝐉-agreement, which needs J local (R-J). The base, (vi)_1 for V4's step-1 groups as functions of U₁ and for V4's cluster weights, needs the locality of V4's insertions in U₁ up to images and the rooted form: V4's image bound eq:bulk-image-error (V4 L3893) is stated for the covariance entries only, at the rate e^{−c₁ι_{T,1}} with ι_{T,1} = L^kι_{T,k+1}; that it covers the group insertions as functions of U₁ is derived in (Z-h) from V4's density-direction estimate (L2178–2186, L2037–2040) applied to V3's physical-data integrals (L4608), with one structural input for the user's confirmation.
  - *(Z-f) The non-embedding clusters from the Kotecký–Preiss weight, and the rooted sum of (Z-d) closed (derived from D2.4(12)(f), (13)(c); no network property of (T) is used).* Let Γ be a cluster of the step-(k+1) reference gas on T (or on T′, or on ℤ⁴). Its groups are connected under incompatibility (D2.3(3) *Hard core*: arcs meeting, domains incompatible, an occurrence hitting a member), and two incompatible groups have supports at distance ≤ one π_{k+1}-cube side M (D2.4(12)(e): the incompatible positions lie in the 3⁴-neighbourhood). A group's support (its cubes, its arc hull I(𝒢), the domains of its merged occurrences' members, all counted in σ_{k+1}(𝒢) by D2.4(12)(c)) is connected with diameter ≤ M#_{k+1}(𝒢) + n_{k+1}(𝒢) = Mσ_{k+1}(𝒢) in step-(k+1) units. So the network of Γ (the union of its groups' supports) has diameter ≤ Σ_{𝒢∈Γ}(Mσ_{k+1}(𝒢) + M) ≤ 2MΣ_𝒢σ_{k+1}(𝒢), and the weight a°(Γ) = Σ_𝒢(ν/32)σ_{k+1}(𝒢) of D2.4(12)(f) satisfies a°(Γ) ≥ (ν/(64M))·diam(network of Γ). The dependence set of Γ (*Embeds*) lies in the R_D-neighbourhood of its network, R_D in step-(k+1) units ((Z-g)); if Γ does not embed in T, diam(dependence set) ≥ ι_T/4, so diam(network) ≥ ι_T/4 − 2R_D ≥ ι_T/8 under (TC). Hence, by FV Thm 5.4 (5.11) in the form of D2.4(12)(f) (Σ_{Γ∋b}|Φ°(Γ)|e^{a°(Γ)} ≤ Σ_{𝒢∋b}|c_𝒢|e^{2a°(𝒢)} ≤ C_S′ρ_{k+1}·pl, the Kotecký–Preiss condition at a°/2):
    Σ_{Γ∋b, not embedding in T}|Φ°(Γ)| ≤ e^{−νι_{T,k+1}/(512M)}·Σ_{Γ∋b}|Φ°(Γ)|e^{a°(Γ)} ≤ C_S′ρ_{k+1}·pl·e^{−νι_{T,k+1}/(512M)},
    on T, on T′ (where the clusters of T′ at a lift b̃ of b that are not lifts of embedding clusters of T are exactly those of dependence diameter ≥ ι_{T,k+1}/4, the covering being injective on the smaller ones), and on ℤ⁴ (the weights Φ°^∞ being the limits of (Z-d), with Fatou per root). No network property beyond the a°-weight is used: the extent of a cluster is paid by its Kotecký–Preiss weight, which (S-w) carries (D2.4(12)(f)); this is what (7)(R)'s "network weight in the reference-gas KP weight" asked for, and it holds because the reference-gas weight a° is linear in σ_{k+1}, which bounds the diameter. So, with the embedding clusters by D2.4(13)(c) (Σ_{Γ∋b, embeds}|Φ°_T(Γ) − Φ°_{T′}(Γ̃)| ≤ 2C_rel^{(k+1)}e^{−cι_{T,k+1}}ρ_{k+1}·pl, c = min(c₁, 1/(32M))), for T ⊂ T′ of (H3) and every step-(k+1) traversal b:

This is (Z) at step k+1 in the form of D(k+1)(vi), given (S-w) and (vi)_k.

Π_{k+1} is a power of L in step-k bonds ((1), *The period*), i.e. L^kΠ_{k+1} fine bonds, a power of L, and LMR₁ is a power of L (M, R₁ powers of L, V4 L397 and CRE (2.5)); so the admissible fine periods are the multiples of one fixed number P := lcm(LMR₁, L^kΠ_{k+1} for k ≤ K) (a power of L), of size at least 8M; the least common multiple of two multiples of P is a multiple of P, so the least-common-multiple torus of two admissible tori is admissible (V4's (H3) family N_μ = 2L^m, V4 L210, is the case P | 2L^m); nested means each period of T divides the corresponding one of T′. ### (5) The coefficients (definitions; convergence from the tail bound (T′), which follows from (S-w))

- *The tail bound (T′) (in place of D2.4's (T), which is not used: every use of (T) in (5)–(7) is a distance statement, and the Kotecký–Preiss weight of the reference gas bounds the diameter of a cluster's network).* For the reference gas on ℓ₀ (on T, and on ℤ⁴ through (Z)), for the straight line, the bent path and the square, for every step-k traversal b and every R ≥ 0:
  Σ_{Γ: b∈J_Γ, diam(network of Γ) ≥ R}|Φ°(Γ)| ≤ C_S′𝔞_{k+1}e^{−c_dR}, c_d := ν/(64ML) per step-k bond, 𝔞_{k+1} := max(τ_k², ρ_{k+1}·pl),
  uniformly in k. *Derivation (from D2.4(12)(f) by the argument of (4)(Z-f)):* the network of Γ (the supports, arc hulls and merged occurrences' domains of its groups, all counted in σ_{k+1}) is connected up to gaps of one cube side and has diameter ≤ 2MΣ_{𝒢∈Γ}σ_{k+1}(𝒢) step-(k+1) units = 2MLΣσ step-k bonds, so the weight a°(Γ) = (ν/32)Σσ_{k+1} is ≥ c_d·diam; and Σ_{Γ∋b}|Φ°(Γ)|e^{a°(Γ)} ≤ Σ_{𝒢∋b}|c_𝒢|e^{2a°(𝒢)} ≤ C_S′ρ_{k+1}·pl by FV Thm 5.4 (5.11) under the Kotecký–Preiss condition at a°/2 (D2.4(12)(f)). On ℤ⁴ by Fatou through (Z). At k = 1 the statement is V4's eq:marginal-cluster-tail and eq:corner-bent-tail with the amplitude τ² and the network length C_netℓ(Γ) ≥ diam, which is why 𝔞 carries both amplitudes. No network property is used: V4's length bound was its way of controlling the diameter at step 1, and nothing below uses the length. The old and vacuum occurrences merged into the groups ((3)) are inside σ_{k+1} (D2.4(12)(c)), so (T′) pays for them.
- *Perimeter.* For v ∈ 𝒱_□ let ℓ_v ⊂ ℤ⁴ be the straight line through the origin in orientation v, with its step-k traversals indexed by b ∈ ℤ. The reference gas on ℓ_v has the groups with short arcs on ℓ_v. By contour locality, their activities and interactions are the values on any contour that contains their arcs, taken on ℤ⁴ by (Z). With J_Γ the traversals in the arcs of Γ's groups, put
  a_v(b) := Σ_{Γ: b∈J_Γ}Φ°^∞_v(Γ)/|J_Γ|, κ^{(k+1)}_v := Σ_{b=0}^{Π_{k+1}−1}a_v(b), δm_{k+1} := −(4Π_{k+1})^{−1}Σ_{v∈𝒱_□}κ^{(k+1)}_v. At an R-step k+1 > N_{k+1} the flat part of the R-gas's cluster series along C enters here: δm_{k+1} is replaced by δm_{k+1} − μ^R_{k+1} and the corner coefficient ζ_{k+1} (z = e^ζ, l.65) by ζ_{k+1} + ζ^R_{k+1} (D2.7 note 15.4).
  - a_v is Π_{k+1}-periodic by the invariance. By (T′) at R = 0, |a_v(b)| ≤ C_S′𝔞_{k+1}, so |δm_{k+1}| ≤ C_S′𝔞_{k+1}.
  - δm_{k+1} is per step-k bond, D2.0(3)'s convention (δm_1 := δm^σP(C_n)/P_0(C_n); V3's eq:marginal-full-perimeter is per unit length, with the factor 1/(Lε)). At a commensurate step, δm_{k+1}P_k(C) = −n_kΣ_vκ^{(k+1)}_v, since P_k(C) = 4s_k = 4n_kΠ_{k+1}.
- *Corners.* For (v, w) ∈ 𝒞_□ (eq:corner-square-pairs), take the bent path of eq:corner-bent-path at the scale of the step-k traversals, with the bend at the origin, a period point. Let a_{v,w}(b) be its rooted density, and k_{v,w}(b) := a_v(b) for b < 0 and a_w(b) for b ≥ 0. Put
  ζ^{(k+1)}_{v,w} := Σ_{b∈ℤ}(a_{v,w}(b) − k_{v,w}(b)), ζ_{k+1} := ¼Σ_{(v,w)∈𝒞_□}ζ^{(k+1)}_{v,w}, z_{k+1} := e^{ζ_{k+1}}.
  - A cluster whose arcs all lie on one ray has the weight of the same cluster on that ray's line. Its groups see C only through their arcs (contour locality), the flat construction on ℤ⁴ does not involve C, and the bend is at a period point. This is V3's argument in the paragraph that leads to eq:corner-localisation-bound, including "Vacuum carriers may cross the bend".
  - So only clusters with arcs on both rays survive in a_{v,w}(b) − k_{v,w}(b). Their network contains the traversal b and a traversal of the other ray, so its diameter is ≥ |b|, and (T′) gives |a_{v,w}(b) − k_{v,w}(b)| ≤ 2C_S′𝔞_{k+1}e^{−c_d|b|} (no network range R_* is needed: by contour locality ((4)) a group sees C through its arcs only). Hence |ζ_{k+1}| ≤ C_ζ𝔞_{k+1}, with C_ζ := 2C_S′(1 + 2/(e^{c_d} − 1)).
- *Independence.* δm_{k+1} and ζ_{k+1} are ℤ⁴ quantities of a straight line and a bent path at flat trivial-label data. They do not depend on the size of C, the torus or ℓ. They depend on k through the couplings and the old objects, and on L, G, χ, σ and the prescriptions. They may depend on the coordinate plane of C: covariance of the step under the hypercubic group is not established (R-tr, l.186; RTR_PROOF_PASS, *Hypercubic covariance*), so κ^{(k+1)}_v and ζ^{(k+1)}_{v,w} are kept per orientation and corner pair. D2 uses only the averages δm_{k+1}, ζ_{k+1} over C's own orientations and corners: C is a square, its four sides have the same length s_k, so the perimeter part of a side of orientation v is (s_k/Π_{k+1})κ^{(k+1)}_v at commensurate s_k and the four sides sum to −P_k(C)δm_{k+1}; the four corners sum to 4ζ_{k+1}. No equality between orientations or planes is used, and the bounds of (4)–(6) do not use one.

### (6) The square: the remainder E°_{k+1} (derived from (5)'s (T′) and (Z), as V4 L1763; constants from (T′))

Put F°_{k+1}(C; T) := log 𝒲°_{k+1}(C; T) = Σ_ΓΦ°(Γ; T) and
E°_{k+1}(C, T) := F°_{k+1}(C; T) + δm_{k+1}P_k(C) − 4ζ_{k+1}.
The torus satisfies (5)(g)'s condition T_ph ≫ ℓ_C, so N^{(k)}_min ≫ s_k.

- *(a) Commensurate steps.* |E°_{k+1}| ≤ C_E𝔞_{k+1}(s_k+1)e^{−c_Es_k} + 4C̄_Z𝔞_{k+1}s_ke^{−c^Zι_{T,k+1}}, 𝔞_{k+1} := max(τ_k², ρ_{k+1}·pl) (corner constant and torus constant separate; amplitude 𝔞 from (4)(Z-f) and (5)(T′)).
  - Allocate by |J_Γ|^{−1}: F°_{k+1} = Σ_ba_□(b; T) over the 4s_k traversals of C, with a_□(b; T) := Σ_{Γ: b∈J_Γ}Φ°(Γ; T)/|J_Γ|.
  - Give each corner the last ⌊s_k/2⌋ traversals of its incoming side and the first s_k − ⌊s_k/2⌋ of its outgoing side (V3's half-sides). Translate the corner to the origin. It is a period point, since the step is commensurate. At the base corner, where the traversals are numbered from, V4 L1763 first uses the cyclicity of the trace; here F° is a scalar cluster series and each Y_𝒢 is the product ordered along its own arc (D2.2(6)(d)), so a group across the base point is treated like any other and no cyclicity step is needed.
  - Let b lie in the half-sides of the corner (v, w), at distance ≤ s_k/2 from it, hence ≥ s_k/2 from the other corners. A cluster Γ ∋ b whose network has diameter < s_k/2 has all its arcs on the two sides of (v, w). It does not wrap the torus, since N^{(k)}_min ≫ s_k. Its groups have short arcs. By contour locality and the invariance, it is a cluster of the bent path, with the same weight on T. The other clusters at b cost at most C_S′𝔞_{k+1}e^{−c_ds_k/2}, by (T′).
  - By (Z), the bent-path weights on T differ from those on ℤ⁴ by at most C_Z^{(k+1)}𝔞_{k+1}e^{−c^Zι_{T,k+1}} per root (constants of (4)'s (Z-f), c := c^Z). So |a_□(b; T) − a_{v,w}(b)| ≤ 2C_S′𝔞_{k+1}e^{−c_ds_k/2} + C_Z^{(k+1)}𝔞_{k+1}e^{−cι_{T,k+1}}, the factor 2 for the same truncation of a_{v,w}.
  - The sum of a_{v,w}(b) over the half-sides is the sum of k_{v,w}(b) over the half-sides, plus ζ^{(k+1)}_{v,w}, minus the part of ζ^{(k+1)}_{v,w} from |b| ≥ s_k/2. By (5) that part is at most C_ζ𝔞_{k+1}e^{−c_ds_k/2}.
  - Over the four corners, the half-sides make up the four sides. Each side is n_k whole period cells, starting at a period point, so its sum of a_v(b) is n_kκ^{(k+1)}_v exactly. The total is n_kΣ_vκ^{(k+1)}_v = −δm_{k+1}P_k(C).
  - Adding up: |E°_{k+1}| ≤ 4s_k·(2C_S′𝔞_{k+1}e^{−c_ds_k/2} + C_Z^{(k+1)}𝔞_{k+1}e^{−cι_{T,k+1}}) + 4C_ζ𝔞_{k+1}e^{−c_ds_k/2}. This is the bound, with c_E := c_d/2 = ν/(128ML) and C_E := 8C_S′ + 4C_ζ for the corner terms (8C_S′𝔞_{k+1}s_ke^{−c_Es_k} and 4C_ζ𝔞_{k+1}e^{−c_Es_k}), and 4C̄_Z for the torus term only (C̄_Z = sup_kC_Z^{(k)} ≤ 2C_S′ + 2max(C_rel^{(1)}, 2C⁎) by D2.3(4)(Z-f) with D2.4(12)(f), (13)(c), given (S-w)).
- *(b) The other steps.* With the admissible sides of (4) there are none, and (b) is void. Without the restriction there are at most a* of them, and the bound is
  |E°_{k+1}| ≤ C_E′𝔞_{k+1}[Π_{k+1} + 1 + s_ke^{−cι_{T,k+1}}] + 4C_S′𝔞_{k+1}s_k (constants from (T′)).
  - Compare a_□(b; T) with the density of the line that carries b's side. For b at distance d from the nearest corner the comparison costs C_S′𝔞_{k+1}(2e^{−c_dd} + e^{−cι_{T,k+1}}), by the argument of (a) with the line in place of the bent path. Summed over b this is at most 8C_S′𝔞_{k+1}(1 + 2/(e^{c_d} − 1)) + 4s_kC_S′𝔞_{k+1}e^{−cι_{T,k+1}}.
  - The two sides through the origin lie on ℓ_{e₁}, ℓ_{e₂} (or their reverses). A side of s_k traversals need not be a union of cells; its sum of a_v(b) differs from (s_k/Π_{k+1})κ^{(k+1)}_v by at most 2Π_{k+1}C_S′𝔞_{k+1}.
  - The two far sides lie on x₁ = s_k and x₂ = s_k, the lines ℓ_v + t·e_⊥ with t = s_k mod Π_{k+1} ≠ 0, which are not period translates of ℓ_v. Their period sums κ_v^{[t]} have no relation to κ^{(k+1)}_v beyond |κ_v^{[t]}|, |κ^{(k+1)}_v| ≤ Π_{k+1}C_S′𝔞_{k+1}, so each far side leaves (s_k/Π_{k+1})|κ_v^{[t]} − κ^{(k+1)}_v| ≤ 2C_S′𝔞_{k+1}s_k.
  - With |4ζ_{k+1}| ≤ 4C_ζ𝔞_{k+1}, this gives the bound.

### (7) The sum over the steps (derived)

- Put τ̄² := sup_{k<K}τ_k². τ = C[g(1+r)³ + e^{−cr²}] increases in g for small g, and g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K (the last inequality of CRE (2.6); reading R-cpl₀ at general G). So τ̄² ≤ Cg_K²(log g_K^{−2})^{6p₀}, as in D2.0(5)(f). Put 𝔞̄ := max(τ̄², sup_{k≤K}ρ_k·pl) ≤ Cg_K²(log g_K^{−2})^{max(6p₀, 4p₀+(q₁−q₀)₊)} ((3)'s ρ_k = C_ρg_k²(1+r_k)⁴ with CRE (2.6)–(2.7)); the amplitude of (5)–(6).
- With m := K − k ≥ 1 and s_k = ℓ_CL^m, (6) gives
  Σ_{k<K}|E°_{k+1}| ≤ C_E𝔞̄Σ_{m≥1}2ℓ_CL^me^{−c_Eℓ_CL^m} + 4C̄_Z𝔞̄Σ_{m≥1}ℓ_CL^me^{−c^Zι_{T,K−m+1}}
  for the admissible sides of (4), where every step is commensurate.
  - Without the restriction, the at most a* steps of (6)(b) add C_E′𝔞̄a*(Π* + 1) + 8C_S′𝔞̄ℓ_CL^{a*} + C_E′𝔞̄Σ_mℓ_CL^me^{−cι_{T,K−m+1}}, with Π* := L²M(L+1)(a*)^{β₀}R_{K−1} bounding Π_{k+1} there, by (4). The term 8C_S′𝔞̄ℓ_CL^{a*} is uniform in K but linear in ℓ_C.
  - The first sum is at most a constant C(c_E, L), for every ℓ_C ≥ 1 and every K: its terms are xe^{−c_Ex} at x = ℓ_CL^m, which grow at most geometrically up to x ≍ 1/c_E and then decay faster than geometrically.
  - The third is D2.0(5)(g)'s torus sum. With N^{(k)}_min ≍ T_phL^{K−k} it is at most Cℓ_Ce^{−c′T_ph}, small for T_ph large.
- So, for the admissible sides, the remainders of all steps sum to at most C_Σ𝔞̄, with C_Σ depending on L, c_E, C_E and C̄_Z, i.e. on L, M, ν, C_S′, C_rel^{(1)} and C⁎ (no R_D-dependent factor under (TC)). It does not depend on K or ℓ_C. C_Σ𝔞̄ ≤ Cg_K² times a power of log g_K^{−2}, small for g_K small. Without the restriction C_Σ also depends on M, β₀, C_E′ and, through a* and R_{K−1}, on g_K, and the bound gains the ℓ_C-linear term above.
- *Consequence for D2.0(5)(b) (with its inputs).* At a commensurate step the corner remainder is at most C_E𝔞_{k+1}(s_k+1)e^{−c_Es_k}, not of order τ_k²g_k^δ. Its sum over the steps is O(𝔞̄) uniformly in K, using g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K only. This closes (b) without an upper bound on the couplings once (S-w) holds with k-independent constants (D2.4(12)(f), given its inputs (13)(d)): (T′), the Kotecký–Preiss condition and (Z) all follow from it ((5), (4)(Z-f)–(Z-h)). Until then (b) stays open, with this as its closing route. (b) was the only user of the upper bound (D2.0(5), *The couplings*), so on this route D2 does not use reading R-cpl (RG I (0.31), Thm 2, SU(2)). R-cpl₀ is still used, here for τ̄².
- *Consequence for V3's Conjecture iteration.* Remark next-step (3)(e) counts the torus and corner part as Cτ²(2+1/c) per step, one source of (k+2)τ². With the exact coefficients of every step kept in κ^{(K)}, this source contributes at most C_Σ𝔞̄ in total. The other source of (k+2)τ², replacing every z_j by 1 (Remark (2)), does not arise.
- *D(k)'s clause on E_const.* The remainder of step j carries τ_{j−1}², since it is E°_j of the step j−1 → j. So |E_const,k| ≤ Σ_{j≤k}Cτ_{j−1}²(n_je^{−cι_{T,j}} + (n_j+1)e^{−c_En_j}), with n_j := s_{j−1}, at the admissible sides. The clause is kept with τ_{j−1}²; a conversion to τ_j² would need CRE (2.6)–(2.7) for both parts of τ, which is not done here. Without the restriction, each of the at most a* other steps adds Cτ_{j−1}²(Π_j + 1 + 4s_{j−1}). The clause is amended so (note in D2.0(3)).

### (8) D(k+1)(i) and (v): the assembly (derived)

On every labelled term ℓ, (3) gives ⟨𝒲_k⟩_{1,1} = κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}S°(ℓ)[f_{k+1} + Σ_{D≠∅}Q^{out}_{k+1}(D)w^d(D)]. Put
- κ^{(k+1)} := κ^{(k)}κ_{k+1}, κ_{k+1} := e^{−δm_{k+1}P_k(C)}z_{k+1}⁴, with δm_{k+1} and z_{k+1} of (5);
- E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ) := log S°(ℓ) − log S°(ℓ₀), the difference of the two cluster series;
- 𝓔_{k+1} := 𝓔_k + E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ) + E°_{k+1}(C, T), and E_const,k+1 := E_const,k + E°_{k+1}(C, T).

Since log S°(ℓ₀) = F°_{k+1} = −δm_{k+1}P_k(C) + 4ζ_{k+1} + E°_{k+1}, this gives κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}S°(ℓ) = κ^{(k+1)}e^{𝓔_{k+1}} exactly.
- D(k+1)(i) holds, with coefficients independent of the size of C, the torus and ℓ ((5)).
- D(k+1)(v) holds: E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ), E°_{k+1}(C, T) and Q^{out}_{k+1}(D) are numbers given ℓ.
- E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ) is the step-(k+1) form of V3's E^∂_ξ, |E^∂_ξ| ≤ Cτ²Σ_je^{−cd_j} (eq:regional-square-boundary-comparison, cited at V4 L210). It is the sum of the cluster terms that see ℓ's data: the clusters of ℓ₀ that ℓ does not have (they meet ℓ's large-field regions or their boundaries), the clusters that only ℓ has, and the changes of the activities near ℓ's data. Its form as a local sum Σ_Xe^{(k+1)}_X with D(k)(ii)'s anchored bound is D2.6's, as IT-1 note §3b and §3i do at step 1. It must not be bounded globally (D2.0(5)(f)).

### (9) What D2.3 leaves

- *To D2.4:*
  - (S-w) with k-independent constants (D2.4(12)(f)), from which the tail (T′) of (5), (Z) and the Kotecký–Preiss condition follow ((T) with C_T, η′, C_net and R_* is not used);
  - (Z), at the rate e^{−c^Zι_{T,k+1}} (derived given (S-w), (4) Status);
  - the Kotecký–Preiss condition that gives S°(ℓ) ≠ 0 and the cluster series of S°^{[D]}, on every ℓ;
  - the absolute convergence used in (3), now over the families with their occurrences;
  - Σ_{Γ hits 𝒢}|Φ°(Γ)| ≤ θ_{k+1}(𝒢) for the cluster form of Q^{out}_{k+1}, which gives |Q^{out}_{k+1}(D)| ≤ ∏e^{θ};
  - the payment, in D2.4's size statement (S) (and so in (T′)), for the occurrences merged into the groups by the third rule of (3): their arcs J_Γ, their supports and the entropy of the occurrence tuples;
  - the sizes of the Y^d_𝒢, D(k+1)(ii) for the deviation parts, including at the reference point (reference clause).
- *To D2.6:* E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ) as attached local terms; the species of the Y^d_𝒢 near ℓ's data; the reference clause's sizes on labelled terms. *Closed in WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §7.4(a) (E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ) = Σ_Xe^{(k+1)}_X over the loop-dependent clusters meeting Coll_{k+1} ∪ Z_{k+1}, anchored bound Cτ̂_{k+1}² per traversal with τ̂² := max(τ², Cρ̂), attached per component) and §2.5; the same-activity step rests on D2.4(4) *Scope* and D2.3, listed as given; status in closure §8.*
- *To the final-scale step:* the four parts of (4)(iv): on ℓ₀ the comparison of dressed loops on the fixed lattice of the stopping scale uL^{−j(C)}; the large-field terms on ε-dependent lattices with their normalisation; the errors as K → ∞; κ^{(K′)}. The continuum note WILSON_IT1_CONTINUUM_LIMIT_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md reduces the normalisation of the large-field terms to one residual quotient (item (7), the bound (Q) not derived), and derives the exact division by κ^{(K′)} (item (6)), the K-uniform bounds on the errors (item (5)), and the existence of the limit along subsequences (item (8), given D(K′) on every labelled term); the convergence along the full sequence, the comparison of two runs, stays OPEN (item (9)). That the stopping scale may depend on the loop is derived in (4) only at the places checked there, for K′ ≤ N; the rest is (4)'s Result's OPEN list.
- *Amendments of D(k) (notes in D2.0(3)):* the reference clause in (iii); clause (vi), (Z); the cluster form of the interaction clause; the E_const bound with τ_{j−1}², and the non-commensurate term if ℓ_C is not restricted.
- *Amendment of D2.2(6)(d):* the third merging rule and the expansion of Q° ((3); note in D2.2(6)(d)).
- *Readings.* R-tr (translation covariance, (1)): derived on ℓ₀ (RTR_PROOF_PASS_2026_10_23.md) and our convention for the choices the prints leave free (one label; the PBG p.411 tie averaged over the tied cubes). The other Bałaban inputs are the partition sizes of (1), CRE p.256 and CRE (2.9), quoted, and RG1 pp.260, 262, quoted in (2).
- *For the user's review:* the restriction of ℓ_C to multiples of L^{q*} ((4)).

## D2.4 The contraction count (reviewed: no fatal finding; repairs not re-reviewed)

### (0) What is needed

- *From D2.3(9):*
  - the Kotecký–Preiss condition for the reference gas on every ℓ, with the absolute convergence used in D2.3(3);
  - in place of (T), which is not used: D2.3(5)'s (T′), which follows from (S-w), (7)(R);
  - (Z) (derived given (S-w), D2.3(4) Status);
  - in cluster form: Σ_{Γ hits 𝒢}|Φ°(Γ)| ≤ θ_{k+1}(𝒢), and |Q^{out}_{k+1}(D)| ≤ ∏e^{θ};
  - the sizes of the Y^d_𝒢: D(k+1)(ii) for the deviation parts, including at the reference point.
- *From D2.2(5)(f):* R-vac(ii) from Bałaban's bounds, with d(Z) = 9 log2·|Z|; c|M| ≤ νσ(M)/16; the sizes of Ĥ(M); the enlargement of D_J to D_J^#.
- *From D2.0(3)(c) and (5)(c):* the matching (γ) of old and new neighbourhoods and radii, the split (ζ) of the maximum, and the recursion for C_χ^{(k+1)}; the tolerance at complex data.
- *Output.* D(k+1)(ii) and the norms of D(k+1) for the insertions and interactions made at step k+1, with constants that satisfy D(k)(iv).

### (1) Quoted (Bałaban from the transcriptions Balaban\tex\<paper>\parts\pNNN.tex)

- *RG1 p.257 (localization domains).* π_j is the family of closed cubes of size M. "Such a domain is a union of a connected, finite family of cubes from π_j. A connected family means that for every pair □, □′ of cubes from the family there exists a sequence … such that two consecutive cubes have a common wall, i.e. their intersection is a d−1-dimensional cube." The class is 𝐃_j. "Consider a class of tree graphs contained in X and intersecting all the cubes in X. A length of a shortest graph in this class, divided by M, is the linear size of X, and is denoted by d_j(X)."
- *RG2 p.14 (the vacuum gas).* (2.9) "(2.1) = Σ_ZH(Z)". (2.10): "if Z = Z₁ ∪ ⋯ ∪ Z_n, where Z_i is a connected component of Z, then H(Z) = H(Z₁)⋯H(Z_n)". (2.11) is the polymer expansion with ∏_{i<j}ζ(Z_i, Z_j), "where the function ζ(Z,Z′) is defined by the condition: ζ(Z,Z′) = 0 if Z∩Z′ contains a cube, or a wall of a cube, and ζ(Z,Z′) = 1 otherwise". (2.12)–(2.13) are the exponentiated series, with ρ^T(Z₁,…,Z_n) = Σ_{g∈C_n}∏_{{i,j}∈g}(ζ(Z_i,Z_j) − 1).
- *RG2 p.15 (domain).* "Thus the activities in (2.13), and the whole sum 𝐄^{(k+1)}(X), are analytic functions of (𝐔,𝐉), on the space 𝐔^c_{k+1}(X,α₀,α₁)." A term of H(Z) is (2.14), with contour integrals in complex σ(Δ), τ(Y) and real Gaussian integrals in X and B: "We consider it as an analytic function of (𝐔,𝐉) in the space 𝐔^c_{k+1}(X,α₀,α₁), and of the complex parameters σ(Z), τ. This complicates estimates of this expression, because the operators in it are not symmetric, and the second measure is complex." (2.15) bounds it.
- *RG2 pp.18–20 (the bound).*
  - (2.30), p.18: "(3·2³)^{−1}M^{−4}|Y| ≤ d_k(Y) ≤ M^{−4}|Y| − 1 holding for localization domains Y ∈ 𝐃_k".
  - p.19: "ε₂ = 2E₀ε₁C₁α₄^{−1}α₆^{−1}M^q exp C₂κ₁".
  - p.20: "Assuming that 2(L+2)⁴O(1)ε₂exp5κ ≤ 1, we obtain …"; "We define the constant C₃ = 2(L+2)⁴O(1)2E₀C₁α₄^{−1}α₆^{−1}M^q exp C₂κ₁."
  - Lemma 3, p.20: "Under all the above restrictions on the constants M, κ, κ₁, α₀, α₁, α₄, α₆, γ₂, γ, ε₁, the activity H(Z) for a localization domain Z ∈ 𝐃_{k+1} satisfies the inequality |H(Z)| ≤ C₃ε₁exp(−(1−8δ)½Lκd_{k+1}(Z))" (2.38).
  - p.21: (2.39)–(2.41) repeat "the considerations and bounds of the paper [26], for κ sufficiently large, and ε₁ sufficiently small", and give |𝐄^{(k+1)}(X)| ≤ O(1)C₃ε₁exp(−(1−10δ)½Lκd_{k+1}(X)). "At first we assume that (1−10δ)½L = 1, or δ = (1/10)(1−2L^{−1})." Also "we take M sufficiently large, so that δ₀M ≥ κ".
- *Check of (2.30), left side (derived).* (2.30), p.18, reads "(3·2³)^{−1}M^{−4}|Y| ≤ d_k(Y) ≤ M^{−4}|Y| − 1". With RG1's d_j, a single cube has d = 0: one point of the cube is a tree graph that meets every cube of the domain. So the left inequality fails for one cube (1/24 ≤ 0), and also for the 16 cubes around a common vertex. (2)(a) gives #(Y) ≤ 162(d_k(Y) + 1) in its place (at scale k+1: #(Z) ≤ 162(d_{k+1}(Z) + 1)).
- *Its uses, and repair R-L3 (derived, a repair of the proof; accepted by the user).* Pages searched, every line, for uses of the left side of (2.30) or of its Section-1 form "M^{−4}|Y| ≤ 3·2³d_k(Y)", cited or not: RG2 pp.7–21 (pp.10–13 contain none: p.11's "M^{−4}|Y| ≤ exp M^{−4}|Y|" and (1.39), (1.40), (1.43) use no lower bound on d_k). Below, #(Y) := M^{−4}|Y| at scale k (so "#(Z_0)" on p.19 is M^{−4}|Z_0|) and (LM)^{−4}|Z| at scale k+1, and (2)(a) gives #(Y) ≤ 162(d_k(Y)+1), resp. #(Z) ≤ 162(d_{k+1}(Z)+1). The uses and their costs:
  - pp.8–9: (1.28) "M^{−4}|Y| ≤ 3·2³d_k(Y) ≤ exp(1/16)(κ₁−2)d_k(Y)" and p.9's "M^{−4}|Y| ≤ 3·2³d_k(Y) ≤ exp δκd_k(Y)" bound the sum over the cubes □₀ (resp. □) of Y. With (2)(a), M^{−4}|Y| ≤ 162(d_k(Y)+1) ≤ 162e^{d_k(Y)}, so each costs a factor ≤ 162 into (1.29)'s absolute O(1), once (κ₁−2)/16 ≥ 1, resp. δκ ≥ 1.
  - p.16, (2.20): "Using (1.28) we obtain" the bound Σ_{Y∈𝐃}α₄exp(−δκd_k(Y)) ≤ O(1)α₄M^{−4}|Y₀| (its second line). This needs no lower bound on d_k: each Y ∈ 𝐃 lies in Y₀ (2.2) and contains a cube of it, so the sum is ≤ Σ_{□⊂Y₀}Σ_{Y∋□}α₄e^{−δκd_k(Y)} ≤ O(1)α₄M^{−4}|Y₀| by (1.26) with δκ in place of κ. No cost. (The quadratic part uses (2.19)'s first exponential, as printed.) *Contingency:* where in (2.20) "Using (1.28)" acts is not visible on p.16. If it acts in passing from (1.43) to the middle line, it costs ≤ 162 per Y by (2)(a), an absolute factor absorbed in α₄'s O(1); deciding which needs RG2 (1.42)–(1.43) on p.11 (not read for this point).
  - p.18, (2.29): "For κ sufficiently large and α₆ sufficiently small we have Σ_𝐃∏_{Y∈𝐃}α₆exp(−δκd_k(Y)) ≤ 1", the sum over the 𝐃 with ∪𝐃 = Y₀ (2.2). Bałaban refers its proof to [26] and adds "In connection with this notice … (2.30)". Derivation without (2.30)'s left side: put n := M^{−4}|Y₀|, w(Y) := α₆e^{−δκd_k(Y)} and t := 1/8. A covering family has Σ_{Y∈𝐃}#(Y) ≥ n, so the sum is ≤ e^{−tn}∏_{Y⊂Y₀}(1 + w(Y)e^{t#(Y)}) ≤ exp(−n(t − ε_t)), with ε_t := sup_□Σ_{Y∋□}w(Y)e^{t#(Y)}. By (2)(a), e^{t#(Y)} ≤ e^{20.25}e^{20.25d_k(Y)} ≤ e^{20.25}e^{δκd_k(Y)/2} once δκ ≥ 40.5, so ε_t ≤ α₆e^{20.25}Σ_{Y∋□}e^{−δκd_k(Y)/2} ≤ α₆e^{20.25}O(1) by (1.26) with δκ/2 in place of κ. Hence (2.29) holds once α₆e^{20.25}O(1) ≤ 1/8, an absolute factor in "α₆ sufficiently small". The single cubes (d = 0, and every d = 0 domain has ≤ 2⁴ cubes) are paid by α₆ per member; Y₀ = □ admits only 𝐃 = {□}, with value α₆ ≤ 1.
  - p.19, after (2.34): "The exponential on the right-hand side multiplied by exp(−δκd_k(Z_0)) can be estimated by 1". The exponent is exp(−(1/20)γ₂ε₁²/γ²)#(Z_0). Per component of Z_0 the cost is exp(162e^{−4κ}(d+1) − δκd) ≤ exp(162e^{−4κ}) ≤ 2, since 162e^{−4κ} ≤ min(δκ, log2) for κ ≥ κ_vac and L ≥ 3, using p.18's "(1/20)γ₂ε₁²/γ² ≥ 4κ".
  - pp.19–20: "The last sum is estimated using (1.28), with κ replaced by δκ, and with an additional sum over (L+2)⁴ cubes □′ from π_k, the cubes touching a fixed LM-cube in Z_i′". (1.28) contains no κ; the sum described (components through a fixed □′, weight e^{−δκd_k}) is the sum of (1.26), which uses no lower bound on d_k, so there is no cost. Read literally as (1.28), it costs ≤ 162 per component (δκ ≥ 1), into "(L+2)⁴O(1)". Either way the factor is absolute.
  - p.20 l.6: (2.29) applied at scale k+1. p.20 prints only the exponent replacement ("We have δ½Lκd_{k+1}(Z_i′) instead of δκd_k(Y), but the inequality (2.29) is valid for all k"); the prefactor 2(L+2)⁴O(1)ε₂ in α₆'s place is our reading of the line. The derivation above covers any prefactor α with αe^{20.25}O_{(1.26)} ≤ 1/8, O_{(1.26)} the constant of (1.26) at rate δκ/2 (resp. δ½Lκ/2), so it covers both α₆ and 2(L+2)⁴O₃ε₂, given: (i) δκ/2 ≥ κ_{(1.26)}, the threshold of (1.26); (ii) 2(L+2)⁴O₃ε₂e^{20.25}O_{(1.26)} ≤ 1/8, which follows from p.20's assumption 2(L+2)⁴O₃ε₂e^{5κ} ≤ 1 once e^{5κ} ≥ 8e^{20.25}O_{(1.26)}; and δ½Lκ ≥ δκ.
  - p.20, in Lemma 3's proof: "We use the factor exp(−δ½Lκd_{k+1}(Z)), and the inequality (2.30), to bound the exponentials by 1". The exponentials are exp(A·#(Z)), A an absolute bound for O(1)(LM)⁴α₅ + exp(−½(κ₁−1)) (p.20: "We assume that (LM)⁴α₀, (LM)⁴α₁, (LM)⁴α₄, (LM)⁴γ₂ are bounded by a constant independent of M, for example by 1", with α₅ = O(1)e^{−δ₀M/3} + O(α₀+α₁) + O(1)α₄ + γ₂, so also (LM)⁴e^{−δ₀M/3} ≤ 1 for M ≥ M(L); A is fixed as an absolute number before κ). With (2)(a), exp(A#(Z) − δ½Lκd_{k+1}(Z)) ≤ e^{162A}e^{(162A − δ½Lκ)d_{k+1}(Z)} ≤ e^{162A} once δ½Lκ ≥ 162A, i.e. κ ≥ 3240A/(L−2) (p.21's δ).
  - p.21, after (2.40): an uncited use for 𝐄^{(k+1)}(X). Not repaired, and not needed: (2) does not use the bound on 𝐄^{(k+1)}.
  - *Repair R-L3 (derived on the uses above).* The proof of Lemma 3 goes through with (2)(a) in place of (2.30)'s left side. The cost is that Bałaban's absolute constants are enlarged by absolute factors: 162 in (1.29)'s O(1) (pp.8–9); e^{20.25} in "α₆ sufficiently small" (p.18); and 2 (p.19), ≤ 162 (pp.19–20, only if literal (1.28)) and e^{162A} (p.20) in the O(1) of "(L+2)⁴O(1)ε₂" on p.20. Call that enlarged O(1) O₃; it is the O(1) of p.20's assumption "2(L+2)⁴O(1)ε₂exp5κ ≤ 1" and of C₃. So Lemma 3 holds as printed, (2.38) with C₃′ := C₃ computed with O₃ (convention: C₃′ always means this). *Definition:* C₃′ := 2(L+2)⁴O₃·2E₀C₁′α₄^{−1}α₆′^{−1}M^q exp C₂κ₁, p.20's formula "C₃ = 2(L+2)⁴O(1)2E₀C₁α₄^{−1}α₆^{−1}M^q exp C₂κ₁" evaluated with O₃, with Lemma 1's constant C₁′ enlarged by the pp.8–9 factor 162, and with α₆′ := e^{−20.25}α₆ (the e^{20.25} of p.18); ε₂ is evaluated with the same constants, so C₃′ε₁ = 2(L+2)⁴O₃ε₂ ≤ e^{−5κ}. p.16's assumption "C₃ ≤ E₀C₁" is about a different constant (p.16's C₃ of (1.43)), not this one. Conditions: L ≥ 3 (δ = 0 at L = 2); κ ≥ max(κ_vac, 3240A/(L−2)), which gives δκ ≥ κ/30 ≥ 65 ≥ 40.5 and 162e^{−4κ} ≤ min(δκ, log2); δκ/2 ≥ κ_{(1.26)} and e^{5κ} ≥ 8e^{20.25}O_{(1.26)} (p.20 l.6 bullet); (κ₁−2)/16 ≥ 1, implied by p.20's "½(κ₁−1) ≥ 2Lκ". All fit Bałaban's order (κ, then κ₁ and M, then the α's, then ε₁; p.21). Basis: every line of RG2 pp.7–21; the per-component bookkeeping through pp.19–20 is Bałaban's and is not redone here; [26] is not used. No counterexample to Lemma 3 itself is known, so under the accepted-claims rule printed Lemma 3 could also be used directly; the repair records why it stays true. *From (10)(d):* (2.32), p.18, is false as printed (eight rods on the walls of one cube of Z₀∖Y₀ give d_k(Z₀) = Σ_id_k(Y_i) + 10); it holds with 18 in place of 4 (derived), so p.18's "(1/20)γ₂ε₁²/γ² ≥ 4κ" becomes "≥ 18κ" (a condition on γ) and p.20's adapted use with "½(κ₁−1) ≥ 2Lκ" becomes ½(κ₁−1) ≥ 9Lκ (a condition on κ₁). And p.20's "2(L+2)⁴O(1)ε₂exp5κ ≤ 1" pays (2.27)'s joins at scale k+1, whose cost is e^{5(1−7δ)½Lκ} per domain Z_i′, not e^{5κ}; the condition is to be read as 2(L+2)⁴O₃ε₂e^{5(1−7δ)½Lκ} ≤ 1, a condition on ε₁. Both are in Bałaban's order of choices (p.21) and change no statement.
- *C₃′ε₁ (derived from the print, with R-L3's O₃).* C₃′ε₁ = 2(L+2)⁴O₃·ε₂ (C₃ is defined from that factor in p.20's paragraph). So p.20's assumption, with O₃, gives C₃′ε₁ ≤ e^{−5κ}.
- *FV Thm 5.4, p.224 (Friedli–Velenik).* Assume (5.2), |δ(γ,γ′)| ≤ 1, and that there is a: Γ → ℝ_{>0} with Σ_{γ′}|w(γ′)|e^{a(γ′)}|δ(γ,γ′)| ≤ a(γ) for each γ (5.10). Then for all γ₁, 1 + Σ_{k≥2}kΣ_{γ₂,…,γ_k}|φ(γ₁,…,γ_k)|∏_{j≥2}|w(γ_j)| ≤ e^{a(γ₁)} (5.11). Here φ(γ₁) = 1 and φ(γ₁,…,γ_m) = (1/m!)Σ_{G connected}∏_{{i,j}∈G}δ(γ_i,γ_j). For a hard-core gas 1 + δ is the compatibility indicator, so δ ∈ {−1, 0}, (5.2) holds, and (1/m!)ρ^T∏H is φ∏w. The polymer set is finite on the torus.
- *CRE (2.38), p.261.* For cubes □ of size CM in Ω_j there is a gauge transformation u on □∩X with "U^u = exp iξA, L^nξ|A|, (L^nξ)²|∇^ξA| < BCMα_{0,n}". This is condition (ii) of the definition of Ũ^c_j(X, α̃₀, α̃₁), p.261: a domain condition with α_{0,n}, not a bound proportional to the deviation.
- *CRE pp.277–278.* "The factor H′(Z₀) is different; it is given by an integral with the integrand including, as one of the factors, a localized term from the expansion of the integrated function in the expectation value in (3.37). Although different, it satisfies the same bound as the other factors." And: "the terms 𝐄₀^{(k+1)}(Λ_{k+1},X,z) of this representation, for X ⊂ Λ_{k+1}, do not depend on Λ_{k+1}, and coincide with the corresponding terms arising from the expressions defined on the whole lattice, i.e. in the framework of [I]."
- *VAR (7), p.278, and Theorem 1, p.279.* (7): "|(∂V)(p′) − 1| < ε₁ for p′ ∈ 𝔅_k". Theorem 1: "for an arbitrary cube □ in the class described above, of a size 2ML^jη, M ≤ M(ε₁), there exists a gauge transformation u defined on a neighborhood of □ and such that on □, U^{u^{−1}} = e^{iηA}, |A| < B₃Mε₁(L^jη)^{−1}, |∇^ηA| < B₃Mε₁(L^jη)^{−2}". The bound is in the global ε₁ of (7).
- *VAR Proposition 9, p.309.* "The minimal configuration U_k(V) = U_k(V′V₀) in the axial gauge has an extension to an analytic function of G^c-valued small configurations V′ on 𝔅_k." "The function U_k(V′V₀)U_k(V₀)^{−1} transformed to the Landau gauge is, by the definition, equal to exp iη𝓗(B), where B = 1/i log V′." 𝓗 "is an analytic function of B, and also of the external gauge field configuration U", satisfies (19)–(21) with ε₂ = B₅ε₁, and its functional derivative (182) satisfies (190).

### (2) The Kotecký–Preiss condition for Bałaban's vacuum gas (derived from Lemma 3 (with repair R-L3) and, on labelled terms, Bałaban's R-vac(i′); replaces reading R-vac(ii))

- *Setting.* The polymers are the Z ∈ 𝐃_{k+1} on T for ℓ₀, and the Z ∈ 𝐃_{k+1} with Z ⊂ Λ_{k+1} on a labelled term ℓ. On a labelled term these are wall-connected unions of π_{k+1}-cubes with RG2's ζ: CRE p.277 expands the normalisation factor "in exactly the same way as in Sect. 7 [I]", by "(I.7.12), with terms given by (I.7.13), but with the localization domains Z_i ∈ 𝐃_k, X ∈ 𝐃_{k+1} having nonempty intersections with Λ_{k+1}", so "the larger class of localization domains" is the 𝐃_{k+1}-domains meeting Λ_{k+1} (R-I7: Sect. 7 [I] is RG2 Sect. 2). *Index:* the quoted Z_i ∈ 𝐃_k are RG2 p.19's scale-k components of Z₀, and the X ∈ 𝐃_{k+1} are the polymers of (2.11)–(2.13) and of Lemma 3 (p.14: "X ∈ 𝐃_{k+1}"); the labelled-term polymer class is the second, the 𝐃_{k+1}-domains meeting Λ_{k+1}. On ℓ₀ the activities are RG2's, and Lemma 3 applies to them as printed (R-L3 of (1) records why it stays true). On a labelled term they are the activities of the gas of CRE (3.27), with the source term, and the bounds below use:
  - *R-vac(i′) (Bałaban's, accepted; re-check row 14).* For t ∈ [0,1] and with the source term at full strength, the activities H(Z), Z ∈ 𝐃_{k+1}, Z ⊂ Λ_{k+1}, of the gas of (3.27) satisfy (2.38) with C₃′. Basis: CRE p.276, "The action in (3.29) differs only by the additional dependence on the field tA_k, but this was already taken into acount in the above considerations", with the analyticity space (3.43); p.277, "The differences, like the restriction of the integration to Λ_{k+1}, or the larger class of localization domains, do not matter"; p.279, "The other expectation values in (3.26), (3.28) are treated in exactly the same way". Accepted as Bałaban's presented-as-true claim (no counterexample known; the re-check row stays). Recorded for the user: p.277 and p.279 do not name the source term, whose coefficient can be of size O(ε_k/g_k) near ∂Λ_{k+1} (D2.2(5)(b)); p.276 is the sentence that covers tA_k. It is the bound form of R-vac(i) (D2.2(5)(b)).
  - R-I7 (CRE's "Sect. 7 [I]" is RG2 Sect. 2).
  - The polymers that meet Λ_{k+1}ᶜ are D2.6's. #(Z) := (LM)^{−4}|Z| is the number of π_{k+1}-cubes of Z. Lemma 3 is a bound for the analytic continuation on 𝐔^c_{k+1}(X,α₀,α₁) (p.15); every polymer's domain contains the common domain 𝐔^c_{k+1}(T,α₀,α₁), so everything below holds uniformly on it.
- *The gas.* By (2.10), H of a domain is the product over its components, and distinct components share no cube and no wall (RG1's connectedness is through walls). So (2.11) is the hard-core gas of the connected Z ∈ 𝐃_{k+1}, with Z ≁ Z′ iff ζ(Z,Z′) = 0.
- *(a) Linear size (derived).* d_{k+1}(Z) ≥ #(Z)/162 − 1.
  - Rescale so that the cubes of π_{k+1} are unit cubes. Let 𝒯 be a shortest tree graph of RG1's class, of length ℓ = d_{k+1}(Z). A closed walk around 𝒯 runs along each edge twice and has length 2ℓ. Choose points p₀, …, p_N on it, consecutive ones at arc length ≤ 1, with N := ⌈2ℓ⌉ (one point if ℓ = 0).
  - Every point of 𝒯 is within arc length 1/2, hence within ℓ^∞-distance 1/2, of some p_i. Each cube of Z meets 𝒯, so it meets the closed ℓ^∞-ball B_i of radius 1/2 about some p_i.
  - B_i is a cube of side 1. In each coordinate a closed interval of length 1 meets at most 3 closed unit intervals of the partition, so B_i meets at most 3⁴ = 81 cubes. Hence #(Z) ≤ 81(N+1) ≤ 81(2ℓ+2).
- *(b) Activities.* With p.21's δ, (1−8δ)½L = L/10 + 4/5 ≥ 1 for L ≥ 2, and d ≥ 0, C₃′ε₁ ≤ e^{−5κ} ((1), R-L3). So (2.38) with C₃′ and (a) give |H(Z)| ≤ e^{−5κ}e^{−κd_{k+1}(Z)} ≤ e^{−4κ}e^{−λ#(Z)}, λ := κ/162.
- *(c) Entropy.* At most 64^{n−1} connected Z with #(Z) = n contain a given cube. A closed depth-first walk from that cube along a spanning tree of the wall-adjacency graph of Z has 2(n−1) steps, each to one of the 8 wall-neighbours, and it determines Z.
- *(d) Incompatibility.* A wall lies in exactly two cubes. So Z′ ≁ Z iff Z′ contains a cube of Z⁺, the cubes of Z and their wall-neighbours, and |Z⁺| ≤ 9#(Z). The same holds for any union M of π_{k+1}-cubes in place of Z, with ≁ read as ζ = 0.
- *(e) The condition.* Let b ≥ 0, w̃(Z) := H(Z)e^{b#(Z)}, a(Z) := c#(Z). By (b)–(d),
  Σ_{Z′≁M}|w̃(Z′)|e^{a(Z′)} ≤ 9#(M)·e^{−4κ}Σ_{n≥1}64^{n−1}e^{−(λ−b−c)n}.
  If λ − b − c ≥ log 128, the series is at most 2e^{−(λ−b−c)} ≤ 1/64. So the left side is at most (9/64)e^{−4κ}#(M). With
  c := (9/64)e^{−4κ},
  this is (5.10) for w̃ and a, and the same bound holds for every union of cubes M. The requirement is κ/162 ≥ b + c + 7 log2.
- *(f) R-vac(ii) (derived from Lemma 3 and, on labelled terms, Bałaban's R-vac(i′)).* Take b := 9 log2, the weight d(Z) = 9 log2·#(Z) of R-vac(ii) (D2.2(5)(b) writes |Z| for the cube count #(Z)). This KP weight d(Z) is not the linear size d_{k+1}(Z). Since c ≤ 1, the requirement holds for
  κ ≥ κ_vac := 162(1 + 16 log2).
  Then Σ_{Z′≁Z}|H(Z′)|e^{a(Z′)+d(Z′)} ≤ a(Z) with a(Z) = c#(Z). This is R-vac(ii) with c fixed. It is derived from Lemma 3 on ℓ₀, and, on a labelled term ℓ, for the subgas of the Z ∈ 𝐃_{k+1}, Z ⊂ Λ_{k+1}, from R-vac(i′). Lemma 3 is used as printed, with R-L3's enlarged absolute constants. On ℓ ≠ ℓ₀ the full gas also contains the polymers meeting Λ_{k+1}ᶜ (CRE p.277's domains "having nonempty intersections with Λ_{k+1}"), and KP for it needs D2.6's bound on those boundary polymers (D2.6; status in WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §8). So (ii) is not a reading of its own; it rests on Bałaban's Lemma 3 and R-vac(i′).
- *(g) Consequence (derived from (5.11)).* For every union of cubes M,
  Σ_{𝒞≁M}|φ^T(𝒞)|e^{d(𝒞)} ≤ c#(M),
  the sum over the ordered clusters with some member ≁ M. By the symmetry of φ, a cluster with a member ≁ M is counted by putting that member first, which costs the factor k of (5.11). So the sum is at most Σ_{Z₁≁M}|w̃(Z₁)|(1 + Σ_{k≥2}kΣ|φ(Z₁,…,Z_k)|∏_{j≥2}|w̃(Z_j)|) ≤ Σ_{Z₁≁M}|w̃(Z₁)|e^{a(Z₁)} ≤ c#(M), by (5.11) and (e).
  - This is sharper than a(M⁺) ≤ 9c|M|, which also holds; D2.2(5)(e) uses c|M|. The restricted gases (activities H(Z)·1[Z compatible with 𝔐]) satisfy (5.10) a fortiori, so D2.2(5)(d)'s ratio formula holds with these constants.
- *The condition on κ (accepted by the user, with κ_D′ of D2.4(12)(g)).* κ ≥ κ_vac is a lower bound on Bałaban's κ, of the kind RG2 p.21 imposes ("for κ sufficiently large"). RG2 fixes κ before M (δ₀M ≥ κ, p.21) and ε₁ (p.20's assumption, p.21's "ε₁ sufficiently small"), so the bound can be imposed in his order of choices. It is added to his list of restrictions, not read into his text.
  - κ_vac is the vacuum part of (9)'s κ_D := max{κ_vac, ¼log(9/(2ν)), 3240A/(L−2)}.
  - The other conditions on κ used here: κ ≥ 3240A/(L−2) of R-L3; p.20's "½(κ₁−1) ≥ 2Lκ"; p.18's "(1/20)γ₂ε₁²/γ² ≥ 4κ". All of them fit the order κ → κ₁, M → ε₁ → γ: each bounds κ by a constant, or a later constant by κ.

### (3) The size count σ_{k+1} and the weights θ_{k+1} (definitions; θ_vac derived)

- *Definition (new norm convention, for the user's review).* For a step-(k+1) group 𝒢 (D2.2(6)(d)) let n_{k+1}(𝒢) be the number of step-(k+1) traversals of C in I(𝒢), and #_{k+1}(𝒢) the number of π_{k+1}-cubes of the domains of 𝒢's constituents (marked vacuum domains, slot supports, the domains of the old groups), counted with multiplicity. Put
  σ_{k+1}(𝒢) := #_{k+1}(𝒢) + n_{k+1}(𝒢)/M ((12)(a)).
  - V3's σ(W) = n(W) + |ℋ(W)|/(LM) has a cube count n(W) and the hull in units of the cube side LM (V3 L4579–4583); n(W) is a cube count, not a traversal count ((12)(a)). At step k+1 the vacuum weight of a group is proportional to the volume of its marked domains ((5)(d) of D2.2, (g) above), and these domains are 4-dimensional, so σ_{k+1} counts cubes, with the arc in cube sides as V3.
  - The decay statements survive. Where the support of 𝒢 is wall-connected, two of its cubes are at step-(k+1) distance at most M·#_{k+1}(𝒢), so the envelope form σ ≥ dist/(C_FM) holds there. Where 𝒢 is linked along C, n_{k+1} gives it.
- *The weight.* θ_{k+1}(𝒢) := θ_vac(𝒢) + θ_ref(𝒢).
  - θ_vac(𝒢) := Σ_MΣ_{𝒞≁M}e^{d(𝒞)}|φ^T(𝒞)|, the sum over the marked domains M of 𝒢: D2.2(6)(c)'s θ(G), taken at every point of the domain. By (2)(g), θ_vac(𝒢) ≤ c#_{k+1}(𝒢) ≤ cσ_{k+1}(𝒢).
  - θ_ref(𝒢) is the reference-gas weight of (7): what |Q_{k+1}(D)| = |S°^{[D]}(ℓ)/S°(ℓ)| costs per group of D.
- *The requirement θ_{k+1} ≤ νσ_{k+1}/16, split as ν/32 + ν/32.*
  - θ_vac: c ≤ ν/32, i.e. (9/64)e^{−4κ} ≤ ν/32, i.e. κ ≥ ¼log(9/(2ν)). With (1)–(2): κ ≥ κ_D := max{κ_vac, ¼log(9/(2ν)), 3240A/(L−2)}.
  - *Which constants ν depends on.* V3 sets ν := η, "the reserve constant of Section c4-6-2" (V3 L2789). η is chosen "after the fixed geometry and forest decay constants" (V3 L4619, with 1000ηA_s < ϖ/8), and ϖ ≤ min(1/16, λ_*/(64C_g)) (V3 §c4-8-2, L4768ff.), λ_* the decay rate of V3's root weights m(Z) = ε_*e^{−λ_*ℓ₁(Z)}. So the bound fits Bałaban's order, κ before M and ε₁, if λ_*, A_s and C_g do not depend on M or ε₁ and λ_* does not decrease as κ grows.
    - *Notation.* In this bullet C_g and δ are V3's (C_g = 162, δ = 1/40), not (4)'s (LPG′) constant C_g or RG2's δ of (1). The identification of V3's κ with Bałaban's κ is a reading of V3's conversion, labelled here; it is not load-bearing: if the two κ are independent, ν does not depend on Bałaban's κ at all.
    - *Checked against V3.* V3 L3839–3843: "Fix, in this order and depending on L and the group only: C_g=162 and δ=1/40; a decay rate κ≥3 with δκ ≥ 2C_g log128, λ′=(1−5δ)Lκ/2049, λ_*=λ′−2C_g ≥ 2C_g log128". So C_g is absolute, and λ_* depends on L and κ only and increases with κ (V3's κ is read as Bałaban's κ, as V3's λ′ = (1−5δ)Lκ/2049 converts it to the LM-scale). Then ϖ = min(1/16, λ_*/(64C_g)) of V3 (c4-8-3) equals 1/16 for every admissible κ, since λ_* ≥ 2C_g log 128 gives λ_*/(64C_g) > 1/16, and η can be taken as any number with 1000ηA_s < ϖ/8 (V3 L4619, L4790). A_s = 1 + A_h (V3 L4790), with A_h of V3 L4780, "|H_γ| ≤ A_h LMn_γ", which "follow[s] from the square geometry and the wall connectivity of a component's domains" (V3 L4798) and is "fixed before the coupling". So ν, hence κ_D's term ¼log(9/(2ν)), is a fixed number for κ large, with no circularity, provided A_h does not depend on κ or M. V3 measures |H_γ| in units of LM and derives A_h from the cube geometry; its dependence is derived in the gaps block (l.177): A_h ≤ 17, absolute, from the diameter ≤ n_γLM of the wall-connected X_γ, the contacts within 2L ≤ LM/4 of X_γ, and the square geometry's arc bound 8D + 1; the halo index bound R_e enters the footprint pin's constant and the g₀-threshold only. So ν can be fixed as an absolute number (η := 1/2 400 000 with A_h := 17, ϖ := 1/16; reviewed in round 45, minors applied).
  - θ_ref ≤ νσ_{k+1}/32: (7), OPEN with the sizes.
- *The old weights do not accumulate.* The bound ν_n^{(k)} = Σ_{W∋n}e^{θ_k(W)}‖Y_W‖ of D(k)(ii) already contains e^{θ_k(W)}. So when an old insertion enters a new group, its factor e^{θ_k(W)}|Q_k|-share is paid inside the group's size by ν_n^{(k)} ((4), (7)). θ_{k+1} is the fresh weight of the step-(k+1) gas. It is not θ_k converted to step-(k+1) units, which would grow by a factor L per step.
  - *Role of θ_vac in D2.3(3)'s cluster form.* The vacuum occurrences are merged into the groups, and Q^{out}_{k+1} carries only the reference-gas Φ°. So θ_vac does not bound an interaction between groups: it bounds the vacuum clusters merged into 𝒢 (the factor paid inside S_𝒢), and θ_ref bounds 𝒢's share of Q^{out}.

### (4) The deviation factor (the Cauchy step derived; the local gauge derived as (LPG′), WILSON_IT1_SW_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §5, not reviewed)

The object is Y^d_𝒢 = Y_𝒢 − c_𝒢T_𝐔(I(𝒢)) of D2.3(2), for a step-(k+1) group 𝒢 with support X(𝒢).
- *The support.* X(𝒢) is the union of the π_{k+1}-cubes that meet the constituents' domains, the comparison boxes of its old insertions, its slots, or the bonds of its arc I(𝒢). The arc must be included: Y_𝒢 and T_𝐔(I(𝒢)) depend on 𝐔 along all of I(𝒢) (D2.1(4) *Locality*; D2.2(6)(d), the 𝐔-transports between consecutive slots), and I(𝒢) can leave every constituent domain (D2.2(6)(d): "a second-rule merge also covers the gap").
  - *X(𝒢) is connected as a closed set (derived).* The cubes meeting I(𝒢) form a closed-connected chain, since I(𝒢) is a connected path. Each constituent meets I(𝒢): an s-component contains bonds of C or meets an old domain that contains its interval; a marked polymer contains its slot; a comparison box contains its traversal. So every cube of X(𝒢) is closed-connected to the chain.
  - *Count (derived).* A closed step-(k+1) bond meets at most 2⁴ = 16 cubes of π_{k+1} (at most 2 intervals of the partition in each coordinate, the cubes having side M ≥ 1). So #(X(𝒢)) ≤ #_{k+1}(𝒢) + #(boxes) + 16n_{k+1}(𝒢), and ℓ_{X(𝒢)} ≤ 2#(X(𝒢)) by the bounds below. (S) pays for this through ℓ_{X(𝒢)}, as set up there.
- *Scope (collar rule, SW closure §1(a3), for review).* D(k+1)(ii) needs a bound by the deviation of U_{k+1} near X(𝒢) (D2.0(5)(c), "Needs D2.4"). Here k+1 ≥ 2. A step-(k+1) group is dev iff ω′ = 0 on it, its hull lies in Ω_k, its polymers carry no boundary-type potential, and 𝒬_{n′}(𝒢)^{∼5} ⊂ Ω_{k+1} for every traversal n′ (𝒬 as below); otherwise it is att(k+1, ·) by the rule of §1(a3) (collar width d₀ = 6), with D2.6's bound. So ω′ = 0 below.

- *The local deviation (region per traversal, SW closure §5(e)).* For X a union of π_{k+1}-cubes put ℓ_X := d_{k+1}(X) + 1, with RG1's linear size d_{k+1} ((1)); diam_∞(X) ≤ M(d_{k+1}(X) + 2) ≤ 2Mℓ_X. For a group 𝒢 and a traversal n′ of its arc I(𝒢) ⊂ X(𝒢), let t = t_{n′}(𝒢) be the least t ≥ 1 with X(𝒢) ⊂ B_∞(n′, R_t^{(k+1)}), R_t = C_boxM(t+1), and 𝒬 = 𝒬_{n′}(𝒢) the smallest union of π_{k+1}-cubes containing B_∞(n′, R_t) (a cube of side ≤ 2R_t + 2M). Put Dev_{n′}(𝒢; U) := ℓ_{X(𝒢)}·E_𝒬(U), E_𝒬(U) := max{η_{k+1}^{−2}δ_p(U) : p a fine plaquette in 𝒬^{∼5}}, in the normalisation of δ*^{(k+1)} (D2.0(3)). The region must be contractible: for a ring X of cubes with flat plaquettes on X⁺ and curvature inside the ring, a bound by the deviation on X⁺ alone would force A = 0 while the holonomy around the ring is not 1 (SW closure §5(e)). 𝒬^{∼5} ⊂ B_∞(n′, R_{t+1}) for C_box ≥ 6.
  - With ℓ_X := d_{k+1}(X), Dev would vanish for one cube and for the 16 cubes at a vertex ((1)), and (LPG′) would force U_{k+1} to be pure gauge there. The +1 removes this.
  - *Bounds (derived).* ℓ_X ≥ 1, and ℓ_X ≥ #(X)/162 by (2)(a). If X is wall-connected, ℓ_X ≤ #(X): order its cubes so that each meets an earlier one in a wall. A point of the tree in that earlier cube is within distance 1 of the wall, so one segment of length ≤ 1 inside the two cubes adds the new cube, and d_{k+1}(X) ≤ #(X) − 1, as in (2.30)'s right side. If X is only connected as a closed set, the same argument through a shared vertex gives ℓ_X ≤ 2#(X).
  - In (S) and (6), ℓ is this ℓ_X.
- *(LPG′), the local proportional gauge (derived, WILSON_IT1_SW_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §5; reading R-17; not reviewed).* For every V_{k+1} in the support of the term's indicators, every dev group 𝒢 and every traversal n′ of I(𝒢), with 𝒬 = 𝒬_{n′}(𝒢), 𝒬^{∼5} ⊂ Ω_{k+1}, E := E_𝒬(U_{k+1}) and M_𝒬 := half the side of 𝒬^{∼2} rounded up to a multiple of R₁M₁ (M_𝒬 ≤ (C_box + 6)Mℓ_{X(𝒢)}), under (H): 2E ≤ min(a₁, c₂), 2M_𝒬E ≤ R₁M₁a₁, there is a gauge transformation u on a neighbourhood of 𝒬^{∼2} with U_{k+1}^u = e^{iη_{k+1}A} on 𝒬^{∼2} ⊇ X(𝒢) and, in the units of CRE (2.38)–(2.39) at n = k+1,
  |A|, |∇^ξA|, |∂^{ξ*}∂^ξA|, |Δ^ξA| ≤ 3B₃M_𝒬E ≤ C_g·Dev_{n′}(𝒢; U_{k+1}), C_g := 3B₃(C_box + 6)M, and |R(u)J_{k+1}| ≤ (4/3)C_gDev_{n′}(𝒢; U_{k+1}) in the units of (2.36) (reading R-J),
  with C_g depending on B₃, C_box, M only. Inputs: CRE (3.17) at 𝒬 (its first line at unions of partition cubes is R-17; its second is VAR Prop. 9, p.309), the argument's bound 22d²E localised by CMP98 Prop. 2's locality (p.26), 𝐇_𝒬 ≤ B₃e^{−δLM₂R_{k+1}}·22d²E with its derivatives (CRE p.268, VAR (190)), VAR Thm 1 (p.279) for the local minimiser with ε₁ = 2E (CMP98 Prop. 2; the lifting of VAR p.279; CRE p.267's "coefficient 1"), condition (C-M) on M, M₁, M₂, R₁, L, and 22d²B₃e^{−δLM₂R_{k+1}} ≤ 1. When (H) fails, C_gDev ≥ c₀ᾱ_{k+1} for g_K small and the Cauchy step below is in its trivial branch.
- *The Cauchy step (derived, given (LPG′)).* Put ᾱ_{k+1} := min(α_{0,k+1}, α_{1,k+1}) and f(z) := Y_𝒢(e^{izη_{k+1}A}, zR(u)J_{k+1}, 0) − c_𝒢T_{e^{izη_{k+1}A}}(I(𝒢)).
  - f(0) = Y_𝒢(1, 0, 0) − c_𝒢𝟙 = 0, by Schur (D2.3(2)).
  - By covariance (D2.2(6)(b)) and since Y_𝒢 depends on 𝐔, 𝐉 only on X(𝒢), which contains the arc (D2.3(4), contour locality, and Bałaban's localisation), f(1) = π(u(x))Y^d_𝒢(U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}, 0)π(u(x′))^{−1}, with x, x′ the ends of I(𝒢). π(u) is unitary, so ‖f(1)‖ = ‖Y^d_𝒢(U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}, 0)‖.
  - For |z| < R_X := c₀ᾱ_{k+1}/(C_gDev_{n′}(𝒢; U_{k+1})), with c₀ an absolute constant (the domain also contains the curvature conditions (2.34)–(2.35) for e^{izηA} and its averages, |∂e^{iξA} − 1| ≤ C(ξ²|∇A| + ξ²|A|²), and (2.37) for the averaged 𝐉; the 4/3 of the 𝐉 radius absorbed in c₀), (LPG′) puts (zA, zR(u)J_{k+1}) in D(k+1)(iii)'s domain at the reference background 1. So f is analytic there, by the reference clause (D2.3(2)).
  - Let S_𝒢 be the supremum of ‖Y_𝒢‖ + |c_𝒢|‖T_𝐔(I(𝒢))‖ over D(k+1)(iii)'s domain about the real points of the support, together with the reference-clause domain. If R_X > 1, Schwarz's lemma gives ‖f(1)‖ ≤ S_𝒢/R_X. If R_X ≤ 1, then ‖f(1)‖ ≤ S_𝒢 ≤ S_𝒢/R_X. In both cases
    e^{θ_{k+1}(𝒢)}‖Y^d_𝒢(U_{k+1})‖ ≤ b_𝒢Dev_{n′}(𝒢; U_{k+1}) for every traversal n′ of I(𝒢), b_𝒢 := (C_g/(c₀ᾱ_{k+1}))e^{θ_{k+1}(𝒢)}S_𝒢.
  - This is the per-insertion form of (5), at the real point U_{k+1}.
  - *Complex data (derived on 𝒟_{k+1}^{sub}; SW closure §6(6b)).* D(k+1)(iii)'s dev clause at 𝐔 = e^{iηa′}U_{k+1}, 𝐉, for a′ in the sub-domain of D2.0(3) (the 𝐉-term bullet): the same Cauchy step on z ↦ Y(e^{iz(ηA + ηa″)}, z(R(u)J_{k+1} + (𝐉 − J_{k+1})), 0) − c_𝒢T with a″ := Ad(u)a′, radius c₀ᾱ_{k+1}/(C_gDev_{n′}(𝒢) + ‖a″‖_{sub} + ‖𝐉 − J‖_{sub}), gives e^{θ}‖Y^d_𝒢(𝐔, 𝐉)‖ ≤ b_𝒢(Dev_{n′}(𝒢) + a*_𝒢 + j*_𝒢) with a*_𝒢 ≤ α_{1,k+1}/C_g, j*_𝒢 ≤ 2α_{0,k+1}/C_g; summed by (S-w), Σ_{𝒢∋n′}e^θ‖Y^d_𝒢(𝐔,𝐉)‖ ≤ C_χρ̃_{k+1}δ*^{(k+1)}_{n′}(U_{k+1}) + 3C_χρ̃_{k+1}α_{1,k+1}Σ_tw_t, the second term O(ρ_{k+1}) under q₀ = q₁.

The dev species and (LPG′) are taken for such groups only; a group with 𝒬_{n′}(𝒢)^{∼5} meeting Ω_{k+1}ᶜ for some n′ is attached to a component of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ met by it (*Scope*; D2.0(3)'s reclassification rule), with D2.6's bound.   then Σ_{𝒢∋n′}e^{θ_{k+1}(𝒢)}‖Y^d_𝒢‖ ≤ (C_gC_S/c₀)e^{η_w}(ρ_{k+1}/ᾱ_{k+1})δ*^{(k+1)}_{n′}(U_{k+1}) (w_t ≤ e^{η_w}w_{t+1}, η_w = (5/16)η_V). (S) is the size statement of (7); it holds as (S-w), (13)(d), with the rate comparison of (13)(a).

- *At k = 1 (step 1 → 2)* the old dev bound is V3's own D1(ii), and the sup-only domain of D(1)(iii) needs no gradient bound. (LPG′) is needed for the groups made at steps k+1 ≥ 2.

### (5) The amendment of D(k)(ii) (for the user's review)

- *The dev constant.* For k ≥ 2 the first term of D(k)(ii) becomes C_χρ̃_kδ*^{(k)}_n, with ρ̃_k := ρ_k/ᾱ_k, ᾱ_k := min(α_{0,k}, α_{1,k}), and ρ̃_1 := ρ_1. The factor 1/ᾱ_{k+1} of (4) is the Cauchy factor of the domain's radius, and S_𝒢 is of size ρ_{k+1} under (S) ((7); it holds as (S-w), (13)(d)). So the amendment is forced by the Cauchy route of (4), not by a choice.
- *Sizes on the support are unchanged in order (derived).* On the support, δ*^{(k)}_n(U_k) ≤ 2c_σB₃ε_kΣ_tw_t (D2.0(3)(b)). So ρ̃_kδ*_n ≤ Cρ_kε_k/ᾱ_k, and ε_k/α_{i,k} = (A₀/C_i)(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀−q_i} ≤ A₀/C_i under R-m's entailments (q₁ > p₀, or q₁ = p₀ with C₁/A₀ large; q₀ > p₀, or q₀ = p₀ with C₀/A₀ large; gaps block). Hence ν_n^{(k)} = O(ρ_k) on the support, as before.
- *At complex data* (D(k)(iii)) the dev term carries δ*^{(k),30}_n + a*^{(k)}_n(A′), and ρ̃_ka*_n(A′) ≤ Cρ_kα_{1,k}/ᾱ_k = Cρ_k·max(1, (C₁/C₀)(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁−q₀}); the same bound holds for j*_n(𝐉), since |𝐉 − J_k|/r_J < 2. So the complex-data size gains the factor 1 + (log g_k^{−2})^{(q₁−q₀)₊}, of the same kind as ρ^c_{ph,k}'s. This is the complex-data tolerance of (0) for the dev term only. The size of the right side is derived here, away from older layers (next bullet); the clause itself is derived on the sub-domain for new groups ((4) *Complex data*) and for carried insertions about U_{k+1} (SW closure §6(6c), given (T-dev)), not reviewed. It is a power of log g_k^{−2}, of the kind D2.0(3)(iii) records for ρ^c_{ph,k}, and whether D(k)'s consumer tolerates that power is the IT-1 note's polylog item ((9)).
  - *Older layers (sub-domain form, SW closure §3, for review).* On the full domain, D(k)(iii) allows |η_kA′| < α_{1,m}L^{−m} per fine bond on Ω_m∖Ω_{m+1}, m < k, so a* and j* would pick up L^{k−m}α_{1,m}, and CRE p.256 lets such a layer lie within about 2M₁ of n. The bound above is therefore asserted on the sub-domain 𝒟_k^{sub} of D2.0(3) (the top-layer radii on every layer), where a*, j* ≤ 3S₃₀α_{1,k} on every layer; the only consumer, (11)(c)(β)'s polydisc points, lies inside it by the chain condition with R-dec.
- *The per-insertion form.* Each dev insertion carries its own bound at the real evaluation points: e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W(U_k, J_k)‖ ≤ b_W·Dev_{Q^#_W}(U_k), with Q^#_W its comparison box. Dev_{Q^#_W} uses the plaquettes of (Q^#_W)⁺, and these are the comparison box of D2.0(3)'s reclassification rule. At step 1 (Q^#_W)⁺ := Q_W, so that its plaquettes lie in Λ₁; for a new group Q^#_𝒢 := X(𝒢) of (4). The reclassification of (6)(ζ) sorts insertions by where Q^#_W lies. That needs a bound per insertion, which D(k)(ii)'s per-traversal sum does not give. At step 1 the form holds by V3's group bounds (IT-1 note §3a); at step k+1 it is (4)'s, given (LPG′).
- *Normalisation.* Dev_{Q^#_W}, ℓ_W := ℓ_{Q^#_W} and b_W stay in the normalisation of the step j(W) at which W was made, as θ and σ do for carried att groups (D2.0(3), "Units of carried groups"). Only δ* is converted from step to step ((6)(γ)). Converting Dev would not contract: by the bounds of (4), ℓ^{(k)}_Q/ℓ^{(k+1)}_Q lies between L⁴/162 and 162L⁴ for wall-connected Q (a π_{k+1}-cube is L⁴ π_k-cubes), and between L⁴/324 and 324L⁴ for closed-connected X(𝒢), so the best statement is Dev^{(k)}_Q ≤ 162L²Dev^{(k+1)}_Q (324L² for X(𝒢)).
- *The tail clause (ii-t) (part of D(k)(ii)).* Put β_W := b_Wℓ_Wη_{j(W)}^{−2}, so that b_WDev_{Q^#_W}(𝐔) = β_W·max{δ_p(𝐔) : p in (Q^#_W)⁺}. Let t^{(k)}(W) be the least t ≥ 1 with (Q^#_W)⁺ contained in the step-k R_t^{(k)}-neighbourhood of n (containment: the Abel step and (6)(ζ) use it), and W_t := Σ_{t′≥t}w_{t′}. The clause is
  (ii-t) Σ_{dev W∋n, t^{(k)}(W)≥t}β_W ≤ C_χρ̃_kη_k^{−2}W_t for every t ≥ 1 and every traversal n (the sum over dev W).

So (ii-t) is the class statement and the sum form is its consequence.

So β_W ≤ K_χC_χe^{η𝔰/8}𝔰³M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W). Q_W lies within C_boxLM𝔰 of n_f, so t(W) ≤ max(1, ⌊𝔰⌋). §3a(6)'s split by ⌊𝔰⌋, with 𝔰³e^{η𝔰/8} ≤ (j+1)³e^{η/8}e^{−(31/8)ηj}e^{4η𝔰} at j = ⌊𝔰⌋ and the contour pin Σ_{W∋n_f}M^{(ρ)}_Λe^{4η𝔰} ≤ Cρ, gives Σ_{W∋n_f, t(W)≥t}β_W ≤ Cρ·sup_{j≥t}w_j ≤ CρW_t for t ≥ 2, and Cρ·max_{j≥0}w_j ≤ C_ηρW_1 for t = 1. Summing over the L fine traversals of a step-1 traversal, with the conversions of D2.0(3)(a), gives (ii-t) at k = 1. Step-1 conventions: t(W) above is relative to a fine traversal, t(W; n_f). Define t^{(1)}(W; n) as the least t with Q_W in the R_t^{(1)}-neighbourhood of n. Fine distance C_boxLM(t+1) is step-1 distance R_t^{(1)}, and n ⊇ n_f, so t^{(1)}(W; n) ≤ t(W; n_f) for every n_f ⊂ n with n_f ∈ W, hence {t^{(1)} ≥ t} ⊂ {t(W; n_f) ≥ t}; summing over the L fine traversals gives (ii-t) at k = 1, with C_χ absorbing L·C(1+β₀)⁶; η₁^{−2} = L² is the conversion factor from fine to step-1 plaquette deviations; ℓ_W := 1 and b_W := β_Wη₁² = β_W/L², so that β_W = b_Wℓ_Wη₁^{−2} holds at step 1 with Dev_{Q_W} the step-1 deviation.
  - *The transfer (derived given the inputs below).* (4) proves the per-insertion bound only at the background of the step j(W) at which W was made, U_{j(W)}, from (LPG′) for that step's minimiser. Carrying W to step k+1 needs it at U_{k+1}:
    (T-dev) e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W(U_{k+1}, J_{k+1})‖ ≤ b_W·Dev_{Q^#_W}(U_{k+1}) for every dev W made at a step j ≤ k with (Q^#_W)⁺ ⊂ Ω_k (scope Ω_k, so that (6)(ζ)'s reclassified insertions are covered).

So e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W(U_j(V_j), J_j(V_j))‖ ≤ b_WDev_{Q^#_W}(U_j(V_j)) for all such V_j, with b_W as in (4) (the evaluation points are the real ones). For j = 1 the same holds with V3's group bounds for every U₁ in V3's step-1 support (IT-1 note §3a), and that V₁^* below lies in that support is a step-1 check (V3's step-1 indicators; flagged). (2) The global induction. For V_{k+1} in the step-(k+1) support put V_i^* := M^iU_{k+1}, j ≤ i ≤ k. Claim: V_i^* lies in the full step-i support of the term, and U_i(V_i^*) = U_{k+1}, for every i = j, …, k. Downward induction on i, three regions at each i, with the induction hypothesis at i+1 (at i = k the hypothesis is V_{k+1} itself in the step-(k+1) support):
    - on the cubes □ ⊂ Ω_{i+1}^∼ outside the band Ω_{i+1}^∼∖Ω_{i+1}^{∼−1}: V_i^* = M^iU_{k+1} = M^iU_{i+1}(V_{i+1}^*) = V^{(i)}(V_{i+1}^*) is in the step-i support by D2.0(5)(c)(β) at step i applied at V_{i+1} = V_{i+1}^*. (β)'s derivation uses the regularity of U_{i+1}(V_{i+1}^*) on Ω_{i+1} ((3.19) is derived from it). That input comes from the induction hypothesis (the step-n membership conditions are too weak for it, by (C₀/2B₃A₀)(log g^{−2})^{q₀−p₀} on older layers): V_{i+1}^* lies in the full step-(i+1) support, so CRE p.268 at the step i → i+1, read on that support as in D2.0(3)(b), gives |∂U_{i+1}(V_{i+1}^*) − 1| < 2B₃ε_{i+1}η_{i+1}² on all of Ω_{i+1}, and U_{i+1}(V_{i+1}^*) = U_{k+1} by the hypothesis. That is (β)'s input, with no comparison of ε's.
    - on the band and on the boundary cubes: D2.6's carrier item, (β)'s both exclusions, proved for all V_{i+1} in the support. *Status: the boundary cubes are derived in WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §9.2 (M ≥ 4LM₂, reading R-avg); the band cubes with □′^{∼4} ⊂ Ω_{i+1} are derived in closure §9.3 (given R-avg, [15], [12], [14] (1.65)'s reasoning at V^{(i)}), and those with □′^{∼4} ⊄ Ω_{i+1} by route (a), mixed-set localisation across ∂Ω_{i+1} (closure §9.3 (1)–(2)); R-avg is derived (closure §9.1); not reviewed. Consumers of (T-dev): D2.4(5) *It carries*, D2.4(6) and SW §3(b4); D2.4(12)(f)'s c_{{W}} = 0 (the reference activity of the trivial group) at the reference point is by f(0) = 0 (D2.3(3)), not by (T-dev).*
    - off Ω_{i+1}^∼, i.e. on Ω_i∖Ω_{i+1}^∼: the step-i field V_i is a pending variable of the term there, and U_{k+1} satisfies the constraint M^jU_{k+1} = V_j on Γ_j for every j ≤ k+1, by the definition of the multi-scale minimiser (CRE p.256: "V = V_j on Γ_j", (2.10); M_𝔹(U) = M^j(U) on Γ_j, (2.11); the minimal orbit of A^η on {U regular, M_𝔹(U) = V}, (2.12); p.266: U_{k+1} "is determined by the sequence" of fields). So V_i^* = V_i pointwise on all of Γ_i. The indicator claim: χ_i(□) depends on V_i through U_{i,□}(V_i) = U(𝔹_i(□^{∼4}), M·(Q_i^{s*}V_i)) (CRE p.257), so for the cubes □ ⊂ Ω_i∖Ω_{i+1}^∼ with □^{∼4} ∩ Ω_{i+1} = ∅ its argument is the same at V_i^* and at V_i; the remaining cubes of Ω_i∖Ω_{i+1}^∼ are boundary cubes, region 2's. At V_i the factors χ_i(□) and χ_{Ax} of those cubes hold on the term's support: CRE pp.265–266 show χ_{Ax} and χ_i equal to 1 on Ω_{i+1}^∼ only; p.267 resums "the characteristic functions and the gauge fixing terms localized in Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}" into ζ(Ω_{k+1}^c) (at k = i); ζ(Ω_{i+1}^c) is a factor of 𝐓^{(i)} ((2.21), p.258) inside 𝐓_{k+1} ((2.18), (2.20)), and an operation 𝐓^{(j)} is changed only by an R-operation ("the characteristic functions are changed", p.258), so it survives for k ≤ N (R-h). That the resummed ζ(Ω_{i+1}^c) contains the old χ_i(□) and χ_{Ax} of the cubes off Ω_{i+1}^∼ is R-ζ (p.267 does not list its factors; CRE p.267's resummation, Bałaban's, accepted).
    And U_i(V_i^*) = U_i(V^{(i)}(V_{i+1}^*)) = U_{i+1}(V_{i+1}^*) = U_{k+1} by (α) at step i, on the whole of Ω_i (U_i is the minimiser for the full sequence; same reference). No composition identity for the averages is needed: V_i^* is defined as M^iU_{k+1} and recognised as V^{(i)} at V_{i+1}^* through the induction hypothesis. (3) At i = j, (1) at V_j = V_j^* gives e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W(U_{k+1}, J_j(V_j^*))‖ ≤ b_WDev_{Q^#_W}(U_{k+1}), in the normalisation of step j, for every dev W of step j with (Q^#_W)⁺ ⊂ Ω_k (the full step-k support; this covers the reclassified insertions of (6)(ζ), whose boxes meet Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}). The 𝐉-argument: J_j is the expression J(U_j) of RG1 p.272 ("we replace the expression J_j by the new variable 𝐉"; D2.2(6)(b): at real configurations 𝐉 = J_{k+1} = J(U_{k+1}), PBG p.395), the same current expression at every step evaluated at the fine field U_j (R-J, printed: RG1 (1.8), (3.12)). Normalisation: at the real step-(k+1) point the 𝐉-argument of a step-j insertion is J̃(U_{k+1}) = D^{ξ*}_{U_{k+1}}ξ^{−2}π Im∂U_{k+1} = (L^{j−1}η)³J_{k+1} (RG1 (3.10) at 𝐀 = 0, and (3.12), p.272). Under R-J and U_j(V_j^*) = U_{k+1}, this is J_j(V_j^*) in step-j normalisation and units, so the bound is at D(k+1)'s evaluation point (U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}) up to that fixed factor and change of units. That is (T-dev), stated for (Q^#_W)⁺ ⊂ Ω_k. Its inputs: (LPG′), D2.6's carrier item at each step, the reference clause D(j)(iii), k ≤ N (R-h), R-ζ, R-J, the step-1 base; these are recorded in the (LPG′) gaps entry and in (9). An alternative, not adopted: (4)'s Cauchy step needs only the reference clause at 1 and (LPG′) at the evaluation point; posing (LPG′) for configurations in Bałaban's multi-scale regularity class, instead of for U_j(V_j) with V_j in the support, would remove the downward induction and its D2.6 dependency, but changes (LPG′).
  - *It carries from k to k+1 (derived given (T-dev)).* An old dev insertion with (Q^#_W)⁺ ⊂ Ω_{k+1} stays dev, with β_W unchanged, by (T-dev). (The β-inequality below is unconditional arithmetic on the β_W; (T-dev) is what gives it its meaning as a bound on the insertions at U_{k+1}.) If (Q^#_W)⁺ lies within step-k distance R_t^{(k)} of n ⊂ n′, it lies within step-(k+1) distance R_t^{(k)}/L ≤ R_t^{(k+1)} of n′, so t^{(k+1)}(W) ≤ t^{(k)}(W). Hence Σ_{W∋n′, t^{(k+1)}(W)≥t}β_W ≤ Σ_{n⊂n′}Σ_{W∋n, t^{(k)}(W)≥t}β_W ≤ L·C_χ^{(k)}ρ̃_kη_k^{−2}W_t = L^{−1}(ρ̃_k/ρ̃_{k+1})C_χ^{(k)}ρ̃_{k+1}η_{k+1}^{−2}W_t, the coefficient of (6)'s recursion.

  - The other dev contributions of D2.0(5)(c)–(d) (fluctuation parts, pair contractions) enter the recursion through their sum bounds. Their tail form, and the per-insertion form that (6)(ζ) needs for them, is to be stated with them, in D2.0(5)(d), which is marked "Needs D2.1–D2.5".

### (6) Matching, split and recursion (derived)

- *(γ) The matching.* Take R_t^{(k)} := C_boxM(t+1) in step-k units at every k, which is D2.0(3)'s R_t^{(1)} at k = 1. Let n be one of the L step-k traversals in the new traversal n′. Step-(k+1) distances are step-k distances divided by L, so a plaquette within step-k distance R_t^{(k)} of n lies within step-(k+1) distance R_t^{(k)}/L ≤ R_t^{(k+1)} of n′. On a common plaquette set, Δ^{(k)}_n(R_t^{(k)}) ≤ Δ^{(k+1)}_{n′}(R_t^{(k+1)}) for every t. With η_k^{−2} = L^{−2}η_{k+1}^{−2},
  δ*^{(k)}_n(U) ≤ L^{−2}δ*^{(k+1)}_{n′}(U) for every U, on the plaquettes of Ω_{k+1}.
  Dev is not converted. Each insertion keeps Dev, ℓ_W and b_W in the normalisation of its step j(W) ((5), *Normalisation*), and the per-traversal bound carries through (ii-t) and the inclusion t^{(k+1)}(W) ≤ t^{(k)}(W) ((5)).
- *(ζ) The split (derived from (ii-t) given (T-dev) of (5)).* An old dev insertion W with (Q^#_W)⁺ ⊂ Ω_{k+1} stays dev ((5), carrying (ii-t), given (T-dev)). The bound below uses b_WDev_{Q^#_W}(U_{k+1}) for the reclassified insertions, whose boxes meet Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}: that is (T-dev) in its scope (Q^#_W)⁺ ⊂ Ω_k ((5); through its global induction it needs D2.6's carrier item at every step j(W), …, k, on all band and boundary cubes). If (Q^#_W)⁺ meets Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}, it becomes att(k+1, Z). By D2.0(3)'s reclassification rule, Z is the first component of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ that the box meets, so the box meets Z, and R^{(k)}_{t^{(k)}(W)} ≥ dist(n, Z).
  - *Its plaquettes.* (Q^#_W)⁺ ⊂ Ω_k for a dev insertion at step k. By membership (2.34) at j = k+1, i.e. U_{k+1} in the step-(k+1) space on Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}, which is D2.2(6)(e)'s (D2.0(3)(c) has it only "once U_{k+1} is shown to lie in the space") (CRE p.261: |∂U − 1| < (1−β(1−2^{−(j−n)}))α_{0,n}ξ²(L^nξ)^{−2}), the fine plaquettes of Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} (n = k) give η_k^{−2}δ_p ≤ c_σ(1−β/2)α_{0,k}, as in D2.0(3)(c). Those of Ω_{k+1} (n = k+1) give η_k^{−2}δ_p ≤ c_σL^{−2}α_{0,k+1} ≤ c_σ(1+β₀)²L^{−2}α_{0,k} ≤ c_σα_{0,k}. So b_WDev_{Q^#_W} ≤ β_Wη_k²c_σα_{0,k}. Only the factor α_{0,k} is used below, so the margin (1 − β/2) is not needed.
  - *The bound.* Let t_Z be the least t with R_t^{(k)} ≥ dist(n, Z). By (ii-t),
    Σ_{W∋n, W ↦ att(k+1,Z)}b_WDev_{Q^#_W} ≤ c_σα_{0,k}η_k²Σ_{W∋n, t^{(k)}(W)≥t_Z}β_W ≤ C_χρ̃_kc_σα_{0,k}W_{t_Z} ≤ Cρ_k·max(1, α_{0,k}/α_{1,k})·e^{−cd_{k+1}^Z(n)/M},
    since ρ̃_kα_{0,k} = ρ_kα_{0,k}/ᾱ_k and W_t decays exponentially in t. This is per component, as D(k+1)(ii)'s att bound is (D2.0(3), "Per component").
  - D(k+1)(ii)'s att constant is Cρ̂_{k+1}, with ρ_k ≤ (1+β₀)⁶ρ_{k+1}. So the split needs α_{0,k}/α_{1,k} = (C₀/C₁)(log g_k^{−2})^{q₀−q₁} bounded, i.e. q₀ ≤ q₁ (*entailment q₀ ≤ q₁, for the user's review*). Otherwise the att(k+1, ·) constant is enlarged by (log g_k^{−2})^{(q₀−q₁)₊}, and D2.6's per-component payment must absorb that power of log (recorded for D2.6).
- *The recursion with ρ̃.* The old dev parts on Ω_{k+1} give, by (ii-t) carried ((5)) and its sum form, Σ_{n⊂n′}C_χ^{(k)}ρ̃_kδ*^{(k)}_n ≤ L^{−1}(ρ̃_k/ρ̃_{k+1})C_χ^{(k)}ρ̃_{k+1}δ*^{(k+1)}_{n′}.
  - By CRE (2.6), first and last lines with n − m = 1, g_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)g_k and g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_{k+1}. By (2.7), first printed line and third (the continuation of the second, with (n−m)^{β₀} = 1 at n − m = 1), (log g_{k+1}^{−2})^p ≤ (1+β₀)(log g_k^{−2})^p and (log g_k^{−2})^p ≤ (1+β₀)(log g_{k+1}^{−2})^p.
  - So ρ_k/ρ_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)⁶ (D2.0(5)(c)) and α_{i,k+1}/α_{i,k} ≤ (1+β₀)², hence ᾱ_{k+1}/ᾱ_k ≤ (1+β₀)² and ρ̃_k/ρ̃_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)⁸.
  - The terms of D2.0(5)(c)–(d) that are linear in the old dev bound carry this ratio in place of (1+β₀)⁶. The quadratic term has two old dev bounds, and one of them is O(ρ_k) on the support by (5). So
    C_χ^{(k+1)} ≤ C_new + (L^{−1} + C_dL²(1+β₀)²g_K²)(1+β₀)⁸C_χ^{(k)} + C_d″ρ_k(C_χ^{(k)})²,
    with C_new := C_gC_S·pl/c₀ from (4) and D2.4(13)(a). It is bounded uniformly in k if L^{−1}(1+β₀)⁸ < 1 and g_K is small.
  - *The condition (1+β₀)⁸ < L (for the user's review).* CRE p.255: "β₀ > 0 can be chosen arbitrarily small, if g is sufficiently small". So for every L ≥ 2 it holds once β₀ < L^{1/8} − 1. For V3's L ≥ 13 with β₀ ≤ 0.22 it holds: 1.22⁸ < 4.91. D2.0(5)(c)'s "L ≥ 2, β₀ small" is right only with "small" read as this bound.
  - At the step 1 → 2 the ratio is ρ̃_1/ρ̃_2 = (ρ_1/ρ_2)ᾱ_2 ≤ (1+β₀)⁶ᾱ_2 < 1, so the first step costs nothing.

### (7) The sizes of the groups and the reference gas ((S) with the H′ bound; D2.3(5)(T′) in place of (T), and (Z), both derived given (S-w))

- *Where the slot factors are evaluated (derived; answers the check left open in the plan).* In D2.2(5)(c) the slot factor 𝒳 is a function of the conditioned Z₀-variable B alone ((2.4)–(2.6) there). It does not depend on X or on the s-, σ-, τ-parameters. In RG2 (2.14) B is a real integration variable, and the complex quantities are (𝐔, 𝐉) ∈ 𝐔^c_{k+1}(X,α₀,α₁), σ and τ. (2.15) bounds (2.14) by an integral of absolute values. So 𝒳 enters (2.15) through sup‖𝒳‖ over real B in the support of the indicators and complex (𝐔, 𝐉) in the domain. That is D(k)(iii)'s domain at U_k(𝐔, B), which D2.2(4)(f) and (6)(e) supply.
- *The H′ bound for marked polymers (Step 1 of (S), derived in (10), with D_J^# replaced by its π_{k+1}-hull D′, the rate (1−5δ)λ_Lκ and C₃′ = e^{81A#_{k+1}(D′)}e^{(5+3λ_L)κ(m−1)}; the display below is the target shape, (10) the derived form).* CRE p.277 says of H′(Z₀), whose integrand contains one localized term of CRE's own integrated function, "Although different, it satisfies the same bound as the other factors". What (S) needs is a different bound for a different object, so it is not a reading of that sentence. It is derived in (10): RG2's estimates of pp.15–20, run with a slot factor 𝒳_M as a bounded factor in (2.15) and with Z₀ ⊇ D_J^#, give, with (10)'s replacements,
  |H̃(M)| ≤ C₃′·sup‖𝒳_M‖·exp(−(1−8δ)½Lκ·d_{k+1}(M; D_J^#)),
  where d_{k+1}(M; D_J^#) is the linear size of M with the cubes of D_J^# contracted to one point.
  - C₃′ is not small. The term with no Mayer bond, no P and no s-derivative, the conditioned Gaussian expectation of 𝒳_M, is present for a marked polymer. Bałaban's smallness C₃ε₁ comes from the nontrivial structure of vacuum polymers, and it is not claimed for marked ones.
  - There is no decay across D_J^#: inside it the integrand need not contain a vacuum polymer. That decay has to come from 𝒳_M's own decay in the spread of its slots, that is, from the old norms and the slot sizes of D2.1.
  - CRE asserts the statement for one inserted term, its own. Our 𝒳_M is a product of loop slots and old insertions. Nothing here rests on CRE's sentence.
- *(S), the size statement (derived in (10)–(13) in the form (S-w) of (12)(b), given the amendments (12)(a)–(b), accepted by the user, and the inputs listed in (13)(d); below is the form its consumers use).* Σ_{𝒢∋n′, t(𝒢)=t}e^{θ_{k+1}(𝒢)}S_𝒢ℓ_{X(𝒢)} ≤ C_Sρ_{k+1}w_t for every t ((4)), and the same with c_𝒢 in place of S_𝒢 for the reference gas, with ℓ_X = d_{k+1}(X) + 1 ((4); ℓ_{X(𝒢)} ≤ 2#(X(𝒢)) when X(𝒢) is connected). Summed over t′ ≥ t it gives (ii-t) for the new groups ((5)). For (Z) it must also give the Kotecký–Preiss root sum with insertion weights a(𝒢) ≥ a_min·n_ins(𝒢) (derived from a° with a_min = ν/(32LM), and occurrences absorbed by 1/m_𝒢!), with its own constant C_KP, and the same count with one old factor replaced by its T–T′ difference (D2.3(4) (Z-d)–(Z-f)). Reduced to:
  - the H′ bound above;
  - the slot sizes per component (D2.1, D2.2(4)), with the old insertions entering through ν_n^{(k)}, which already contains e^{θ_k} ((3));
  - the entropy of the groups: linked families of marked polymers, old groups and slots, counted with FV Thm 5.4 on the gas of linked families, as D2.2(5)(d) does for one family;
  - the comparison of the decay rate (1−8δ)½Lκ/162 per cube with (31/8)η_V/(C_boxM) per step-(k+1) unit of R_t, plus the entropy. *Done in (12)–(13): the entropy by the tree bound of (12)(f) with the charges of (12)(d); the comparison is replaced by the extent weight ν_e = η_V/(2C_box) inside (S-w), with w_t's rate weakened to (5/16)η_V ((12)(b), (13)(a)).*
- *(R), the reference gas (derived given (S-w); (T) is not used).* What is derived: given (S-w), the Kotecký–Preiss condition for the reference gas holds at a°/2 ((12)(f)), FV (5.11) gives |Q^{out}_{k+1}| ≤ ∏_{𝒢}e^{a°(𝒢)}, and the tail bound D2.3(5)(T′) — Σ_{Γ∋b, diam(network) ≥ R}|Φ°(Γ)| ≤ C_S′𝔞_{k+1}e^{−c_dR} — follows because a°(Γ) bounds the diameter of Γ's network (D2.3(4)(Z-f)). (T′) is what D2.3(5)–(7) and (Z) use; (T) is not used: only distances enter, not the network length, and the amplitude is 𝔞_{k+1} = max(τ_k², ρ_{k+1}·pl) throughout D2.3(5)–(7). The gas: by D2.3(3) it is a pure hard-core gas of groups that carry their old and vacuum occurrences, with no Mayer links. Given (S-w), FV Thm 5.4 applies with a°(𝒢) := (ν/32)σ_{k+1}(𝒢), as in (2)(e)–(g) (D2.4(12)(f)). It gives:
  - S°(ℓ) ≠ 0 and its cluster series, hence the absolute convergence used in D2.3(3);
  - the tail (T′) at step k+1 with the constant C_S′ of (S-w) and the amplitude 𝔞_{k+1}, uniformly in k (no recursion, no network-length bound);
  - Σ_{Γ hits 𝒢}|Φ°(Γ)| ≤ a°(𝒢), FV (5.11), so Q^{out}_{k+1}(D) = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits D}Φ°(Γ)) satisfies |Q^{out}_{k+1}(D)| ≤ ∏_{𝒢∈D}e^{θ_ref(𝒢)} with θ_ref(𝒢) ≤ a°(𝒢) = (ν/32)σ_{k+1}(𝒢);
  - *The weights.* The polymer weight of a group includes ∏|φ_k(Γ_j)|/m_𝒢! of its old occurrences and the factors of its vacuum occurrences, and (S) bounds the sum over groups with their occurrences, with the arcs J_Γ in σ_{k+1}. The induction uses D(k)'s Σ_{Γ hits W}|φ_k(Γ)|e^{a(Γ)} ≤ θ(W) with a size weight a(Γ) that pays the arcs; that weighted form is part of (S).
- *(Z) (derived given (S-w) by D2.3(4)(Z-f)–(Z-h) with D2.4(12)(f), (13)(c)).* Two parts.
  - The activities made at step k+1. For polymers that do not wrap the torus, CRE p.278 says the terms "for X ⊂ Λ_{k+1}, do not depend on Λ_{k+1}, and coincide with the corresponding terms arising from the expressions defined on the whole lattice". Reading this as independence of the torus as well is not supported by the sentence, which is about Λ_{k+1} on a fixed lattice. The covariances on T and on ℤ⁴ differ by images of size e^{−cι_T}. The step from a fixed lattice to two tori is made by R-loc (derived cross-torus identity with the accepted lifted-partition convention) (walk pieces and local operators, D2.3(4)), not by p.278; with it the new parts need no image sum ((Z-c)), and the image sums sit in the step-1 objects only.
  - The old insertions. They need torus consistency as functions of 𝐔, so D(k)(vi) covers the insertions and the cluster weights φ_k(Γ) as well as the reference weights Φ° (D2.0(3)), proved at step 1 from V4 and carried. *In D2.3(4), (Z-e): (vi)_k in local-agreement form, summed per traversal ((I) for insertions, (O) for the numbers φ_k), with a relative constant C_rel; it carries from k to k+1 by (13)(c), since the new parts are exactly local ((Z-c)). The step-1 base, V4's insertions as functions of U₁ local up to images in rooted form, is derived in D2.3(4)(Z-h) from V4's density-direction estimate applied to V3's physical-data integrals (one structural input for the user's confirmation; V4 L3813 covers the covariance entries only). The first part above (the new activities): their new parts are the same functionals on T and T′ given R-loc ((Z-c)); the old factors carried in them are the second part. The rooted sum ((Z-f)) uses (S)'s Kotecký–Preiss root sum with weights a(𝒢) ≥ a_min·n_ins(𝒢) on the old insertions only, the occurrences being absorbed by 1/m_𝒢! (constant C_KP = C_S′, (12)(f)); the a°-weight bounds the non-embedding clusters (D2.3(4)(Z-f)), so (T)'s network property is not needed. With (12)(f), (13)(c) and (Z-f)–(Z-h), (Z) is derived given (S-w).*

### (8) Complex data (derived; the analyticity of the reference-gas sums given (S))

- *Transports.* On Ω_{k+1}, |η_{k+1}A′| < α_{1,k+1}L^{−(k+1)} per fine bond, so ‖T_𝐔(I(𝒢))‖ ≤ e^{c_πα_{1,k+1}n_{k+1}(𝒢)}, c_π the constant of π. It is at most e^{νσ_{k+1}(𝒢)/64} once c_πα_{1,k+1}M ≤ ν/64 (n_{k+1}(𝒢) ≤ Mσ_{k+1}(𝒢), (12)(a)), which holds for g_{k+1} small, since α_{1,k} → 0 as g_k → 0. For an arc of at most 4M traversals the bound is e, under 4c_πMα_{1,k+1} ≤ 1. The older layers Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, n ≤ k, are att and D2.6's. Transports between groups are not needed: whole-word norms are never used (D2.0(5)(h)).
- *Analyticity of the sums (D2.2(6)(e)).* Bałaban's bounds (2.38) and (2.15) hold at every point of the domain with the same constants. So the absolute-value integrand of (2.15), with the sup of 𝒳 over the domain, is a majorant, locally uniform in the complex parameters. Morera's theorem under the integrals and Hartogs' theorem then apply. The cluster series converge uniformly on the domain by (2) and (R), so their sums are analytic by Weierstrass. For the reference gas this uses (R), hence (S).
- *The enlargement D_J^#.* D_J^# is determined by J. It is not summed over, so it adds no entropy. Its cubes are counted in #_{k+1}(𝒢) and in #(M), so their cost is inside θ_vac ≤ c#_{k+1} and inside (S).
- *Slot values at complex backgrounds.* The bounds of the loop slots and old insertions at complex (𝐔, 𝐉) are D(k)(iii)'s at step k. At step k+1 they are part of (S) (D2.2(6)(e), derived given R-𝐇, whose bound is derived).

### (9) What D2.4 leaves
