### (10) Step 1 of (S): the H′ bound for marked polymers (derived from RG2 pp.12–21 and D2.2(5), read in full; reviewed: no fatal finding; repairs not re-reviewed)

*(a) The object.* A marked polymer M (D2.2(5)(c)) is a linked union of connected Z ∈ 𝐃_{k+1} with slot set J(M) ≠ ∅. D := D_J^#(M) ⊂ M is the union of π_k-cubes of D2.2(5)(c) for the slots of M, m(M) its number of wall-connected components; every connected component of M meets D (one disjoint from D would be a vacuum polymer of its own). H̃(M) is the sum over (𝐃, P, Z₀) with Y₀ = ∪_{Y∈𝐃}Y ∪ D, P ⊂ Y₀^{c*}, Z₀ the smallest localisation domain containing Y₀ ∪ P (so D ⊂ Z₀), Z₀′ the π_{k+1}-hull of Z̃₀, Z₀′ ⊂ M with every component of M containing a component of Z₀′ (p.14), of the terms (2.14) with 𝒳_M in the B-integrand and Z = M. Notation:
- ‖𝒳_M‖_sup := sup of ‖𝒳_M(B; 𝐔, 𝐉)‖ over real B with |B(b)| < t_k := δ_k/g_k = r_k for every bond b ⊂ D (CRE (3.16)'s test in the variable B of (2.14), (11)(b); RG2 (2.3)'s threshold ε₁/g_k is replaced by t_k throughout (b) and (e): the P-factor is e^{−½γ₂t_k²|P|}, the conditions "(1/20)γ₂ε₁²/γ² ≥ 18κ", "e^{−γ₂ε₁²/(20γ²)} ≤ ε₂" and the term 4M⁴e^{−γ₂ε₁²/(10γ²)} of A read with t_k² in place of ε₁²/γ², and are smallness conditions on g_K, since r_k ≥ A₁(log((1+β₀)^{−2}g_K^{−2}))^{p₀} uniformly in k ≤ K; the support lies in (1.34)'s disc since δ_k < ε₁^{RG2} for g_K small) and over (𝐔, 𝐉) in the domain of (7)'s first bullet; ‖·‖ is the projective norm on ⊗_{i∈J(M)}End(V_π), a cross norm, the same on both sides ((12)(f)). 𝒳_M depends on B through the bonds inside D only, and not on X, s, σ, τ (D2.2(5)(c)).
- Contracted linear size. For a union X of cubes of one partition (side a) and a union D ⊂ X of its cubes, d(X; D) := a^{−1} inf |G ∖ D| over the finite unions G of segments and points with G ⊂ X, G meeting every cube of X and G ∪ D connected; |·| is the one-dimensional measure. d(X; ∅) is RG1 p.257's d(X). A cube of X that touches D costs nothing (a point of the cube in D); distinct components of D are joined through G, so their distance is charged. For M with components M^j, d_{k+1}(M; D′) := Σ_j d_{k+1}(M^j; D′ ∩ M^j). d_k is at scale k (a = M), d_{k+1} at scale k+1 (a = LM).
- D′ := the π_{k+1}-hull of D̃ (the smallest union of π_{k+1}-cubes containing D̃, D̃ := D with its touching π_k-cubes adjoined; p.13's convention for Z₀′). Z₀ ⊇ D gives Z₀′ ⊇ D′, and #_{k+1}(D′) ≤ #_k(D̃) ≤ 81#_k(D).

*(b) Result.* Let λ_L := L/max{2, 1 + 486/L} (= L/2 for L ≥ 486), r := (1 − 5δ)λ_Lκ (= (L+2)κ/4 with RG2 p.21's δ = (1−2/L)/10 for L ≥ 486), and A := O(1)(LM)⁴α₅ + e^{−½(κ₁−1)} + L⁴(O_{(1.26)}α₆ + 4M⁴e^{−γ₂t_k²/10}), this (10)'s constant (R-L3's A is p.20's), an absolute constant that Bałaban's choices make as small as wanted — an added restriction, not a reading: p.20's "(LM)⁴α ≤ 1" is strengthened to "≤ a small number"; α₆ ≤ that number times L^{−4}; M, κ₁ large; g_K small for the last term. Then, uniformly on the domain of (7)'s first bullet and in k,
  |H̃(M)| ≤ ‖𝒳_M‖_sup · e^{81A·#_{k+1}(D′)} · e^{(5 + 3λ_L)κ(m(M) − 1)} · exp(−r·d_{k+1}(M; D′)),
under Lemma 3's restrictions with these constants made explicit: (1/20)γ₂t_k² ≥ 18κ (p.18 prints 4κ with ε₁²/γ²; (d)), ε₂e^{max(5, 3λ_L)κ} ≤ 1 (p.18's ε₂e^{5κ} ≤ 1; (e)), ½(κ₁−1) ≥ 2Lκ (p.20; this is what (10) itself uses, charging 3 per parent edge; Lemma 3 as printed needs ½(κ₁−1) ≥ 9Lκ by (d), a condition on κ₁ recorded in R-L3), 324A ≤ δλ_Lκ (the absorption of (1)'s p.20 bullet in contracted form, with (d)'s counting constant 324), and R-L3's conditions on κ. No smallness factor: the term 𝐃 = ∅, P = ∅, Z₀ = D, M = Z₀′ = D′ is the conditioned Gaussian expectation of 𝒳_M and is bounded by ‖𝒳_M‖_sup·e^{A#_{k+1}(D′)} only. Every other term of H̃(M) carries a factor ϑ := max{e^{−20δκ}, e^{−γ₂ε₁²/(20γ²)}, e^{−Lκ/2}} ≤ e^{−2κ/3} ((e)). So H̃(D′) = (the plain expectation term) + O(ϑ), and H̃(M) = O(ϑ) for M ≠ D′, each with the bound above. Analyticity in (𝐔, 𝐉): (8); the bound is a majorant. This is (7)'s H′ bullet with C₃^{(10)} := e^{81A#_{k+1}(D′)}e^{(5+3λ_L)κ(m−1)} (not R-L3's C₃′), D_J^# replaced by its hull D′, and the rate (1−5δ)λ_Lκ in place of (1−8δ)½Lκ (the same up to 3δ for L ≥ 486). The per-term bounds (2.15)–(2.26) are printed for RG2's measure; their use for the measure of CRE (3.27), with its source term and multi-domain cover (D2.2(5)(b)–(c)), rests on CRE pp.276–279 ("exactly the same way", "do not matter") and R-I7: Bałaban's claim, accepted as in R-vac(i′).

*(c) The per-term bound: (2.14)–(2.26) with 𝒳_M.*
- (2.14): the Cauchy representations in σ(Δ) and τ(Y) act on the operators and on exp Σ_Yτ(Y)𝐕_k(Y); 𝒳_M carries no s, σ, τ. So the term is (2.14) with 𝒳_M(B; 𝐔, 𝐉) multiplying the B-integrand, B the real variable of the complex Gaussian measure dμ_{C^{(k)}(Z₀,σ(Z))}.
- (2.15) is a pointwise bound in B. On the support of χ_{k,Y₀}χ^c_{k,P} every bond of D ⊂ Y₀ is small-field, so ‖𝒳_M(B; 𝐔, 𝐉)‖ ≤ ‖𝒳_M‖_sup there, and (2.15) holds with the factor ‖𝒳_M‖_sup in front (‖∫f‖ ≤ ∫‖f‖ for the tensor-valued integral).
- (2.16)–(2.25) concern the operators, the determinants, the potentials 𝐕_k(Y) and the indicators; none contains 𝒳_M. (2.20)'s second line is a sum over Y ∈ 𝐃 only, still ≤ O(1)α₄M^{−4}|Y₀| (R-L3's p.16 bullet). So (2.26) holds with the factor ‖𝒳_M‖_sup, for every (𝐃, P, Z₀) with Y₀ ⊇ D, including 𝐃 = ∅ (empty product) and P = ∅:
  |term| ≤ ‖𝒳_M‖_sup · e^{−(κ₁−1)#_{k+1}(Z∖Z₀′)} · ∏_{Y∈𝐃}ε₂α₆e^{−(1−3δ)κd_k(Y)} · e^{−½γ₂(ε₁²/g_k²)|P|} · e^{O(1)α₅|Z|},
  with ε₂α₆ = 2E₀ε₁C₁α₄^{−1}M^q e^{C₂κ₁} (p.19) and #_{k+1}(Z∖Z₀′) = (LM)^{−4}|Z∖Z₀′|.

*(d) Geometry (derived): (2.27), (2.32), (2.36) in contracted form.* Lengths in the cube side of the scale concerned. A join between two pieces that share a cube or a wall, or between a piece and a cube it touches, costs at most √7 < 3 (the diameter of two wall-adjacent 4-cubes) from a point of one graph in the shared or adjacent cube to a point of the other.
- (2.27′) For Y_i a wall-connected component of Y₀, 𝐃_i := {Y ∈ 𝐃 : Y ⊂ Y_i}, D_i := D ∩ Y_i with m_i components: Σ_{Y∈𝐃_i}d_k(Y) ≥ d_k(Y_i; D_i) − 5(|𝐃_i| + m_i − 1). Proof: the pieces Y ∈ 𝐃_i and the components of D_i cover Y_i and their touching graph is connected (a wall-path of cubes passes from piece to piece through shared cubes or walls); a spanning tree has |𝐃_i| + m_i − 1 edges; G := ∪_YT_Y ∪ joins, T_Y a shortest tree of Y, meets every cube of Y_i, G ∪ D_i is connected, and |G∖D_i| ≤ Σ_YMd_k(Y) + 3M·(edges). For D_i = ∅ this is (2.27) (with 3 in place of 5).
- (2.32′) For a wall-connected component Z_i of Z₀ with Y-components Y_j ⊂ Z_i and D_i := D ∩ Z_i: Σ_jd_k(Y_j; D_j) + 18#_k(Z_i∖Y₀) ≥ d_k(Z_i; D_i). Proof: for each cube C ⊂ Z_i∖Y₀ take its main diagonal (length 2; its endpoints lie on all 8 walls); across each wall of C shared with a cube of Z_i join the diagonal's endpoint on that wall to the neighbour's graph (≤ 2 each; ≤ 8 walls). With the pieces' graphs G_j ∪ D_j this is a connected G meeting every cube of Z_i (distinct Y_j share no wall, so every wall-path between pieces passes through cubes of Z_i∖Y₀), and |G∖D_i| ≤ Σ_jMd_k(Y_j; D_j) + 18M#_k(Z_i∖Y₀).
  - *(2.32) as printed is false.* P.18: "By a simple geometric argument we have Σ_id_k(Y_i) + 4M^{−4}|Z₀∖Y₀| ≥ d_k(Z₀)". Counterexample (d = 4): one cube C ∈ Z₀∖Y₀ and eight rods Y₁, …, Y₈ of n ≥ 2 cubes each, one on each wall of C, pointing outward. The rods are distinct wall-components of Y₀ (two rods meet along a 2-face at most), d_k(rod) = n − 2, and d_k(Z₀) ≥ 8(n−1): the eight regions {x_μ > 1} ∩ Z₀ and {x_μ < 0} ∩ Z₀ are pairwise disjoint, and a connected graph meeting every cube of a rod has length ≥ n − 1 inside its region (the measure of its x_μ-projection). So d_k(Z₀) ≥ Σ_id_k(Y_i) + 8 > Σ_id_k(Y_i) + 4 (the main diagonal of C plus eight axis segments gives d_k(Z₀) ≤ 8(n−1) + 2; the exact value is not needed). The inequality holds with 18 (above; the sharp constant lies between 8 and 18). Its use, p.18's "(1/20)γ₂ε₁²/γ² ≥ 4κ", becomes "≥ 18κ", a condition on γ (on g_K with CRE's threshold, (a)); p.20's adaptation at scale k+1 ("(2.32), properly adapted") with "½(κ₁−1) ≥ 2Lκ" becomes ½(κ₁−1) ≥ 9Lκ, a condition on κ₁. Both are in the order of choices that (1)–(2) record (κ; κ₁ and M; the α's; ε₁; γ after ε₁, since γ is bounded by a function of ε₁, γ₂, κ — p.21 prints no order), so Lemma 3 holds as printed with these conditions. Added to R-L3 in (1).
- (2.36′) For Z_i, D_i as above, Z_i′ the π_{k+1}-hull of Z̃_i and D_i′ that of D̃_i: L·d_{k+1}(Z_i′; D_i′) ≤ (1 + 486/L)·d_k(Z_i; D_i) if D_i ≠ ∅. Proof: let G realise d_k(Z_i; D_i) (G ⊂ Z_i ⊂ Z_i′, G ∪ D_i connected, D_i ⊂ D_i′). A π_{k+1}-cube Q ⊂ Z_i′∖D_i′ that touches D_i′ is met at a point of Q ∩ D_i′, at no cost. One that does not touch D_i′ has dist_∞(Q, D_i′) ≥ LM, and D_i′ ⊇ D̃_i ⊇ {dist_∞(·, D_i) ≤ M}. Q contains a π_k-cube of Z̃_i (hull), which touches a π_k-cube □_Q ⊂ Z_i; so □_Q touches Q and dist_∞(□_Q, D_i) ≥ LM. G meets □_Q at a point x_Q ∉ D_i, and a segment from x_Q to Q inside □_Q costs ≤ 2M. The piece of G∖D_i containing x_Q has its closure on D_i (G ∪ D_i is connected), so its length ℓ is ≥ dist(x_Q, D_i) ≥ LM. Such a piece may branch (G is a Steiner-type minimiser), so it is covered by a closed walk around it, of length 2ℓ, cut into ⌈2ℓ/(LM)⌉ arcs of length ≤ LM; the M-neighbourhood of an arc has ∞-diameter ≤ LM + 2M < 2LM for L ≥ 3 and meets ≤ 3⁴ cubes; so the piece lies within ∞-distance M of at most 81(2ℓ/(LM) + 1) ≤ 243ℓ/(LM) cubes Q (ℓ ≥ LM; the closed walk is needed: a cover by 81⌈ℓ/(LM)⌉ doesn't work for a tripod with legs just over LM/2). So the stubs cost ≤ 2M·243|G∖D_i|/(LM) = (486/L)|G∖D_i|, and G′ := G ∪ stubs ∪ touching points meets every cube of Z_i′∖D_i′ with G′ ∪ D_i′ connected. (For the vacuum components Bałaban's (2.36), 2d_k(Z_i) ≥ Ld_{k+1}(Z_i′), is his and stays accepted; (2.36′) replaces it for the marked components only and agrees with it for L ≥ 486.)
- Coarse assembly (p.20's "(2.32), properly adapted", charged to parent edges). For Z = M connected, with the components Z_i of Z₀ (vacuum: D_i = ∅, n_v of them; marked: D_i ≠ ∅, m′ ≤ m of them), the pieces Π_i := G_i′ ∪ D_i′ (G_i′ realising d_{k+1}(Z_i′; D_i′); for vacuum pieces D_i′ = ∅) and the π_{k+1}-cubes of Z∖Z₀′: the touching graph is connected (Z is wall-connected and Z₀′ = ∪_iZ_i′). Root a spanning tree at a marked piece; each cube of Z∖Z₀′ pays its parent edge (≤ 3; its own point is the edge's endpoint), each vacuum piece its own (≤ 3), and the m′ − 1 other marked pieces' edges are unpaid: d_{k+1}(Z; D′) ≤ Σ_id_{k+1}(Z_i′; D_i′) + 3#_{k+1}(Z∖Z₀′) + 3(n_v + m′ − 1).
- Counting (replaces (2.30)/(2)(a) near D). For X wall-connected and ∅ ≠ D ⊂ X: #(X∖D) ≤ 80#(D) + 324·d(X; D), so #(X) ≤ 81#(D) + 324·d(X; D). Proof: a cube touching D is one of the ≤ 3⁴ − 1 = 80 neighbours of a cube of D; a cube not touching D is at ∞-distance ≥ a from D and is met by a piece of G∖D whose closure reaches D, so of length ℓ_p ≥ a; a piece may branch, so cover it by a closed walk of length 2ℓ_p cut into ⌈2ℓ_p/a⌉ arcs of length ≤ a, each of ∞-diameter ≤ a, meeting ≤ 3⁴ cubes: the piece meets ≤ 81(2ℓ_p/a + 2) ≤ 324ℓ_p/a cubes, and Σ_pℓ_p = a·d(X; D). (A bound 9ℓ/a per curve doesn't work: a segment of length a along a lattice edge meets 24 cubes.)

*(e) The resummation.* Bałaban's order (𝐃 with Y₀ fixed; Y₀, P with Z₀ fixed; Z₀ with Z₀′ fixed; Z₀′ ⊂ Z) pays the entropy of Z₀'s shape with the decay e^{−δκd_k(Z_i)} of each component. Near D there is no decay, so the entropy is paid instead by the smallness of the Y's and P-bonds that build Z₀ ∖ D: the decay is taken as a sup over the data and the entropy as an unconstrained sum. Fix M = Z connected (for several components the bound is the product of the components' bounds with ‖𝒳_M‖_sup once: the per-term bound (2.26) is a product over the components of M, each meeting D, and Σ_j(m_j − 1) ≤ m − 1). Split each Y-factor of (c) as [α₆e^{−δκd_k(Y)}]·[ε₂e^{−(1−4δ)κd_k(Y)}], and each P-factor as on p.18: e^{−½γ₂(ε₁²/g_k²)|P|} ≤ e^{−(1/10)γ₂(ε₁²/γ²)|P|}·e^{−(1/5)γ₂(ε₁²/γ²)#_k(Z₀∖Y₀)} (|P| ≥ ½#_k(Z₀∖Y₀), p.18; g_k ≤ γ), the second factor being four twentieths: e^{−18κ#_k(Z₀∖Y₀)} (one twentieth, (1/20)γ₂ε₁²/γ² ≥ 18κ), and three spare twentieths e^{−(1/20)γ₂(ε₁²/γ²)#_k(Z₀∖Y₀)} each, each ≤ ε₂^{#_k(Z₀∖Y₀)} by p.19's e^{−γ₂ε₁²/(20γ²)} ≤ ε₂.
- *Decay part of a term:* ∏_{Y∈𝐃}ε₂e^{−(1−4δ)κd_k(Y)}·e^{−18κ#_k(Z₀∖Y₀)}·e^{−(κ₁−1)#_{k+1}(Z∖Z₀′)}·(one spare twentieth per cube of Z₀∖Y₀).
  - In each Y-component Y_i, by (2.27′): root the piece tree at a component of D_i if D_i ≠ ∅, else at a Y. Each non-root Y pays its parent edge, ε₂e^{5(1−4δ)κ} ≤ ε₂e^{5κ} ≤ 1, with e^{−20δκ} to spare. So a marked Y-component gives e^{5κ(m_i−1)}e^{−(1−4δ)κd_k(Y_i;D_i)} (all its Y's pay; the m_i − 1 edges of the other D-components are unpaid), and a vacuum one gives ε₂e^{−(1−4δ)κd_k(Y_i)} ((2.28), one ε₂ survives).
  - In each component Z_i of Z₀, by (2.32′): every cube of Z₀∖Y₀ pays its 18 at rate (1−4δ)κ ≤ 18κ. A vacuum Z_i keeps a factor ≤ ε₂ (from a vacuum Y-component, or one spare twentieth of a P-cube if it has no Y). So Z_i gives e^{5κ(m_i−1)}e^{−(1−4δ)κd_k(Z_i;D_i)} if marked, and ε₂e^{−(1−4δ)κd_k(Z_i)} if vacuum.
  - To scale k+1: by (2.36) for vacuum and (2.36′) for marked components, (1−4δ)κd_k(Z_i; D_i) ≥ (1−4δ)λ_Lκd_{k+1}(Z_i′; D_i′), since λ_L ≤ L/2 and λ_L ≤ L/(1 + 486/L).
  - Coarse assembly: each vacuum piece pays its parent edge with its ε₂, ε₂e^{3(1−4δ)λ_Lκ} ≤ 1; each cube of Z∖Z₀′ pays its edge, 3(1−4δ)λ_Lκ ≤ ½(κ₁−1), from half of its factor, with e^{−Lκ/2} to spare (½(κ₁−1) ≥ 2Lκ, λ_L ≤ L/2); the m′ − 1 unpaid marked edges cost e^{3(1−4δ)λ_Lκ} each. With Σ_i(m_i − 1) ≤ m − m′ over the marked Y-components (each marked Z_i has one at least):
  decay ≤ e^{(5+3λ_L)κ(m−1)}·e^{−(1−4δ)λ_Lκ·d_{k+1}(Z; D′)}·e^{−½(κ₁−1)#_{k+1}(Z∖Z₀′)}.
- *Entropy part of a term:* ∏_{Y∈𝐃}α₆e^{−δκd_k(Y)}·e^{−(1/10)γ₂(ε₁²/γ²)|P|} (the remaining spare twentieths ≤ 1). Summed over all subfamilies 𝐃 ⊂ 𝐃_k of domains Y ⊂ Z₀′ and all bond sets P in Z₀′, every constraint dropped ((𝐃, P) determines Z₀ and Z₀′, so the constrained sum is smaller): ≤ ∏_{Y⊂Z₀′}(1 + α₆e^{−δκd_k(Y)})·∏_{b⊂Z₀′}(1 + e^{−γ₂ε₁²/(10γ²)}) ≤ exp[(O_{(1.26)}α₆ + 4M⁴e^{−γ₂ε₁²/(10γ²)})#_k(Z₀′)] (each Y contains a cube of Z₀′ and (1.26) holds at rate δκ ≥ κ_{(1.26)}, R-L3; 4M⁴ bonds of T^{(k)} per π_k-cube). This needs neither (2.29) nor (2.34), nor the sums of pp.19–20 over the components Z_i and the families Z_i′.
- *Fixed Z₀′ ⊂ Z:* Σ_{(𝐃,P): Z₀′(𝐃,P)=Z₀′}|term| ≤ ‖𝒳_M‖_sup·e^{O(1)α₅|Z|}·(entropy sum)·sup(decay) ≤ ‖𝒳_M‖_sup·e^{(O(1)(LM)⁴α₅ + L⁴B₂)#_{k+1}(Z)}·e^{(5+3λ_L)κ(m−1)}e^{−(1−4δ)λ_Lκd_{k+1}(Z;D′)}e^{−½(κ₁−1)#_{k+1}(Z∖Z₀′)}, B₂ := O_{(1.26)}α₆ + 4M⁴e^{−γ₂ε₁²/(10γ²)}, using #_k(Z₀′) ≤ L⁴#_{k+1}(Z).
- *Sum over Z₀′ ⊂ Z* (constraints dropped): Σ_{Z₀′}e^{−½(κ₁−1)#_{k+1}(Z∖Z₀′)} ≤ (1 + e^{−½(κ₁−1)})^{#_{k+1}(Z)} ≤ e^{e^{−½(κ₁−1)}#_{k+1}(Z)}. Total per π_{k+1}-cube of Z: e^{A}, A as in (b).
- *Absorption:* by the counting inequality at scale k+1 (D′ ≠ ∅), e^{A#_{k+1}(Z)} ≤ e^{81A#_{k+1}(D′)}e^{324A·d_{k+1}(Z;D′)} ≤ e^{81A#_{k+1}(D′)}e^{δλ_Lκ·d_{k+1}(Z;D′)} once 324A ≤ δλ_Lκ. This is (b), with the rate (1−5δ)λ_Lκ.
- *Smallness ϑ:* every Y of 𝐃 leaves e^{−20δκ} (a Y ⊂ D needs no join and leaves ε₂), every P-cube a spare twentieth e^{−γ₂ε₁²/(20γ²)}, every cube of Z∖Z₀′ e^{−Lκ/2}. The only term without a Y, a P-bond or a cube of Z∖Z₀′ is (𝐃, P, Z₀, Z) = (∅, ∅, D, D′), the conditioned expectation of 𝒳_M. With δκ ≥ κ/30 (R-L3), e^{−γ₂ε₁²/(20γ²)} ≤ ε₂ ≤ e^{−5κ} and L ≥ 3, ϑ ≤ e^{−2κ/3}.

*(f) What Step 1 gives and leaves.*
- Gives: (b) for every marked polymer, with absolute constants, uniform in k and on the complex domain, and the structure "plain expectation + O(ϑ)" at M = D′, O(ϑ) with decay for M ≠ D′. The slot factor enters through ‖𝒳_M‖_sup only. No decay inside or across D′ is claimed. The cost of D′ is e^{81A} per π_{k+1}-cube, A small at will, and e^{(5+3λ_L)κ} per component of D beyond the first (a constant per slot-component, to be paid by the slot's own size, D2.0(5)(c)). (7)'s three caveats stand as stated: C₃′ is not small; the decay across D′ must come from ‖𝒳_M‖_sup, that is, from the old norms and the slot sizes of D2.1; CRE p.277 is not used.
- Leaves, for steps 2–4 of (S): (2) ‖𝒳_M‖_sup from D2.1 and D2.2(4)'s slot sizes and the old norms with their e^{θ_k}-weights, and the count check that e^{81A} per π_{k+1}-cube of D′ and e^{(5+3λ_L)κ} per extra component of D are paid by the slots' own sizes (Step 2: (11)); (3) the entropy of the linked families (FV Thm 5.4) and the sum over M ∋ n′ with t(M) = t, with (b)'s decay at rate r per unit of d_{k+1}(M; D′) ≥ (#_{k+1}(M) − 81#_{k+1}(D′))/324; (4) the comparison of r with (31/8)η_V/(C_boxM) per step-(k+1) unit of R_t. *(3) and (4) are (12)–(13): the comparison is replaced by the extent weight ν_e = η_V/(2C_box) of (12)(b), and r, κ_s are spent on the entropy.* For L < 486 the rate is λ_Lκ < ½Lκ; whether the contracted (2.36) holds with Bałaban's factor 2 for every L is a geometric question, needed only if step 4 fails with λ_L. Components of M meeting Λ_{k+1}ᶜ are D2.6's. The reference activities c_𝒢 ((7)(R)) obey the same bound at the reference point.

### (11) Step 2 of (S): the slot sizes ‖𝒳_M‖_sup and the count check (derived from D2.1(4), D2.2(4)(e)–(g), (5)(a), (6)(c)–(e), D2.0(3), D2.4(3)–(5), RG2 pp.9–15, CRE pp.265, 267–268, 276–277; reviewed: no fatal finding; repairs not re-reviewed)

*(a) The slots.* By D2.2(5)(a), 𝒳_M = ⊗_{i∈J(M)}Y_{G_i}, the new insertions of the groups G_i of D2.2(4)(e) whose slots M carries; the untouched old insertions are not in 𝒳 (they do not depend on A). Each G_i is a set of s-components Y (connected unions of π_k-cubes, D2.1(4) *Setting*; the σ_k of RG2's example is π_k, D2.2(5)(c)) with the old insertions W whose intervals lie in I(G_i) or whose domains X_W (or blocks, for ω-dependent W) meet a component (D2.2(4)(e) *Arcs and new groups*). Three kinds, by components: (α) *pure loop groups*, no old insertion, every component in Ω_k (case (A) of D2.1(4)); (β) *groups with old insertions*, every component in Ω_k; (γ) groups with a component meeting Ω_kᶜ (case (B)), whose sizes are D2.6's and are not bounded here. Put n(G) := the number of components of G, #(G) := Σ_{Y∈G}#(Y) π_k-cubes, and D_i := the union of the π_k-cubes containing in their interior every bond on which Y_{G_i} depends, so that D = ∪_iD_i. Y_{G_i} depends on B through B′ on the components of G_i (D2.1(4) *Locality*; D2.2(4)(e) *Locality*), and B′|_Y depends on B within the range of RG2 (1.19)'s operators C and hD̃ about Y (D2.1(4)(b)); so D_i ⊂ (∪_{Y∈G_i}Y)^{∼c_B}, c_B the number of π_k-cubes of that range, and #_k(D_i) ≤ (2c_B+1)⁴#(G_i) =: c_D#(G_i). c_B must not depend on M, since κ_s is chosen before M ((d)); it does not: the range of (1.19)'s C and hD̃ is the range of RG1's step-k operators as localised by R-loc (D2.1(4)(b)), fixed with L and R-loc's convention before M, so c_B ≤ 1 and c_D ≤ 81 once M exceeds that range. The wall-components of D number m(M) ≤ #_k(D) ≤ c_D#(G_M) (D need not have one component per s-component); n(M) := Σ_in(G_i). The domains X_W of the touched old insertions are not part of D: Y_{G_i} depends on B through B′ on the components and through 𝐇(s, B′), which vanishes off the components (D2.2(4)(e)); D2.2(6)(d) states the same, and the touched W are counted inside the M-vertex in (12)(c).

*(b) The threshold on the support (derived; units of CRE (3.16)).* ‖𝒳_M‖_sup is a sup over real B on the support of χ_{k,Y₀}, which is CRE (3.16)'s test on the bonds of D ((10)(a); D2.2(5)(c)). In the variable B of RG2 (2.14), the Gaussian variable of CRE (3.27) after p.267's scaling A_k′ = g_kA_k (p.267 l.44; A_k′ = (1/i)log V_k′, l.30), that test is g_k|B(b)| < δ_k, with δ_k = (A₁/A₀)g_kp₀(g_k) = A₁g_k(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀} = (A₁/A₀)ε_k (p.265 prints "δ_k = g_kA₁/A₀p₀(g_k)"; (3.19) uses ε_k = (A₀/A₁)δ_k): p.268 prints "sup|A_k(b)| < δ_k", read as the phase A_k′ = g_kA_k (the phase reading). P.268's use of it, "The first [(3.3)'s test, |V_k(V^{(k)}_{□′})^{−1} − 1| < 2δ_k on group elements, p.265] is equal to 1, because of the bound on A_k introduced by the second, and the fact that V^{(k)}(V^{(k)}_{□′})^{−1} − 1 is small, much smaller than δ_k", holds under both readings (under the scaled reading |g_kA_k| < g_kδ_k ≪ δ_k passes the test too), so it does not force the phase reading. Step 2 uses only g_k|B| < δ_k, i.e. |B| < r_k := δ_k/g_k = A₁(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀}, which either reading implies (the scaled reading gives a smaller support), so (b)–(f) hold under both. The phase reading is the one consistent with RG2's own pattern ((2.3)'s threshold ε₁/g_k on the scaled variable; (2.22), (2.31)) and with D2.0(5)(f)'s p_k ≤ e^{−c(log g_k^{−2})^{2p₀}}, the Gaussian tail at the threshold r_k that ρ_k = C_ρg_k²(1+r_k)⁴ records. So on the support, for every component Y and its neighbourhood: g_k|B|_Y < δ_k, |B′|_Y ≤ C₁^{RG2}g_k|B|_Y < C₁^{RG2}δ_k =: b_k (RG2 (1.20), valid since δ_k < ε₁^{RG2} for g_K small), and x_k := δ_k/ε₁^{RG2} ≤ 1/2 bounds g_k|B|_Y/ε₁^{RG2}. All three are O(g_k(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀}), uniformly in k ≤ K for g_K small (g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K, CRE (2.6), R-cpl₀). This agrees with D2.1(4)'s *The hypothesis* (|B′| < O(1)ε_k from (3.14)) and sharpens its constant.

*(c) The per-slot bounds (derived, at the complex data of (7)'s domain).* Let the background be 𝐔 = e^{iηa′}U_{k+1} in D(k+1)(iii)'s domain, C a square (the target's C), and assume that the hull I(G) with its allowance lies in Ω_k, where D(k+1)(iii)'s radius is ≤ Lα_{1,k} ((4)(f); on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} with n < k it is α_{1,n}L^{k−n}, not small), and that 20c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 there (D2.2(6)(d) *Arcs*; true for g_K small since α_{1,k} = g_kC₁(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁} → 0 with L, M fixed, and implied by (12)(d)'s c_πα_{1,k+1}·41M/L ≤ ν_r/16); groups whose hull leaves Ω_k are older-layer groups ((5) *Older layers*), left with the kind (γ).
- (α) Pure loop groups: D2.1(4)'s Claim with the merged-arc constant, ‖Y_G(B)‖ ≤ ∏_{Y∈G}(|B′|_Y/ε_H)e^{−(κ_s−3)#(Y)} pointwise in B, hence ≤ (b_k/ε_H)^{n(G)}e^{−(κ_s−3)#(G)} on the support. The factor per component is the Schwarz factor 1/T_Y with T_Y = ε_H/|B′|_Y; the decay per cube is Cauchy's e^{−(κ_s−1)} in s(Δ) less e^{1} for the 𝐇-transports (|B′|_Y ≤ ε_H, c_πc_CM4B₀e^{16κ_s}ε_H ≤ 1) and e^{1} for the a′-transports (20c_πM‖a′‖_∞·length ≤ #(G) + n(G) by D2.2(6)(d), absorbed as e^{−(κ_s−3)#(Y)}·e^{n(G)}, the e^{n(G)} going into the per-component constant below).
- (β) Groups with old insertions (D2.2(4)(e)–(f), (6)(d)–(e)): Y_G is the s-derivative of the ordered product along I(G) of the touched old insertions W ∈ 𝔅(G) and the transports, and the Schwarz lemma in t_Y on the polydisc |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s}, |t_Y| ≤ T_Y gives ‖Y_G‖ ≤ ∏_{Y∈G}(1/T_Y)e^{−(κ_s−3)#(Y)}·∏_{W∈𝔅(G)}sup_{polydisc}‖Y_W‖, with 1/T_Y = max(|B′|_Y/ε_H, 16B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′|_Y/(min(β,1)α_{1,k}), C′B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′|_Y/(min(β,1)α_{0,k})) ≤ C_Tb_k/ᾱ_k ((4)(f) *T_Y*; (6)(e) *The curvature conditions*; ᾱ_k = min(α_{0,k}, α_{1,k}), C_T := max(1/ε_H, 16B₀e^{16κ_s}/min(β,1), C′B₀e^{16κ_s}/min(β,1))), and the 𝐇-transports as in (α) (𝐇 vanishes off the components). The a′-transports differ from (α): I(G) is the smallest arc containing the components' bonds and the intervals of the touched W, so its gaps reach the touched intervals inside X_W and have length up to 2(diam Y + diam X_W); by D2.2(6)(d) *Arcs*, length(I(G)) ≤ 20Σ(diam + 1) over the components and the touched W, so under 20c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 the a′-cost is ≤ exp(#(G) + n(G) + Σ_{W∈𝔅(G)}20c_πM‖a′‖_∞(diam X_W + 1)) ≤ e^{#(G)+n(G)}·∏_{W∈𝔅(G)}e^{(ν_r/16)σ_k(W)} once c_πα_{1,k+1}·20M/L ≤ ν_r/16 (diam X_W ≤ (M/L)σ_k(W), (12)(d)); the factor e^{(ν_r/16)σ_k(W)} is paid from W's reserve weight ν_rσ_k(W) inside (ii-w) ((12)(b); the budget of (12)(e)), never from e^{θ_k(W)}, which D2.4(3) reserves for the Q_k-shares. The polydisc points e^{iη(𝐇(s,tB′) + a″)}U_{k+1} lie in D(k)(iii)'s domain by (4)(f)'s chain condition (with R-m, R-dec, R-adm on the older layers, (6)(e)); they are general complex configurations off the frame family, so D(k)(iii) applies there in its covariant form (*The ω-clause at complex configurations*): each touched W is read at the frame point ρ_k(V_k), whose B′-dependent change of representative ŵ goes through (4)(c)'s budget as w does, while the B′-independent c₀ of the configuration rotates the slot values of the clause and is absorbed by the covariant clause ((6)(b7), *G^c-valued u*), never charged to (4)(c)'s budget ((4)(d), (d′) Step 8); so each touched W is bounded by D(k)(iii)'s complex-data clause — the inductive input of (4) *Complex data*, (5) and (9), derived on the sub-domain (SW closure §6): for a dev W, e^{θ_k(W)}‖Y_W‖ ≤ b_W(Dev_{Q^#_W} + a*_W + j*_W) ≤ β_W(Δ^{(k)}_W + C_ah_kη_k^{−2}·… ) in the notation of (5), and by (5) *At complex data* its size is ρ̃_k·O(ᾱ_k(1 + (log g_k^{−2})^{(q₁−q₀)₊})) = O(ρ_k(1 + (log g_k^{−2})^{(q₁−q₀)₊})) per insertion, away from older layers ((5) *Older layers*); att/lw/wrap species are D2.6's. So a group of kind (β) is bounded by (C_Tb_k/ᾱ_k)^{n(G)}e^{−(κ_s−3)#(G)}·∏_{W∈𝔅(G)}e^{−θ_k(W)}·[e^{θ_k(W)}‖Y_W‖]_{polydisc}, and the old weights e^{θ_k(W)} are not consumed (D2.4(3)).
- The sup. ‖𝒳_M‖_sup ≤ ∏_{i∈J(M)}(bound of G_i). Every factor above is uniform in k and on the domain.

*(d) The count check for the exponential costs (derived).* (10)(b) charges e^{81A} per π_{k+1}-cube of D′ and e^{(5+3λ_L)κ} per wall-component of D beyond the first, and nothing else inside D′. By (a), #_{k+1}(D′) ≤ 81#_k(D) ≤ 81c_D#(G_M), #(G_M) := Σ_i#(G_i), and m(M) − 1 ≤ c_D#(G_M) ((a)). The slots pay e^{−(κ_s−3)#(G_M)} and the e^{n(G)} of (c). The 𝐃 ≠ ∅ odd terms of (f) cost e^{243A#_{k+1}(D′)} in place of e^{81A#_{k+1}(D′)} (3A in (10)'s counting), so
  e^{243A#_{k+1}(D′)}e^{(5+3λ_L)κ(m(M)−1)}·∏_ie^{n(G_i)}e^{−(κ_s−3)#(G_i)} ≤ e^{−κ_s′#(G_M)}, κ_s′ := κ_s − 4 − (5+3λ_L)c_Dκ − 19683c_DA,
and κ_s′ ≥ κ_s/2 once κ_s ≥ 8 + 2(5+3λ_L)c_Dκ + 39366c_DA. With λ_L ≤ L/2, c_D ≤ 81 ((a)) and (10)(b)'s 324A ≤ δλ_Lκ (an a-priori bound on A, which is fixed after M) this is a condition κ_s ≥ 81(10 + 3L)κ + O(κ) on RG2's κ_s, which is chosen after κ and before M (δ₁M ≥ κ_s, D2.1(4) *Conditions used*), so it is in Bałaban's order of choices. The remaining decay e^{−(κ_s/2)#(G_M)} is per π_k-cube of the components; per π_{k+1}-cube of D′ it is at least e^{−κ_s/(162c_D)}. This is what Step 3 spends on the entropy of the components and Step 4 compares with w_t over the extent of D′.

*(e) The size check in powers of g (the count-check of the amplitudes).* (S) needs S_𝒢 of size ρ_{k+1} = C_ρg_{k+1}²(1+r_{k+1})⁴, and ρ_k ≤ (1+β₀)⁶ρ_{k+1} (D2.0(3)). From (b)–(c), per component the sup factor is b_k/ε_H or C_Tb_k/ᾱ_k, both O(g_k(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀}) resp. O((log g_k^{−2})^{p₀−q}) with q = min(q₀,q₁); per touched old insertion it is O(ρ_k(log)^{(q₁−q₀)₊}). So:
- a slot of kind (β) is O(ρ_k·(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀−q+(q₁−q₀)₊}) at least, which is ≤ Cρ_{k+1} when q ≥ p₀ + (q₁−q₀)₊, i.e. (with R-m's q₀, q₁ ≥ p₀) iff q₁ ≤ q₀ or 2q₀ ≥ p₀ + q₁; with (6)(ζ)'s entailment q₀ ≤ q₁ the condition is 2q₀ ≥ p₀ + q₁, and otherwise a power of log of the kind recorded in (5) *At complex data* and (9)'s polylog item remains. The amplitude of a (β) slot is the old insertions', not a new ρ_{k+1}: (12)(f) carries this as a recursion in k;
- a pure loop slot with n(G) ≥ 2 is O(b_k²) = O(g_k²(log g_k^{−2})^{2p₀}) ≤ (eC₁^{RG2}/ε_H)²ρ_k/C_ρ, since δ_k² = g_k²r_k² ≤ ρ_k/C_ρ;
- a marked polymer with two or more slots is a product of such factors, each O(b_k) or O(ρ_k·polylog) and ≤ 1, two of them O(b_k) at least;
- **the single-slot, single-component pure loop polymers** (|J(M)| = 1, G = {Y}, kind (α)) have ‖𝒳_M‖_sup = O(b_k) = O(g_k(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀}) only, which is not O(ρ_{k+1}): a factor 1/g_k short. These are the first-order fluctuations of the leading word (D2.1(3) *The first-order term*: linear in A_k, odd), which D2.0(5)(d) treats as zero-mean at first order and of size g_k² at second order. Within (10) the sup bound cannot see this. The parity argument that recovers it is (f).

*(f) Parity: the single-component slots (derived).* *Scope.* ω′ = 0, which is D2.4(4)'s scope for the dev groups: at ω′ ≠ 0 the action of CRE (3.27) contains the source term −⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}Cσ_k⟩ (D2.2(5)(b), (5)(e), (6)(b6)), linear hence odd in the integrated field, and the argument below fails; such polymers are att (D2.6). And Y₀ away from older layers: there the potentials of (3.27)'s localised action are the terms of 𝐄_k and of 𝐑_k, which have Lemma 2's structure ((1.42), (1.43), (1.36), (2.18)) — for 𝐑_k by CRE p.276, "The expression determined by 𝐑_k is treated in exactly the same way as 𝐄_k, except the second renormalization connected with the coupling constant renormalization counterterm, which is not needed here", with the analyticity space (I.6.34) for Y ⊂ Λ_{k+1} (p.276): Bałaban's, accepted as in R-vac(i′). The boundary-type terms — 𝐁_k, A(g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2}, ·) and V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}) — are analytic only on the space (3.43) (|A| < C₁p₁(g_k), a polylog radius in the scaled field, so no Cauchy gain) and carry "the additional factor O(ε_k)" (CRE p.277); they are localised at Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, Z_k and S_{k+1} (p.277: "supp(g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2}) ⊂ Z_k", "at least one localization in S_{k+1}"), and belong to (5)'s older-layer item (CRE pp.276–277 read). Fix M with J(M) = {i}, G_i = {Y}, Y ⊂ Ω_k, no old insertion. Split Y_G(B) = Y_G^{ev}(B) + Y_G^{od}(B) into its even and odd parts under B ↦ −B on the support (the support is symmetric: sup-tests).
- *The even part is O(δ_k²).* In the Claim's proof, t ↦ Y_G(tB′) is analytic for |t| ≤ T_Y = ε_H/|B′|_Y ≥ 2 with sup ≤ S_Y := e^{−(κ_s−3)#(Y)} (the polydisc bound), and Y_G(0) = 0, so Y_G(B′) = L(B′) + R with L linear in B′ (the s|_Y-derivative of D2.1(4) *First order*), ‖L(B′)‖ ≤ S_Y|B′|_Y/ε_H, and ‖R‖ ≤ S_YΣ_{n≥2}T_Y^{−n} ≤ 2S_Y(|B′|_Y/ε_H)². In B, B′ = B′_od + B′_ev with B′_od the odd part: by RG2 (1.19), B′ = g_kCB − hD̃(g_kCB) with C the linear operator of RG1 ("B′ = CB", D2.2(6)(b) *The operator C and B′*), so g_kCB is odd and B′_ev = −[hD̃(g_kCB)]_ev; hD̃(·) is analytic for g_k|B| < ε₁^{RG2}, vanishes to second order at 0 (RG1 p.267: D̃ "has an expansion beginning with quadratic terms"; only h is linear) and is bounded there by (C₁^{RG2} + O(1))ε₁^{RG2} ((1.20)), so Cauchy's remainder estimate gives |hD̃(g_kCB)|_Y ≤ 2(C₁^{RG2} + O(1))x_k²ε₁^{RG2} = C_evδ_k² for x_k ≤ 1/2, C_ev := 2(C₁^{RG2} + O(1))/ε₁^{RG2} (the C here is (1.19)'s linear operator, not (1.3)'s nonlinear C). Hence |B′_ev|_Y ≤ C_evδ_k². Hence ‖Y_G^{ev}‖ ≤ ‖L(B′_ev)‖ + ‖R‖ ≤ S_Y(C_ev/ε_H + 2(C₁^{RG2}/ε_H)²)δ_k² =: c_evδ_k²S_Y on the support, and ‖Y_G^{od}‖ ≤ (C₁^{RG2}δ_k/ε_H + c_evδ_k²)S_Y. δ_k² ≤ (A₁²/C_ρ)ρ_k ≤ (A₁²(1+β₀)⁶/C_ρ)ρ_{k+1}: the even part is ρ_{k+1}-size.
- *The odd part vanishes in the plain term.* The term (𝐃, P, Z₀, Z) = (∅, ∅, D, D′) of (10)(b) is (2.14) with 𝐃 = P = ∅: ∫dμ₀(X)|_Ze^{−½⟨Γ_kX, C^{(k)}Γ_kX⟩}∫dμ_{C^{(k)}(Z₀,σ(Z))}(B)e^{−⟨B,Γ_kX⟩}χ_{k,Y₀}(B)𝒳_M(B) (RG2 (2.14), p.15; here Z = Z₀′ so no s, σ). The joint density in (X, B) is exp of a quadratic form (two centred Gaussian densities and the bilinear coupling e^{−⟨B,Γ_kX⟩}; a quadratic form is even whether or not its operators are symmetric — RG2 p.15 says they are not), even under (X, B) ↦ (−X, −B); χ_{k,Y₀} is even; 𝒳_M = Y_G depends on B only. So the integral of Y_G^{od} vanishes, and the plain term is bounded by ‖Y_G^{ev}‖_sup·e^{A#_{k+1}(D′)} ≤ c_evδ_k²S_Ye^{A#_{k+1}(D′)}.
- *The odd part in the other terms.* For (𝐃, P, Z₀, Z) with 𝐃 = ∅ the same symmetry kills Y_G^{od}: χ^c_{k,P} is even, and the s, σ, τ-structure of (2.14) does not touch B's sign. For 𝐃 ≠ ∅ the only non-even factor is exp[Σ_{Y′∈𝐃}τ(Y′)𝐕_k(Y′,B)], and with y(B) := Σ_{Y′}τ(Y′)(𝐕_k(Y′,B) − 𝐕_k(Y′,−B)) its odd part is e^{ΣτV(B)}·½(1 − e^{−y}), |½(1 − e^{−y})| ≤ |y|e^{|y|}. By (1.42), 𝐕_k(Y′,B) = ½⟨Q(Y′,B)B,B⟩ + 𝐕″_k(Y′,B).
  - The 𝐕″_k-part (derived). 𝐕″_k(Y′, ·) is analytic on (1.34)'s disc |B| < ε₁g_k^{−1} on Y′ with |𝐕″_k| ≤ E_{Y′} := E₀ε₁C₁M^qe^{C₂κ₁}e^{−(1−2δ)κd_k(Y′)} there (RG2 p.11: "satisfies the bound (1.36)"). Cauchy's estimate on t ↦ 𝐕″_k(Y′, tB), |t| < ε₁/(g_k|B|_{Y′}), bounds its homogeneous part of degree n by E_{Y′}(g_k|B|_{Y′}/ε₁)^n, so |𝐕″_k(Y′,B) − 𝐕″_k(Y′,−B)| ≤ 2E_{Y′}Σ_{n odd}x_k^n ≤ 4E_{Y′}x_k on the support (x_k ≤ 1/2, (b)). With Bałaban's radius |τ(Y′)| = 2e^{(1−3δ)κd_k(Y′)}/(ε₂α₆) (read off (2.18), p.16, and (2.26), p.17: the factor 2/|τ(Y′)| of (2.15) becomes ε₂α₆e^{−(1−3δ)κd_k(Y′)}; ε₂ is defined on p.19), |τ(Y′)|E_{Y′} = α₄e^{−δκd_k(Y′)} (ε₂α₆ = 2E₀ε₁C₁α₄^{−1}M^qe^{C₂κ₁}, p.19). So the 𝐕″-part of |y| is ≤ 4x_kΣ_{Y′∈𝐃}α₄e^{−δκd_k(Y′)} ≤ 4x_k·O(1)α₄#_k(Y₀) by (2.20)'s second line with (1.26) (each Y′ contains a cube of Y₀; 𝐃 may overlap, so |𝐃| is not bounded by #_k(Y₀)). This part is of the same form as the quadratic-form part below and is carried with it (the constant y_k).
  - The quadratic-form part (derived from RG2 pp.10–11 and 16–17). Q(Y′,B) depends on B: it is the Taylor-remainder coefficient (1.40), ∫₀¹dt(1−t)∂²_{B′_μ(x)B′_ν(y)}[(1.38) at tB′], composed with B′ = B′(B) of (1.19) (p.10: "we expand (1.38) with respect to B′ up to the second order. The terms of zeroth and first order vanish, and the second order term is written as a quadratic form with coefficients given by second order derivatives"; the same for all terms of 𝐏^{(k)}, p.11, "possibly with other absolute constants"). So Q(Y′, ·) is analytic on (1.34)'s disc, as 𝐕_k is (Lemma 2; (1.38) is analytic in B′ for e^{16κ₁}|B′| ≤ a₁ on Y, p.10, and (1.19) is analytic in B), and its matrix elements obey (1.43) there. Bałaban's (2.18)–(2.19), p.16: "The quadratic form in (1.42), after multiplication by |τ(Y)|, can be bounded by ½Σ_{b,b′⊂Y}α₄M^{−4}exp(−¼(κ₁−1)M^{−4}|Y| − (1/16)(κ₁−1)M^{−1}|b_− − b_−′|)|B(b)||B(b′)|", i.e. |τ(Y′)||Q(Y′,B,b,b′)| ≤ α₄M^{−4}q̂_{Y′}(b,b′) with q̂ the printed exponential, uniformly in B on the disc (the bound comes from (1.43), which Lemma 2 states on (1.34)); and (2.20): the sum over Y ∈ 𝐃 of these forms is ≤ ½O(1)α₄Σ_{b⊂Y₀}|B(b)|², "In fact the constant is small for κ₁ large, hence we can bound it by 1". Cauchy's estimate on t ↦ Q(Y′,tB)_{bb′}, |t| ≤ ε₁/(2g_k|B|_{Y′}) (inside the disc), gives |τ(Y′)||Q(Y′,B) − Q(Y′,−B)|_{bb′} ≤ 2α₄M^{−4}q̂_{Y′}(b,b′)Σ_{n odd}(2x_k)^n ≤ 8x_kα₄M^{−4}q̂_{Y′}(b,b′) for x_k ≤ 1/4. The odd part of ½⟨Q(Y′,B)B,B⟩ under B ↦ −B is ½⟨(Q(B) − Q(−B))B,B⟩ (the form ⟨·B,B⟩ is even), so by (2.19)–(2.20) the quadratic-form part of |y| is ≤ 8x_k·½O(1)α₄Σ_{b⊂Y₀}|B(b)|² ≤ 8x_kα₄·4M⁴r_k²#_k(Y₀) on the support (|B(b)| < r_k = δ_k/g_k, (b)). With the 𝐕″-part, |y| ≤ y_k#_k(Y₀), y_k := 4α₄x_k(8M⁴r_k² + O(1)) = O(g_k(log g_k^{−2})^{3p₀}), uniformly in k ≤ K for g_K small. So the factor |y|e^{|y|} of the odd part is ≤ y_k#_k(Y₀)e^{y_k#_k(Y₀)} ≤ y_k(L⁴/A)e^{2A#_{k+1}(Z)} once y_k ≤ A/L⁴ (g_K small), B-independent, and (2.15)–(2.26) go through for the odd part with this constant in front: every term with 𝐃 ≠ ∅ carries, besides (10)(c)'s bound with ‖Y_G^{od}‖_sup, the factor c_ody_ke^{2A#_{k+1}(Z)}, c_od := L⁴/A. Its absorption in (10)(e) is the counting inequality with 3A in place of A: condition 972A ≤ δλ_Lκ. The product ‖Y_G^{od}‖_sup·c_ody_k ≤ (C₁^{RG2}/ε_H + c_evδ_k)δ_k·(L⁴/A)4α₄(8M⁴r_k² + O(1))δ_k/ε₁^{RG2}·S_Y = O(g_k²r_k⁴)S_Y ≤ c_odd′ρ_kS_Y (g_k²r_k⁴ ≤ ρ_k/C_ρ): ρ_{k+1}-size. (The factor r_k² = δ_k²/g_k² is a polylog, r_k = A₁(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀}, and the (1+r_k)⁴ of ρ_k is exactly the room for it.)
- *Consequence for (S) (derived).* Every marked polymer of kinds (α)–(β) has |H̃(M)| ≤ c_Mρ_{k+1}·(the polylog factor of (e)'s first bullet, absent iff 2q₀ ≥ p₀ + q₁; for kind (β) the amplitude is the touched insertions', (e), carried by (12)(f))·e^{−(κ_s/2)#(G_M)}·e^{−r·d_{k+1}(M;D′)}·e^{(5+3λ_L)κ·0} with the costs of (d) already paid, c_M depending on A₁, C₁^{RG2}, ε_H, C_T, c_ev, c_od, α₄, M, L, A and Bałaban's constants only, uniformly in k ≤ K for g_K small; for the single-component slots this is the sum of the plain term (even part, c_evδ_k²S_Ye^{A#_{k+1}(D′)}) and of the O(ϑ) terms (even part with (10)(b)'s factors; odd part with the extra c_ody_k). The sum over polymers is Step 3's.

*(g) What Step 2 gives and leaves.*
- Gives: the per-slot bounds (c) at complex data; the threshold (b) with its units; the exponential count (d), which costs only a condition on κ_s; the amplitude count (e): all slots but the single-component pure loop slots are ρ_{k+1}-size in sup norm; for those, (f) by parity, with RG2 (2.18)–(2.20) as the bound on the quadratic form. So every marked polymer of kinds (α)–(β) is ρ_{k+1}-size with the decay of (d) and (10)(b), uniformly in k.
- Leaves: D2.6's kinds (γ) and species (including the ω′ ≠ 0 polymers, where (f)'s parity fails); (5)'s older-layer complex-data size, including the arcs that leave Ω_k ((c)) and CRE's boundary-type potentials ((f) *Scope*); the polylog factor unless 2q₀ ≥ p₀ + q₁; D(k)(iii)'s complex-data dev clause for the touched insertions ((4) *Complex data*, (5), (9)), on which (c)(β) rests; then Step 3 (entropy of linked families, FV Thm 5.4, the sum over M ∋ n′ with t(M) = t, the merged occurrences of D2.3(3)) and Step 4 (rate comparison). *Steps 3 and 4 are (12)–(13). The items left are closed in WILSON_IT1_SW_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §1–§6 (collar rule, sub-domain, q₀ = q₁, (LPG′)) and, for D2.6's kinds (γ) and species, the older-layer complex-data size and CRE's boundary-type potentials, in WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §2–§4 (status in its §8).*

### (12) Step 3 of (S): the rooted sums over the linked families (derived given the amendments (a)–(b), accepted by the user; from D2.2(6)(c)–(d), D2.3(3), D2.4(2)–(6), (10)–(11), V3 L2789–2806, L4579–4583, L4610–4615, FV Thm 5.4; (a)–(b) reviewed: no fatal finding; (c)–(g) not reviewed)

*(a) The size σ_{k+1} (the definition (3) uses; accepted by the user).* V3's σ(W) = n(W) + |ℋ(W)|/(LM): by V3 L4579–4581, n(W) is the sum of N₁ over the roots and over every constituent domain of every occurrence in W's cluster list, with multiplicities, a *cube* count, and |ℋ(W)| is the number of fine traversals of the hull, so |ℋ(W)|/(LM) is the hull length in units of the π₁-cube side LM (V3 L4583: the footprint F(W) of LM-cubes has |F(W)| ≤ C𝔰(W)). The consistent step-(k+1) size is
  σ_{k+1}(𝒢) := #_{k+1}(𝒢) + n_{k+1}(𝒢)/M,
  #_{k+1}(𝒢) the number of π_{k+1}-cubes of the constituents' domains (as in (3): marked vacuum domains, slot supports, the supports of the vacuum-link clusters of (c), the domains of the old groups and of the members of the merged old occurrences, with multiplicity) and n_{k+1}(𝒢) the number of step-(k+1) traversals of C in I(𝒢); M is the π_{k+1}-cube side in step-(k+1) units, so the arc term is the hull length in cube sides, as V3's. With this σ: (3)'s *decay statements survive* bullet holds as written (its second sentence is the definition); the vacuum weight θ_vac ≤ c#_{k+1} ≤ cσ_{k+1} holds; (7)'s and D2.3(4)(Z-d)'s a_min is ν/(32LM), since n_ins(𝒢) ≤ L·n_{k+1}(𝒢) ≤ LMσ_{k+1}(𝒢); and the reference-gas requirement θ_ref ≤ (ν/32)σ_{k+1} is the one (f) delivers. An old group W and each member of a merged old occurrence contributes #_{k+1}(X_W), the number of π_{k+1}-cubes of its domain as a set. (8)'s transport condition is c_πα_{1,k+1}M ≤ ν/64 (n_{k+1}(𝒢) ≤ Mσ_{k+1}(𝒢)).

*(b) The form of (S) that the induction needs: the reserve and the extent weights (amendment of D(k)(ii)–(iii) and of w_t; accepted by the user).* A merged old member W of a step-(k+1) group contributes its domain, its arc and the gap to its neighbours to σ_{k+1}(𝒢), and so to the fresh weight e^{θ_{k+1}(𝒢)} ≤ e^{νσ_{k+1}(𝒢)/16}, and its extent to X(𝒢) and t(𝒢). The class norm ν_n^{(k)} = Σ_{W∋n}e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ of D(k)(ii) has no decay in σ_k(W) or in the extent of W, so it cannot pay for either. V3 has both at step 1: Lemma c5-5-8(a) (pins for the t-family), V3 L2795–2796, is sup_ΔΣ_{W: Δ∈F(W)}e^{2νσ(W)}K_χe^{θ°(W)}b̄_W ≤ ε̄_t and the same per fine traversal n ∈ ℋ(W); the pins of V3 L2791 carry e^{4νσ(W)}M_Λ(W) with the first-order root weight ρ_t (M_Λ(W) = (1/k(W)!)∏_{Z∈𝒜(W)}ρ_tm(Z)∏_j|Φ|^+_Λ(𝔠_j), V3 L2674 and L2722, with m(Z) = ε_*e^{−λ_*ℓ₁(Z)}, V3 L2647, and |Φ|^+_Λ the incompatibility-tree sum of the m(D_i): a positive weight of the field-independent geometry, "defined without reference to the source data" (L2674), so the pins of L2791 hold at every background, real or complex; at complex 𝐔 the group bounds (c5-5-8) by M_Λ(W) hold with the extra factor e^{C_UL_fρ₁} ≤ e^{C′𝔰ρ₁}, IT-1 §3e(3.9), l.1075), and the ρ-size pins per traversal and per cube are (c4-6-5), V3 L4619–4622: sup_{n∈I_C}Σ_{W: n∈ℋ(W)}M(W)e^{4η𝔰(W)} + sup_{Δ∈π₁}Σ_{W: Δ∈F(W)}M(W)e^{4η𝔰(W)} ≤ Cρ (this is the base of (ii-w) and of the reference gas's c-version at k = 1); and V3 L2797–2798 give σ(W) ≥ dist(Δ, C)/(C_FLM) for Δ ∈ F(W), C_F = A_E the envelope constant (V3 L2789). The step-(k+1) count needs the same two weights inside the inductive norms, so D(k)(ii) and (iii) are amended: for every step-k traversal n of C (traversal positions only: a per-cube clause does not carry, the carry factor for cube positions being ~L², and the cube positions are recovered by the box reduction, (e)) and with the per-insertion sizes of (ii) (real points; the dev clause with b_WDev) and of (iii) (complex data),
  (ii-w) Σ_{W∋n}e^{θ(W)}e^{ν_rσ_k(W) + ν_e·ext_k(W)}·(size of W) ≤ C_χρ̃_k·(the right side of (ii)/(iii) at n),
  with the *reserve* ν_r := ν, the *extent weight* ν_e := ν/(2C_box) (D2.6 closure §6.5's budget, for review), and ext_k(W) := the least R ≥ 0 such that X(W) ∪ Q^#_W lies in the ℓ∞-ball of radius R π_k-cube sides about every traversal n of W's interval (for carried insertions, in the units of the step of production, as σ and θ, D2.0(3) *Units of carried groups*; both weights are then in those units too, and a step-(k+1) unit is L step-k units, which only helps below; the extent is measured in the step's own metric, so that the rate comparison (13)(a) reads ext_{k+1} ≥ C_boxt − 2, and C_box ≥ 4A_E, accepted by the user, so that ν_e ≤ ν/8; A_E = 9(2R_e+1)⁴ ≥ 729 since R_e = ⌈2max(R_h,L)/(LM)⌉ ≥ 1, V3 L4611, so SW closure's C_box ≥ 6 holds too). The dev species of (ii) is the one with the weights: wrap boxes do not lift, so the clause is stated for the dev parts only. The size σ_k is (a)'s at every step k ≥ 2 and V3's 𝔰 at k = 1. Consequences and base:
  - *Base, k = 1 (derived from V3 L2791, L2795–2798 and (5) *At step 1*).* By (E₁): X(W) ∪ Q_W lies within C_box(𝔰(W)+1) π₁-sides of every fine traversal of W, since the envelope is connected with at most A_E𝔰(W) cubes and contains the cubes of the hull (V3 Lemma c4-6-env, L4611 (a)–(b) and proof L4615: 𝔈(W) = 𝔑_{R_e}(X(W) ∪ π₁(ℋ(W))) is connected under maximum-norm adjacency with |𝔈(W)| ≤ A_E𝔰(W), so any two of its cubes are at index distance ≤ A_E𝔰(W) − 1, and it contains X(W) and the cube of every traversal of ℋ(W); quoted and checked) and the comparison box lies within C_boxLM𝔰 of n_f ((5) *At step 1*), once C_box ≥ A_E; C_box ≥ 4A_E is chosen. With ν_e := ν/(2C_box) (D2.6 closure §6.5), ν_e·ext ≤ (ν/2)(σ+1). So e^{ν_rσ + ν_e·ext} ≤ e^{νσ}e^{(ν/2)(σ+1)} = e^{ν/2}e^{(3/2)νσ}, and V3's e^{4νσ} pins cover it together with §3j(6)'s 2νσ and e^{θ°(W)} ≤ e^{νσ/16} (V3 L2806) and, at complex backgrounds, IT-1 §3e(4)'s e^{C′𝔰ρ₁} ≤ e^{η𝔰/8} (IT-1 l.1040; the factor at 𝐔 is IT-1 l.1075), leaving (5/16)νσ for the tail (D2.6 closure §6.5 *Budget*). At complex backgrounds (iii)'s rate (D2.6 closure §6.5 *(iii)'s complex rate*) is 61/16 with V3's θ°-booking, and with the weights 3/2 and §3j(6)'s 2 inside the sum it is 5/16 = η_w/η_V, not below η_w. At k = 1 the tail clause (ii-t) is derived from §3a(6)'s split 𝔰³e^{η𝔰/8} ≤ (j+1)³e^{η/8}e^{−(31/8)ηj}e^{4η𝔰} ((5) *At step 1*), which spends the whole e^{4η𝔰}; with the reserve and extent weights and §3j(6)'s share inside the sum the same split, with θ° at V3's 1/16 and the complex 1/8, gives 𝔰³e^{(1/16 + 1/8 + 2 + 1 + 1/2)η𝔰} ≤ C(j+1)³e^{−(5/16)ηj}e^{4η𝔰}. So the tail weight is weakened to
    w_t := (t+1)³e^{−η_wt}, η_w := (5/16)η_V, in place of (t+1)³e^{−(31/8)η_Vt} (D2.0(3) l.270 and (5)); w_t enters D2.0(3), (5) and (6) only through W_t = Σ_{t′≥t}w_{t′}, its monotonicity, Σ_tw_t < ∞ and "W_t decays exponentially" ((6)(ζ)), so nothing else changes; D2.0(3)(b)'s constant Σ_tw_t grows by (62/5)⁴.
  - *It carries (as (5) *It carries*; traversal positions only).* An old W keeps σ, ext, θ and β_W in its production units; t^{(k+1)}(W) ≤ t^{(k)}(W) as before. A step-(k+1) traversal n′ is met by L step-k traversals, so (ii-w) at step k+1 for the carried insertions follows from (ii-w) at step k with the factor L^{−1}(ρ̃_k/ρ̃_{k+1}) of (6), unchanged; the cube form would carry with a factor growing with L (~L²) and is not inductive.
  - *New groups.* (ii-w) at step k+1 for the groups made at step k+1 is the statement (S-w) below, which this (12) derives: for every position p at step k+1,
    (S-w) Σ_{𝒢: p ∈ X(𝒢) or p ∈ I(𝒢)}e^{θ_{k+1}(𝒢) + ν_rσ_{k+1}(𝒢) + ν_e·ext_{k+1}(𝒢)}S_𝒢ℓ_{X(𝒢)} ≤ C_S′ρ_{k+1}·(1 + (log g_k^{−2})^{(q₁−q₀)₊}),
    with ext_{k+1}(𝒢) defined as above with Q^#_𝒢 = X(𝒢) ((5)). (S) of (7) is (S-w) restricted to t(𝒢) = t and is (13). (S-w) also contains the c-version (|c_𝒢| ≤ S_𝒢) that the reference gas needs.
  - *Order of the derivation (not circular).* (e)–(f) first bound the sums with the weight e^{(ν/16 + ν_r)σ_{k+1} + ν_e·ext_{k+1}}S_𝒢ℓ without θ_{k+1}; the c-version then gives the Kotecký–Preiss condition for the reference gas with a°(𝒢) = (ν/32)σ_{k+1}(𝒢), hence θ_ref ≤ a° by FV (5.11) and θ_{k+1} = θ_vac + θ_ref ≤ νσ_{k+1}/16 for κ ≥ κ_D ((3)); so e^{θ_{k+1}} ≤ e^{νσ_{k+1}/16} and (S-w) follows.

*(c) The vertices and the links (from D2.2(6)(c)–(d) and D2.3(3); derived).* A step-(k+1) group 𝒢 is a set of *vertices* v of three kinds, with its arc I(𝒢) (D2.2(6)(d)'s union of linked hulls); the touched insertions and the vacuum links are part of the marked polymer's vertex:
- an *M-vertex* v = (M, 𝔅(M), 𝒱(M)): a marked polymer M of kinds (α)–(β) ((11)(a)), its touched old insertions 𝔅(M) := ∪_i𝔅(G_i) (the old insertions in the products of its slot groups, D2.2(4)(e)), and its *vacuum links* 𝒱(M): the pairs (T, 𝒞) of a vacuum cluster 𝒞 incompatible with M's domain and a factor that chose it in D2.2(6)(c) *Localisation* (a scalar link T ∈ 𝒮 with M ∈ T, or T = {M} for e^{−δΨ(M)}), or a vacuum occurrence merged at M (D2.2(6)(c), D2.3(3); as in D2.2(6)(d), a pair (T, 𝒞) lies in 𝒱(M_T), M_T the first M of T in one fixed order, and a vacuum occurrence 𝒞 at the first M of S_𝔐(𝒞); its multiplicity, at most 2^s, is counted by 2^s ≤ e^{d(𝒞)} below). Activity: |e^{−Ψ°(M)}H̃(M)|·∏_{(T,𝒞)∈𝒱(M)}(the weight of 𝒞 below), e^{−δΨ(M)} entering only through its links (D2.2(6)(c) *Localisation*), and H̃(M) containing ∏_{W∈𝔅(M)}Y_W ((11)(c)(β)); the touched insertions are counted once, inside the vertex. Support: M ∪ ∪_{W∈𝔅(M)}X_W ∪ ∪_{𝒱(M)}supp 𝒞; interval int(v) := I(M), the smallest arc of C containing the arcs I(G_i) of M's slot groups (D2.2(6)(d), D2.1(4)'s rule), which contains the touched intervals (D2.2(4)(e): I(G_i) contains the intervals of the insertions in G_i's product).
- a *W-vertex*: an untouched old insertion merged by the second rule, the third rule (hit by an old occurrence of 𝒢) or the hull rule; activity sup‖Y_W‖ at (U_{k+1}, ω), where D2.2(4)(e) evaluates an untouched insertion, in D(k)(iii)'s domain (D2.2(4)(f), (6)(e)); support X_W; interval its own.
- a *Γ-vertex*: an old occurrence of the step-k reference gas hitting a W of 𝒢 (a W-vertex or a touched W ∈ 𝔅(M)); activity |φ_k(Γ)| with D2.3(3)'s 1/m!; support the members' domains; its intervals are the members' arcs J_{W′}, and its window is the union of the members' windows, each with the member's own diam_{W′}.
*The vacuum factors.* For a scalar link T ∈ 𝒮, x_T := ε_TδΨ_≥(T) is a sum over the 𝒞 incompatible with every M ∈ T, |x_T| ≤ Σ_𝒞2|φ^T(𝒞)|_sup, so Σ_T|x_T| ≤ Σ_𝒞2^s·2|φ^T|_sup ≤ 2cΣ_M#(M) ((2)(g); s = |S_𝔐(𝒞)| ≤ 9#(𝒞), D2.2(6)(c) *Left to D2.4*); in the expanded form of D2.2(6)(c) *Localisation* a cluster 𝒞 is chosen by n_𝒞 ≤ 2^s factors (the T ⊆ S_𝔐(𝒞) with |T| ≥ 2 and the e^{−δΨ(M)}, M ∈ S_𝔐(𝒞)), each with |e^{±y_𝒞} − 1| ≤ u_𝒞 := |y_𝒞|_supe^{|y_𝒞|_sup}, |y_𝒞|_sup ≤ 2|φ^T(𝒞)|_sup, so the sum over the nonempty sets of factors choosing 𝒞 is (1 + u_𝒞)^{n_𝒞} − 1 ≤ n_𝒞u_𝒞e^{n_𝒞u_𝒞}; the factors e^{n_𝒞u_𝒞}e^{|y_𝒞|} are bounded over all 𝒞 ≁ M by exp(Σ_{𝒞≁M}(2^s + 1)u_𝒞) ≤ e^{5c#(M)} ((2)(g): (2^s + 1)u_𝒞 ≤ 4e^{2c}e^{d(𝒞)}|φ^T(𝒞)|_sup, and 4e^{2c} ≤ 5 since c = (9/64)e^{−4κ} ≤ 1/100) and absorbed into the flat factors: |e^{−Ψ°(M)}| ≤ e^{c#(M)}, so e^{6c#(M)} per M (e^{−δΨ(M)} enters only expanded), and each 𝒞 keeps the weight 2|φ^T(𝒞)|_sup with the choice of factors counted by 2^s ≤ e^{d(𝒞)} (no bound |x| ≤ 1 is needed). A vacuum occurrence, with factor e^{(s_𝔐(𝒞)−1)φ^T(𝒞)} − 1 (D2.3(3)), has |factor| ≤ 2·2^s|φ^T(𝒞)|_sup ≤ 2e^{d(𝒞)}|φ^T(𝒞)|_sup, sup over the domain. No deviation factor is needed in S_𝒢, since (4)'s Cauchy step supplies it for Y^d_𝒢 as a whole.
*Sizes.* #(v) is the sum of the cube counts #_{k+1} of the vertex's pieces (M, the X_W of the touched W and of the members, the supp 𝒞 of the vacuum links; an old group W contributes #_{k+1}(X_W), the union as a set); ext_v (π_{k+1}-sides, from the vertex's interval) is that of its support; and diam_v, in step-(k+1) bonds, is the largest ℓ∞-distance from a point of the support to int(v) (for a Γ-vertex, member by member). The *window* win(v) is int(v) extended along C by 20(diam_v + 1) bonds at each end, |win(v)| = |int(v)| + 40(diam_v + 1) (the interval need not lie in the support: an arc around a corner, or D2.2(4)(e)'s arcs, which reach touched intervals). With the arc covered by the windows (covering lemma below): σ_{k+1}(𝒢) ≤ Σ_v(#(v) + |win(v)|/M), ext_{k+1}(𝒢) ≤ Σ_v(ext_v + |win(v)|/M + 2), and #(X(𝒢)) ≤ Σ_v#(X_v) + 8n_{k+1}(𝒢)/M + 80 ≤ Σ_v(#(X_v) + 8|win(v)|/M + 80) (the gap cubes of I(𝒢) included; X_v the cubes meeting v's support, interval and box): from any n′ ∈ I(𝒢) every cube of X(𝒢) is reached along the arc and then within some vertex's extent. *Arc cubes (sharpens (4)'s 16n_{k+1}).* An arc of n step-(k+1) bonds of a square C has at most 5 straight pieces, and a straight piece of n_i bonds meets at most 8(n_i/M + 2) cubes of π_{k+1} (≤ n_i/M + 2 partition intervals along it, ≤ 2 in each transverse coordinate), so the cubes meeting an arc number at most 8n/M + 80.

*Links: pair relations, and the covering lemma.* v ~ v′ when (ii) their supports are incompatible (D2.3(3)'s hard core); (iii) one is a Γ-vertex hitting a W of the other; (iv′) an interval of v′ meets win(v), or an interval of v meets win(v′). The relation is defined on the vertices alone, as the tree bound needs (a segmentation of I(𝒢) doesn't work: it depends on 𝒢). *Lemma.* (1) On a square C the shorter arc between two points is at most twice their ℓ∞-distance (D2.2(6)(d) *Arcs*: on opposite sides the arc is ≤ 2·side ≤ 2·distance; on adjacent sides a + b ≤ 2max(a, b)). (2) If the supports of v, v′ meet or touch (as for (ii), and for (iii) when the member hits by contact; a hit by arc overlap gives overlapping intervals and (iv′) directly, and a hit through the member's own occurrences reduces to contact or overlap of constituents contained in X_{W′} and J_{W′}), a traversal of int(v) and one of int(v′) are within ℓ∞-distance diam_v + diam_{v′} + 1 (each support point is within diam of its vertex's interval), so by (1) the shorter of the two arcs of C between the intervals has length ≤ 2(diam_v + diam_{v′} + 1) ≤ 20(max(diam_v, diam_{v′}) + 1): it lies in the window of the one with the larger diam, whose window the other's interval meets, i.e. (ii), (iii) ⇒ (iv′). (3) Let 𝒢₁, …, 𝒢_m be the classes of 𝒢 under D2.2(6)(d)'s joined pairs (these are (ii)–(iii) pairs here; the M–𝒞 rule and the touching of a touched W are internal to the M-vertex, whose constituents all have the piece I(M); the second-rule pair of a touched W₀ ∈ 𝔅(M) and a vacuum-link pair are (ii) pairs, X_{W₀} and supp 𝒞 lying in supp v_M; two incompatible members of one occurrence are internal to the Γ-vertex and covered by (2) member by member), with linked hulls H_j, so that I(𝒢) is the union of the H_j, connected by overlaps, the hull rule adding the classes with an interval inside it. By (2) the shorter arc of each joined pair lies in a window of the pair; an enlargement at an end of H_j lies in the interval of the insertion that it reaches, which contains that end, a point of a window of 𝒢_j, so that insertion is (iv′)-linked to 𝒢_j; with these insertions added to 𝒢_j, H_j ⊂ ∪_{v∈𝒢_j}win(v). At an overlap point x ∈ H_i ∩ H_j: if x lies in an interval of 𝒢_j, that interval meets the window of 𝒢_i covering x, (iv′); likewise if x lies in an interval of 𝒢_i; otherwise x lies in a gap g_i of H_i and a gap g_j of H_j (maximal subarcs without intervals of the class, bounded by intervals at both ends), two overlapping arcs, so one contains an end of the other or they share an end — the interval of 𝒢_j (say) adjacent to that end meets H_i ⊂ ∪_{𝒢_i}win, again (iv′). So every group is (ii)–(iv′)-connected, I(𝒢) ⊂ ∪_v win(v), and length(I(𝒢)) ≤ Σ_v|win(v)|. Distinct groups may be linked: the tree bound needs only that each group is a linked-connected family; the hard core across groups is the reference gas's (D2.3(3)).

*(d) Shares, charges and the budgets (derived).* Put ν_f := ν/16 + ν_r = 17ν/16, and ν_e = ν/(2C_box) ≤ ν/8 (C_box ≥ 4A_E ≥ 4, (12)(b)). Per vertex, the *share* sh(v) := ν_f(#(v) + |win(v)|/M) + ν_e(ext_v + |win(v)|/M + 2) + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(v)| + ℓ(v) (ℓ(v) the ℓ_X part below), so that (ν/16 + ν_r)σ_{k+1}(𝒢) + ν_e·ext_{k+1}(𝒢) ≤ Σ_v sh(v) by (c), and the transports ‖T_𝐔(I(𝒢))‖ ≤ e^{c_πα_{1,k+1}n_{k+1}(𝒢)} ≤ ∏_ve^{c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(v)|} ((8), (c)) are inside the shares. The unit of #_k is the π_k-cube, nested in its π_{k+1}-cube (RG1's partitions), and M/L is the π_k-side in step-(k+1) units. For an M-vertex, #(v) is the sum over its pieces, a point of M is within M#(M) + max_{W∈𝔅(M)}diam_W of I(M) (M is connected, (10); I(M) contains a fine bond of C in a component of M, or a touched interval whose X_W meets one), a point of a touched X_W within diam_W (its interval lies in I(M)), and a point of a 𝒞, which touches M, within M#(𝒞) + 1 more; so diam_v ≤ M#(M) + Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}(diam_W + 1) + Σ_{𝒱(M)}(M#(𝒞) + 1), |win(v)| ≤ |win_M| + Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}40(diam_W + 1) + Σ_{𝒱(M)}40(M#(𝒞) + 1) with |win_M| := n_{k+1}(M) + 40(M#(M) + 1), and sh(v) ≤ sh(M) + Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}sh(W) + Σ_{𝒱(M)}sh(𝒞), with:
  - *M:* #(M) cubes (M ⊇ D′ ⊇ D ⊇ slots, (10)(a)); ext_M ≤ #(M) + 8n_{k+1}(M)/M + 81 + max_{W∈𝔅(M)}(ext_k(W)/L + 1) (M's cubes, the arc cubes, the boxes of its touched insertions, whose intervals lie in I(M)). *The arc of M.* I(M) is the smallest arc containing the I(G_i) = ∪_{Y∈G_i}I(Y), I(Y) the smallest arc containing S′(Y) (Y's fine bonds of C and the intervals of the touched W with X_W meeting Y), enlarged at each end by at most one old interval (D2.2(4)(e)). A smallest arc H of a set S has length ≤ 4·diam_ℓ∞(S) (D2.2(6)(d) *Arcs*), and each point of H is within ℓ∞-distance 2diam_ℓ∞(S) of S (half of a gap of H). With S_M := ∪_YS′(Y), diam_ℓ∞(S_M) ≤ M#(M) + 2max_{W∈𝔅(M)}(diam_W + |int(W)|) (a touched W's support reaches S_M through its interval), and diam_W + |int(W)| ≤ (M/L)σ_k(W) (below): every point of ∪_iI(G_i) is within 2diam(S_M) + max_W|int(W)| of S_M, so n_{k+1}(M) ≤ 4(5diam(S_M) + 2max_W|int(W)|) ≤ 20M#(M) + 48(M/L)Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}σ_k(W). With ℓ(M) (below), for M ≥ 100, sh(M) ≤ ν_f(142#(M) + 11) + ν_e(221#(M) + 84) + (3ν_f + 9ν_e)(48/L)Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}σ_k(W) + ν_eΣ_{W∈𝔅(M)}(ext_k(W)/L + 1) + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win_M|.
  - *W* (old; and each member W′ of an old occurrence, in its Γ-vertex's share): its domain X_W is contained in the closed-connected set X_W ∪ (cubes meeting I(W)) of at most #_k(X_W) + 8n_k(W)/M + 80 ≤ 8σ_k(W) + 80 π_k-cubes (*Arc cubes* of (c) at step k), and a closed-connected union of N π_k-cubes meets at most 16 + 128N/L cubes of π_{k+1} (L ≥ 6): cut a closed spanning walk of 2(N−1) steps between vertex-adjacent cubes into ≤ 8N/L + 1 pieces of ≤ ⌊L/2⌋ − 1 steps; the cubes of one piece lie in an ℓ∞-ball of radius < ½ π_{k+1}-side, which meets at most 2⁴ cubes. So #_{k+1}(X_W) ≤ 16 + 10240/L + 1024σ_k(W)/L; |int(W)| = n_k(W)/L and diam_W ≤ (M/L)#_k(W) (the support, connected and of #_k(W) π_k-cubes of side M/L step-(k+1) bonds, meets W's interval), so diam_W + |int(W)| ≤ (M/L)σ_k(W) (at k ≥ 2 by (4): each constituent meets I(𝒢) and is counted in σ_k; at k = 1 V3's domains lie within 2L fine units of ℋ, a factor 1 + O(1/M) absorbed below), so |win(W)| ≤ 41(M/L)σ_k(W) + 40; and ext_W = ext_k(W)/L. With ℓ(W) (below), sh(W) ≤ ν_f(16 + 10240/L + 80/M) + (1106ν_f + 41ν_e)σ_k(W)/L + ν_e(1/8 + 1/L)ext_k(W) + ν_e(2 + 40/M) + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(W)|, hence sh(W) ≤ (ν_r/4)σ_k(W) + (ν_e/8 + ν_e/L)ext_k(W) + c_sh + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(W)|, c_sh := 2ν_f(16 + 10240/L + 41/M) + 2ν_e(2 + 41/M), for L ≥ L₀ := 5000 ((1106·17/16 + 41/8)/L₀ ≤ 1/4 with ν_e ≤ ν/8). A lower bound on L only, accepted by the user (as is κ_D′); it is compatible with (6)'s (1+β₀)⁸ < L, (f)'s 8(1+β₀)^{15} ≤ L (8·1.5^{15} < 3504 for β₀ ≤ 1/2) and V4's L ≥ 3(d+3), and the rate of (10) is r = (1 − 5δ)λ_Lκ = (L+2)κ/4 for L ≥ 486 ((10)(b): λ_L = L/2 there and δ = (1−2/L)/10). (c_sh, the share constant, is not the reference activity c_𝒢 of a group; that is written c_𝒢 below, also for the trivial group {W} and for the step-k groups W′.)
  - *Γ-vertex:* sh ≤ Σ_{W′∈Γ}((ν_r/4)σ_k(W′) + (ν_e/8 + ν_e/L)ext_k(W′) + c_sh + c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(W′)|) (the members' domains, arcs and windows are the vertex's).
  - *𝒞:* #(𝒞) cubes, a point within M#(𝒞) + 1 of M, ext ≤ #(𝒞) + 1 beyond M's: with ℓ(𝒞), sh(𝒞) ≤ 2(83ν_f + 86ν_e)#(𝒞) ≤ #(𝒞)/4 for ν ≤ 1/1000.
  - *The polynomial ℓ_{X(𝒢)}.* ℓ_X ≤ 2#(X(𝒢)) ((4)) ≤ 1 + Σ_v(2#(X_v) + 16|win(v)|/M + 160) by (c) ≤ ∏_{pieces}(1 + 2#(X_piece) + 16|win_piece|/M + 160) (1 + Σa_i ≤ ∏(1 + a_i); the pieces of an M-vertex are M, its touched W and its 𝒞, with |win_W| := |win(W)| and |win_𝒞| := 40(M#(𝒞) + 1)), with #(X_M) ≤ #(M) + 8n_{k+1}(M)/M + 81, #(X_W) ≤ (2ext_k(W)/L + 3)⁴ (the box, which contains W's support and interval cubes; 4-dimensional, (5) *At step 1*), #(X_𝒞) ≤ #(𝒞). Then (2x/L + 3)⁴ ≤ C_ℓe^{(ν_e/8)x} for the boxes and 1 + y ≤ C_τe^{τy}, τ := ν_f/16, for the rest: ℓ_X ≤ ∏_{pieces}C_pce^{ℓ(piece)}, C_pc := 483C_τmax(C_τ, C_ℓ) (an M or 𝒞 piece takes C_τ² for its two terms, a W piece C_τ for its window and 1 + 2(2x/L + 3)⁴ ≤ 3C_ℓe^{(ν_e/8)x} for its box), ℓ(M) := (ν_f/8)(#(M) + 8n_{k+1}(M)/M + 81) + ν_f|win_M|/M, ℓ(W) := ν_f|win(W)|/M + (ν_e/8)ext_k(W), ℓ(𝒞) := (ν_f/8)#(𝒞) + ν_f|win_𝒞|/M, which the bounds above include. The constant C_pc per piece multiplies the activities in w̃ ((e)): for old pieces it is absorbed by the smallness of the old rooted sums (g_K small), for M in C_M ((f)).
  *Transports.* The transport part of the shares: c_πα_{1,k+1}|win(W)| ≤ (ν_r/16)σ_k(W) + 40c_πα_{1,k+1} once c_πα_{1,k+1}·41M/L ≤ ν_r/16; c_πα_{1,k+1}|win_M| ≤ ν_f#(M) + (ν_r/64)Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}σ_k(W) + 40c_πα_{1,k+1} once c_πα_{1,k+1}·60M ≤ ν_f and c_πα_{1,k+1}·48M/L ≤ ν_r/64; c_πα_{1,k+1}|win_𝒞| ≤ ν_f#(𝒞) once c_πα_{1,k+1}·80M ≤ ν_f. All hold under c_πα_{1,k+1}·4096M ≤ ν (α_{1,k} = g_kC₁(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁} → 0, R-m, with L, M, ν fixed before g), and the per-piece constant e^{40c_πα_{1,k+1}} ≤ e is absorbed in the activities. (11)(c)(β)'s a′-transports along the slot arcs are a separate factor ∏_{W∈𝔅(M)}e^{(ν_r/16)σ_k(W)} inside |H̃(M)| (under its c_πα_{1,k+1}·20M/L ≤ ν_r/16), paid from the touched W's reserve below. *Charges.* b(W) := (ν_r/4)σ_k(W) + (ν_e/4)ext_k(W) + c_sh; b(Γ) := Σ_{W′∈Γ}b(W′); b(𝒞) := #(𝒞)/2; b(M-vertex) := (κ_s/4)#(G_M) + (r/648)#(M∖D′) + 1 + Σ_{W∈𝔅(M)}b(W) + Σ_{𝒱(M)}b(𝒞) (with (10)'s counting constant 324: e^{−rd_{k+1}(M;D′)} ≤ e^{−(r/324)#(M∖D′)}e^{20c_Dr#(G_M)}, since #(M∖D′) ≤ 80#(D′) + 324d and #(D′) ≤ 81c_D#(G_M)). *The reserve budget of an old W:* of the weight ν_rσ_k(W) in (ii-w), sh(W) takes ν_r/4, its transports ν_r/16, b(W) ν_r/4, the window counts of (e) ν_r/8, the interaction weight of (e) (carried by every member of an occurrence) ν_r/16, (11)(c)(β)'s a′-transports of a touched W ν_r/16, and the share parts an M-vertex passes to its touched W ν_r/16: (3ν_f + 9ν_e)(48/L)σ_k(W) + (ν_r/64)σ_k(W) ≤ (ν_r/16)σ_k(W) for L ≥ L₀; in all 7/8, with ν_r/8 to spare. *The extent budget of an old W:* sh(W)'s ν_e/8 + ν_e/L, b(W)'s ν_e/4, the box reduction's ν_e/4 ((e)) and the M-vertex's ν_e/L (ext_M's touched boxes): ≤ (5/8 + 2/L)ν_e ≤ ν_e. The M-vertex's constant ν_e per touched W is a per-piece constant, absorbed as ℓ_X's.

*(e) The rooted sums and the Kotecký–Preiss condition (derived).* Positions: a step-k traversal n or a π_k-cube (W- and Γ-vertices), a step-(k+1) traversal n′ or a π_{k+1}-cube (M-vertices, and every vertex at the step-(k+1) positions of (f)); "v at p" means p in an interval of v for a traversal, p ∈ X_v for a cube. w̃(v) := C_v·|activity|_sup·e^{sh(v)}, C_v the product over the pieces of ℓ_X's C_pc and the transports' e^{40c_πα_{1,k+1}} ((d)), one factor C_pc′ := C_pce^{40c_πα_{1,k+1}} per piece; the rooted sums ε_p := Σ_{v at p}w̃(v)e^{b(v)} include the window and box spares named below. A step-(k+1) traversal contains L step-k traversals and a π_{k+1}-cube L⁴ π_k-cubes, so at step-(k+1) positions the old sums are ε̄^{old} := max(Lε^{old}_t, L⁴ε^{old}_c), and likewise ε̄^{ref,k}:
- *ε^{old}_t, ε^{ref,k}_t per step-k traversal*: from (ii-w) at step k and its c-version, since sh(W) + b(W) + (the transports, the interaction weight, the a′-transports and the M-vertex's share parts of (d), ν_r/16 each)σ_k(W) ≤ (3/4)ν_rσ_k(W) + (3/8 + 1/L)ν_e·ext_k(W) + 2c_sh: the activity's decay beyond this is e^{−(1/4)ν_rσ_k(W)}e^{−(5/8 − 1/L)ν_e·ext_k(W)}, of which ν_r/8 pays the window positions below and ν_e/4 the box reduction. At complex data the size is (5)'s, O(ρ_k·pl) away from older layers; at k = 1 the base is (c4-6-5), (12)(b), whose majorant M(W) bounds the reference activities a_W as well (V3 L4608: "the physical and reference data having the same numerical majorant"), so that (c4-6-5) is also the c-version at k = 1.

*(f) The tree bound with the root at the position, ε^{new}, and the recursion in k (derived).* T(v) := Σ over finite rooted trees at v in the linking graph of (c) of ∏_{v′≠v}w̃(v′); by the depth induction (FV Thm 5.4's mechanism, in the rooted-tree form; T_0 = 1, the depth-d sums increase to T), T(v) ≤ exp(Σ_{v′~v}w̃(v′)T(v′)) ≤ e^{b(v)/2} under (e). Every linked family has a spanning tree rooted at any of its vertices, and the hard core only removes terms. *Marked sums.* Let m be a weight on a class of vertices with sup_pΣ_{v′ at p}m(v′)e^{b(v′)} ≤ ε_m, and F(v) := the tree sum at v with exactly one marked non-root vertex, whose w̃ is replaced by m. The mark is in exactly one child subtree, so F(v) ≤ T(v)·Σ_{v′~v}[m(v′)T(v′) + w̃(v′)F(v′)], and by induction on the depth F(v) ≤ λb(v)e^{b(v)/2}, λ := 2ε_m/ε_*, where ε_* is (e)'s threshold, (positions of v)·ε_* ≤ b(v)/2 for every v: Σ_{v′~v}m(v′)e^{b(v′)} ≤ (ε_m/ε_*)b(v)/2 and Σ_{v′~v}w̃(v′)b(v′)e^{b(v′)/2} ≤ Σ_{v′~v}w̃(v′)e^{b(v′)} ≤ b(v)/2 (xe^{x/2} ≤ e^x), so F(v) ≤ e^{b(v)/2}b(v)(ε_m/(2ε_*) + λ/2) ≤ λb(v)e^{b(v)/2}. *The root.* Fix a position p at step k+1: a traversal n′ ∈ I(𝒢), which lies in win(v₀) for some vertex v₀ of 𝒢 by the covering lemma of (c), or a cube of X(𝒢), which lies in X_{v₀} for some v₀ or is a gap cube of I(𝒢), met by ≤ 4M + 4 traversals n′. Root 𝒢 at v₀. The root's position is paid by the window or cube sums of (e) with the root's own spare (only e^{b(v₀)/2} of e^{b(v₀)} is used by T), not by the shares, which already dominate e^{ν_fσ + ν_e·ext}. *Amendments proposed by the D2.6 closure §5.2 ((T2) and (T0) accepted by the user): (T2) the slots' factor e^{−(κ_s−3)#(G_M)} of (11)(d) is split as e^{−ν_e#(G_M)}e^{−(κ_s−ν_e−3)#(G_M)} before (11)(d), which then runs with κ_s − ν_e in place of κ_s, under κ_s − ν_e ≥ κ_s^{(12)}, so that e^{−ν_e} per π_k-cube of the components is reserved, unspent by (c)–(e), for the carry lemma's bridges, which lie in the components (closure §5.2 (T1)); (T0) the rooted sums at an old vertex are run in the lineage form: the old att members of a term are its fixed data, and F(v₀) ≤ λb(v₀)e^{b(v₀)/2} bounds the sum over the new constituents rooted at v₀; (e)–(f) are re-run over the vertex set of the new constituents and the dev old members; a (β)-vertex touching only fixed att members adds at most K′b_n/ᾱ_n per position (n the step of the re-run, b_n/ᾱ_n as in (11)(c)(β), K′ closure §5.2's constant), so ε^{new}_{re-run} ≤ C_Mb_n·pl + K′b_n/ᾱ_n, which is first order in g and serves (e)'s smallness only, not an amplitude ρ_{n+1}; (e)'s condition needs K′b_n/ᾱ_n below its threshold, a smallness of g under (R-m′); the merged untouched W of closure 5.5 are rooted here with b(W) = (ν_r/4)σ(W) + c_sh.* Three classes:

*ε^{new}, the marked-polymer sum, with the (α)/(β) split.* Fix a position: a π_{k+1}-cube Δ (ε^{new}_c) or a step-(k+1) traversal n′ (ε^{new}_t). An M-vertex at Δ has Δ ∈ M, or Δ meeting I(M), or Δ in the box of a touched W ∈ 𝔅(M) (which contains X_W), or Δ ∈ supp 𝒞 of a vacuum link. Its data: the domain M ∈ 𝐃_{k+1} (wall-connected); D′ ⊂ M; the π_k-cubes of D′ that are the components Y, grouped into the G_i; the touched insertions 𝔅(M); the arc I(M) (determined by the slots' arcs and the touched intervals, D2.2(4)(e)); the vacuum links 𝒱(M). With #(M) ≤ #(M∖D′) + 81c_D#(G_M) ((11)(d)), n(M) ≤ #(G_M), the charge b(v) and the share sh(M) of (d), and two units of exponent per cube of M reserved, one for (e)'s window decay and one for (13)(c)'s vacuum tail, a cube of M∖D′ costs log 64 + log 2 + 2 + 1/8 + 24c + 143ν_f + 221ν_e (≤ 8 for ν ≤ 1/1000) against r/324 − r/648 = r/648, and a π_k-cube of the components costs 20c_Dr + 81c_D(L⁴log2 + log 128 + 2 + 1/8 + 24c + 143ν_f + 221ν_e) + log 4 + 1 + 1/8 against κ_s/2 − κ_s/4 = κ_s/4; the sums over #(G_M) ≥ 1 and #(M∖D′) ≥ 0 then converge to a constant, and
